# Implementation Decisions for Review

**Status:** Informative. This log records implementation choices that need human review; it does not amend the normative language design.

## Open for review

### IR-001: Build the reference implementation incrementally from Stage 0

- **Decision:** Start with source locations and diagnostics, then implement the Stage 0 frontend and tools in dependency order. Do not create placeholder crates for later phases before they have a concrete implementation.
- **Reason:** The repository initially contained only specifications. A working, testable vertical slice gives each later phase a real foundation and avoids a workspace that appears complete while containing empty compiler phases.
- **Review:** Confirm this incremental crate rollout is the preferred interpretation of the fixed workspace architecture in `docs/impl/01-architecture.md` §1.3.

### IR-002: Use Unicode data crates for normative identifier handling

- **Decision:** Pin `unicode-ident` 1.0.18, `unicode-normalization` 0.1.24, and `unicode-general-category` 1.1.0, whose tables all use Unicode 16.0.0. The lexer uses them for XID properties, NFC checks, and Lu/Lt case classification. Permit these crates and `unicode-normalization`'s `tinyvec` dependency in the shipped dependency list.
- **Reason:** Rust's standard library does not expose the required XID, NFC, and General Category operations. Pinning one Unicode data version makes identifier behavior portable and reproducible; implementing partial tables locally could accept or reject names inconsistently.
- **Review:** Confirm the Unicode 16.0.0 baseline for v0.1. Future data upgrades must update all three crates together, amend the lexical spec and dependency allowlist, and rerun Unicode-focused conformance tests.

### IR-003: Use crate tests as the development gate before conformance exists

- **Decision:** Until the conformance suite and harness exist, gate each implementation milestone on its focused crate tests, workspace tests, formatting, and an independent adversarial review. Do not make a language conformance claim during this period.
- **Reason:** The documented merge gate requires a complete Stage 0–4 claim, but the repository has no test suite, harness, compiler, or executable yet. The full gate cannot be meaningful for the initial implementation commits.
- **Review:** Decide whether the eventual CI should add an explicit bootstrap gate and at what point it should switch to the normative conformance gate in `docs/impl/15-conformance-and-testing.md` §15.12.6.

### IR-004: Defer Stage 0 package discovery semantics until the CLI milestone

- **Decision:** Implement the frontend independently of package discovery first. Before implementing `sai check` or `sai new`, reconcile the Stage 0 command requirements with the Stage 3 package contract and Stage 4 default target.
- **Reason:** The architecture needs package identity and a source directory to load files, while the roadmap assigns the full package contract to Stage 3. Implementing package resolution early would either duplicate the later package system or silently create an incompatible subset.
- **Review:** Define the Stage 0 behavior for reading `sai.package`, external dependencies, and the `sai new` template before claiming Stage 0 conformance.

### IR-005: Keep diagnostic JSON values internal and deterministic

- **Decision:** Represent diagnostic data with an internal JSON value enum and ordered maps; do not add `serde_json` to the shipped compiler.
- **Reason:** `docs/impl/11-diagnostics.md` models `data` with `serde_json::Map`, while `docs/impl/01-architecture.md` §1.3.2 rule 4 limits shipped dependencies and explicitly places `serde_json` among test-only dependencies. Ordered maps also make output ordering explicit and deterministic.
- **Review:** Confirm the diagnostic chapter should describe this internal value model, with the specified deterministic JSON form produced by the compiler's own writer.

### IR-006: Split structural fix validation from syntax-aware planning

- **Decision:** `sai_diagnostics` validates operation schemas and structural edit constraints. A `FixOperationPlanner` must provide both the operation's resulting file bytes and the minimal text edits; `Fix::new` applies those edits to the snapshot and rejects the fix unless both results match exactly. Parsing, node containment, CST rewriting, and format-unit formatting belong to the syntax-aware planner. Non-built-in effect sets require a `CanonicalEffectSet` populated from resolved identities; duplicate atom identities and ambiguous output spellings are rejected.
- **Reason:** The fixed crate graph places `sai_diagnostics` below `sai_syntax` and `sai_semantic`; adding either dependency here would create a cycle. The planner boundary lets the lower diagnostics layer check semantic/text parity without duplicating CST logic. Resolved effect identity is a trusted compiler-internal input because the resolver is above this crate.
- **Review:** Confirm the eventual semantic planner is the only production implementation of `FixOperationPlanner`, derives identities from actual resolution, and tests each operation against both result channels before exposing fixes.

### IR-007: Bind fixes and diagnostics to immutable source snapshots

- **Decision:** A `Fix` captures its complete primary `Location` and an opaque VFS snapshot ID. Construction checks the primary and every edit's file identity, canonical path and package identity, per-document version, raw-file bounds, UTF-8 boundaries, and CR LF boundaries against the same immutable source set. A `Diagnostic` retains that source snapshot, validates all primary, secondary, related, lint-level, and cause spans when bound, and cannot be rebound after fixes are attached.
- **Reason:** Diagnostic spans and edits use raw file byte offsets, while the frontend uses decoded-text offsets; the VFS retains raw bytes and provides explicit decoded-to-original range conversion. Document versions are per-file, so only the opaque ID can bind a multi-file set without comparing unrelated version numbers.
- **Review:** This VFS value currently represents the consistent file/document set only. Extend its session identity to include the package graph, lock state, target, profile, test options, and other §12.1.2 inputs when the package and query layers are implemented; the driver must create a fresh identity whenever any such input changes. Verify the producer also includes all required package and manifest documents, and keep path/version parsing aligned with the package crate.

### IR-010: Keep effect identities as trusted semantic inputs

- **Decision:** `CanonicalEffectSet` validates uniqueness and ordering from the identities and output spellings supplied to it; the type does not independently resolve source names or authenticate identity strings.
- **Reason:** Name resolution and WIT identity are owned by higher crates, and importing those crates into `sai_diagnostics` would violate the dependency graph. This value is an internal handoff from the semantic resolver, not a parser for untrusted external identity claims.
- **Review:** Keep construction at the resolver/operation-planner boundary and ensure production callers never synthesize identity strings from source spelling or aliases.

### IR-008: Start with a copy-only stable-address arena

- **Decision:** Keep `Arena<T: Copy>` as thread-safe stable storage and add `TypeArena<T: Copy + Eq + Hash>` for structural interning. `TypeArena` returns the same stable address for equal values with consistent `Hash`/`Eq`, including concurrent calls; a separate append-only Box owner keeps every returned address live even if a key violates that trait contract.
- **Reason:** The type arena needs pointer equality to mean structural equality and must allow query workers to intern in parallel. Both types restrict entries to `Copy` to avoid destructor-order hazards for values that may refer to other arena allocations; the mutex-backed implementation favors a simple safety argument over allocation throughput.
- **Review:** Benchmark contention and memory use against compiler workloads. If future HIR/type values require destructors, design and test a destructor-safe arena before storing them.

### IR-009: Use a safe radix tree for lock-free interner reads

- **Decision:** The append-only symbol vector uses a sparse radix tree of `OnceLock` slots. Interning initializes a slot once; resolving a published symbol reads initialized slots without taking a lock.
- **Reason:** This satisfies the documented lock-free read behavior without an external atomic-pointer crate or unsafe append-only vector access. Insertions still serialize briefly while assigning dense session IDs.
- **Review:** Measure memory and lookup costs under the symbol counts and concurrency expected by the reference compiler; retain this representation only if it meets the stated compilation targets.

### IR-011: Require dependency source references to agree on package identity

- **Decision:** A `dep:<identity>/<path>` source path is accepted only when the separate `package` field is exactly the same `<identity>`.
- **Reason:** The diagnostic source-reference contract carries both fields, and allowing them to name different packages can misattribute a diagnostic while still passing snapshot validation.
- **Review:** Confirm this equality rule matches every dependency and embedded-library source reference produced by the driver.

### IR-012: Bound numeric prerelease identifiers by `u64`

- **Decision:** Treat a purely numeric SemVer prerelease identifier as `Num` for the resolver rule, and reject it if it exceeds `u64`; clarify the package-contract wording to match.
- **Reason:** The resolver grammar says every `Num` fits in `u64`, while the package lexer summary previously omitted the bound for prerelease components.
- **Review:** Confirm the package parser, resolver, and lock reader all share this same version rule.

### IR-013: Keep source text and offset metadata immutable

- **Decision:** Make `SourceFile` identity, decoded text, BOM state, and line index private; expose read-only accessors. Reject decoded text that starts with an unrecorded BOM.
- **Reason:** Decoded-to-original offset conversion depends on those fields matching the retained raw bytes. Publicly replaceable fields and an unrecorded leading BOM allowed callers to invalidate that mapping after construction.
- **Review:** Check downstream crates use the read-only API and do not need to construct or mutate partial source snapshots.

### IR-014: Keep lexical query diagnostics independent of opaque snapshot IDs

- **Decision:** Lexical diagnostics carry the source path, package, document version, and validated raw span, but remain unbound to the VFS snapshot object. `lex()` preserves that query value; `lex_with_fixes()` binds at the snapshot-aware boundary and plans token-level fixes for normalization, unnecessary raw prefixes, lone `!`, and semicolons. Before binding, it confirms the candidate file's raw bytes exactly match the bytes lexed.
- **Reason:** The opaque snapshot ID is a freshness token, not lexical input. Retaining it in a query value would make equal source text produce unequal diagnostics across otherwise identical snapshots and defeat deterministic incremental equality. Comparing immutable source bytes at the later boundary prevents stale fix intents from being rebound to changed content.
- **Review:** Ensure the diagnostic collection boundary binds or validates every location before applying snapshot-sensitive operations, and that future full-session snapshots cover all semantic inputs. Extend token-level planning to the remaining lexical fixes as their parser operations are implemented.

### IR-015: Keep forbidden-character names in a closed local table

- **Decision:** Map every code point admitted by the three forbidden-character classes to its Unicode name in `sai_syntax`; include both `U+XXXX` and the name in a diagnostic note.
- **Reason:** The language defines a finite forbidden set, and a closed table avoids a runtime Unicode-name dependency while keeping diagnostic spelling stable across Rust toolchains.
- **Review:** Check the table against the Unicode 16.0.0 source data and retain coverage for every code point admitted by `forbidden_class`.

### IR-016: Preserve literal boundaries for malformed UTF-8

- **Decision:** When a decoded U+FFFD replacement comes from malformed UTF-8 inside a quote-delimited literal, keep the enclosing `STRING`, `BYTE_STRING`, `CHAR`, or `BYTE` token and set `HAS_ERROR`. Outside literals, emit an `ERROR` token. Report `INVALID_UTF8` once from decoding and do not add a second literal diagnostic for the same bytes.
- **Reason:** Splitting a quote-delimited literal around a replacement character would destroy the token boundary the parser uses to recover it as one expression. The literal token's existing `HAS_ERROR` contract lets later phases suppress value-dependent diagnostics while preserving the source structure.
- **Review:** Confirm the parser, AST literal accessors, and HIR lowering all honor `HAS_ERROR` and never interpret a replacement-containing literal as valid. Revisit if conformance requires a distinct `ERROR` token inside literals.

### IR-017: Check indentation on whitespace-only multiline-string lines

- **Decision:** Treat every non-empty multiline-string content line, including a line containing only spaces or tabs, as subject to the base-indentation prefix check. Use the exact bytes before the closing delimiter as the required prefix; additional indentation after that prefix is allowed.
- **Reason:** The lexical specification exempts empty lines and requires every non-empty line to start with the base indentation. Applying that wording literally avoids silently exempting lines that contain source bytes and keeps indentation removal deterministic.
- **Review:** Confirm whitespace-only lines should follow the same prefix rule as lines with content, and confirm that base indentation is a required prefix rather than an exact count of leading whitespace.

### IR-018: Keep green-tree operations safe for deep syntax

- **Decision:** Compute each green node's structural hash from its children, compare nodes and find their last token iteratively, and empty uniquely owned child lists iteratively during destruction. Intern nodes with at most three children by structural child values; leave larger nodes uncached.
- **Reason:** Left-associative expressions can produce trees whose depth grows with token count even when parser nesting is bounded. Recursive hashing or destruction can overflow the native stack. Address-only cache keys avoid recursive work but fail to intern equal parents around structurally equal, uncached larger children; cached child hashes keep ordinary construction linear while structural equality resolves collisions exactly.
- **Review:** Retain the 10,000-level equality, hashing, token lookup, and drop regression test. Profile the cached-hash and small-node-key costs on representative source files before expanding interning to larger nodes.

### IR-019: Preserve lexer token discriminants when adding syntax nodes

- **Decision:** Append CST node kinds after `EOF` in the `#[repr(u16)]` `SyntaxKind` enum, and treat `ERROR` as both a raw token kind and a recovery node kind.
- **Reason:** Existing lexer token discriminants remain unchanged for consumers that store or compare their numeric representation. `ERROR` is emitted for malformed source and is also the documented wrapper for parser recovery.
- **Review:** Keep `is_token()` tied to the token range ending at `EOF` and keep `is_node()` explicitly admitting `ERROR`; update the boundary and error-tree tests when adding token kinds or recovery nodes.

### IR-020: Permit zero-child nodes only for recovery errors

- **Decision:** Reject every zero-source-width green node except `ERROR` and the root `SOURCE_FILE` of an empty file. Delimited list and block nodes must contain at least one real opening or closing delimiter token.
- **Reason:** An empty file necessarily has a zero-width root and EOF, while a semantically empty delimited list still has source delimiters. Synthetic NEWLINE or EOF tokens cannot stand in for the list's source text.
- **Review:** Keep coverage for the empty-file root, a zero-width `ERROR`, an empty `ARG_LIST` with and without unrelated text, and a correctly delimited empty list. When parser recovery is added, ensure missing delimiters are represented without manufacturing source text.

### IR-021: Index red tokens by source text range

- **Historical decision, superseded in part by IR-055:** Build a root-wide green-node/token interval index lazily on the first `token_at_offset` call, then use binary search within the queried node's token interval. Create red node handles only for the returned token's parent path. Ignore zero-width synthetic `NEWLINE` tokens but keep zero-width `EOF` addressable. The token-offset index remains lazy, but IR-055 now eagerly builds a compact node/child index and its red handles to support allocation-free structural AST accessors.
- **Reason:** Several synthetic tokens can share one source offset. Returning the first two inclusive matches can hide EOF, while scanning descendants from the start makes repeated editor lookups quadratic. NEWLINE has no source text to point into; EOF is useful at the end-of-file position.
- **Review:** Keep tests for adjacent token boundaries, trivia gaps, an EOF sharing an offset with NEWLINE, lookup scoped to a descendant, and sorted index construction. The remaining eager node/child index and handle memory cost is tracked for human review under IR-055.

### IR-022: Make red-tree diagnostics and teardown shallow

- **Historical decision, superseded in part by IR-055/056:** Format red nodes as a shallow summary and iteratively release parent chains. The earlier implementation stored only green metadata in the offset index and cached strong red handles on lookup. The current implementation eagerly caches handles in the compact node/child index, keeps the token-offset index lazy, and stores no cache back-reference in `NodeData`.
- **Reason:** Derived debug output recursively follows green children or red parents, and a strong red-parent chain can recurse during destruction. Caching lazily materialized red paths avoids repeated deep reconstruction without making the entire red tree eager; keeping `NodeData` independent of its cache prevents a reference cycle.
- **Review:** Retain deep debug/drop regression tests and verify that token-indexed red nodes release cleanly both when handles outlive the root wrapper and when the cache is dropped. IR-056 records the current reverse-preorder cache teardown and no-allocation shared-handle drop behavior.

### IR-023: Reject invalid parser token streams and marker lifecycles

- **Decision:** Validate that parser input contains only raw token kinds followed by one final EOF; require ordered, in-bounds UTF-8 token spans; allow zero-width ranges only for NEWLINE, EOF, and error-marked FSTRING_END; and require EOF to be at the end of the source. Require every marker to complete or abandon in LIFO order, accept only node kinds at completion, undo a child's forward-parent link when its wrapper is abandoned, and return an error if events leave tokens unconsumed.
- **Reason:** The tree builder consumes a lossless, structurally valid event stream. The empty error-marked FSTRING_END follows the documented f-string recovery algorithm, while limiting the empty-token exception to that kind and flag keeps ordinary raw tokens from being fabricated. Catching invalid inputs and marker misuse at the parser boundary prevents false progress, mismatched node spans, and successful event extraction that can only fail during tree construction.
- **Review:** Keep regression tests for composite kinds in raw input, zero-width ordinary tokens, error-marked and unmarked zero-width FSTRING_END tokens, nonempty NEWLINEs, malformed or out-of-order spans, out-of-order completion, abandoning a preceding marker, invalid completion kinds, unfinished markers, and EOF consumption. The parser validates token range order and UTF-8 boundaries; the tree builder remains responsible for proving that tokens and trivia partition the source exactly.

### IR-024: Bind expected-token errors to snapshot files

- **Decision:** Build `EXPECTED_TOKEN` diagnostics only when a parser is created from a `SnapshotFile`. Convert the current decoded token span back to original-file byte offsets through `Location::from_decoded_source`; emit no detached diagnostic when snapshot context is missing, and report at most once per token position.
- **Reason:** The parser reads decoded text and token ranges, while diagnostics and edits use original bytes. Snapshot-file context supplies the file path, package, document version, and decoding map required to attribute the error correctly, including BOM and malformed-UTF-8 cases.
- **Review:** Keep tests for token retention after an expectation fails, one diagnostic for repeated expectations at the same position, alternative-token messages, empty/invalid expected sets, composite-token consumption, and decoded-to-original location conversion. Revisit the one-per-position rule when implementing `UNEXPECTED_NEWLINE` and recovery diagnostics so their precedence and suppression remain deterministic.

### IR-025: Omit absent module headers and reject unimplemented item forms

- **Decision:** Do not create a zero-width `MODULE_HEADER` when a file has neither inner docs nor an `unsafe module` declaration. The current source-file parser handles module headers and use declarations; other item kinds return `UnsupportedItem` instead of being wrapped as apparently valid syntax.
- **Reason:** Green-tree invariants reject empty nodes, and treating unimplemented declarations as arbitrary tokens would create a misleading CST. The typed boundary lets the grammar grow incrementally without misclassifying valid future forms as user syntax errors.
- **Review:** Confirm the omission of an absent `MODULE_HEADER` matches AST consumers, and decide whether `UnsupportedItem` should remain an implementation error or become a diagnostic once all Stage 0 item forms are dispatched.

### IR-026: Keep attribute parsing generic and syntax-only

- **Decision:** Parse item attributes with the recursive `AttrArg` grammar, retain identifiers as `NAME_REF` nodes, and defer attribute-name, target, and argument validation to later compiler stages.
- **Reason:** The grammar explicitly makes attribute arguments generic, while the implementation plan assigns the closed name set and per-attribute rules to validation. This allows the parser to preserve unknown attributes for tooling without embedding semantic policy in syntax parsing.
- **Review:** Confirm `NAME_REF` is the expected AST representation for attribute names, keys, and word values before adding typed attribute accessors.

### IR-027: Synchronize a missing use-list close at the next line-start item

- **Decision:** When a selective-use list reaches a top-level item after a physical line break, leave that item unconsumed and report the missing `}`. Recognize the boundary through `AFTER_LINE_BREAK` when the preceding comma prevents the lexer from producing a `NEWLINE` token.
- **Reason:** Commas are not newline-ending tokens in the lexer, but they can precede a malformed list followed by a valid top-level declaration. Preserving the following declaration limits recovery to the malformed list and keeps later syntax available, including item forms whose parser is not implemented yet.
- **Review:** Check recovery with comments, attributes, visibility, and newly added top-level item starters; retain coverage for both emitted `NEWLINE` and `AFTER_LINE_BREAK` synchronization paths.

### IR-028: Demote misplaced doc comments at their parser context

- **Decision:** Emit `MISPLACED_DOC_COMMENT` warnings over the original source bytes and demote misplaced outer and inner doc tokens to line-comment trivia. Keep outer docs in `DOCS` only when attached before an item and inner docs in the module header only at file start.
- **Reason:** Placement depends on grammar context, and demotion preserves exact source text while preventing invalid documentation from appearing as attached API docs.
- **Review:** Extend placement handling as block, field, variant, and expression grammars are added; each context must distinguish valid attachment from a misplaced comment.

### IR-029: Bound the first function-parser slice and recursive grammar depth

- **Decision:** Parse top-level function signatures, generic and associated type bounds, function clauses, effectful function-pointer types, and bodies supported by the initial block/expression slice (IR-030). Restrict associated-type bindings to immediate generic arguments of trait-reference bounds. Emit `MISSING_FN_BODY` for a free function without a body. Preserve valid syntax in `FN_DECL` when clauses are duplicated or out of order, while reporting `DUPLICATE_CLAUSE` or `CLAUSE_ORDER`; leave an unparenthesized `where` bound's following `effects` clause attached to the function. Accept `pub export` before a function as redundant visibility, retain both tokens in `VISIBILITY`, and report `REDUNDANT_PUB_EXPORT`. Accept attributes on parameters and generic parameters to retain their syntax, but report `INVALID_ATTRIBUTE_TARGET` once at the first attribute of each sequence. Keep `requires`/`ensures` and expression forms outside the initial slice behind `UnsupportedSyntax`, abandoning active markers so incomplete grammar is not presented as a valid CST. Limit recursive type, attribute-argument, expression, and block parsing to 512 levels, recover into an `ERROR` node, preserve synchronization tokens and following syntax, and report `IMPLEMENTATION_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` once per file.
- **Reason:** The parser should build truthful nodes for implemented syntax and avoid presenting unparsed body or contract tokens as valid expressions. The depth limit follows the frontend resource-limit contract and keeps hostile nesting from exhausting the native stack while preserving later source for recovery.
- **Review:** Parameterize body requirements when trait, interface, and extern contexts are parsed; attach the specified machine-applicable clause-reordering fix and the delete-`pub` fix for `REDUNDANT_PUB_EXPORT` after parser diagnostics receive the workspace snapshot and CST node identity needed by the fix planner; and verify the shared depth counter covers every recursive grammar routine added later. The diagnostics crate defines the `reorder_clauses` operation, but this parser slice cannot yet attach a snapshot-validated fix to its diagnostic.

### IR-030: Parse the first useful block and expression subset

- **Decision:** Parse newline-separated blocks containing `let` with a single binding or `_`, `var`, `assert`, assignment, and expression statements. Parse scalar literals (excluding f-strings), single-segment names, `self`/`Self`, unit/group/tuple expressions, nested blocks, unsafe blocks, `return`, prefix operators, and Pratt binary/range operators at the precedence in D-SYN-05. Diagnose missing initializers/expressions and chained comparisons/ranges; preserve `&&`/`||` as composite tokens while reporting `USE_AND_OR`. Parse `as` into a recovery-only `CAST_EXPR` so later type checking can report `NO_AS_CAST`. Parse generic arguments on a primary path using the D-SYN-04 speculative type parser (IR-031). Keep arrays, field/call/index postfixes, closures, implicit members, and control-flow expressions behind `UnsupportedSyntax` until their productions are implemented. When a mandatory operator operand is missing at a newline, add guidance to the expected-expression diagnostic. When an expression-ending statement is followed by a binary-only operator on the next line, emit `UNEXPECTED_NEWLINE` with a note and wrap the operator continuation in one `ERROR` node. Keep a zero-width missing-expression `ERROR` outside `EXPR_STMT` so parser recovery does not create an invalid empty node. On a missing statement separator, skip balanced nested delimiters and preserve the enclosing block's `}`.
- **Reason:** This subset lets common function bodies form useful CSTs while retaining a hard boundary for valid constructs whose grammar is not yet implemented. Recovery-only casts follow the frontend plan and retain enough syntax for a later type-aware diagnostic. Explicitly returning `UnsupportedSyntax` prevents unimplemented postfix, implicit-member, and control-flow forms from being misreported as valid syntax. Operator-continuation recovery produces one targeted error without cascading into a missing left operand. The missing-operand note tells users how this grammar treats a continued expression, while keeping the existing zero-width recovery-node invariant intact. Balanced recovery keeps malformed statements from consuming nested blocks or the enclosing function body.
- **Review:** Attach the machine-applicable operator-move and missing-operand fixes once parser diagnostics have the workspace snapshot and syntax-aware fix planner; add destructuring patterns and let-else validation; complete postfix, array, f-string, closure, control-flow, and condition-restriction grammar; implement statement attributes and item-in-block diagnostics; and keep delimiter recovery and the shared 512-level limit covered as recursive forms are added. Verify that same-position diagnostic coalescing keeps `EXPECTED_EXPRESSION` more specific than `EXPECTED_TOKEN`, and verify casts reach the eventual `NO_AS_CAST` type-check diagnostic.

### IR-031: Speculate over expression generic arguments with the type grammar

- **Decision:** At a joint `<` immediately after an identifier in a primary path, parse `GENERIC_ARG_LIST` with the existing type grammar and accept it only when parsing emits no diagnostics and the next token is in D-SYN-04's follow set. Disable associated-type bindings for expression arguments. Keep successful syntax as a child of `PATH_EXPR`. On failure, restore the token cursor, parser events, expected-token state, marker state, and nesting-limit state, then parse `<` as a comparison; memoize failed speculation positions.
- **Reason:** Type-aware speculation follows D-SYN-04 and rejects malformed or impossible type arguments without blocking valid comparisons. Parsing into the existing `GENERIC_ARG_LIST` node reuses type syntax and produces a faithful CST. Restoring all parser state prevents speculative diagnostics or marker events from leaking into the committed comparison parse; memoization bounds repeated attempts at the same candidate.
- **Review:** Verify speculative rollback across every parser state that future grammar routines add and measure worst-case work on long comparison chains; add generic arguments after member segments when postfix parsing is implemented. Calls and other postfix operations after a successfully parsed generic path remain `UnsupportedSyntax` until that grammar is added.

### IR-032: Parse postfix and array expressions

- **Decision:** Parse postfix field/member access, generic arguments after member names, decimal tuple indices, `.await`, calls, indexing, and `?` at binding power 25. Parse `.UIDENT` as an implicit member expression and bracketed comma-separated expressions as arrays. Preserve positional, named, and base call arguments as `ARG`, `NAMED_ARG`, and `BASE_ARG`; report `BASE_ARG_NOT_LAST` when a later argument follows a base argument. Report `INVALID_TUPLE_INDEX` when the integer token is not plain decimal digits or has a leading zero. Sort diagnostics returned by tree construction by primary span, severity, code, and message.
- **Reason:** These productions reuse the existing path/generic, Pratt parser, name-reference, delimiter-validation, and expression-recovery mechanisms. Sorting the diagnostics after event collection handles cases where a parser discovers an error at a later token before it can determine that an earlier base argument is non-final; this keeps the syntax result source ordered without complicating grammar speculation. List recovery preserves unmatched enclosing closers and newline boundaries so malformed nested delimiters do not consume later constructs.
- **Review:** Keep syntax tests for chained postfix forms, generic methods, implicit members, arrays, malformed and empty argument slots, tuple-index spellings, base-argument order, nested delimiter recovery, and diagnostic ordering. `NAMED_ARGUMENT_ON_FUNCTION` and whether named/base arguments target a valid record constructor require later validation or name resolution; `.await` stage gating likewise belongs to the later feature-validation pass. Verify diagnostic spans and error recovery for malformed member, index, and argument lists as those validations are implemented.

### IR-033: Parse recursive binding and constructor patterns

- **Decision:** Parse recursive patterns in `let` statements, including binding modes, wildcards, `@` patterns, alternatives, literals, inclusive and exclusive ranges, tuple/group patterns, qualified constant paths, constructors, implicit members, slice patterns, and rest patterns. Represent a pattern with a leading `|` as `OR_PAT` even when it has one alternative so the leading token remains inside a syntax node. Wrap a bare constructor path in `CTOR_PAT` and retain its path as `CONST_PATH`. Add the parser diagnostics `FLOAT_PATTERN` and `MULTIPLE_REST_PATTERNS`.
- **Reason:** The documented grammar distinguishes syntax using token case and immediate lookahead, so the parser can build the full shape without deciding whether a path resolves to a constant or constructor. The syntax tree needs an owner for an optional leading alternative token; `OR_PAT` provides that owner. Duplicate bindings, record punning, constructor-vs-constant resolution, and binding-set equivalence across alternatives depend on semantic information and remain later validation work.
- **Review:** Keep tests for every pattern family, malformed/incomplete ranges, integer-only signed literals, float literals and endpoints, final constructor rest without a trailing comma, repeated slice rest, and balanced/unclosed nested-depth recovery that preserves following statements. Check that semantic validation later enforces duplicate-binding and or-pattern binding consistency, and that constructor resolution applies record punning only to record constructors.

### IR-034: Parse conditional and if-let expressions

- **Decision:** Parse `if Condition Block (else (IfExpr | Block))?`, with an optional physical newline before `else`, and retain the `if` condition and else branch in `CONDITION`, `LET_CONDITION`, and `ELSE_BRANCH` nodes. Enforce the no-leading-block condition restriction with `BLOCK_IN_CONDITION`; clear it inside parentheses, arrays, call/index arguments, and blocks. Preserve optional open-range and `return` operands at the following body brace, while still requiring an inclusive range endpoint and parsing required binary operands.
- **Reason:** This implements the documented conditional grammar while preserving braces as unambiguous condition/body boundaries. Separate condition and else nodes retain the source structure needed by later AST/HIR lowering. The shared parser depth limit also bounds recursive `else if` chains.
- **Review:** Keep coverage for `if`, `if let`, `else if`, line-broken `else`, direct and parenthesized blocks, delimited block operands, optional and required range operands, `return`, and over-deep chains. Attach the parenthesizing fix to `BLOCK_IN_CONDITION` when syntax-aware fix planning is available. Complete the sibling control productions (`match`, loops, jumps, and closures) separately.

### IR-035: Parse match expressions and arm lists

- **Decision:** Parse a restricted `match` scrutinee followed by a brace-delimited `MATCH_ARM_LIST`; parse each arm as a pattern, optional `MATCH_GUARD`, `=>`, and expression. Accept comma or newline separators, including a trailing comma, and preserve empty arm lists for later exhaustiveness validation. If malformed arm recovery reaches a confirmed block-statement boundary before the arm list's closing brace, retain the remaining list tail in an `ERROR` node and match nested braces by depth so each closing brace keeps its structural owner. If recovery reaches a later top-level item before a closing brace, diagnose the missing arm-list and containing-block closers at that boundary, retain a zero-width `ERROR` node for each missing closer even when same-position diagnostics coalesce, then resume parsing the item at file scope. Unexpected closing delimiters already at file scope are retained as `ERROR` tokens with `EXPECTED_TOKEN`.
- **Reason:** Pattern parsing is now available for arm heads, while the parser remains syntax-only and does not decide exhaustiveness, reachability, or guard typing. Separate list, arm, and guard nodes preserve the source boundaries needed by later HIR lowering. Keeping malformed source in recovery nodes prevents it from being presented as a valid arm. Nested brace depth preserves delimiter ownership; a following top-level item is a synchronization point when malformed source has omitted one or more closing braces.
- **Review:** Keep tests for empty and nested matches, guarded arms, block bodies, comma/newline separators, missing separators and recovery, scrutinee block restrictions, and source preservation after malformed arms. Verify exhaustiveness and unreachable-pattern checks in the semantic phase.

### IR-036: Parse loops, labels, and jumps

- **Decision:** Parse `loop`, `while`/`while let`, and `for`/`for await` with optional `LABEL ':'` prefixes into `LOOP_EXPR`, `WHILE_EXPR`, `FOR_EXPR`, and `LOOP_LABEL` nodes. Parse `break` with an optional target label and same-line value, and `continue` with an optional target label. Apply the no-leading-block restriction to `while` conditions and `for` iterables. Accept `for await` syntax for source preservation and defer its Stage 5 feature check.
- **Reason:** These productions are fully described by the current grammar and can be represented without type or control-flow context. The parser preserves labels and jump operands while leaving outside-loop jumps, undeclared or shadowed labels, invalid loop break values, irrefutable-pattern checks, and feature gating to later semantic validation.
- **Review:** Test labeled and unlabeled loops, `while let`, iterator patterns, optional jump operands, line-ending behavior, direct versus parenthesized blocks in conditions and iterables, and `for await`. Verify loop target resolution, closure/async boundaries, duplicate labels, break-value legality, and Stage 5 feature diagnostics in later phases.

### IR-037: Parse closures and async blocks

- **Decision:** Parse `move? fn(...) (-> TypeNoFx)? Block` into `CLOSURE_EXPR`, with `CLOSURE_PARAM_LIST` and `CLOSURE_PARAM` nodes for `var? (LIDENT | _) (: Type)?`. Parse `async move? Block` into `ASYNC_BLOCK_EXPR` as Stage 5 syntax. Reject `async fn` and `async move fn` in expression position with `NO_ASYNC_CLOSURE`, retaining the closure subtree under an `ERROR` node.
- **Reason:** The documented forms can be represented syntactically without inferring closure signatures, captures, effects, or function boundaries. Async blocks are retained for later feature validation, while the explicit prohibition on async closures has a dedicated diagnostic instead of cascading into a missing-block error.
- **Review:** Test parameter and return-type forms, same-line closure headers, move closures, async blocks, malformed lists, both forbidden forms, and deep-nesting recovery. Adversarial review led to synchronizing a missing parameter-list close at the body brace; the missing-comma case already retains the next valid parameter and now has a regression test. A second adversarial pass found no remaining parser or recovery findings and confirmed the fix deferral. The specified `NO_ASYNC_CLOSURE` rewrite fix depends on a syntax-aware planner and a real workspace snapshot: parser diagnostics currently retain only a `SnapshotFile`, and the green tree does not assign the CST `NodeId` required by `FixOperationPlanner`. Do not fabricate an expression ID from a token offset or bind a fix to a synthetic one-file snapshot; either would make the operation invalid for the caller's snapshot. Add the fix with the parser's snapshot-aware fix pipeline and CST identity support. Verify capture inference, loop/return boundaries, and Stage 5 feature gating in later phases.

### IR-038: Parse contextual region expressions

- **Decision:** Recognize `region LIDENT Block` as `REGION_EXPR` only when a non-raw `LIDENT` spelled `region` is followed immediately by `LIDENT` and `L_BRACE` tokens. Represent the region binder as `NAME` and parse its body with the existing block grammar. Keep `region` as an ordinary path expression in every other expression context.
- **Reason:** The specification makes `region` contextual to this exact expression-start lookahead rather than a reserved keyword. Reusing the existing identifier and block productions preserves lexical behavior and captures the binding/body structure without embedding arena allocation, borrow checking, or effect semantics in the parser.
- **Review:** Test a declared value named `region`, bare and raw `region` paths, a region expression in an `if` condition, and the binder text/order before the body block in the CST. Adversarial review prompted the binder and raw-identifier assertions; the follow-up found no remaining findings. Keep malformed-body and nesting recovery covered; verify arena desugaring, the implicit `borrow Arena` binding, escape checks, and the `alloc` effect in later semantic stages.

### IR-039: Lex and parse format strings as syntax

- **Decision:** Lex `f"..."` into `FSTRING_START`, interpolation delimiter/expression/spec tokens, optional `FSTRING_TEXT` segments, and `FSTRING_END`. Keep the first text run with `FSTRING_START` and the final text run with `FSTRING_END`. Treat `:name(INT)` as one contiguous raw token, while allowing trivia after it before `}`. Parse the sequence into `FSTRING_EXPR` with an `FSTRING_INTERP` node per interpolation, and parse each interpolation with the ordinary expression grammar. Do not lower or type-check format strings in the syntax crate. On an unterminated string or interpolation, retain any final text in an error-marked `FSTRING_END`; when there is no final text, emit an empty, error-marked `FSTRING_END` before the line break or EOF. Permit that single empty token form in parser input validation.
- **Reason:** The token sequence makes interpolation expressions visible to tooling while preserving every source byte as either a token or trivia. The empty end token follows the implementation plan's §2.4.7 recovery algorithm and keeps the lexer/parser view of an unterminated f-string consistent; placing it before line-break trivia also preserves newline generation when the last expression token is an operator. Limiting the empty-token exception to an error-marked `FSTRING_END` preserves the existing invariant for every other raw token. Delimiter matching is scoped to the current interpolation so malformed inner closers cannot change enclosing expression state. Error recovery uses the string escape scanner and shared bidi checks. Keeping semantic desugaring and feature gating out of the syntax layer follows the Stage 3 boundary.
- **Review:** The contiguous-only `:name(INT)` spelling is a deliberate reading of its one-token grammar; trivia after that token remains allowed. Hostile reviews found and prompted fixes for empty end tokens, escaped quotes during malformed-spec recovery, interpolation delimiter isolation, forbidden characters in malformed spec errors, expression recovery, unexpected-token diagnostics, and EOF token lookup when an empty end shares the offset. A further review found that an invalid numeric width such as `pad(01)` reported `MALFORMED_NUMBER` without marking the enclosing `FSTRING_SPEC`; the lexer now propagates `HAS_ERROR`, with a regression test. The final hostile review found no remaining issues. A review also proposed rejecting non-bidi foreign whitespace inside f-string text; this was not applied because spec/02 §2.1 item 4 permits foreign whitespace inside string literals while forbidding bidi controls everywhere. The implementation documentation and a test record this boundary; malformed format-spec syntax remains subject to the ordinary source rules. The tree and red-offset documentation covers the permitted empty end token. Implement Format/format-spec validation, desugaring, and Stage 3 feature diagnostics in the semantic pipeline.

### IR-040: Diagnose item declarations and attributes in block statement position

- **Decision:** Detect item starters at the beginning of a block statement, including named functions, contextual declaration keywords with their grammar lookahead, visibility modifiers, and sequences of attributes. Prefer `ITEM_IN_BLOCK` over `INVALID_ATTRIBUTE_TARGET` when an attribute sequence leads to an item. Parse item forms already supported by `item` and wrap the result in `ERROR`; for unimplemented item productions, retain the declaration through balanced token recovery in `ERROR` and resume at the next block separator or enclosing `}`. For an attribute sequence before a non-item statement, emit one `INVALID_ATTRIBUTE_TARGET` at the first `@`, wrap the attributes in `ERROR`, then parse the statement normally. Do not classify `fn(` as an item; it begins a closure expression.
- **Reason:** This follows spec/15 §15.2.1 and impl/02 §2.5.5 while preserving later block statements and source text when the declaration grammar is not implemented yet. Keeping unsupported top-level item handling unchanged avoids converting an implementation limitation into a language diagnostic.
- **Review:** Test supported nested functions, closure disambiguation, item attributes, invalid attributes before declaration and expression statements, contextual item starters, balanced recovery of unsupported declarations, and preservation of later statements and items. Hostile reviews found that recovery must recognize declaration-specific newline `where` clauses and bodies, including `unsafe` prefixes, while an effect's `covers` set must not consume a separate block expression. A second pass found that an unclosed generic header could absorb later statements and that malformed attributes needed item lookahead again after attribute recovery. Recovery now resets angle depth at a body brace and synchronizes on clear later statement/item starts; attribute diagnostics are deferred until the parser determines whether the recovered sequence leads to an item. Each finding has a regression test. The suggested nested-function rewrite requires a CST node identity and syntax-aware fix planner, which are not available in the current parser boundary; record and add that fix with the snapshot-aware parser fix pipeline rather than fabricating an edit. Continue implementing the remaining item productions; this diagnostic/recovery layer must use their normal parser as each is added.
- **Follow-up:** Hostile review found later expression statements could be swallowed while declaration generic parameters remained unclosed, and a valid `const` parameter could be mistaken for an item. Recovery now tracks whether a declaration generic parameter is expected, validates type-parameter lookahead (so a constructor call such as `Some()` synchronizes as an expression), and recognizes expression starts that cannot continue the generic syntax even at nested angle depth. Type, enum, trait, distinct, and impl generic headers are covered. Regressions cover integer and constructor-call statements, a following const item, multiline const generics, nested generic bounds, effect sets in bounds, and associated-type bindings. Recovery keeps angle tracking active across nested `=` bindings and distinguishes an `effects {…}` set from the declaration body. Generic, `unsafe`, and `async` nested functions do not receive a plain-closure suggestion: Help directs them outside the block. Public or exported nested functions receive the same treatment so a local closure cannot silently discard visibility or export status. Safe, synchronous, non-generic, private, non-exported nested functions receive an example such as `let inner = fn(..) { .. }`. A machine-applicable edit still needs CST identity and snapshot-aware parser fix planning. Another review report said `type Alias =` was split from a next-line RHS; inspection showed `=` is not in the lexer's NEWLINE end-token set, so the line break is suppressed and the unsupported declaration already retains its RHS. Alias and const tests pin this behavior. The hostile review found that the help lookahead skipped `pub` and `export`; the parser now records both modifiers and has regression coverage including `pub export`. A second hostile pass found attributes at expression-list elements were not diagnosed and an attribute before a later statement could be swallowed by malformed generic recovery. Attribute handling now wraps call, array, and tuple expression-list attributes in `ERROR` nodes, and generic-item recovery synchronizes before an attributed statement. Regressions cover positional, named, base, array, and tuple elements plus the malformed generic reproduction. A third hostile pass found the base-argument ordering lookahead did not skip an intervening attribute, so it missed `BASE_ARG_NOT_LAST`; lookahead now skips valid attribute sequences and a regression checks that both diagnostics are emitted. The normative block-item guidance now limits the closure suggestion to declarations whose signature and visibility a closure can preserve.
- **Final review:** A fresh hostile review of the completed diff found no actionable issues.

### IR-041: Parse type aliases and record declarations

- **Decision:** Dispatch `type` items to a `TYPE_DECL` production that parses the name and optional generic parameters, then an `ALIAS_DEF` after `=` or a `STRUCT_DEF` with an optional `WHERE_CLAUSE` and a brace-delimited `FIELD_LIST`. Fields accept outer docs, attributes, optional `pub`, and the existing type grammar. Aliases accept an optional `REFINEMENT_CLAUSE`. Add `INVALID_EXPORT_TARGET` and diagnose `export` on non-function items while preserving their syntax; `pub type` remains valid. Stop generic-parameter and generic-argument parsing at a non-closing newline. If a comma suppresses that newline token, also synchronize before a physical line-start item or statement that cannot be a valid generic parameter/argument, preserving valid generic parameters and function-pointer type arguments. Let the block parser resume a statement after item-in-block recovery stops at that boundary.
- **Reason:** These productions are the next implemented item in impl/02 §2.5.4 and spec/03 §3.6. Reusing generic, where, type, and expression parsing keeps their grammar consistent. The parser preserves syntactically valid generic refined aliases for the later validation diagnostic. Record field and incomplete-header recovery preserve docs, attributes, source bytes, and following items/statements. The extra physical-line check accounts for the lexer's continuation rule after commas, preventing a malformed generic list from swallowing a following function or block statement.
- **Review:** Cover simple and generic aliases, refinements, empty and populated records, where clauses, field docs/attributes/visibility, comma/newline separators, nested item-in-block wrapping, malformed generic headers, and `INVALID_EXPORT_TARGET`. Verify the parser leaves the next item or statement intact after incomplete record syntax. Hostile review found that `type Broken<T,\nfn later() {}` and a malformed generic argument list could swallow the following function because the comma suppresses the `NEWLINE` token. Recovery now checks physical line-start flags plus item lookahead; regressions cover both forms. The first fix misclassified a valid multiline `const` generic parameter, so the boundary excludes syntactically valid generic-parameter starts and distinguishes a const item by its top-level initializer `=`. Further passes found the same issue for block statements after the comma, including `let`, constructor-call and method-call expressions, unsafe blocks, and closures. Generic-list recovery now recognizes statement starts that cannot be valid type/constant arguments, validates qualified type-path segments before treating an identifier path as a type, and lets the block parser resume statements after item-in-block recovery. Its `fn(...)` lookahead distinguishes a function-pointer type, including a return type and effects, from a closure body; it keeps a closure body even when the optional return type is missing. Regressions cover these cases, valid qualified paths and multiline function-pointer type arguments, plus later statements after recovery. A final fresh hostile review found no actionable issues.

### IR-042: Parse enum declarations and variants

- **Decision:** Parse `enum` items as `ENUM_DECL` with optional generic parameters and `WHERE_CLAUSE`, followed by a `VARIANT_LIST`. Each `VARIANT_DECL` contains its uppercase name, optional tuple or record shape, and optional signed integer `DISCRIMINANT`. Distinguish record shapes by `LIDENT ':'` after `(`. Parse variant field attributes but diagnose them as `INVALID_ATTRIBUTE_TARGET`. Diagnose `export enum` as `INVALID_EXPORT_TARGET`, while allowing `pub enum`. Diagnose `EXPLICIT_DISCRIMINANT_ON_DATA_ENUM` once per enum at the first explicit `=` when that enum has any payload variant. If a closing brace is missing, synchronize at a following physical-line statement or item so the block parser can continue.
- **Reason:** Enum declarations are the next item production in impl/02 §2.5.4 and spec/03 §3.6. The existing type and attribute parsers keep variant payloads aligned with other type syntax. This repository has no separate CST validation pass yet, while the discriminant rule is local to one enum and can be checked without name resolution, so the parser tracks only whether a payload variant and an explicit discriminant occurred. Reporting once at the earliest discriminant gives one actionable location without duplicating an enum-level rule across its variants. Since uppercase constructor calls are also valid expressions, recovery recognizes a shaped variant only when its shape begins with a record-field prefix or a nonempty type start; this lets a following `Some()` expression survive a missing enum brace. Line-start recovery preserves later statements and the original source text after an incomplete list.
- **Review:** Cover empty and generic enums, a `where` clause, unit/tuple/record variants, docs and attributes, signed discriminants, the enum-level discriminant diagnostic and its range/count, invalid export and field attributes, malformed lists, and missing-close recovery. The first hostile pass found an infinite loop when a record payload was missing `)`, loss of following statements when a tuple payload was missing `)`, and uppercase calls misclassified as variants during enum-brace recovery. Recovery now stops at enclosing braces and physical statement boundaries; tuple lookahead rejects empty or non-type payload starts, and regression tests preserve `Some(1)`, later literals and `let` statements. The second hostile pass found the same ambiguity for a named call with a non-type argument (`Some(value: 1)`) and a closure mistaken for a function-pointer type after a missing tuple close. Record lookahead now checks for a valid type start, and tuple recovery uses the existing closure-body lookahead; regressions cover both reproductions. The third hostile pass found closure-valued constructor arguments, unsafe blocks mistaken for types, and malformed record fields rejected before parsing. Lookahead now distinguishes closure bodies and `unsafe fn` types from unsafe blocks, while record-vs-tuple disambiguation uses the specified `LIDENT ':'` prefix independently of whether the field type is present. Regressions cover each case. The fourth hostile pass found parenthesized expression calls treated as tuple payloads, `(1)` not synchronized as a statement start, and malformed `V(1)` variants discarded without a diagnostic. Parenthesized type lookahead now follows nested type starts; a call with a clearly expression-shaped payload becomes a recovery boundary only when a later physical-line statement follows, keeping malformed variants in the CST and diagnosing their bad payloads. Regression tests cover nested calls, `(1)` recovery, and the diagnostic. The fifth hostile pass found that nested parentheses were recursively inspected outside the parser nesting limit, type-looking paths followed by operators were accepted as payload starts, and malformed calls repeatedly scanned the remaining tokens. Lookahead now scans nested parentheses iteratively, requires path-type payloads to end at a tuple separator, and uses a precomputed closing-parenthesis index for constant-time call matching. Regression tests cover 10,000 nested parentheses, `Some(Foo + 1)`, and repeated unclosed tuple variants. The sixth hostile pass found borrow expressions such as `Some(borrow value + 1)` mistaken for variants; the same ambiguity applied to a `Self` path followed by an operator and to consecutive expression-shaped calls. Lookahead now validates `borrow`/`owned` operands and `Self` path endings, and iteratively checks consecutive uppercase calls before synchronizing at a later statement. Regression tests cover both prefix expressions in consecutive calls. The seventh hostile pass found stack overflow risk from alternating borrow/parenthesis nesting, a parenthesized type prefix followed by an expression operator, and quadratic scans over consecutive expression-shaped calls. The lookahead now peels prefixes and matched parentheses iteratively, checks the complete grouped type boundary and expression operators, precomputes matching braces for function-pointer effects, and caches each call-chain boundary result. Regression tests cover 10,000 nested prefixes, `Some(borrow (Self) + 1)`, and a 512-call chain. The final post-fix hostile review attempt did not complete; the regressions and workspace checks are recorded above.

### IR-043: Parse distinct type declarations

- **Decision:** Dispatch contextual `distinct` only when followed by an uppercase name. Build `DISTINCT_DECL` with optional generic parameters, `=`, optional inner `pub`, a base type, and an optional `REFINEMENT_CLAUSE`. Preserve outer `pub` visibility, diagnose `export distinct` through `INVALID_EXPORT_TARGET`, and keep generic refined distinct declarations in the CST for later validation. When the base type is missing before another item, emit `EXPECTED_TOKEN` and preserve the following item.
- **Reason:** `DistinctDecl` follows enums in spec/03 §3.6. The inner `pub` controls constructor and `.value` visibility, while the outer visibility controls the nominal type's visibility; retaining them in their grammar positions avoids conflating those meanings. Generic refinement and the rule against exposing `pub` representation on a private type require semantic context and remain for validation. Physical line breaks after `=` and inner `pub` are suppressed by spec/02 §2.10 because neither token is in the terminator set E; parsing must therefore accept the following type without consuming an explicit `NEWLINE`.
- **Review:** Cover plain, outer-public and constructor-public forms, type parameters, refinement on the same and next line, generic refined syntax preservation, `INVALID_EXPORT_TARGET`, missing-base recovery, use-list and block recovery, and source preservation. The first hostile review found that an incomplete `borrow` base could consume a following line-start function as a function-pointer type. Type-prefix recovery now diagnoses the missing operand and leaves the next item untouched; a regression covers `distinct Broken = borrow` followed by `fn later()`. A second hostile review flagged an unconditional newline skip after `=`. That skip was removed to match the grammar; a physical line break after `=` or `pub` remains accepted because the lexer emits no `NEWLINE` there, as tested. A third hostile review confirmed the newline reasoning and distinct parsing, and its enum-recovery finding is tracked under IR-042.

### IR-044: Parse resource declarations

- **Decision:** Dispatch contextual `resource` only when followed by an uppercase name. Build `RESOURCE_DECL` with the name and an optional body; reuse `field_list` and `field_decl` for resource fields. Let the generic item visibility logic handle `pub` and diagnose `export` as `INVALID_EXPORT_TARGET`. Keep interface-resource syntax separate.
- **Reason:** `ResourceDecl` is the next production in spec/03 §3.6. Reusing record field parsing preserves the shared field grammar and recovery behavior, while the ordinary resource body remains distinct from Stage 4 interface resources and semantic ownership checks.
- **Review:** Cover no-body and empty-body resources, fields with public visibility, braces on the next line, invalid export, item-in-block recovery, missing-close recovery that preserves the next item, and source preservation. Hostile review found no actionable issues.

### IR-045: Parse trait declarations and associated types

- **Decision:** Parse `trait` and `unsafe trait` declarations with generic parameters, supertrait bounds, an optional `where` clause, and a brace-delimited item list. Parse associated type declarations with optional bounds and trait functions with optional bodies; parse `requires` and `ensures` as contract expressions. Diagnose `export trait` as `INVALID_EXPORT_TARGET`. Trait items have no visibility in the grammar, so retain an invalid `pub` or `export` marker in an `ERROR` node and report `EXPECTED_TOKEN`. When a missing trait brace is followed by a physical-line declaration that cannot be a trait member, synthesize the missing close and leave the declaration for the enclosing parser.
- **Reason:** These productions follow spec/03 §3.7 and reuse the existing bounds, type, function, contract, attribute, and block parsers. Bodyless trait functions are required for abstract methods, while a body denotes a default method. The grammar gives trait functions and top-level functions the same leading syntax, so recovery cannot reliably distinguish a following top-level `fn` from another trait member when `}` is missing; the parser keeps it as a trait member and synchronizes on unambiguous declarations.
- **Review:** The initial hostile review found that valid `OuterDocs` on trait members were incorrectly demoted and that a block-recovery fixture used an invalid semicolon without checking for extra diagnostics. The parser now leaves outer docs for `trait_item`, demotes only inner or unattached outer docs, and the fixture asserts `ITEM_IN_BLOCK` is its sole diagnostic. The final post-fix hostile review attempt did not complete. Confirm the use of `EXPECTED_TOKEN` for visibility markers on trait items, and whether preserving a line-start function as a trait member is the preferred recovery for a missing trait brace. Cover bounds and where clauses, associated types, abstract and default methods, contract clauses, attributes/docs, invalid visibility/export, missing-brace recovery, item-in-block behavior, and source preservation.

### IR-046: Parse impl declarations and associated type definitions

- **Decision:** Parse `unsafe` and generic impl headers, inherent type targets, trait references followed by `for` and a self type, and lowercase `ModPath` interface targets. Enable associated-type bindings only while parsing a trait reference. Parse optional `where` clauses, associated type definitions, and impl functions with required bodies. Permit `pub` only on functions in inherent impls; use `VISIBILITY_ON_TRAIT_IMPL_ITEM` for `pub` on trait or interface impl functions, and `EXPECTED_TOKEN` for visibility on associated types or an `export` marker. Diagnose `pub impl` with `EXPECTED_TOKEN`; `export impl` uses `INVALID_EXPORT_TARGET`. When an associated type's RHS is missing and the lexer suppresses the newline after `=`, diagnose the missing type and continue with the following line-start impl item. On a missing closing brace, preserve a following declaration only when it cannot begin an impl item.
- **Reason:** These productions follow spec/03 §3.7. The `for` token distinguishes trait impls, while a lower-case `ModPath` identifies an interface target; ordinary type parsing handles inherent targets. Associated-type bindings are valid on trait references and must not leak into unrelated type parsing. The visibility diagnostic is specified for impl functions, so an invalid marker on an associated type uses the general expected-token diagnostic. Missing-close recovery is inherently ambiguous when a following `fn` or `type Name = Type` can be either a top-level declaration or an impl member; retaining it as a member avoids dropping valid impl syntax. The lexer does not emit `NEWLINE` after `=`, so incomplete associated types need a local recovery path that does not discard the next member.
- **Review:** Cover generic trait references with associated-type bindings, trait/inherent/interface targets, `unsafe`, `where`, associated type definitions, body-required methods, public inherent methods, invalid visibility and export markers, docs and attributes, block recovery, missing-close and missing-RHS recovery, and lossless source preservation. Three hostile subagent review attempts did not return findings before shutdown, so no completed subagent review is claimed. The local adversarial pass added regressions for newline-suppressed missing associated-type RHS recovery, visibility restricted to functions, and export markers; the ambiguous missing-brace cases remain recorded for human review.

### IR-047: Parse constant declarations

- **Decision:** Parse `const UIDENT ':' Type '=' nl Expr` into `CONST_DECL`, using the existing type and expression parsers. Preserve `pub const`; diagnose `export const` through `INVALID_EXPORT_TARGET`. When a required type is absent before an item, retain the following item and attach a missing-type error. After `=`, diagnose a missing initializer without consuming a line-start item, while still allowing valid multiline expressions and `fn()` closure initializers.
- **Reason:** This follows spec/03 §3.8 and reuses the same expression grammar used by statements. Newlines after `=` are suppressed by the lexer, so recovery must distinguish a declaration-shaped `fn name` from a valid `fn()` closure or another expression beginning on the next physical line.
- **Review:** Cover public and exported constants, arithmetic and literal initializers, multiline and closure initializers, missing type and initializer recovery, item-in-block handling, and source preservation. One hostile subagent review was requested but shut down without returning a report, so no completed subagent review is claimed. Local adversarial tests caught and fixed a case where a missing initializer let the expression parser consume a following `fn name` declaration as a closure; valid multiline `fn()` closure initializers remain covered.

### IR-048: Parse invariant declarations

- **Decision:** Dispatch contextual `invariant` only when followed by a `UIDENT`. Parse its target name and a required braced `INVARIANT_EXPR_LIST` using the existing expression parser and comma/newline separators. Diagnose empty predicate lists as `EXPECTED_EXPRESSION`. `export invariant` uses `INVALID_EXPORT_TARGET`; `pub invariant` uses `EXPECTED_TOKEN`. When the closing brace is missing, preserve a following physical-line item that cannot be an expression.
- **Reason:** This follows spec/03 §3.8 and `Sep ::= ',' nl | NL`. A dedicated list node retains the predicate grouping and its braces in the CST. The invariant remains contextual so the spelling can still be used as an identifier in ordinary expression and pattern positions. Missing-close recovery must distinguish a declaration-shaped `fn name` from a valid `fn()` predicate expression.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover comma and newline separators, empty lists, contextual-name behavior, invalid visibility/export, item-in-block handling, missing-close recovery, line-start `fn()` closures, and lossless source preservation. Hostile subagent review completed with no concrete reproducible issues; it confirmed separator handling, the empty-list diagnostic, exact contextual-keyword lookahead, closure-versus-function disambiguation, and preservation of the following item after a missing close.

### IR-049: Parse effect declarations and covers clauses

- **Decision:** Dispatch contextual `effect` only when followed by a `LIDENT`; parse the name and optional `covers EffectSet` into `EFFECT_DECL` and `COVERS_CLAUSE`, reusing the existing effect-set grammar. Allow `pub effect` and diagnose `export effect` as `INVALID_EXPORT_TARGET`. In `EffectSet` recovery, preserve a physical-line item that cannot continue an effect reference, including a line-start `effect name` declaration.
- **Reason:** This follows spec/03 §§3.1.2, 3.5 and 3.8. `EffectSet` is shared by declarations, function clauses, bounds, and function-pointer types, so adding the item production should not create a separate set grammar. A line-start `effect name` has two adjacent identifiers; it cannot be an `EffectRef`, which requires dotted segments, so it is an unambiguous recovery boundary when the closing brace is missing. An `EffectRef` named `effect` followed by a newline remains a valid reference and is not classified as a declaration.
- **Review:** Tests cover declarations with and without `covers`, same-line and next-line `covers`, empty and populated sets with dotted references and a trailing comma, visibility/export, block recovery, missing-close recovery before both another effect and a function declaration, and lossless source preservation. A hostile subagent review found no concrete issues; it confirmed contextual lookahead, shared `EffectSet` behavior, separators, visibility/export handling, and preservation of the next item after a missing close. The reviewer noted that recovery before ordinary items was not directly tested, so a function-declaration regression test was added.

### IR-050: Parse interfaces and interface resources

- **Decision:** Dispatch contextual `interface` only when followed by a `LIDENT`; parse interface members as record/alias declarations, enums, interface resources, or function heads. Parse optional resource member lists as function heads. Members have no visibility marker. Require bodies on ordinary functions, allow them on trait functions, and forbid them on interface functions through an explicit function-body policy; a forbidden body is diagnosed as `INTERFACE_FN_BODY` and retained under `ERROR`.
- **Reason:** This follows spec/03 §3.9 and spec/13 §§13.2.1–13.2.2, reusing the existing type, enum, attribute, and function-head parsers. A missing interface close can preserve a following top-level item only when it cannot be an interface member. A line-start `FnHead`, `TypeDecl`, `EnumDecl`, or `IfaceResource` is ambiguous because each also starts a valid interface member, so it remains a member.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover record and alias types, enums, resources with and without bodies, documented functions, forbidden bodies and their error wrappers, generic and clause restrictions on function heads, pre-Stage-5 async rejection, missing-close recovery before a constant, ambiguous line-start members, and lossless source preservation. Hostile review found two documentation/coverage issues: a validation-table row still claimed world-body diagnostic support before `world_decl` exists, and the ambiguous recovery set also includes types, enums, and resources. The docs now state the current boundary and a recovery regression exercises each ambiguous member form.

### IR-051: Parse world declarations and references

- **Decision:** Dispatch contextual `world` only when followed by a `LIDENT`; parse import, export, and include items into their dedicated node kinds. Parse `WorldRef` as `PKG_PATH` or `MOD_PATH`, and parse imported/exported `FnHead`s with `FnBodyPolicy::Forbidden`. Enforce the interface-function restrictions on world function heads: no generics or clauses, no `unsafe` marker, and `FEATURE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` with `stage = 5` and `feature = "async-fn"` for `async fn` before Stage 5. Diagnose an import/export `PKG_PATH` without an interface segment as `WORLD_IMPORT_NEEDS_INTERFACE`; diagnose attributes on world items as `INVALID_ATTRIBUTE_TARGET`. A malformed reference consumes one offending token under `ERROR`.
- **Reason:** This follows spec/03 §3.9, spec/02 §2.9, spec/13 §§13.2.2 and 13.4.1, and D-WIT-03. Package paths already have a dedicated lexer mode after `import`, `export`, and `include`, while local references reuse the existing module-path parser. A world `FnHead` inherits the interface restrictions explicitly stated by spec/13 §13.4.1. Consuming an invalid reference token once keeps malformed input lossless and avoids cascaded item diagnostics. Whether a reference resolves to the required entity kind and whether an include names a whole package remain later semantic checks. On a missing world close, unambiguous top-level items are preserved; `export FnHead` remains inside the world because it is also a valid world item.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover external package and local module paths, all three world item forms, function imports/exports, package-only import/export diagnostics, generic and clause restrictions, unsupported async functions and their structured stage/feature data, forbidden function bodies, invalid attributes, contextual identifiers, malformed-reference recovery without cascades, missing-close recovery before a constant, the ambiguous exported-function case, and lossless source preservation. Hostile review found three concrete issues across two passes: world function heads accepted generics/clauses, malformed references left their token for later recovery, and the async diagnostic lacked its required structured data. All are fixed and regression-tested; the final hostile follow-up confirmed no concrete issues remain.

### IR-052: Parse extern blocks

- **Decision:** Parse `extern` and `unsafe extern` blocks into `EXTERN_BLOCK`, retain an optional `module STRING` in `EXTERN_MODULE`, and represent each documented or attributed `FnHead` as an `FN_DECL` within the item list. Decode the ABI and module strings in the parser so escaped and raw spellings are classified by their value. Report `UNKNOWN_ABI` for unsupported valid ABI strings, and do not add an `INVALID_EXTERN_BLOCK` cascade for that case. Require a valid module string for `"wasm"` and `unsafe` for `"C"`. Report unsafe C as `FEATURE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` with `stage = 7` and `feature = "extern-c"`, and async extern functions as Stage 5. `FnBodyPolicy::Extern` leaves the body optional but reports a supplied body as `UNEXPECTED_FN_BODY` and retains it under `ERROR`. Visibility markers on blocks and members are diagnosed. Missing-close recovery preserves an unambiguous following top-level item but retains a line-start function as an extern member.
- **Reason:** This follows spec/03 §3.10, spec/12 §§12.7.1 and 12.9.1, and the language's staged implementation policy. Comparing decoded values handles equivalent spellings such as `"\u{77}asm"` and avoids treating raw-string syntax itself as an ABI name. Parsing body policy alongside the function head keeps forbidden extern bodies and their blocks recoverable without prematurely implementing signature checking, name binding, or native linking.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover decoded escaped/raw strings, docs and attributes, ABI diagnostics, Stage 7 structured data, Stage 5 async diagnostics, forbidden bodies, visibility markers, missing-close recovery, ambiguous line-start functions, and source preservation. Hostile review found three recovery bugs: one-token recovery cascaded and could consume a nested `}`; consecutive invalid members in a closed block could be mistaken for top-level declarations; and an extern function after a newline suppressed by `=` triggered an extra separator diagnostic. Recovery now scans balanced member boundaries, uses the block's matched closing brace to distinguish a closed list from a missing close, and continues directly at a valid physical-line-start `FnHead`. Each finding has a regression test. The final hostile pass confirmed no concrete issues remain. Human review is still useful for whether this early parser-local string decoder should later be replaced by the shared literal-value layer, and whether `UNKNOWN_ABI` should stay parser-owned when that validation layer is completed.

### IR-053: Parse test and property declarations

- **Decision:** Dispatch contextual `test` and `property` only in item position when followed by a `STRING`. Parse tests and properties with required blocks, and parse property parameters as `NamedParam`s with optional `var`, lowercase or `_` names, and types. Accept an empty property parameter list. Diagnose `pub` with `EXPECTED_TOKEN`, `export` with `INVALID_EXPORT_TARGET`, and nested declarations with the existing `ITEM_IN_BLOCK` recovery. Preserve invalid attributes before property parameters inside their `PARAM` nodes and report `INVALID_ATTRIBUTE_TARGET`. Keep test execution and semantic checks for names, parameter types, body types, and effects in later stages.
- **Reason:** This follows spec/03 §3.11 and §3.5, impl/02 §2.10.1, and spec/15 §15.2.1. Although the `PropertyDecl` EBNF omits `?` on its parameter list, spec/14 §14.2.2 item 8 explicitly says a property without parameters is valid; the explicit semantic prose resolves the grammar ambiguity. Retaining invalid parameter attributes inside the parameter follows the parser's general attribute-recovery contract. Test/property declarations are Stage 0 syntax and Stage 3 execution, so parsing their CST does not implement test analysis or execution.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover contextual declarations, documented and attributed items, mutable/named/unnamed/empty parameters, visibility/export diagnostics, self-parameter rejection, invalid parameter attributes, declarations inside blocks, missing-body recovery, and lossless source preservation. Two hostile read-only reviews found no concrete issues; the follow-up confirmed the contextual lookahead, parameter forms, attribute placement, recovery progress, and source preservation. `cargo test --workspace` passed all 397 tests, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` passed, and `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that empty property parameter lists follow spec/14's explicit rule despite the EBNF omission.

### IR-054: Start typed AST views for test and property syntax

- **Decision:** Introduce the `AstNode` and `AstToken` casting traits and typed views for `SourceFile`, `TestDecl`, `PropertyDecl`, `ParamList`, `Param`, `Name`, `Block`, `StringLit`, and the `Type` node union. Expose direct-role accessors and lazy iterators for top-level tests, properties, and property parameters. Keep these initial wrappers hand-maintained in `ast.rs`; add the remaining grammar-driven views and `sai.ungram` build-time generator in subsequent frontend steps.
