- **Reason:** These views provide typed access to the syntax just added without changing the lossless CST or searching arbitrary descendants at each use site. Keeping this first slice small makes it immediately usable while the much larger generated-view inventory is still pending.
- **Review:** The initial hostile review found that `Name::token()` reached through an allocating descendant scan and that the initial casting tests and literal-decoder status needed clearer coverage/documentation. The accessor now inspects the direct token child; the docs distinguish structural accessors from scalar accessors and explicitly defer decoded string values. Regression coverage now checks `can_cast`/`cast` agreement for every implemented node wrapper and type variant, wrong-kind string casts, and missing property parameter lists/types. A follow-up found that optional structural getters could allocate red-node handles; IR-055 materializes the compact node index at root creation. Two further hostile reviews found that temporary handles allocated in `SyntaxNode::drop`, and that eager token-offset indexing was unnecessary. Handle drops now avoid placeholder allocation, token-offset indexing is lazy, and `token_at_offset` selects at most two matches without collecting a temporary vector. `cargo test --workspace` passed all 401 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. The final hostile review found no code issues and found stale IR-021/022 and pending-status wording; those review-log entries now identify the superseded design and completed review. Human review should confirm that hand-maintaining this initial subset is a useful staged bridge before implementing the documented grammar-driven generator.

### IR-055: Materialize red-node handles with the root index

- **Decision:** Build the compact red-node/child index eagerly when `SyntaxNode::new_root` is called, and store a cached `Rc<NodeData>` for every syntax node. Build the token-offset index lazily on the first `token_at_offset` call. Child traversal and typed structural accessors clone existing node handles rather than allocating a `NodeData` on first access.
- **Reason:** The typed AST API returns owned `SyntaxNode`-backed views through optional structural accessors, while impl/02 §2.7.2 requires those accessors not to allocate. The previous lazy red-node cache allocated on first child access. Eagerly preparing only the node/child index lets structural accessors meet the contract while token-offset storage and its construction cost remain deferred until requested.
- **Review:** Regression coverage checks that every indexed node has its red handle at root creation and that the token-offset index remains uninitialized until lookup. Hostile reviews found and prompted removal of a per-handle placeholder allocation during drop; token lookup also no longer allocates a temporary match vector. The token-offset index remains lazy. Workspace tests, clippy, formatting, and diff checks pass. The final hostile review found no code issues and prompted this log to clarify that IR-021/022 describe the superseded lazy-handle design. Human review should measure the remaining eager per-file node-handle index memory and construction time against representative files.

### IR-056: Avoid transient allocations while traversing and dropping red nodes

- **Decision:** When dropping a shared `SyntaxNode` handle, return without allocating; when the handle uniquely owns its node, detach the parent chain iteratively with `Rc::get_mut`. During cache teardown, drain red handles in reverse pre-order so children release parent references before their ancestors. In `token_at_offset`, find the matching token interval with binary searches and construct no intermediate match vector.
- **Reason:** AST structural accessors iterate over children and often discard non-matching handles. Their `Drop` path must not allocate if the accessor contract is to hold in practice. Red parent chains can be deep, so teardown remains iterative. Offset lookup returns at most two tokens, making a temporary vector of all inclusive boundary matches unnecessary.
- **Review:** Focused red-tree tests cover deep-tree teardown, cached handle lifetime, sorted/lazy token indexing, EOF and zero-width format-string boundaries, and source-order traversal. `cargo test --workspace` passed all 401 tests; clippy, formatting, and diff checks pass. The final hostile review found no code issues. Human review should confirm the reverse-order teardown invariant and that the no-allocation drop fast path remains valid if red-node ownership changes.

### IR-057: Preserve lexical error state in green tokens

- **Decision:** Copy the lexer's `HAS_ERROR` bit into each green token and expose it through `GreenToken` and `SyntaxToken`. Include the bit in green-token equality and interning. Do not retain the other lexer flags in the green tree.
- **Reason:** Literal value decoders must distinguish malformed numeric and quoted-literal tokens from valid text, and the same generic flag is set on malformed non-literal tokens such as package paths. The immutable green tree outlives the lexer output, so the AST cannot consult the original token array. Retaining one bit is enough for this contract and avoids carrying parser/layout flags into the syntax tree.
- **Review:** Tests confirm malformed integer, string, and package-path tokens retain the error bit, valid integer tokens do not, interning does not merge equal text with different error state, and red tokens expose the retained bit. `cargo test --workspace` passed all 405 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. The initial hostile review found no code issue and caught wording that made `HAS_ERROR` sound literal-only; the token flag and design record now describe its general lexical-error meaning, with a package-path regression test. A hostile follow-up found no further issues. Human review should confirm no later AST or tooling consumer requires other lexer flags on green tokens.

### IR-058: Decode integer and float literal values through AST views

- **Decision:** Add `IntLit` and `FloatLit` token views. `IntLit::value()` returns `None` for a token with `HAS_ERROR`, malformed digit/separator syntax, or checked `u128` overflow; it supports decimal and lowercase `0x`/`0o`/`0b` prefixes. `FloatLit::value_f64()` and `value_f32()` return `None` for `HAS_ERROR`, remove `_` only when present, and parse directly into the requested float width.
- **Reason:** HIR and type checking need literal values while the lossless CST remains independent of semantic numeric types. Checked integer accumulation avoids temporary storage. Parsing to `f32` directly prevents introducing an intermediate `f64` rounding step.
- **Review:** Tests cover all integer radices, `u128::MAX`, integer overflow, malformed integer and float tokens, decimal separator removal, `f32`/`f64` values, overflow to infinity, and a decimal value that distinguishes direct `f32` parsing from parsing through `f64`. The hostile review found no implementation defect but noted that `0.1` alone did not guard the target-width rounding requirement; the added regression test confirms direct parsing rounds up while an `f64`-then-`f32` cast rounds down. `cargo test --workspace` passed all 407 tests, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `git diff --check`, and the focused float AST value test passed. Human review should confirm the float-decoder allocation for underscore-bearing spellings and the target-width parsing behavior.

### IR-059: Expose checked tuple indexes through a typed AST view

- **Decision:** Add a `TupleIndexExpr` AST node view whose `index()` parses only plain decimal digits, rejects leading zeroes except for `0`, and returns `None` when the value does not fit in `u32`.
- **Reason:** This mirrors the tuple-index source grammar and keeps oversized indexes distinguishable from parser syntax errors so impl/03 can report `TUPLE_INDEX_OUT_OF_RANGE` after type information is available. The parser remains responsible for `INVALID_TUPLE_INDEX`.
- **Review:** Tests cover zero, `u32::MAX`, `u32` overflow, leading zeroes, separators, and a radix prefix, and confirm the parser reports `INVALID_TUPLE_INDEX` for invalid spellings. The hostile review confirmed the range/error split and found the prefix-case coverage missing; the `0x1` regression case is now included. Follow-up review confirmed `0x1` remains one `INT` token inside `TUPLE_INDEX_EXPR`, returns `None`, and produces the expected diagnostic; the six values and three syntax diagnostics are in source order. `cargo test --workspace` passed all 408 tests, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `git diff --check`, and the focused tuple-index AST test passed. Human review should confirm that invalid spellings yield `None` in the typed view while retaining the parser's existing diagnostics.

### IR-060: Decode character and byte literals with the lexer escape scanner

- **Decision:** Extend `EscapeScan` to carry a decoded scalar or byte for valid escapes, then add `CharLit::value()` and `ByteLit::value()` using that shared result. Invalid character literals yield U+FFFD; invalid byte literals yield `0`.
- **Reason:** Literal validation and value decoding must share escape boundaries and accepted forms so they cannot disagree. These scalar accessors need no allocation.
- **Review:** Tests cover raw Unicode characters, Unicode escapes, common byte escapes, hexadecimal byte escapes, invalid escapes, multiscalar characters, non-ASCII byte literals, escaped quote/backslash/NUL in both character and byte literals, unclosed literals, and malformed UTF-8 fallback through the green tree. The hostile review found no implementation defect but identified the missing edge-case coverage; its follow-up confirmed the invalid-token paths and requested byte quote/backslash cases, which are now included. `cargo test --workspace` passed all 410 tests, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. The focused character and malformed-UTF-8 accessor tests also passed. Human review should confirm malformed token fallback behavior and that adding decoded values leaves lexer diagnostics unchanged.

### IR-061: Decode string and byte-string values through the shared escape scanner

- **Decision:** Add `ByteStringLit`, `StringLit::value() -> Cow<str>`, and `ByteStringLit::value() -> Cow<[u8]>`, retaining `StringLit::text()` for raw token text. Share `scan_escape` with literal value decoding and the existing extern-ABI string reader; keep that reader's `HAS_ERROR` gate. Borrow plain contents when decoding makes no changes. Return invalid escapes, inconsistent-indentation lines, and forbidden bidi controls as U+FFFD, encoded as UTF-8 bytes for byte strings. For multiline strings, remove the opener and closing-line breaks and base indentation, replace each inconsistent-indentation line with one U+FFFD, and preserve internal line endings as written, including diagnosed lone CRs. Preserve C0 controls and foreign whitespace inside literals.
- **Reason:** The shared scanner keeps lexer validation and AST values consistent. The fallback is total for malformed tokens so later stages can continue after lexical diagnostics. The context rules in §2.2.3 permit C0 controls and foreign whitespace in literals and forbid bidi controls; §2.6.4 preserves written string content apart from its explicit multiline transformations.
- **Review:** Coverage includes simple, escaped, continued, multiline, and raw forms; invalid escapes and indentation; byte escapes, invalid byte escapes and non-ASCII content; escaped terminal quotes on unterminated tokens; allowed controls/foreign whitespace; internal CRLF and lone CR; malformed UTF-8; and borrowed `Cow` results. The first hostile review found that allowed controls/foreign whitespace were being replaced; this was fixed and covered by regression tests. The next review found that raw/multiline lone CRs were replaced despite the raw-content rule; decoding now preserves those code points while the lexer still reports `INVALID_LINE_ENDING`, and the regression test checks both the value and all three diagnostics. The final hostile follow-up found no further actionable issues. `cargo test --workspace` passed all 415 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. The focused `cargo test -p sai_syntax` run passed all 343 tests, and the lone-CR regression passed after its fix. Human review should confirm replacing an entire inconsistent-indentation line with one U+FFFD, preserving diagnosed internal lone CR and CRLF, and encoding byte-string replacement characters as UTF-8.

### IR-062: Expose f-string pieces and typed format specs

- **Decision:** Add an `Expr` union over the current expression node kinds, including `FSTRING_INTERP`, `FStringExpr::pieces()`, and an `AstToken` view for `FormatSpec`. `pieces()` decodes string escapes and doubled braces, replaces unmatched closing braces with U+FFFD, omits empty text, merges adjacent text, and retains each interpolation's direct expression and optional spec. `FormatSpec::name()` returns the identifier without its optional raw-identifier prefix; `arg()` parses the optional integer with checked accumulation and returns `None` when absent, malformed, or out of range.
- **Reason:** This follows §2.7.3's AST API while keeping f-string text decoding aligned with the lexer's escape scanner and exposing existing interpolation CST children without reparsing source. The `Expr` union lets callers handle all currently implemented expression nodes through one typed accessor.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that `Expr` omitted the parser-produced `FSTRING_INTERP` node; added a typed wrapper and regression coverage for casting it. The hostile follow-up found no further issues. Tests cover text escape and brace decoding, borrowed text, expression and spec extraction, raw format names, radix arguments, malformed numbers, overflow, invalid text, and missing interpolation expressions. `cargo test --workspace` passed all 418 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the raw-name normalization and the U+FFFD fallback for unmatched closing braces.

### IR-063: Add syntax pointers and per-file AST IDs

- **Decision:** Add `SyntaxNodePtr`, typed `AstPtr<N>`, typed `AstId<N>`, `ErasedAstId`, and `AstIdMap`. Store the workspace snapshot identity and a pre-order red-node index with each pointer, in addition to its file, kind, and range, so same-range recovery nodes stay distinct and pointers resolve in constant time after recreating a red tree for the same file snapshot. Cache each node's text range in the red-tree index. Expose `parse(snapshot, file_id)` as the public route to a snapshot-bound root; keep the lower-level binding constructor crate-private and verify the tree's lossless text against the snapshot source. Merge lexical and parser diagnostics in source order. Scope typed `AstId<N>` values to their file and snapshot while keeping the erased item-tree index as `u32`; require a snapshot-bound, parentless `SOURCE_FILE` root for `AstIdMap`. Assign zero-based IDs in pre-order to item-like nodes, including generic parameters, `USE_DECL`, and `EXTERN_BLOCK` because their DefIds or item-tree entries need source identities; exclude closures, async blocks, and all expressions, statements, and patterns because their IDs are derived from HIR/owner traversal instead. Add `TypeParam` and `ConstParam` to the ItemTree child kinds.
- **Reason:** This implements §2.7.4 using the item-tree categories in §2.8.7 and DefPathData in §2.8.2. Snapshot identity makes pointers, typed IDs, and maps usable only with the exact workspace snapshot, including when a different file changed; the public parse facade prevents callers from binding a different tree structure to an existing snapshot identity. Lossless-text validation remains a guard for crate-internal root binding. A node index is compact and avoids storing every ancestor path for each source-map pointer; cached text ranges keep pointer validation constant-time. Requiring the source-file root prevents subtrees from assigning locally numbered IDs that collide with the file's actual IDs. Only the erased numeric index is stable across an edit that preserves item order; typed IDs are newly scoped. Generic parameters receive AstIds because they have DefIds and appear among ItemTree children, while `USE_DECL` receives one for its ItemTree source entry.
- **Review:** The initial hostile review found that kind/range-only pointers collide for empty error nodes, that FileId alone does not distinguish snapshots, and that excluding generic parameters conflicts with their DefIds and ItemTree representation. A follow-up found that subtree maps could return another item's pointer, repeated `.nth` lookup made wide sibling resolution quadratic, deep paths duplicated memory quadratically, ItemTree lacked generic-parameter kinds, and the cross-map test did not isolate snapshot identity. The next review found that callers could bind a different tree with the same text to a snapshot, §2.6.7 overstated AstId stability, maps accepted unbound roots, and a test assertion still named child paths. Restricting snapshot binding to the crate and adding the public parse facade closed that construction path. A later review found stale AstId stability claims in the HIR change table, an incomplete explanation of `USE_DECL`, wording that implied FileId checks also apply to unbound roots, and no same-source/new-snapshot regression test. Corrected the stability table and API descriptions and verified that same-source roots with new snapshot identities reject old pointers and typed IDs while preserving the erased index. The final hostile follow-up found no further actionable issues. `cargo test -p sai_syntax --quiet` passed all 349 tests; `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 421 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Coverage includes file and snapshot mismatch, lossless-source binding, pointer resolution after same-snapshot reparsing, same-source/new-snapshot rejection, erased-index stability, and all documented item-like categories. Human review should confirm inclusion of generic parameters and source-reference nodes such as `USE_DECL`, plus workspace-snapshot scoping.

### IR-064: Add item, statement, and pattern AST unions

- **Decision:** Add concrete AST wrappers and union views for source items (`Item`), statements (`Stmt`), and patterns (`Pattern`). Define each union from its corresponding spec/03 grammar alternatives; keep grammar helper nodes such as `LET_ELSE`, `REST_PAT`, and `CONST_PATH` as concrete nodes rather than union alternatives. Use one internal macro to implement `AstNode::{can_cast, cast, syntax}` for each union, including the existing `Type` union.
- **Reason:** HIR lowering and syntactic tooling need to dispatch over grammar categories without repeating raw `SyntaxKind` matches. The unions follow §2.7.1 and spec/03 §§3.3, 3.12, and 3.14 while preserving incomplete CSTs and recovery nodes through the existing optional accessor contract.
- **Review:** `item_statement_and_pattern_unions_cover_their_grammar_nodes` exercises every `ItemBody`, `Stmt`, and `Pattern` alternative with parser-produced nodes and verifies that `can_cast`, `cast`, and `syntax()` agree. The hostile review found no actionable issues. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 422 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the exact `ItemBody` alternatives and the exclusion of grammar helper nodes from the `Pattern` and `Stmt` unions.

### IR-065: Generate concrete AST node wrappers from `sai.ungram`

- **Decision:** Maintain the concrete syntax-node inventory in `crates/sai_syntax/sai.ungram`; generate every concrete `AstNode` wrapper and `AST_NODE_KINDS` at build time. Make the build script compare the inventory exactly with `SyntaxKind`'s node variants, including the special `ERROR` node, and reject duplicate names or kinds. Keep existing AST type names explicit in the inventory to preserve the public API.
- **Reason:** A single checked inventory prevents newly added parser node kinds from silently lacking typed wrappers and keeps the public node-kind list synchronized with the syntax enum. Generating only the wrappers first makes this a small vertical slice that can be verified without prematurely guessing child roles; the grammar-derived accessor generator and spec/03 conformance tests remain follow-up work.
- **Review:** The hostile reviews found that a final `SyntaxKind` variant without a trailing comma could be omitted from both sides of the parity check, generated names could collide with reserved or existing AST types, node parity used `SOURCE_FILE` instead of the `EOF` boundary, braces or commas inside comments and attributes could truncate or corrupt enum parsing, and build-support tests were outside the normal test gate. The generator now recognizes `ERROR` plus every variant after `EOF`, skips line and nested block comments and quoted strings in attributes, rejects reserved and colliding AST type names, and shares its support module with the crate's `#[cfg(test)]` target. Ten regression tests cover these cases and generated output; all run under `cargo test`. The final hostile follow-up found no further actionable issues. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 433 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that an inventory-only `.ungram` is an acceptable intermediate format and that explicit AST names should remain API-stable when the grammar later gains child-role declarations.

### IR-066: Generate direct AST accessors from child roles

- **Decision:** Extend `sai.ungram` with `child`, `children`, and `token` role declarations. A `child` declaration includes a zero-based position among direct children castable to its AST type; generate an optional typed accessor using that position. Generate iterators for repeated `children` roles and optional `AstToken` accessors for `token` roles. Keep scalar predicates, literal decoders, and accessors that must select by token/operator position hand-written.
- **Reason:** This moves the stable structural relationship between CST children and AST roles into the same source as the node inventory, while preserving the spec's partial-tree behavior. Explicit positions let differently named roles select distinct children of the same AST type, as required for binary and range expressions. Start with the existing source-file, test/property, and parameter accessors so their behavior can be compared directly with the established API and tests.
- **Review:** Hostile review required explicit child positions because two roles with the same AST type would otherwise select the same first child; `child` now specifies a zero-based index among direct children castable to that type, and duplicate `(parent, type, index)` selectors are rejected. `BinExpr::lhs/rhs` exercise positions 0 and 1 against parser-produced operands. The generator also rejects duplicate/unknown roles, unknown parent or target types, collisions with `AstNode` methods, and collisions with hand-written accessors. Fourteen build-support tests cover role validation and output; AST parser tests exercise each generated getter, including a missing-body case that must return `None`. The final hostile follow-up found no further actionable issues. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 438 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the ordinal is zero-based among direct children of the declared AST type and that all currently declared child roles select the intended syntax nodes.

### IR-067: Generate function and statement AST accessors

- **Decision:** Add generated accessors for source-file items, function names/parameters/return types/bodies, return types, block statements, and the core let/var/assignment/assert/expression statement roles. Extend the role syntax with `child_before` and `child_after`, which select a typed child on one side of a required direct-token boundary. Keep all selectors optional so parser recovery and context-forbidden bodies remain representable without panics.
- **Reason:** These direct relationships are stable across the implemented Stage 0 grammar and are needed by early HIR lowering and syntax tooling. Same-type ordinal selectors alone misidentify later children when an earlier optional child is missing, as in an assert message after a missing condition; comma-anchored selectors preserve the documented partial-tree behavior.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that `AssertStmt::condition()` could return the message expression when the condition was missing. Added comma-relative selectors, build-time validation that boundary names exist in `SyntaxKind`, and a malformed-assert regression proving `condition()` remains `None` while `message()` resolves correctly. A second hostile pass found that the boundary could be a node kind; the generator now rejects node-kind boundaries and the runtime `before` selector checks tokens explicitly. Another review note identified uncovered declared roles; the regression now exercises every newly declared accessor, including return type contents, let-else, typed `var`, and heterogeneous source-file items. Fifteen build-support tests cover role validation/output, and parser tests cover successful and incomplete trees. Final hostile follow-up found no remaining actionable issues. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 440 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm source-file item coverage and that block statement iteration deliberately omits recovery `ERROR` nodes.

### IR-068: Generate control-flow expression AST accessors

- **Decision:** Add generated accessors for conditions and branches of `if`, scrutinees and arms of `match`, loop labels/conditions/patterns/iterables/bodies, closure parameters/return types/bodies, and region/block expressions. Expose expression operands for `return`, `break`, and prefix expressions.
- **Reason:** These roles follow direct, typed relationships in the implemented expression grammar. The regression covers ordinary and let-conditions, else-if versus else-block branches, guarded match arms, each loop form, closure headers, and nested block constructs.
- **Review:** Hostile review found missing recovery coverage for `MatchArmList::arms()` and no test for a closure without a return type. Extended the missing-separator test to verify both valid arms remain available after recovery, and added an arrowless closure whose `ret_type()` is `None`. The final hostile follow-up found no remaining actionable accessor or recovery issues. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 441 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that `children` iteration intentionally skips recovery `ERROR` nodes and that optional loop labels and closure return types remain `None` when absent.

### IR-069: Generate postfix, collection, and argument AST accessors

- **Decision:** Add generated accessors for path names/generic arguments, grouped/tuple/array elements, postfix bases and their direct operands, call argument lists, positional/named/base argument forms, and f-string interpolation expressions/specs. Select the two same-typed `IndexExpr` operands on opposite sides of `L_BRACKET`.
- **Reason:** These roles expose existing CST structure without reparsing token text. The regression uses parser-produced paths, calls, field and tuple access, indexing, propagation, await, collections, mixed call arguments, and a formatted interpolation.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that the initial index test did not distinguish its two `Expr` roles and that argument iterator tests did not check repeated entries. Anchored `IndexExpr::{base, index}` to opposite sides of `L_BRACKET`, asserted the valid roles are `PATH_EXPR` and `LITERAL`, and covered `values[]` recovery (base present, index absent). Expanded argument coverage to two positional, two named, and two base arguments, including the expected `BASE_ARG_NOT_LAST` diagnostic while retaining all nodes. The hostile follow-up found no remaining actionable issues. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 442 tests; Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the chosen `L_BRACKET` boundary and retaining structurally valid argument nodes when the parser diagnoses a non-final base argument.

### IR-070: Generate type and generic-argument AST accessors

- **Decision:** Add generated roles for path type segments, borrow/owned/tuple/parenthesized type operands, generic type/const/associated-type arguments, path-segment generic arguments, and cast expressions.
- **Reason:** These accessors map one-to-one to existing direct CST children and allow the typed AST to cover ordinary type syntax and the generic-argument forms used in trait bounds. The regression covers a qualified path, nested arguments, reference-like and owned types, tuple and parenthesized types, and a cast.
- **Review:** Hostile review first found missing recovery coverage and single-entry generic iterator checks. Added `type Broken = pkg.` and `type Broken = borrow` regressions, plus two entries for each generic-argument category; also covered `SelfType::segments`, `PathSegment::name`, and `TypeArg::ty`. The follow-up found no remaining actionable issues and confirmed every declared role matches direct CST children. Function-pointer parameters and returns were deferred here because they are same-typed children separated by `THIN_ARROW`; IR-071 later added the boundary-aware repeated-child role to cover them. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 443 tests; Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm associated-type bindings are only tested in the trait-bound context where the parser permits them.

### IR-071: Generate token-bounded repeated AST child accessors

- **Decision:** Add a `children_before` role that yields direct children of a given AST type only until a direct token boundary. Use it for `FnPtrType::params()` and pair it with `child_after` for the optional return type after `THIN_ARROW`.
- **Reason:** Function-pointer parameters and return values are all direct `Type` children; a plain repeated-child accessor would incorrectly include the return value as a parameter. The boundary-aware iterator represents both signatures without counting delimiters or conflating their same-typed children.
- **Review:** The hostile review found no actionable issues. The focused parser tests verify a function pointer with a return type, an arrowless signature, and a `THIN_ARROW` with a missing return type; all preserve the parameter list and expose only the structurally present return type. Build-support tests verify generation and reject node kinds as token boundaries. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 444 tests; Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm using the first direct `THIN_ARROW` as the parameter boundary.

### IR-072: Generate core recursive pattern AST accessors

- **Decision:** Add child roles for alternation alternatives, `@` bindings and subpatterns, binding names/modes, tuple and parenthesized patterns, slice elements/rests, constructor paths/arguments, constructor argument variants, implicit-member patterns, and constant-path segments. Anchor both `AtPat` roles around `AT`.
- **Reason:** The roles mirror parser-produced direct children. `BindingPat` also casts to the `Pattern` union, so selecting `AtPat::pattern()` by union-child ordinal would return the left binding instead of the right subpattern; the `AT` token identifies the two sides even during recovery.
- **Review:** Regression authoring exposed that a plain `Pattern` child ordinal would select `AtPat`'s left `BindingPat`, since `BindingPat` is also a `Pattern`; changed both roles to select on opposite sides of `AT`. The test covers this malformed right-hand-side case and confirms the binding remains available while the subpattern is `None`. It also covers repeated alternatives/elements/arguments, present and absent rest bindings, and qualified constant paths. Hostile review found no remaining actionable issues. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 445 tests; Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. IR-073 resolves the deferred range-pattern endpoint roles by using a union of literal patterns and constant paths and token boundaries that accept both range operators. Human review should confirm the `AT` boundaries and the endpoint union alternatives.

### IR-073: Generate range endpoint AST accessors across both operators

- **Decision:** Add `child_before_any` and `child_after_any` selectors whose boundary is a pipe-separated list of direct token kinds. Reject child roles that can select the same direct child, including selectors across a union and one of its concrete members. Use the selectors for `RangeExpr::{start, end}` with `Expr` children and for `RangePat::{start, end}` with a new `RangePatEndpoint` union over `LiteralPat` and `ConstPath`. Keep all four accessors optional.
- **Reason:** Both `DOT2` and `DOT2EQ` delimit range endpoints, while range-pattern endpoints have two concrete node kinds that are not both members of the existing `Pattern` union. A typed endpoint union exposes the grammar's alternatives without widening `Pattern`; token-relative access also keeps prefix, open-ended, and malformed ranges representable. Rejecting potentially overlapping selectors prevents later schema additions from silently exposing two role names for the same child.
- **Review:** Tests cover both operator spellings, infix and prefix range expressions, open endpoints, literal and qualified-constant pattern endpoints, and malformed inclusive endpoints. The first hostile review found that distinct token boundaries can select the same child; overlap validation now models boundary order and child-type ranks. Follow-up reviews found union-member overlaps, nested unions, substring-based `AstNode` implementation detection, multiline variants, comments and strings inside union declarations, and an overly broad rejection of valid union/member indexes. The scanner now strips Rust comments and strings, resolves union members transitively, and compares role indexes against the concrete child-type sets while allowing provably disjoint indexes. Regressions cover each case, including `Expr`/`PathExpr` overlap and a valid disjoint-index pair. The final hostile follow-up found no actionable findings. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 448 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the `RangePatEndpoint` alternatives and the pipe-separated boundary syntax.

### IR-074: Expose literal-pattern sign and value tokens

- **Decision:** Add `LiteralPat::minus_token() -> Option<SyntaxToken>` and `LiteralPat::value_token() -> Option<SyntaxToken>`. The latter returns the direct token for integer, float, string, byte-string, character, byte, or boolean literals, and never returns the optional minus sign.
- **Reason:** `LiteralPat` has no child nodes, and its first token is the sign for a negative number. Returning the raw value token also covers `true`/`false` and diagnosed float patterns, which do not all have the same typed `AstToken` wrapper.
- **Review:** Parser tests cover each value token kind, negative integer and float signs, a rejected `-'a'` pattern, a missing value after `-`, and a lexically malformed integer whose token remains present but whose decoder returns `None`. The initial hostile review identified missing sign/value coverage for diagnosed negative floats and rejected signed literals; both regressions are now covered, and hostile follow-up found no remaining actionable findings. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 449 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that the accessor returns a raw `SyntaxToken` and exposes float-pattern tokens despite their diagnostic.

### IR-075: Generate type and record declaration accessors

- **Decision:** Add optional child accessors for `TypeDecl`'s generic parameters and its mutually exclusive `StructDef` or `AliasDef`, then expose record where clauses and field lists, field docs/attributes/names/types, and alias types/refinement expressions.
- **Reason:** These roles follow spec/03 §3.6 and impl/02 §2.5.4 while keeping the grammar's record-versus-alias alternatives visible on their existing concrete AST nodes. Optional children preserve malformed declarations for tooling without implying that recovery produced a complete definition.
- **Review:** Parser tests cover generic records, record constraints, documented/attributed and plain fields, aliases with refinements, empty records, and a missing alias type. Hostile review found no actionable findings. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 450 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that `TypeDecl` exposes separate optional accessors for its two syntactic alternatives and that record-field docs and attributes belong to the field node.

### IR-076: Generate enum declaration and discriminant accessors

- **Decision:** Expose enum names, generic parameters, constraints and variants; variant docs, attributes, names and optional tuple/record/discriminant children; tuple payload types; record payload fields and attributes; and each discriminant's integer token and optional minus token.
- **Reason:** The grammar makes tuple and record variant shapes mutually exclusive and represents a signed discriminant as a minus token plus an integer token. Keeping these parts separately addressable preserves malformed CSTs and lets callers reuse `IntLit::value()` without silently discarding the sign.
- **Review:** Parser tests cover both variant shapes, unit variants, empty enums, docs/attributes, signed and unsigned discriminants, an invalid-but-retained field attribute, and a missing variant list. Hostile review found no actionable findings. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 451 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm whether exposing `Discriminant::integer()` and `minus_token()` separately is the preferred API for signed values.

### IR-077: Generate distinct-type and resource accessors

- **Decision:** Expose distinct names, generic parameters, base types and refinements, plus the direct inner `pub` token; expose resource names and optional field lists.
- **Reason:** The inner `pub` changes visibility of the distinct constructor independently of the declaration's outer visibility. Returning its token preserves the exact source marker, while optional base/refinement/field nodes keep incomplete declarations and bodyless resources representable.
- **Review:** Parser tests cover generic and refined distinct types, inner visibility, resources with absent, empty, and populated bodies, shared field accessors, and recovery after a missing base type. The initial hostile review found two test gaps: outer-only versus inner `pub` needed source-position assertions, and the retained resource fields needed to be checked through the accessor after missing-close recovery. Both regressions are covered, and hostile follow-up found no remaining actionable findings. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 452 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that `DistinctDecl::inner_pub_token()` names the inner constructor-visibility marker clearly.

### IR-078: Generate const, invariant, and effect accessors

- **Decision:** Expose constant names/types/initializers, invariant names and expression lists, effect names and optional covers clauses, effect sets on covers and function clauses, and each effect reference's path segments.
- **Reason:** Constant, invariant, and effect declarations follow spec/03 §3.8, and effect-set syntax is shared by spec/03 §§3.4 and 3.5.1 and impl/02 §2.5.4. One role on `EffectSet` therefore serves declaration covers clauses and function signatures. Missing initializers, incomplete covers clauses, and empty invariant lists remain represented as optional children or empty iterators, matching parser recovery without treating errors as valid values.
- **Review:** Parser tests cover constants, multiple invariant predicates, populated and empty covers sets, segmented effect paths, function effect clauses, a missing initializer, an empty invariant, and a retained covers clause with a missing effect set followed by another declaration. The initial hostile review found that final recovery case missing and caught an incorrect spec/03 section reference; both were fixed, and hostile follow-up found no remaining actionable findings. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 453 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that the invariant accessor exposes the list node while individual predicates are iterated from that node.

### IR-079: Generate trait and impl declaration accessors

- **Decision:** Expose trait names, generic parameters, bounds, where clauses, and item lists; associated type declaration names/bounds; impl generic parameters, where clauses, item lists, associated type definitions, and all three impl target shapes. Expose unsafe markers as direct tokens.
- **Reason:** The parser stores trait and impl members in a shared `ITEM_LIST`, so typed iterators for each member node let callers inspect each context without inventing a single overly broad item union. An impl target is either a trait reference followed by `for` and a type, an inherent type alone, or an interface `ModPath`; conditional accessors keep these forms distinct through recovery.
- **Review:** Parser tests cover bounded traits, associated type declarations/definitions, methods, unsafe markers, all three impl target forms, a missing `for` type, and the source text on both sides of `for`. The initial hostile review found that presence-only assertions did not prove the two target accessors selected the correct sides; exact source-slice assertions were added, and hostile follow-up found no remaining actionable findings. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 454 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that using `FOR_KW` to distinguish target shapes matches the grammar's impl-target selection rule.

### IR-080: Generate interface, world, and extern accessors

- **Decision:** Expose interface/world names and item lists; typed iterators for interface declarations and world item kinds; world import/export function or reference children; package paths as a typed `PkgPath` token and local references as `ModPath`; and extern ABI/module strings, items, and the optional unsafe marker.
- **Reason:** These declarations share `ITEM_LIST` while their child grammars vary by context. Typed iterators preserve the existing CST node kinds, and world functions remain distinguishable from type references. A `PkgPath` token view gives package references the same typed-token ergonomics as strings without adding a new syntax node.
- **Review:** Parser tests cover interface types/enums/resources/functions, bodyless and populated interface resources, package/module world references, function imports/exports, includes, decoded ABI/module strings, extern methods, and unsafe extern blocks. Hostile read-only review found no actionable issues. `cargo test --workspace` passed all 455 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm adding the `PkgPath` AST token wrapper for package-path tokens used by `WorldRef`.

### IR-081: Generate function signature and clause accessors

- **Decision:** Expose function generic parameters and unsafe/async markers; surface the optional self parameter separately from named parameters; add accessors for where predicates, trait and `Fn`-sugar bounds, borrows provenances, effect caps, and contract expressions; and retain repeated requires/ensures clauses as iterators. Generic parameters and mixed bound forms also have ordered union iterators, alongside typed convenience iterators.
- **Reason:** The parser represents each clause and bound form as distinct CST nodes. Ordered unions preserve source order for mixed type/const parameters and trait/`Fn`-sugar bounds. Token-relative selection assigns `Fn`-sugar parameter and return types around `->`. The self parameter is a distinct node kind, so `ParamList` exposes it independently from named parameters.
- **Review:** The initial hostile review found that separate bound iterators could not retain mixed source order and that function generic parameters exposed only the list node. Ordered `Bound` and `GenericParam` views plus per-parameter name/type/bounds accessors were added, and parser coverage checks interleaved bound order and both generic parameter forms. Hostile follow-up found no remaining actionable issues. `cargo test --workspace` passed all 456 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the public `Bound::{Trait, FnSugar}` and `GenericParam::{Type, Const}` union variant names.

### IR-082: Generate module header and use accessors

- **Decision:** Expose the optional module header and its inner docs/unsafe marker; expose each use declaration's docs, visibility, package path, optional alias and selective items; and distinguish an imported name from its optional renamed name.
- **Reason:** The parser omits `ModuleHeader` when both docs and the unsafe marker are absent, and represents a `pub use` modifier as a `Visibility` child inside `UseDecl`. Reusing the typed `PkgPath` token and selecting the renamed use-item name after `as` keeps package identity and alias roles consistent with existing AST patterns.
- **Review:** Parser coverage checks omitted/present module headers, inner docs, unsafe markers, a documented `pub use` with package/module path and alias, both selective import forms, a plain root-package import, and export visibility. Hostile read-only review found no actionable issues. `cargo test --workspace` passed all 457 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm exposing the `Visibility` wrapper for `pub use` and that `UseItem::alias()` selects only the name after `as`.

### IR-083: Generate declaration metadata and attribute accessors

- **Decision:** Expose docs, repeated attributes, and grammar-supported visibility on item declaration nodes; expose record-field `pub` directly; expose attribute names and nested arguments, including assigned identifier values and direct string/integer values; and retain invalid-target attributes on named and self parameters. Keep the existing concrete declaration node kinds and nest trait/impl member metadata within their existing declaration nodes.
- **Reason:** The parser previously started trait/impl member nodes after consuming their docs, attributes, and visibility, leaving metadata as siblings that declaration accessors could not reach. Starting the existing declaration marker before metadata associates it with the function or associated type node without adding a new CST kind. Record fields use a direct `pub` token rather than a `Visibility` node. Attribute names and assigned identifier values are separated around `=`, while literal values remain typed tokens. The parser also retains attributes on parameters while reporting the target error, so those nodes remain reachable for tooling.
- **Review:** The initial hostile review found that record fields lacked a visibility accessor and that assigned identifier values were not tested. `FieldDecl::pub_token()` and public/private field assertions were added; coverage now exercises the identifier value after `=`. Parser tests also cover nested attribute calls, literal value forms, outer docs/attributes/visibility on functions and types, metadata on traits and impls, metadata on associated types and methods, and retained attributes on invalid self/named parameter targets. Hostile follow-up confirmed both findings were resolved and found no functional regressions; the formatter-only cleanup it noted was applied afterward. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 459 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the `AttrArg::{name, value, args, string, integer}` roles and direct `FieldDecl::pub_token()` representation.

### IR-084: Generate loop-label and control-transfer label accessors

- **Decision:** Add a typed `Label` token view and expose labels on loop declarations, `break`, and `continue`, preserving `break` values as expression accessors.
- **Reason:** Loop declarations and control transfers all use the lexical `LABEL` token. Typed token accessors expose the original spelling and range without adding CST nodes. A `LABEL` followed by `:` after `break` begins a labeled loop expression used as the break value; the parser's direct-token role keeps that nested label separate from a break-target label.
- **Review:** The hostile review found that this `break`/loop-label ambiguity lacked a regression test. Added coverage confirms `break 'value: loop { ... }` has no direct break label and exposes the labeled loop as its value; follow-up review confirmed the finding was resolved and found no other actionable issues. Coverage also checks labels on `loop`, `while`, and `for`, labeled and unlabeled `break`/`continue`, and `break` values. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 459 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that `Label` is the intended public token-view name and that labeled-loop expressions in `break` value position remain distinct from break-target labels.

### IR-085: Expose binary operators through typed AST views

- **Decision:** Add a `BinOp` enum and `BinExpr::op()`, returning the direct operator token together with its semantic operator kind.
- **Reason:** The parser already glues joint tokens for `>>`, `>=`, `&&`, and `||`; returning both the token and normalized operator preserves source spelling while making syntax consumers independent of those token encodings. Deprecated `&&` and `||` map to logical `And` and `Or`. Range and assignment operators remain outside `BinOp` because they have distinct `RangeExpr` and `AssignStmt` nodes.
- **Review:** Hostile review confirmed the mapping covers all 21 binary operator kinds and all 23 spellings, including deprecated boolean spellings and parser-glued tokens, with no actionable findings. The parser test checks each token spelling and enum result. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 460 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the public `BinOp` variant names and that both deprecated spellings intentionally map to the logical variants.

### IR-086: Expose assignment operators through typed AST views

- **Decision:** Add an `AssignOp` enum and `AssignStmt::op()`, returning the direct operator token together with its typed assignment kind.
- **Reason:** The parser already distinguishes all assignment forms, including parser-glued `>>=`. Returning both the token and a normalized enum supports semantic consumers without losing the written spelling. The assignment variants parallel `BinOp` where an assignment has a corresponding binary operator, with `Set` for plain `=`.
- **Review:** Hostile review confirmed all 14 operators in spec/03 are covered, the parser's glued `SHR_EQ` token maps correctly, and direct-token lookup remains scoped to the assignment node during recovery. Parser coverage checks every spelling and enum variant. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 461 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the public `AssignOp` names, especially `Set` and the `Wrapping*` variants.

### IR-087: Expose prefix operators through typed AST views

- **Decision:** Add a `PrefixOp` enum and `PrefixExpr::op()`, returning the direct operator token and classifying `borrow mut` as one operator variant.
- **Reason:** The parser represents `borrow mut` with a `borrow` token followed by a direct `mut` token. Looking only among direct tokens identifies the compound form without confusing a nested operand's own `mut` token or operators with the outer prefix expression.
- **Review:** Hostile review confirmed the direct-token lookup and `borrow mut` classification match the parser, and found no actionable issues. Tests cover all six accepted forms (`-`, `~`, `*`, `borrow`, `borrow mut`, and `not`) and verify the source token and enum result. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 462 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm `PrefixOp::BorrowMut` as the public variant name while the returned token remains the `borrow` token.

### IR-088: Expose typed literal tokens from literal expressions

- **Decision:** Add a `LiteralToken` `AstToken` union and `LiteralExpr::token()` for integer, float, string, byte-string, character, byte, true, and false tokens.
- **Reason:** Existing typed token wrappers already own the literal value decoders, but a `LiteralExpr` previously had no typed route to its direct token. The union reuses those wrappers and preserves boolean spelling as distinct variants. Format strings are parsed as `FStringExpr` and continue to expose their pieces there.
- **Review:** Hostile review confirmed all eight `LiteralExpr` token forms are covered, boolean variants are distinguishable, direct lookup is safe for recovery and error-marked tokens, and format strings correctly remain outside the union. No actionable findings. Tests verify each token spelling and variant. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 463 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the public `LiteralToken::{True, False}` variants should retain their raw `SyntaxToken` payloads without separate boolean token wrappers.

### IR-089: Expose explicit pattern binding modes

- **Decision:** Add `BindingModeKind::{Var, Borrow, BorrowMut}` and `BindingMode::kind()`. Leave ordinary by-value bindings represented by an absent `BindingMode` node.
- **Reason:** Spec/03 makes the mode optional, while the HIR represents an omitted mode as `BindingMode::Value`. The syntax AST therefore reports only explicit source modes; the same accessor works for named bindings and slice-rest bindings.
- **Review:** Hostile review confirmed the omitted-mode representation matches the grammar and HIR, `borrow mut` classification matches parser consumption, and there are no malformed mode nodes produced by the parser. No actionable findings. Tests cover by-value, `var`, `borrow`, `borrow mut`, and a slice-rest binding. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 464 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that `Value` remains implicit in the syntax AST and appears only after HIR lowering.

### IR-090: Expose type modifier tokens

- **Decision:** Add `BorrowType::mut_token()`, `OwnedType::owned_token()`, and `FnPtrType::unsafe_token()` for their direct modifier tokens.
- **Reason:** Type lowering needs to distinguish shared from mutable borrows and safe from unsafe function pointers. Direct-token access also prevents an outer type from inheriting a modifier that belongs only to a nested type. The explicit `owned` spelling remains available for source-aware validation and tooling.
- **Review:** The initial hostile review found missing nested-type regressions for direct-token selection. Tests now cover `borrow borrow mut I32` and `fn(unsafe fn())`, proving the outer types do not acquire the inner modifiers; follow-up review confirmed the finding was resolved and found no remaining actionable issues. Tests also cover direct mutable/shared borrow tokens, the `owned` spelling, and safe/unsafe function pointers. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 464 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the direct-token API names and that mutability/safety remain optional when absent.

### IR-091: Expose the let-else block

- **Decision:** Add `LetElse::block()` as a generated structural accessor for the block directly contained by a let-else clause.
- **Reason:** The let statement already exposed its `LetElse` wrapper, but callers could not reach the failure block through typed AST roles. The direct child role follows the CST shape and keeps the result optional for incomplete recovered trees.
- **Review:** A hostile subagent review checked the grammar, parser construction, generated-role conventions, and recovery behavior and found no actionable issues. The regression test parses a valid let-else statement and verifies that its block contains one statement. The focused test passed; `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 464 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that the public accessor belongs on `LetElse` and should return `Option<Block>` when recovery omits the body.

### IR-092: Expose type declaration names

- **Decision:** Add `TypeDecl::name()` as a generated role selecting the declaration's direct `Name` child.
- **Reason:** The parser already retains the declared uppercase name on `TYPE_DECL`, but the typed AST exposed only generic parameters and the record/alias body. The direct role makes names available to lowering and tooling without searching descendants, where a generic parameter or field could be mistaken for the declaration name.
- **Review:** A hostile subagent review confirmed that the parser creates exactly one direct `Name` child and that nested names cannot compete with this role; no actionable findings. Regression assertions cover a record, an alias, and a recovered alias with a missing right-hand type. The focused test passed; `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 464 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that exposing the type name on `TypeDecl` matches the intended public AST shape.

### IR-093: Expose const-argument tokens

- **Decision:** Add `ConstArg::minus_token()` and `ConstArg::value_token()` for the optional direct sign and integer, character, or boolean value token.
- **Reason:** Generic const arguments lower to distinct HIR values, but the AST previously exposed only the opaque `ConstArg` node. Direct token access lets lowering decode the source value without descendant searches and preserves a missing value on recovered syntax.
- **Review:** Hostile review checked parser recovery and the planned HIR forms; no actionable findings. Tests cover negative and positive integers, a character, both boolean values, and `Foo<-,>` with a retained minus and absent value. The focused test passed; `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 464 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the token-level API is sufficient for const-argument lowering and that literal decoding stays in the existing typed token wrappers.

### IR-094: Expose module path segments

- **Decision:** Add `ModPath::segments()` as a generated iterator over direct `NameRef` children.
- **Reason:** Interface implementation and world references already exposed a `ModPath` node, but consumers could not inspect the module names without raw descendant traversal. Direct-child iteration preserves source order and keeps package paths, represented by a separate token, distinct.
- **Review:** Hostile review confirmed the parser builds each module segment as a direct `NameRef` in every use of `mod_path()`, including world and interface-impl contexts; incomplete paths retain any segments that were parsed. No actionable findings. Tests verify segment count and order for `wasi.http`, `storage`, and `common.base`. Both focused tests passed; `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 464 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that package paths should remain separate from the `ModPath` segment API.

### IR-095: Expose implicit-member expression names

- **Decision:** Add `ImplicitMemberExpr::name()` as a generated role for its direct `NameRef` child.
- **Reason:** `.Variant` expressions lower to `ExprKind::ImplicitMember(Name)`, but the syntax AST had no typed path to the variant name. The parser creates that name as the sole direct child, so an optional role is simple and remains safe for incomplete syntax.
- **Review:** Hostile review confirmed the role matches parser construction and the HIR shape, including `.Some(` recovery; no actionable findings. The regression test obtains `Some` from `.Some(1)`. The focused test passed; `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 464 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm that `.Variant` names remain `NameRef` syntax nodes before HIR lowering interns them as `Name`.

### IR-096: Add package and definition indices

- **Decision:** Add `PackageId` and `PackageInterner` to `sai_intern`, and add `DefIndex` as a typed session index with tag `0x85` but no individual `StableHash` implementation.
- **Reason:** HIR definition and owner IDs depend on these identifiers. Package IDs must be assigned by a session interner; definition indices must remain typed for `IndexVec` while their session-local raw values stay out of stable hashes.
- **Review:** Initial hostile review found that the first `DefIndex` implementation hashed its raw value and that `PackageId::new` bypassed interning. Both were corrected: `DefIndex` now implements only `Idx`, and the private `PackageId` constructor is used by an idempotent `PackageInterner` with fallible resolution. Follow-up review found no actionable issues. Tests verify package interning, resolution, unknown-ID handling, and typed `DefIndex` indexing. `cargo test -p sai_intern --quiet` passed all 10 tests; `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 465 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm package keys are canonicalized before interning and that package ID raw values or ordering never reach persisted data or output.

### IR-097: Add HIR identities and stable definition paths

- **Decision:** Add the initial HIR identity, owner, body, span, visibility, safety, and mutability types. Add all 22 `DefPathData` variants and compute `DefPathHash` from the canonical package key and resolved symbol text using explicit persistent tags `0x01..0x16` and length-prefixed UTF-8 payloads. Keep `DefPath` fields private and bind each path to the package and symbol interner sessions that created it. Tag `Symbol` and `PackageId` with an opaque session ID plus their local `u32` index; keep session IDs out of debug output, `StableHash`, and ordering.
- **Reason:** Persistent identity must not depend on session-local IDs, and its encoding must stay unambiguous across compiler versions. Hostile review showed that untagged IDs with equal local indices could be accidentally resolved by another session, producing a valid but incorrect persistent hash. Session tags make those invalid cross-session uses detectable at lookup and path construction boundaries.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found no stable hash construction path and session-dependent ordering on `DefPath`; stable encoding was added and `Ord` removed from session-local path and ID types. A follow-up found cross-session interner aliasing; session-tagged IDs and path checks address it. The final hostile review found no actionable issues. Tests cover typed IDs, all primary span context, disambiguators, stable hashes across different allocation orders, rejection of cross-session IDs, and rejection of foreign interners. `cargo test -p sai_hir -p sai_intern --quiet` passed all 15 focused tests; `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 470 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the persistent tag and payload assignments, and whether the session-tagged pair is the right memory and representation tradeoff against the original `u32` model.

### IR-098: Intern definition paths with scoped local IDs

- **Decision:** Add an append-only, mutex-protected `DefPathInterner` per package definition table. Interning an equal path is idempotent; concurrent callers share one `LocalDefId`. Bind `LocalDefId` to its package and table instance, and preserve that table scope in local `DefId`s. Foreign `DefId`s have no local-table scope and cannot resolve through a local table.
- **Reason:** The package and symbol session checks on `DefPath` do not distinguish two separate definition tables created for the same package and interner sessions. Without a table scope, both can assign index zero to different paths, and downstream maps can conflate them. The scope is session-local, omitted from Debug and persistent hashing, and has no ordering implementation.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that `LocalDefId` could be resolved by another table; adding package and table identity fixed that case. The follow-up found `to_def_id()` discarded the table scope; local `DefId`s now preserve it, and foreign IDs use a separate constructor. Final hostile review found no actionable issues. Tests cover idempotent and concurrent registration, resolution after later insertions, wrong package/session rejection, cross-table LocalDefId and DefId rejection, and foreign DefId rejection. `cargo test -p sai_hir --quiet` passed all 8 tests; `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 473 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed. Human review should confirm the private table scope on `LocalDefId` and local `DefId` alongside the package/index identity described in §2.8.2.

### IR-099: Collect source item trees

- **Decision:** Add `ItemTree::collect` for the current declaration grammar, retaining direct children in source order and collecting `use` declarations separately. Store module docs as a snapshot-bound pointer plus source text; carry raw attributes on use entries; add `has_error` to `RawAttr`; and compare item trees by semantic values without snapshot pointers or absolute ranges. Require the `AstIdMap` and root to match and verify the supplied decoded source against the root's lossless text. Reserve `Symbol::ERROR` at `(session 0, index u32::MAX)`, which the interner cannot allocate or resolve, and encode it for stable hashes with the otherwise impossible `u64::MAX` string length. Represent world imports/exports with one parent `WorldItem` entry; tuple-shape members remain in signature/type syntax rather than becoming separate item entries. Normalize interned identifier symbols to NFC after lexical validation.
- **Reason:** Item collection needs a name value for malformed declarations without inventing a resolvable symbol, and stable identities must distinguish that sentinel from every real string. The grammar assigns module docs to `MODULE_HEADER` rather than attributes, allows `@target` on uses, and gives world declarations one semantic item even when a function has a nested `FnDecl`. Direct CST-child traversal plus source offsets preserves mixed ordering without relying on separate AST iterators. Snapshot identity and source ranges are required to resolve pointers and diagnostics but are not item-tree structure, so body-only edits can preserve structural equality while the newly collected value keeps pointers bound to its own root. Checking lossless text prevents a caller from combining a valid root and ID map with unrelated source text. NFC normalization lets later semantic stages treat canonically equivalent identifiers as the same name after the required lexical error has been reported.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that an effect-set brace could truncate an impl header hash, an NFC error left the decomposed spelling interned, and byte-range movement in a preceding body made an unchanged item tree compare unequal. Hashing now starts at `impl` and stops at the item-list node, name interning normalizes identifiers and world path segments, and structural equality ignores source positions while retaining current snapshot-bound pointers. Regression tests cover distinct hashes for function-pointer effect sets, NFC error recovery, and body-length edits that move later attributes and use items. The review also confirmed that unknown attributes were being dropped without a diagnostic; that parser-validation gap is tracked as a separate follow-up. The hostile follow-up found no further actionable issues. Tests cover broad declaration collection, nested source order, stable impl-header hashes, same-file cross-snapshot rejection, mismatched source rejection, equality across body-only edits with fresh resolvable pointers, and collision-free error-symbol hashing. The final integrated workspace test run passed all 487 tests.
- **Human review:** Confirm that fields and tuple-shape members should have different item-tree status; that `WorldImport`/`WorldExport`, rather than their nested `FnDecl`, own the world-item source ID; and that query backdating retains the newly collected equal value so snapshot-bound pointers do not go stale.

### IR-100: Diagnose unknown attribute names

- **Decision:** Validate exact attribute spellings in the snapshot-bound `parse` query against one shared 17-name `ATTRIBUTE_NAMES` table. Report `UNKNOWN_ATTRIBUTE` at every unknown name, including raw identifiers. Attach a machine-applicable token replacement only when exactly one table name is within byte-wise Damerau–Levenshtein distance 2; do not choose a nearest candidate when multiple names meet the bound. Reuse `LexicalFixPlanner` so fixes replace the exact name token and preserve decoded-to-original offsets.
- **Reason:** Attribute syntax stays generic in the parser, while the implementation plan assigns the closed-name check to the parse query. A shared table keeps parser diagnostics and HIR attribute decoding synchronized. The existing token fix planner validates the operation against the same workspace snapshot and handles BOM and raw-byte spans. Filtering candidates by byte-length difference before computing distance bounds memory for very long identifiers.
- **Review:** The hostile review found no actionable issues. It confirmed the 17-name table matches spec/15 §15.2.3, raw identifiers remain unknown, diagnostics cover the name, fixes use the exact token and snapshot-bound original-byte mapping, and only a single candidate within distance 2 receives a fix. It also checked that length filtering bounds the distance matrix for long names; `@col` exercises an ambiguous candidate set (`cold` and `doc`). Tests cover a unique transposition fix, raw-identifier rejection with a prefix-removal suggestion, ambiguous and distant names without fixes, Damerau–Levenshtein transposition behavior, and original-byte offsets after a BOM. The focused syntax/HIR run passed all 413 tests; the workspace run passed all 487 tests. Formatting and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that “unique within distance 2” means exactly one table name satisfies the bound, even if that candidate is not the closest one, and that a raw attribute such as `@r#derive` may offer the `@derive` fix while still being diagnosed as unknown.

### IR-101: Define attribute validation schemas

- **Decision:** Add `sai_hir::attrs::ATTRIBUTE_SCHEMAS`, indexed by the existing `AttrName`, with the 17 attribute stages, broad target kinds, argument grammar categories, repeatability, conflicts, and special target cases. Represent contextual target restrictions as `TargetRule` conditions; represent `@inline`/`@cold` with the conditional `InlineIsHint` conflict. Keep this milestone as schema data and expose no validator yet.
- **Reason:** The attribute checks are driven by one closed spec table, and a typed HIR-owned schema lets later validation consume the same source of truth. Some target decisions need context beyond an item kind (such as function body, export visibility, extern ABI, enclosing interface, or declaration owner); encoding those facts explicitly avoids pretending that a kind-only bitset is sufficient. Keeping this milestone data-only allows its spec mapping to be reviewed before diagnostics or validation behavior depend on it.
- **Review:** The spec-matrix audit found that the implementation note incorrectly allowed lint groups; it now follows §15.15.5 and admits only lint codes. The first hostile code review found that `@must_use` did not record its non-empty string constraint; `OptionalString` was changed to `OptionalNonEmptyString`, and the follow-up found no further mismatches. Tests cover all 17 names in shared order, all stage values, argument grammar categories, the three special target cases, context-sensitive target rules, repeatability, conflict conditions, and the complete 25-kind target inventory. `cargo test --workspace --quiet` passed all 493 tests; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the `TargetCondition` inputs can be derived from the item tree/CST for every attribute target, particularly inherent versus trait/interface impl functions, extern ABI and safety, and trait-owned associated types. Confirm the scope of the special target cases and the use of a conditional conflict rule for `@inline(never)` with `@cold`.

### IR-102: Validate Stage 0 attributes

- **Decision:** Implement `validate_attrs` in `sai_hir::attrs` over a raw attribute list, caller-supplied syntactic target context, symbol interner, and workspace snapshot. Validate target and argument rules, duplicate attributes and arguments, and conflicts; retain decoded Stage 0 values; gate later-stage attributes with `FEATURE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`; and omit attributes marked `has_error` to avoid cascading diagnostics. Compare duplicate `@target` operands by their written source spelling. Preserve raw-identifier and value-span information on key/value attribute arguments so the plain `instead` key and the string-specific diagnostic range can be represented exactly. When an empty `instead` path is invalid, drop that part and retain a valid message. Require bodies for `@inline` and `@cold` on every function target kind. Retain plain lint identifier spellings, including lowercase names, for later registry validation. Add `sai_diagnostics` as a direct dependency of `sai_hir` so the validator can return snapshot-bound diagnostics using the shared diagnostic model. Record the dependency in the architecture table.
- **Reason:** The Stage 0 rules are owned by HIR validation, and failures must use the compiler's structured diagnostic codes, labels, child help, source references, and original-byte spans. Returning diagnostics through an ad hoc HIR type would duplicate that contract. The specification says target operands must be written identically, so decoded string equality would incorrectly flag distinct spellings; it also explicitly keeps the deprecation message when an invalid replacement path is dropped. Lint argument grammar accepts plain identifiers regardless of case; the registry later classifies an unknown lowercase spelling and offers its uppercase fix. Inline and cold rules require an implemented function body, including for recovered function declarations. A missing target kind suppresses checks because recovery cannot establish which target rules apply. The item tree does not yet retain enough contextual information for every rule, so this slice accepts that context explicitly; deriving it and integrating validation with item collection remain follow-up work.
- **Review:** The first hostile pass identified four issues: raw `instead` keys lost their raw flag, lint validation rejected lowercase identifiers too early, `@inline`/`@cold` did not require a body on free and impl functions, and normalized `@target` predicates could be mistaken for identically written operands. The raw-key and spelling fixes were applied while the review was running; body gating and lint-shape handling were then corrected. A second pass found that an empty `instead` diagnostic covered the whole key/value argument; `RawAttrArg::KeyValue` now retains the value-token span, the diagnostic uses the string literal span, and the regression checks its exact two-byte range. The final adversarial follow-up found no remaining issues. Regression tests cover all five findings, invalid `instead` part retention, an actual lexer error in an attribute, and BOM offsets. Focused HIR tests pass (33 tests); the full workspace suite passes (504 tests); workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. The current slice validates Stage 0 values and `@target` predicate syntax; it does not evaluate predicates to filter items, resolve `@deprecated(instead = ...)`, consult the lint registry, or run type/package-level attribute checks.
