A separate name lookup lets that rule work without pretending every documented code already has a diagnostic producer. The test suite compares the checked-in name snapshot, hard warning/info sets, and lint metadata directly with the specification tables. Marking the public enum non-exhaustive makes later additions compatible with downstream matches; the pre-1.0 API transition is recorded for review.
- **Review:** The first hostile pass found a bogus name in the generated snapshot, a conflict between the fixed distance-2 rule for `UNKNOWN_LINT` and the generic candidate threshold, and an undocumented `data` schema. The extra name was removed and a workspace test now compares all 492 names with Appendix A. Spec/15's specific distance-2 rule is stated as an exception in impl/11 §11.4.5; the diagnostic's `name` and `candidates` fields are documented there and covered by HIR tests. A distance-3 regression ensures it is excluded. The review also prompted the explicit non-exhaustive API decision. Follow-up hostile review found no remaining issues. All 757 workspace tests passed, including 35 diagnostics and 280 HIR tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that marking `Code` non-exhaustive is the intended pre-stable API transition. IR-187 supersedes the partial `Code::ALL`/`Code::from_name` state by generating the complete registry while preserving existing enum ordering. `sai explain` metadata and reuse of this validation for package `lints { }` and CLI flags remain follow-up work.

### IR-185: Resolve effective lint levels

- **Decision:** Add a pure `sai_diagnostics::resolve_lint_occurrence` API over a registered lint code and caller-supplied validated configuration. Resolve sources in policy, innermost applicable attribute, CLI, manifest, and default order. Determine attribute applicability from the diagnostic primary span, requiring the same file and complete containment in the target syntax range; choose the smallest containing range. Represent CLI levels as a location-free `OverrideLintLevel`, and manifest levels as `ManifestLintLevel` with a required source location. Support the three v0.1 lint policies, with `AMBIENT_IMPORT` and `CONTRACT_RUNTIME_CHECK` scoped to the occurrence's package and root `DUPLICATE_PACKAGE_LINE` policy applying across the graph. Suppress `allow`, then apply the dependency cap, then `--deny-warnings`. Keep `LevelInfo.value` at the pre-post-processing effective level when `--deny-warnings` changes a warning's severity, and set its source to `deny_warnings`; hard warnings use the same severity helper and keep `level: null`.
- **Reason:** The source precedence and both post-processing rules are normative, but the workspace has not yet implemented manifest loading, a CLI driver, a query engine, or package collection. A pure resolver makes the rules executable and testable now, while explicit inputs avoid inventing package or query state that the repository does not have.
- **Review:** The first adversarial pass found that a shared CLI/manifest override type allowed a CLI level to carry a source location, contrary to §11.3.4. The inputs now have source-specific types, and a regression asserts the CLI result has no location. Follow-up adversarial review found no actionable issue. The diagnostics-crate tests cover every precedence source, innermost range selection and cross-file exclusion, package-local and graph-wide policy behavior, dependency suppression, warning promotion for lint and hard diagnostics, info preservation, and hard/unknown code rejection. All 763 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that callers convert both the primary diagnostic span and HIR attribute syntax ranges to the same original-file byte offsets before resolution; wire policy and root/workspace membership from the package collector; integrate `LintResolution` into the diagnostic collection pipeline; and add the `lint_levels(OwnerId)` query when the query system is introduced.

### IR-186: Collect source-bound lint attribute scopes

- **Decision:** Add `TargetFilterResult::scoped_lint_levels` to resolve each retained `use` and ItemTree entry's AST ID against the current root, then pair its validated lint attributes with the syntax node's text range. Convert attribute and scope spans from decoded text to original-file offsets and source references using the current workspace snapshot. Require exact correspondence between retained tree IDs and `validated_attrs` keys; reject stale roots, snapshots, foreign symbols, invalid levels, and spans outside their target.
- **Reason:** The diagnostics resolver needs full declaration ranges to choose the innermost applicable attribute. `TargetFilterResult` already holds validated attrs for retained entries, while the AST map and lossless syntax tree own the exact source ranges. Producing this bridge now connects those existing layers without inventing the absent query engine or owner database.
- **Review:** A focused regression covers outer doc-comment and attribute starts, a nested field override, a `use` target, original-file offsets, resolution through `resolve_lint_occurrence`, and suppression of attributes on a false `@target`. Adversarial review found no actionable issue. All 764 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that parser-rejected parameter, generic-parameter, record-variant-field, and world-item positions remain excluded; connect returned scopes to the future `lint_levels(OwnerId)` query and ensure the build driver supplies the exact `TargetFilterResult` for the diagnostic's file and target.

### IR-187: Generate the complete diagnostic code enum

- **Decision:** Generate `Code`, `Code::ALL`, `Code::as_str`, and `Code::from_name` from the checked-in Appendix A name snapshot. Preserve the pre-existing variant order in `diagnostic_code_legacy_order.txt`, then append the remaining registered codes in snapshot order so derived `Ord` and existing ordered collections keep their prior behavior. Reject malformed, unsorted, duplicate, invalid, or colliding code and variant names at build time.
- **Reason:** The lint resolver recognizes all 492 codes, while the smaller typed enum prevented callers from naming registered codes that do not yet have producers. Generating both APIs from the same snapshot removes drift. Keeping a separate frozen legacy order protects a public ordering behavior that the derived traits expose.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that generating all variants in snapshot order changed derived `Ord` for existing codes and that the review ledger still claimed the enum was partial. The second pass found that the legacy order file was also serving as its own test expectation and that impl/02 did not describe the legacy-first order. Pinned the independent 76-entry count and FNV-1a sequence fingerprint in the build script, documented the ordering in impl/02, and updated IR-184. A follow-up adversarial review found no remaining issues. The exact old variant sequence was compared with the previous enum; all 764 workspace tests passed, workspace Clippy with warnings denied passed, and rustfmt plus `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that preserving derived `Ord` for the old variants is the intended public compatibility rule. Before the first stable release, review whether callers should rely on `Code` ordering or sort by `as_str`.

### IR-188: Start the revisioned query runtime

- **Decision:** Add `sai_query` with typed synchronous `InputQuery` and `DerivedQuery` handles. Start revisions at 1; compare input values by `Eq`; advance each changed input's durability stamp and every less durable stamp; and compute derived durability as the minimum of its observed dependencies. Track absent inputs through `get_opt`, retain tombstones after removal, record dependencies in read order, and use stable fingerprints only to select memo buckets before checking keys by `Eq`. Verify dependencies recursively, replace dynamic dependencies on recomputation, backdate equal derived values, detect same-key cycles, expose an atomic cancellation token, and convert query panics to `QueryError`.
- **Reason:** The query system is a Stage 0 dependency for VFS, frontend, and the driver, but this workspace has no query runtime to connect them to. A synchronous typed core makes the red-green and durability rules executable and testable before adding query-group generation, persistence, or parallel execution. Fingerprint collision buckets preserve equality semantics, and an explicit optional-input read lets queries depend on absence without treating it as an error.
- **Review:** The first adversarial pass found that mutating an input from a query callback could commit a stale value at a later revision, and that a parent could swallow a child's cycle or panic and memoize a fallback. Input set/remove now fail during query execution, query failures poison active parent frames, and the API provides tracked `get_opt` for absent inputs. Regression tests cover mutation attempts, swallowed cycles and panics, and absent-to-present-to-removed input transitions. The follow-up adversarial review found no remaining actionable issues. All 778 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the synchronous database is the right first milestone and keep it explicitly short of the full architecture contract. Later work must add query-group traits, interned and transparent query kinds, concurrent execution and wait-for cycle detection, LRU behavior, cancellation integration with input updates, VFS/frontend wiring, and persistent caching before this runtime can serve as the compiler's complete query system.

### IR-189: Add session-local interned and transparent query kinds

- **Decision:** Add `InternedQuery<T>` with equality-checked stable-fingerprint buckets and IDs scoped to both the interner table and database session; do not implement `StableHash` for those IDs. Add uncached `TransparentQuery<K, V>` calls that attribute reads to the nearest enclosing derived query. Keep revision and durability counters private to the query runtime. Any query runtime error other than cancellation poisons the session, preserves the first non-cancellation error, and raises cancellation so later work cannot reuse a compromised session. Check cancellation after a transparent callback completes; do not permit resetting cancellation during query execution.
- **Reason:** The architecture defines four query kinds and requires interned values to live for the session, transparent queries to remain uncached, cycles to be internal compiler errors, and caught panics to poison the session. Source errors are diagnostics carried in query values, so every other `QueryError` represents a runtime or invariant failure and is treated as session-fatal. Session-bound IDs avoid accidentally treating process-local identity as stable data. Routing transparent reads to the nearest memoized parent preserves invalidation without introducing a memo or dependency node for the transparent function itself.
- **Review:** Adversarial reviews found that transparent cycles and panics were returned as recoverable errors, cancellation arriving during a transparent callback could be missed, a parent could replace an earlier ICE with a later error or panic, query callbacks could inspect the revision, cancellation could be reset during a query or hide a later fatal error, and some invariant errors did not stop the session. The runtime now retains the first fatal failure across callback results, including failures after cancellation, exposes counters only inside the crate, poisons on every non-cancellation query error, permits cancellation reset only between queries, checks cancellation after callback completion, and has regressions for swallowed or replaced failures, transparent panics, input mutation, invalid interned IDs, and in-callback cancellation and reset attempts. Final hostile review found no actionable findings. All 786 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that interned IDs should be valid only within their originating query table and database session, that transparent reads should be attributed to the nearest enclosing derived query, that only cancellation is recoverable by the session, that every other query runtime error should poison it, and that query callbacks should not inspect revision or durability counters or clear cancellation. Concurrent execution, wait-for cycle detection, query-group traits, LRU behavior, VFS/frontend wiring, and persistent caching remain unimplemented.

### IR-190: Add query-backed raw VFS file inputs

- **Decision:** Key `file_contents` inputs by a validated `FileKey` containing an explicit stable package key and canonical package-relative path, never by session-local `FileId` or editor document version. Normalize versioned `dep:` source references after checking that the selected release belongs to the stable key's compatibility line; accept the built-in `sai:core` and `sai:alloc` keys as well. Store exact raw bytes or the I/O error kind as `FileContents`, track absent reads with `get_opt`, assign durability per file, and reconcile immutable `VfsInputSnapshot` values in one query revision. Keep `WorkspaceSnapshot` as the separate decoded-source view used by existing syntax and diagnostic consumers. Implement `StableHash` for `Arc<T>` by hashing its contents, never its pointer.
- **Reason:** The query runtime requires stable-hashable keys and must not hash session identifiers. `FileId` remains the syntax/span handle. A source snapshot path may be workspace-relative, so the driver must combine the configured source directory with a `PackageSourcePath` before constructing a package-relative stable key; manifest and WIT paths are already package-relative. The stable package key is the resolver's `<namespace>:<name>#<compatibility-line>` form, so a dependency reference's exact release version must not become part of the file key. Document versions only identify editor snapshots, while byte/error equality determines whether file input changed. Raw bytes and read failures are inputs so a later `source_text` query can own decoding and diagnostics.
- **Review:** The first adversarial review found that package-less files could collide, snapshots could bypass identity validation, read errors were unrepresentable, all package roles were forced to low durability, update batches created multiple revisions, and the direct `sai_stable_hash` dependency was absent from the architecture table. A follow-up found that built-in package references were rejected, read failures could not survive snapshot replacement, the decoded workspace snapshot was being used as a raw-input source, and workspace-relative paths could be mistaken for stable key paths. The input and decoded snapshot types are now separate; snapshot reconciliation preserves read-error values and applies removals atomically; built-in dependency references are accepted; and the raw-key constructor validates canonical path syntax. IR-196 makes source path conversion explicit and normalizes both path forms to one package-relative key. A final hostile review found no actionable issues. All 798 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm the resolver's stable-key spelling, the package-source-relative path supplied for workspace members, and the driver's mapping between `FileKey` and session-local `FileId`. Package and WIT inputs, syntax query wiring, and package graph-driven durability assignment remain for later steps.

### IR-191: Add tracked source-text decoding

- **Decision:** Add a `source_text` derived query keyed by stable `FileKey` that reads and decodes `file_contents`. Retain the original bytes, BOM state, and invalid UTF-8 ranges. Represent missing input, read failure, and source-size overflow as `SourceTextStatus` values with an empty `SourceFile` so callers can continue syntax recovery. Rebind the requesting session's `FileId` only after retrieving the memoized value; exclude it from `SourceText` equality and stable hashing. When an input's value stays equal but its durability changes, preserve its `changed_at` revision and update dependent memo durability metadata without rerunning query functions.
- **Reason:** Decoding is deterministic from raw file bytes and belongs behind the query boundary so unchanged files do not need to be decoded again. Querying by stable `FileKey` avoids retaining a second unbounded cache of historical contents. `FileId` is session-local and must not leak into the stable query key or result fingerprint. Read failures and implementation limits are source conditions that the driver should report as diagnostics, not query-runtime failures.
- **Review:** The first adversarial review found that a durability-only input update could recompute `source_text` and decode unchanged bytes again. A proposed content-keyed decoding query avoided that recomputation but retained every historical source value because query memos have no eviction; it was removed. The final design fixes the root cause in `InputQuery`: equal values keep their `changed_at` revision while durability stamps advance, allowing dependency validation to refresh durability metadata without invoking pure query bodies. Regression coverage verifies both the query-function behavior and source-text behavior across durability-only updates. Focused VFS tests cover BOM removal, invalid UTF-8 metadata, `FileId` rebinding, content invalidation, durability-only updates, missing files, read failures, and oversized sources. The final adversarial review found no actionable issues. All 800 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Verify how the driver maps `Missing`, `ReadError`, and `TooLarge` into `SOURCE_READ_FAILED` and `IMPLEMENTATION_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, and confirm the stable-key-to-`FileId` mapping remains session-local. The VFS does not emit diagnostics directly; the existing syntax layer continues to report invalid UTF-8 from the retained source metadata.

### IR-192: Add tracked package and WIT file snapshots

- **Decision:** Add a validated `PackageKey` query key, source-relative paths and typed file/directory/symlink entries for `package_files`, and package-relative `.wit` paths for `wit_files`. Sort both listings by UTF-8 bytes, reject invalid paths and duplicate entries, and preserve missing-source-directory and not-applicable/empty-WIT states. Store all three logical VFS input families in one typed input table so a full snapshot replacement reconciles their additions, changes, and removals in one revision.
- **Reason:** `PackageId` and `FileId` are session-local and must not enter stable query keys or fingerprints. The current VFS therefore records stable package keys and paths; the future driver maps them to the session IDs required by package and WIT queries. One input table lets file bytes and both directory listings become visible as a coherent filesystem snapshot.
- **Review:** The first adversarial review found that the package inventory accepted descendants below a symlink or regular file, and that the WIT listing accepted nested `.wit` files. The package snapshot now requires every ancestor to be a listed directory; the WIT snapshot records the declared directory and accepts only direct child `.wit` files. Follow-up adversarial review found no remaining actionable issues. Focused VFS tests pass for these cases along with path validation, deterministic ordering, symlink-kind retention, missing versus present directories, not-applicable versus empty WIT listings, tracked absence and removal, and reconciliation of all input families. All 804 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the driver's `PackageId` to `PackageKey` mapping uses the canonical stable package key, that source-directory entries remain relative to the configured source directory before `intern_source_file` assigns `FileId`, and that WIT paths remain relative to the package directory before `intern_package_file` assigns `FileId`. Ensure `WIT_DIR_NOT_FOUND` is reported by package loading independently of the `NotApplicable` WIT listing state.

### IR-193: Convert source-text statuses into diagnostics

- **Decision:** Convert `Ready` to no diagnostic, `Missing` and `ReadError` to `SOURCE_READ_FAILED`, and `TooLarge` to `IMPLEMENTATION_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`. Use the canonical `SourceRef.path` in `data.path`, normalize I/O reasons to lowercase single-line text, and map a missing `file_contents` input for a requested listed file to the reason `not found`. Attach an empty primary span at byte zero of the VFS's empty placeholder source and copy the source package identity onto the diagnostic.
- **Reason:** The architecture requires listed-file read failures and source-size overflows to be ordinary diagnostics and retains an empty source so later stages can continue safely. The empty span names the known file despite there being no offending source bytes, while the structured fields preserve canonical paths and machine-readable cause/limit values. `not found` matches the lowercase display text for a not-found I/O error and gives the missing-input status a deterministic diagnostic. Normalization makes malformed enum-style or multiline input conform to the lowercase, single-line diagnostic contract, and a bounded reason keeps the message within the documented 100-character guideline.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that arbitrary `io_error_kind` strings could violate lowercase, single-line, bounded-message, and no-trailing-period requirements. The diagnostic boundary now converts CamelCase kind names to lowercase words, removes later lines, collapses whitespace, strips trailing periods, uses `unknown error` for an empty value, and truncates long reasons with a Unicode ellipsis; regressions cover mixed case, newlines, trailing punctuation, and long reasons. The final hostile review found no actionable issues. All 810 workspace tests passed, including 47 diagnostics tests; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a missing VFS input for a listed file should be reported as `SOURCE_READ_FAILED` with reason `not found`, and that byte-zero empty spans, I/O reason normalization/truncation, and the source-text diagnostic wording fit the established output contract.

### IR-194: Memoize line indexes by stable file key

- **Decision:** Add `LineIndex` equality and stable hashing over its decoded text, line starts, BOM shift, invalid UTF-8 metadata, and offset deltas. Expose a VFS `line_index(FileKey)` derived query that reads `source_text` and returns the same shared `Arc<LineIndex>`, without adding session-local file IDs to the key or value.
- **Reason:** Line indexes are immutable data derived from decoded source, and the existing decoder already constructs the complete index needed for CRLF, BOM, invalid UTF-8, and UTF-16 positions. Reusing that allocation keeps the query layer deterministic and avoids another per-file scan. Keying by `FileKey` preserves stable identity while leaving `FileId` available for spans at the syntax boundary.
- **Review:** VFS tests verify BOM-aware byte positions, reuse across durability-only updates, and invalidation both when BOM metadata changes and when line structure changes. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 811 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the VFS-level `line_index(FileKey)` query is the intended precursor to the compiler-facing `line_index(FileId)` API, and that the future driver preserves the session-local mapping when syntax spans are produced.

### IR-195: Intern stable file keys into session-local file IDs

- **Decision:** Add a per-session `FileInterner` keyed by validated `FileKey`. Assign IDs in insertion order, return the existing ID for an equal key, support reverse lookup, and report exhaustion instead of wrapping the `u32` ID space. Keep `FileId` out of stable hashing and use the interner's ID when requesting VFS `source_text`.
- **Reason:** The VFS queries use stable package-and-path keys, while syntax spans require a session-local `FileId`. A session-owned mapping bridges these layers and normalizes dependency release references through `FileKey` before assigning IDs. Insertion-order IDs remain internal and never affect output ordering or query identity.
- **Review:** VFS tests verify release-reference normalization, ID reuse, reverse lookup, and rebinding the ID into `source_text`. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 812 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that source paths are combined with the configured source directory into a canonical package-relative `FileKey`, package-root paths use the same key representation, and the driver owns exactly one `FileInterner` per session.

### IR-196: Bridge package IDs to interned file IDs

- **Decision:** Add `FileInterner::intern_source_file` for a `PackageSourceDirectory` plus a `PackageSourcePath` relative to that directory, and `FileInterner::intern_package_file` for `PackageRelativePath` values relative to the package root, including `sai.package` and WIT files. Both resolve a `PackageId` through the supplied `PackageInterner` and intern a stable `FileKey` whose path is canonical and package-relative. Keep diagnostic `dep:` references separate from the typed path inputs; `FileKey::new` normalizes those references to the same package-relative identity. Return explicit errors for foreign/unknown package IDs, invalid file keys, and exhausted file-ID space.
- **Reason:** Source snapshots expose workspace-relative paths, package inventories expose source-directory-relative paths, and manifests/WIT are package-root-relative. Requiring the configured source directory alongside the source-relative path lets the VFS construct the canonical package-relative identity that also matches dependency references. Resolving `PackageId` through its owning interner validates session membership while keeping session IDs out of stable query keys.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that one `intern_file` argument could not distinguish source-directory-relative files from package-root-relative metadata/WIT. The API now has `intern_source_file(PackageSourceDirectory, PackageSourcePath)` and `intern_package_file(PackageRelativePath)`. A follow-up found that dependency `dep:` references are package-relative and could produce a different key for the same file when the source directory was not the package root. `FileKey` now stores only canonical package-relative paths; the source API joins the configured source directory and source-relative path, and dependency-reference normalization resolves to that same key. A later review found that `<stdin>` could alias an ordinary package path, so typed filesystem paths now reserve it while `FileKey::new` keeps the sentinel; another stale architecture table was updated to match the stable `PackageKey` inputs and path-valued WIT snapshot. Regressions cover `.`, path traversal, same-file key/ID equivalence, paths under different package roots, and the stdin sentinel distinction. Final hostile review found no actionable issues. All 813 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that source paths are combined with the configured source directory, package metadata/WIT paths are already package-relative, diagnostic dependency references normalize to the same `FileKey`, and the driver owns exactly one `FileInterner` per session.

### IR-197: Give green syntax trees stable structural fingerprints

- **Decision:** Implement `StableHash` for `SyntaxKind`, `GreenTrivia`, `GreenToken`, and `GreenNode`. Encode all equality-significant token and trivia data, including leading/trailing trivia, token error state, text, and text length. Traverse green nodes iteratively in source order rather than recursively, and exclude pointer identity, allocation layout, cached child offsets, and the process-local structural hash.
- **Reason:** `GreenNode` is the immutable, source-independent result that can be shared across snapshots; the red `SyntaxNode` and AST IDs remain snapshot-bound. A structural stable fingerprint is a prerequisite for memoizing parse results without hashing session IDs or recursively walking attacker-controlled syntax depth. Cache-only metadata must not influence semantic equality or query fingerprints.
- **Review:** The hostile review checked equality coverage, tag stability, determinism, and deep-tree behavior and found no actionable findings. The regression constructs independent trees, verifies identical fingerprints, and checks that token text and trivia edits change the fingerprint. All 400 `sai_syntax` tests passed; crate Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the `SyntaxKind` discriminant encoding and the syntax-specific stable-hash tags are suitable for the query/cache compatibility boundary. The derived `parse(FileId) -> Parse` query and snapshot-specific diagnostic/fix rebinding remain unimplemented; this change only makes the green-tree value stable-hashable.

### IR-198: Add snapshot-independent diagnostic templates

- **Decision:** Add `Diagnostic::to_template` and `DiagnosticTemplate::{bind, StableHash}`. Preserve diagnostic code, severity, message, byte ranges, labels, children, structured data, and node/symbol/package metadata; omit `FileId` and `SourceRef`, then bind them from the requesting snapshot. Reject mixed-file diagnostics and diagnostics with resolved levels, related locations, fixes, a bound workspace snapshot, or poison facts because those carry query- or snapshot-specific state. Reject diagnostic data nested deeper than 128 values; retain iterative hashing for structured data.
- **Reason:** A memoized parse result must return the same source diagnostic semantics after file IDs, paths, and editor document versions change. Storing only ranges allows the query consumer to bind the same diagnostic to its current source. Snapshot-bound fixes and later diagnostic enrichment need to be recreated after binding instead of being retained by the query memo. `Value` uses recursive clone/equality/drop, so the template boundary caps its nested structured data while keeping the stable-hash walk iterative.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that unrestricted nested `Value` data could overflow while cloning, comparing, formatting, or dropping a template, and that equal-only fingerprint assertions did not prove field sensitivity. Template conversion now rejects over-limit nested data, and regressions verify both the rejection and iterative hashing of a 20,000-level fixture; the fixture is intentionally retained until test-process exit to avoid testing the pre-existing recursive `Value` destructor. Fingerprint tests change the message and nested data independently and assert that each changes the fingerprint. Snapshot rebinding is also checked against `WorkspaceSnapshot`. Final hostile review found no actionable findings. All 817 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm the 128-value nesting bound for memoized diagnostic data and whether every parse diagnostic's structured data stays within it. Confirm that query callers should recreate snapshot-bound fixes and apply any diagnostic level/related-location enrichment after binding.

### IR-199: Memoize syntax parsing by stable file key

- **Decision:** Add `SyntaxQueries` with a `DerivedQuery<FileKey, GreenParse>` that reads VFS `source_text`, builds a lossless green tree, and retains snapshot-independent diagnostic templates and fix recipes. Keep the existing `parse(snapshot, file_id)` API; the query adapter resolves the stable key through `FileInterner`, checks exact raw-byte equality with the workspace snapshot, and binds a fresh red root and diagnostics to that snapshot. Recreate unknown-attribute fixes from token index, decoded range, and replacement text after binding. Use the reserved `Tree(u64::MAX)` identity for the temporary red root used only for query-local AST traversal, so parsing does not mutate the global tree-ID counter; do not let its pointers or IDs escape. Treat `ParseError` from the query computation as an internal invariant failure for the query runtime to capture.
- **Reason:** A syntax memo must not retain a `FileId`, snapshot ID, document version, source path, or snapshot-bound fix. The stable VFS key and bytes determine syntax; the requesting snapshot supplies the current source reference and file identity. Reusing one parsing pipeline for direct and memoized entry points keeps diagnostics, ordering, and fix semantics aligned, while exact byte validation prevents rebinding an old result to changed content.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable findings; it checked stable-key dependency tracking, `FileId` and diagnostic-metadata rebinding, BOM/CRLF fix ranges, and query failure propagation. Regressions verify agreement with direct parsing, memo reuse, rebinding to a different file ID and document version, source-change invalidation, and rejection of uninterned, missing-snapshot, or byte-mismatched inputs. All 819 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that `FileKey` is the intended identity for memoized parses while `Parse` remains snapshot-bound at the API edge; that `Tree(u64::MAX)` remains private to the temporary traversal root; and that internal parse-construction failures should poison the query session as invariant errors.

### IR-200: Memoize snapshot-independent AST ID shapes

- **Decision:** Add a derived query keyed by stable `FileKey` whose value is the ordered `SyntaxKind` sequence of item-like green nodes. Traverse the green tree iteratively in source order. When constructing the public `AstIdMap`, verify that sequence against the item-like nodes of the requesting snapshot-bound red root, then build the current `SyntaxNodePtr` table and typed IDs. Reject a stale or mismatched shape explicitly.
- **Reason:** `AstIdMap` contains `FileId`, workspace-snapshot identity, and syntax pointers, so it cannot be reused as a stable query value across sessions or snapshots. The erased AST IDs depend on the preorder sequence of item-like nodes; storing only their kinds preserves that sequence while excluding ranges and pointers that change after body edits. The query engine can then backdate the shape when a body-only edit leaves item-like ordering intact, while each caller receives pointers scoped to its current root.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable findings. It confirmed that the iterative green traversal matches the red-tree preorder, the shape includes the ordered item-like kinds and validates kind plus count before rebinding, and snapshot pointers are recreated for each root. Regressions cover cross-session rebinding, rejection of old snapshot IDs, shape backdating after a body edit, and shape invalidation after adding a declaration. All 819 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm that ordered item-like kinds fully describe the stable part of the AST ID assignment, while ranges and snapshot pointers must be rebuilt for every requesting root. Confirm that body-only edits should preserve the memoized shape when no item-like node is added, removed, or reordered.

### IR-201: Add stable semantic item-tree shapes

- **Decision:** Add `ItemTree::stable_shape(&Interner) -> StableItemTree`. Store all semantic item-tree fields, including ordered erased AST IDs, module-document presence and text, declaration kinds and visibility, impl header hashes, and nested raw-attribute values. Resolve each non-error `Symbol` to owned text and encode `Symbol::ERROR` as a distinct sentinel. Exclude `FileId`, workspace-snapshot identity, AST pointers, and source ranges. Give every shape and enum variant an explicit stable-hash tag.
- **Reason:** `ItemTree` cannot be used directly as a query value because it includes session-local symbols and snapshot-bound pointers and ranges. A stable projection separates the semantic structure that query equality and fingerprints need from the source locations that consumers must rebuild for the current snapshot. Retaining erased IDs preserves the preorder identity already assigned by `AstIdMap`. This is query groundwork only; it does not yet implement `def_collector` or rebind an `ItemTree` from the stable shape.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable findings. It checked the semantic field projection, sentinel and foreign-interner behavior, omission of snapshot-local locations, and explicit enum encodings. Regressions verify equal shapes and fingerprints across interner sessions and body edits that move later ranges, changed fingerprints after a declaration rename, the error-name sentinel, and rejection of foreign symbols. All 821 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm the stable-hash tag assignments, that module-document presence and text both belong in semantic equality, and that erased preorder IDs should be retained in a reusable item-tree shape. The shape is not yet persisted or used by a query.

### IR-202: Expose ephemeral AST views to stable queries

- **Decision:** Add `SyntaxQueries::with_query_ast`, which supplies the tracked decoded source, a query-local red root, and an `AstIdMap` rebound from the memoized item-kind shape to a callback. Require the callback result to be `Clone + Eq + StableHash + Send + Sync + 'static`. Give each callback root a unique `RootIdentity::Query` from its own checked atomic counter; keep the fixed `Tree(u64::MAX)` root only for internal syntax analysis. Keep `AstIdMap::new` rejecting tree- and query-local roots; only the query binder accepts the new query identity and validates kind order and count.
- **Reason:** HIR needs AST traversal and item IDs while a stable query is running, but constructing a `WorkspaceSnapshot` there would allocate an unrelated snapshot identity and bind pointers to a synthetic snapshot. The callback lets the HIR layer consume memoized syntax without that identity, while `StableItemTree` can discard its temporary pointers and ranges. A unique root identity makes an accidentally retained AST ID or pointer fail to resolve on a later callback, even when the temporary file ID is the same.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that reusing `Tree(u64::MAX)` and `FileId(0)` across callbacks let stale IDs resolve to different nodes. The implementation now gives callback roots distinct query identities; regressions verify old AST IDs and pointers are rejected, item-kind and item-count mismatches fail, and the public `AstIdMap::new` still rejects query roots. Follow-up review found no remaining identity or shape-validation issue. The callback contract still cannot statically prevent a caller from capturing query-local handles or wrapping one in a custom stable result; the intended HIR caller returns the private-field `StableItemTree` projection, and escaped handles fail closed because identities are unique. All 821 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm the separate atomic identity space for query roots and the callback contract that query-local pointers, IDs, and ranges must not be retained. The trait bounds cannot prevent all side effects or custom wrappers from capturing handles; verify actual query callers return only snapshot-independent values.

### IR-203: Memoize item-tree shapes by stable file key

- **Decision:** Add `ItemTreeQueries::stable_item_tree(FileKey) -> Arc<StableItemTree>` as the derived query named `def_collector`. It reads decoded source and the green parse through `SyntaxQueries::with_query_ast`, collects an ephemeral `ItemTree`, and returns its stable projection. Make `ItemTree.snapshot_id` optional so query-local trees can exist without a fabricated workspace snapshot; only snapshot-bound trees can match AST maps or enter target filtering. Add the direct `sai_query` dependency to `sai_hir`.
- **Reason:** Query outputs must not retain `FileId`, snapshot IDs, AST pointers, absolute ranges, or session-local symbols. The `StableItemTree` projection is now produced inside the `FileKey` query, and red-green backdating can retain the old value after a body-only edit because it contains no source locations. The stable item-kind ordinals are kept because consumers need source identities for current-root rebinding. Returning the snapshot-bound `ItemTree` through the query still requires a later rebinding API and query-group adapter.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable findings. It confirmed tracked dependencies on VFS source and memoized syntax, equal-shape backdating, exclusion of snapshot-local values, and rejection of query-local trees by target filtering, lint-scope collection, and lowering. Regressions verify `Arc` reuse on repeated reads and body-only edits, invalidation on declaration rename, and equal shapes across different stable file keys. All 822 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm the new `sai_hir -> sai_query` dependency, that ephemeral item trees must have no snapshot identity, and that retaining old location-free shapes is safe after backdating. The `def_collector(FileId) -> ItemTree` API, query-group trait, and current-snapshot rebinding remain open.

### IR-204: Rebind item trees to the requesting snapshot

- **Decision:** Add `ItemTreeQueries::def_collector` as a snapshot adapter returning a fresh `Arc<ItemTree>`. Resolve the `FileKey` from `FileInterner`, parse and build the AST ID map through `SyntaxQueries` for the supplied `WorkspaceSnapshot`, collect the current tree with the caller's symbol interner, and compare its stable shape with the memoized `FileKey` query value. Return `FileNotInterned` before parsing when the session has no matching key.
- **Reason:** Snapshot pointers, IDs, and ranges must belong to the requesting root, while the memoized semantic shape intentionally excludes them. Recollecting through the current parse ensures every returned pointer and span is fresh; checking the stable shape catches a mismatch between the session adapter and the stable-key query. Query-local trees with no snapshot ID remain ineligible for APIs that require exact snapshot binding.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that `FileNotInterned` was unreachable because key lookup followed a parse call that performed the same check. The lookup now happens first, and a regression asserts the public error for an uninterned `FileId`. Follow-up review found no remaining actionable issues. Regressions also verify that equal body-only edits produce structurally equal trees with different, current-snapshot pointers and ranges, and that the old tree is rejected by the new AST ID map. All 823 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** Confirm the adapter's snapshot validation and its fresh-pointer behavior. It still recollects the full item-tree structure on each call; use the stable semantic shape to rebuild source locations directly and connect this adapter to the eventual HIR query-group trait if that performance or API work is required.

### IR-205: Bind cached item-tree shapes without recollecting declarations

- **Decision:** Replace the snapshot adapter's full `ItemTree::collect` pass with `StableItemTree::bind_to_snapshot`. Resolve each stored erased AST ID through the current `AstIdMap`, re-intern stable names in the caller's symbol interner, rebuild raw attributes and their pointers/spans from current syntax, rebuild use entries and ranges from current syntax, and bind module-document pointers from the current root. Check that stored IDs are in range, unique, and resolvable; do not require them to cover every item-like AST node because a world import's nested function syntax is represented by the enclosing world-item entry.
- **Reason:** `def_collector` already validates that the requesting snapshot's raw bytes match the stable VFS input used by the derived query. Thus a memoized shape reused after a body-only edit still describes the declaration metadata, while current source locations can be rebuilt against the requesting root. Reusing the semantic shape avoids repeating declaration classification and nested item-tree collection on each snapshot request. The binder checks source-bound data that it reconstructs and preserves the exact snapshot identity on the returned tree.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable issues. It confirmed that `def_collector` checks the requesting snapshot's raw bytes against the tracked VFS input before reading the same `FileKey` query, and that stored IDs are validated without incorrectly requiring the shape to cover every item-like syntax node. Regressions compare the rebound tree with full collection for a fixture containing module docs, nested item families, attributes, and `use` entries; verify current-snapshot matching and pointer/range freshness after a body-only edit; and reject duplicate or unresolvable stored IDs. All workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, rustfmt check, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the stable `def_collector(FileKey)` query is the sole producer of shapes passed to the snapshot binder, that the adapter's exact-byte check is sufficient for reusing declaration metadata across body-only edits, and that item-like syntax nested within a world item should not require an independent `ItemTreeEntry`. Wiring the snapshot adapter into the query-group trait remains open.

### IR-206: Introduce a syntax query provider boundary

- **Decision:** Define `SyntaxQueryGroup` for parsing, current-snapshot AST-ID-map binding, and query-local AST access. Implement it for `SyntaxQueries`, and make `ItemTreeQueries<S = SyntaxQueries>` consume any provider implementing the trait. Store the provider in `Arc<S>` and manually implement `Clone` for the holder so neither construction nor cloning requires `S: Clone`; retain `Send + Sync + 'static` because the current `DerivedQuery` stores a thread-safe `'static` callback. Add a non-`Clone` forwarding provider regression that exercises stable-shape collection, snapshot rebinding, and shared memo access after cloning the holder.
- **Reason:** The item-tree query should depend on syntax operations, not the concrete syntax query holder, so a root database or test provider can supply the same query group. The repository does not yet have `sai_driver::RootDatabase` or a database-owned snapshot/input model; passing `QueryDatabase`, `FileInterner`, and `WorkspaceSnapshot` explicitly preserves today's validated API instead of inventing an incomplete root database. The stable-shape query also needs query-local AST access, so that callback is part of the provider contract.
- **Review:** The first hostile review confirmed static provider substitution and identified the unnecessary `Clone` bound, missing alternate-provider coverage, and the transitional runtime/snapshot parameters. The implementation removed the provider bound by storing it in `Arc` and added a non-`Clone` provider regression that exercises collection, parsing, AST-ID binding, and rebinding. Follow-up review found that derived `Clone` still inferred `S: Clone`; a manual implementation now clones the `Arc` and query handle, and a regression confirms both query holders return the same memoized `Arc`. Final hostile review found no further actionable issues. The context parameters remain intentionally explicit until a root database owns them. Workspace tests, Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that this adapter-level trait is a useful intermediate query-group boundary, especially its explicit `QueryDatabase`, interner, and snapshot parameters. When `RootDatabase` is added, decide whether to split stable `parse(FileId)` query calls from snapshot-bound rebinding and move context ownership onto that database. This change does not implement the VFS/HIR query groups or `RootDatabase`.

### IR-207: Declare the implemented HIR query-group operation

- **Decision:** Add `HirQueryGroup` with the implemented `def_collector` operation and implement it for `ItemTreeQueries<S>`. Re-export the trait, query holder, and error type from `sai_hir`. Keep the current explicit query-runtime, file-interner, snapshot, and symbol-interner arguments, and retain the inherent method as a compatibility entry point.
- **Reason:** The repository currently implements only the item-tree HIR query. Declaring that actual capability gives future phase code a trait boundary without adding speculative signatures for `hir_owner`, `hir_source_map`, or `attrs`, which are still unimplemented.
- **Review:** Hostile review confirmed that the trait and inherent adapter have matching signatures and error propagation, that the generic consumer calls through the trait, and that no API regression was introduced. It found one omission in the public trait documentation (`hir_source_map` was not listed among the missing queries); the comment now enumerates `hir_owner`, `hir_source_map`, and `attrs`. The workspace test suite, Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a partial `HirQueryGroup` is useful while the remaining HIR queries are absent, and that the explicit runtime and snapshot parameters are an acceptable interim API until `RootDatabase` owns those values.

### IR-208: Add the VFS read-query group

- **Decision:** Define `VfsQueryGroup` for `file_contents`, `package_files`, `wit_files`, `source_text`, and `line_index`; implement it for `VfsInputs`. Generalize `SyntaxQueries<Q = VfsInputs>` to consume any provider implementing this trait, and store it in `Arc<Q>` so providers do not need `Clone`. Expose `SourceText::from_file_contents` so alternate providers can construct decoded results, and make the non-`Clone` syntax test provider build `SourceText` from its tracked `file_contents` query instead of forwarding `source_text`.
- **Reason:** Syntax queries should read tracked source through a provider boundary instead of depending on the concrete VFS storage type. The current runtime stores thread-safe `'static` derived-query callbacks, which requires `Q: Send + Sync + 'static`. Input mutations (`set_*`, `remove_*`, `apply_updates`, and `replace_snapshot`) remain operations on `VfsInputs`; `RootDatabase` now forwards driver updates to that input holder while the query group stays read-only.
- **Review:** Hostile review confirmed the group signatures, tracked-query delegation, default `SyntaxQueries<VfsInputs>` compatibility, and `Arc` cloning without a `Q: Clone` bound. It found that the private `SourceText` fields and rebinding helper prevented a provider with its own backing store from constructing a result; `SourceText::from_file_contents` now decodes and binds one, and the alternate-provider regression constructs it from the tracked `file_contents` query. Tests, Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that the VFS query group should contain only reads while driver-side input mutation stays concrete, and that `SourceText::from_file_contents` is the right constructor boundary for alternate providers. As root-database query groups are integrated, decide whether input updates should remain driver methods on `VfsInputs` or gain a database-owned group contract.

### IR-209: Add a frontend root-database facade

- **Decision:** Add `sai_driver::RootDatabase` as the owner of one `QueryDatabase`, `VfsInputs`, `SyntaxQueries`, `ItemTreeQueries`, and the package, file, and symbol interners. Expose driver-level APIs for file/package interning, VFS input updates, source and line-index reads, snapshot-bound parsing, AST-ID-map binding, and item-tree collection. Resolve source from a `FileId` through this database's file interner, and provide checked resolution for returned session-local symbols.
- **Reason:** The architecture assigns session state and the root database to `sai_driver`. Owning the existing adapters in one place makes their input and query context consistent and enables callers to use the implemented frontend without manually coordinating seven components. This is a composition facade because the current group traits still accept query/runtime context explicitly and owner-level HIR results borrow from an arena.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that accepting a separate `FileKey` and `FileId` could bind one file's text to another file's spans, and that callers could not resolve `ItemTree` names because the symbol interner was private. `source_text` now resolves the key from the supplied interned ID and reports `FileNotInterned`; `resolve_symbol` exposes the session-owned checked lookup. Regression coverage verifies both behaviors and resolves names across an input update. Follow-up review confirmed the code fixes and found a wording ambiguity about partial root-database status; the frontend note now distinguishes the facade from full group integration. Workspace tests, Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm the current `RootDatabase` scope: it owns the implemented VFS, syntax, and item-tree services but does not yet implement every query-group trait or expose `hir_owner`, `hir_source_map`, or `attrs`. When those query values and arena lifetime ownership are designed, decide how group methods should move from explicit-context adapters to root-database-owned state.

### IR-210: Intern WIT inventory paths in the driver

- **Decision:** Add `InternedWitFiles::{NotApplicable, Listed(Vec<FileId>)}` and `RootDatabase::intern_wit_files(PackageId)`. Resolve the stable package key through the session package interner, read its tracked WIT inventory, and intern all listed package-relative paths through the existing file interner as one all-or-nothing batch.
- **Reason:** The VFS correctly stores stable package-relative paths, while syntax and diagnostics consume session-local `FileId`s. The driver is responsible for that identity conversion. Preserve three distinct states: no WIT input, an explicitly inapplicable WIT directory, and a listed directory that may be empty.
- **Review:** Initial hostile review found that exhausting the `FileId` range partway through a listing could leave earlier IDs interned after returning an error. `FileInterner::intern_package_files` now validates all stable keys and preflights the number of new IDs before mutating the interner. Regressions cover invalid package-key atomicity, duplicate and existing path reuse, input order, capacity boundaries, and rejection of foreign-session package IDs. Follow-up hostile review found only stale human-review wording, which was corrected. Workspace tests, Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm `InternedWitFiles` is the appropriate driver result type and that the all-or-nothing `FileInterner::intern_package_files` API is the right boundary for adapting canonical package-relative WIT paths.

### IR-211: Intern tracked package source inventories

- **Decision:** Add `RootDatabase::intern_package_files(PackageId, PackageSourceDirectory)` and return `Option<InternedPackageFiles>`. Preserve whether the source directory exists and every sorted source-relative entry; assign a session-local `FileId` only to file entries, retaining directory and symlink entries without IDs. Intern file paths through an all-or-nothing `FileInterner::intern_source_files` batch.
- **Reason:** `package_files` stores source-directory-relative paths and entry kinds under stable package keys, while syntax requires canonical package-relative file keys and session-local IDs. The configured source directory must be combined with each file path, and non-file entries must remain visible for package loading without being treated as readable files. Missing inventory input, a missing source directory, and an existing empty source directory remain distinct.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable findings. Tests cover missing inventory, missing and empty directories, file/directory/symlink entries, source-root path binding, and foreign package IDs. Workspace tests, Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm the configured source directory is the correct base for every `PackageSourcePath`, and that directory and symlink entries should remain in the adapted inventory without `FileId`s.

### IR-212: Resolve root-database line indexes by session file ID

- **Decision:** Change `RootDatabase::line_index` to accept `FileId`, resolve it through the root database's `FileInterner`, and request the existing memoized VFS line-index query by the resulting stable `FileKey`. Return `FileNotInterned` when the numeric index is outside this interner.
- **Reason:** The VFS query is intentionally keyed by stable paths, while syntax-facing APIs use session-local file IDs. The root database performs the stable-key lookup and rejects indexes outside its table. `FileId` is represented by a bare `u32`, so same-session origin remains a caller precondition and an in-range foreign ID cannot be detected.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that in-range foreign or fabricated IDs alias the local file at the same numeric index because `FileId` carries no session provenance. The root database and frontend docs now state the same-session caller precondition and the limits of the out-of-range check. Follow-up review found one ledger phrase that implied all foreign IDs were rejected; it was narrowed to out-of-range numeric indexes. Tests verify a local file lookup and reject an out-of-range ID; workspace tests, Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that session provenance should remain a caller precondition for `FileId` at this stage, while source-facing driver APIs take `FileId` and `VfsInputs::line_index` remains keyed by `FileKey`.

### IR-213: Pair tracked source text with status diagnostics

- **Decision:** Add `RootDatabase::source_text_and_diagnostic(FileId, SourceRef)`, returning the VFS `SourceText` and the optional result of `source_text_status_diagnostic` together. Require the caller to supply a `SourceRef` identifying the same file and a snapshot/version corresponding to the VFS source returned by the call.
- **Reason:** Missing or unreadable files and files over the documented source-size limit remain values so syntax can recover and report further diagnostics. Pairing those values with their diagnostic at the root database keeps callers from dropping the status diagnostic while still allowing them to use the retained source. The driver supplies `SourceRef` because `FileKey` stores package-relative identity but not workspace-relative display paths or document versions. The identity and version match cannot be verified by this adapter and are caller preconditions.
- **Review:** Initial hostile review found that a mismatched caller-supplied `SourceRef` could put a different path/package/version on the diagnostic than the `FileId` encoded by its span. The root API and frontend docs now state that the reference must identify the same file and its snapshot/version must correspond to the VFS source returned by the call; the adapter does not validate the correspondence. Follow-up review found and corrected wording that made the file ID sound snapshot-bound; final review found no further overclaims. Workspace tests, Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that `SourceRef` should remain caller-supplied and that same-file/same-snapshot correspondence is a caller precondition.

### IR-214: Add package-version primitives

- **Decision:** Add the `sai_package` crate and implement a strict `Version` value for canonical SemVer versions without build metadata, including SemVer precedence and the compatibility-line mapping from D-PKG-02. Store numeric core components and numeric pre-release identifiers as `u64`; preserve nonnumeric pre-release identifiers as their validated ASCII text.
- **Reason:** The package contract and lock file share version semantics, and the resolver depends on them. A small, dependency-free value layer gives later requirement parsing, manifest validation, and resolution a single implementation of version identity and ordering. This step intentionally does not parse partial version requirements or manifests.
- **Review:** Initial hostile review found that the public enum allowed impossible line values for which `Ord` equaled distinct `Eq` values, and that any `+` was classified as build metadata even when the prefix was not a valid version. `CompatibilityLine` is now an opaque value with derived ordering, and build metadata is reported only when its prefix parses as a complete version. Regressions cover both findings; follow-up review found no actionable issues. The package tests and full workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that an opaque, core-based compatibility-line value and the canonical SemVer representation are suitable shared package-system primitives, and that numeric pre-release identifiers should follow the specification's `u64` bound.

### IR-215: Parse and evaluate version requirements

- **Decision:** Add partial-version parsing and validated `VersionReq` intervals for `^`, `~`, `=`, `>=`, `<`, and `<=`. Keep raw interval matching distinct from resolution matching: `matches` follows comparator SemVer intervals, while `matches_for_resolution` additionally requires the selected version to share the minimum's compatibility line. Represent implicit release-core endpoints with `u128` components so a calculated `u64::MAX + 1` boundary remains exact.
- **Reason:** The resolver specification defines interval membership separately from its rule that selected versions stay on the minimum's compatibility line. Keeping both checks named avoids silently narrowing comparator semantics. `u128` represents every implicit endpoint generated from a `u64` component without overflow or accidentally widening the range into a later compatibility line.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that a partial explicit `<=` endpoint was not checked; the parser now rejects it and a regression covers `>=1, <=2`. Follow-up confirmed the `u128` endpoint comparisons and found no remaining correctness issues, while requesting direct coverage for a prerelease at the release-core boundary. A regression now asserts that `2.0.0-alpha` belongs to the raw `<2.0.0` interval but is excluded from resolution for a line-1 requirement; final review confirmed the case and found no new issues. All 14 package tests and workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm the separation between pure interval matching and resolution-line filtering, particularly for prereleases at compatibility-line boundaries, and the use of `u128` for calculated release-core endpoints.

### IR-216: Validate package identities

- **Decision:** Add an opaque `PackageIdentity` value that accepts exactly two valid package-name segments separated by one colon, rejects slash paths, enforces the 1-to-64-byte ASCII segment grammar, and rejects the reserved `wasi` namespace and `sai:core` / `sai:alloc` identities.
- **Reason:** The package declaration identifies a package but dependency and WIT paths may contain additional slash-separated components. Keeping identity separate from those path forms prevents resolver keys from accepting package references that are not declarations.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no actionable issues. It confirmed the byte limits, segment grammar, reserved names, slash rejection, and consistency of equality and ordering. All 18 package tests and workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that identity parsing should reject reserved identities during value construction and that slash-qualified package paths should use a separate model.

### IR-217: Validate package-contract paths

- **Decision:** Add separate `ContentPackagePath` and `DependencyPackagePath` types so validated use context cannot be discarded. Preserve each validated spelling; allow `..` only for dependency paths; allow the single path `.`; reject absolute paths, empty and dot components, paths over 4096 bytes, components over 255 bytes, backslashes, host-invalid or non-printable ASCII, trailing dots or spaces except on the documented `.` / `..` forms, Windows device names, and `.git` components (ASCII case-insensitive). For content paths, also reject first components `.sai` and `out` (ASCII case-insensitive). Record these cross-host constraints as D-PKG-06 and N-G-20.
- **Reason:** The manifest uses one path grammar with two parent-traversal contexts and additional checksum/build-output exclusions for package content. Host path normalization can otherwise turn a spelling such as `".. "` into a parent component, while case-insensitive filesystems can alias reserved directories and Windows reserves device names even with extensions. Distinct types prevent a dependency-validated path from being passed accidentally as a content path; the 4096/255-byte limits match the lock-file path rules and supported filesystem constraints.
- **Review:** Initial hostile review found that `".. /outside"` bypassed the exact `..` check and could become traversal after Windows trims the trailing space; it also found case-sensitive reserved-name checks and missing Windows device-name checks. A follow-up found that the generic path value discarded its content/dependency validation context and that the validator omitted the existing 4096-byte path and 255-byte component limits. The implementation now rejects trailing dots/spaces, matches reserved names without ASCII case sensitivity, excludes Windows device names with optional extensions, enforces both length limits, and uses distinct content and dependency path types. Regression tests cover the findings, forbidden characters, and both length boundaries. Final hostile review found no remaining actionable issues. All 22 package tests and workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Confirm that a path's use-site context is the right input for parent traversal and reserved-root validation, and that preserving its source spelling is useful for diagnostics and formatting.

### IR-218: Lex package contracts and lock files

- **Decision:** Add a shared byte-input lexer for `sai.package` and `sai.lock`. Emit typed words, package paths, integers, versions, strings, punctuators, generated `NEWLINE`, and `EOF` with UTF-8 byte spans; preserve comments and whitespace as ranged trivia. Implement the package-specific terminator and continuation sets, nested comments, source-text checks, simple-string escapes, unsupported-string diagnostics, and SemVer lexeme validation by reusing `PartialVersion`.
- **Reason:** Both file formats specify one lexical grammar, while parsing owns contextual keywords and format-specific field meaning. Keeping tokenization in `sai_package` avoids coupling package syntax to source-language `sai_syntax` tokens and lets both future parsers share stable byte ranges and trivia.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that `from:pkg` could be mistaken for the continuation word `from` and that malformed `1x.2` retained an integer token kind. Continuation detection now applies package-path precedence, and malformed digit-started tokens containing a dot retain `Version` kind. Regression tests cover both findings and all four continuation-word prefixes. Follow-up hostile review found no remaining actionable issues. All 35 package tests and workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The lexer returns the first invalid UTF-8 span and an EOF token without attempting lossy decoding, so offsets remain exact; decide during VFS/diagnostic integration whether malformed-byte recovery should resume after that span. The public token model stores trivia ranges globally and leaves contextual keyword interpretation to the parser.

### IR-219: Parse package contracts

- **Decision:** Add `sai_package::manifest::parse` and a spanned `PackageFile` syntax tree for every declaration in package/01 §1.3. Parse headers and version requirements into the existing validated value types; retain decoded string values and their original source ranges; validate content and dependency paths only after escape decoding and in their use-site context. Preserve lexical errors separately, recover later declarations after malformed syntax, report declaration duplicates and unknown names, and expose duplicate-list-item warnings plus conflicting lint-level errors.
- **Reason:** The shared lexer and package value types now supply stable tokens and reusable validation, so the package crate can own a parser without depending on the SAI source parser. A partial tree supports diagnostics after one malformed declaration, and original byte ranges let later diagnostics point into the input even when a string's decoded length differs.
- **Review:** Hostile review found and drove fixes for invalid or lexically damaged strings retained in the AST, skipped valid list entries after malformed items, missing duplicate-list warnings and conflicting lint-level errors, imprecise comparator spans, duplicate top-level diagnostics, duplicate keys hidden by invalid first values, unknown artifact kinds retained as valid nodes, and an overly broad duplicate-profile span. Follow-up review also found missing-opening-brace AST nodes, incomplete missing-closing-brace recovery, comma recovery failures, omitted overflowing budget values, and insufficient documentation of partial lists. The parser now preserves valid recovered entries with diagnostics, tracks duplicates independently of successfully parsed values, retains raw budget integers for semantic range checks, and synchronizes at unambiguous grammar boundaries while preserving valid profile `allow` lists and known artifact forms. Regression tests cover the findings. The final Hume hostile review found no actionable issues. All 66 package tests and workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The parser returns partial syntax trees alongside diagnostics, but stops syntax parsing after invalid UTF-8 because the lexer cannot provide trustworthy token boundaries. Recovered lists may contain valid entries alongside errors, so semantic consumers must reject or otherwise account for syntax diagnostics before using the AST. It validates content and dependency paths immediately after decoding because traversal safety depends on their declaration context. Budget integers retain their raw decimal spelling so the semantic stage can report overflow and range violations without losing source data. Recovery prefers preserving later declarations at unambiguous boundaries and leaves ambiguous input to the current grammar production. Cross-declaration checks such as required `targets`, known target names, missing dependency sources, and capability relationships remain for semantic validation.

### IR-220: Validate package-contract semantics

- **Decision:** Add `manifest::parse_for_role` and standalone `validation::validate` for rules checkable from one package contract. Validate target presence and non-emptiness, content, requirement identity/source/uniqueness/self-reference, target membership, capability and policy spelling, root profile/lint/allocator/override rules, compiler-version compatibility when supplied, artifact/WIT declaration relationships, and metadata. Retain an empty `targets {}` list in the syntax tree so semantic validation reports `MISSING_TARGETS`. Root-only declarations remain in dependency syntax trees but duplicate-declaration and duplicate-list diagnostics for them are suppressed when parsing as a dependency. Keep graph membership, resolved WIT names, package-directory existence, and artifact-file checks for later stages.
- **Reason:** These rules need parsed declarations and role/toolchain context but not a fetched graph or filesystem access. Reusing the diagnostic registry prevents hard errors from being accepted as lint names. Use strict SPDX parsing rather than a hand-maintained identifier table so syntax, current SPDX identifiers, exceptions, and `LicenseRef-...` follow the standard parser. Shared package-identity spelling validation enforces the same 64-byte segment rule for requirements, allocators, overrides, capabilities, and external WIT world packages.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found overlong referenced identities, duplicate root-only declarations diagnosed in dependencies, duplicate overrides suppressed by unrelated syntax errors, and missing description/license checks. Those cases now have regressions. A follow-up audit found that empty `targets {}` was rejected by the parser before semantic validation; the parser now retains it and validation reports `MISSING_TARGETS`. `INVALID_DESCRIPTION` was added to the package contract and diagnostic registry because the existing normative description rule had no diagnostic code; it covers decoded CR/LF and values over 1024 UTF-8 bytes. A fresh review then found that dependency policy lists still warned on duplicate `duplicate_lines`, and capability/WIT package segments bypassed the 64-byte identity limit; both are fixed with regressions. The review also identified conflicting general wording and a concrete `>=0.7.1, <0.8` example for `VERSION_REQ_SPANS_LINES`. The implementation follows the explicit example: only an interval containing the stable start of the next line triggers the warning, and the normative contract, resolver text, diagnostic table, and appendix now say so. Its final audit found the newly added `spdx` and transitive `smallvec` dependencies missing from the shipped dependency allowlist; `docs/impl/01-architecture.md` now records them and their use. It also found the artifact-only local-world segment rule, which is now enforced after package kind is known; regressions confirm hybrid worlds may remain nested. Final hostile review found no remaining actionable findings. Validation passes: 91 package tests, 922 workspace tests, warning-denied Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check`.
- **Human review:** Review whether decoded CR/LF should make `description` invalid, and whether the new hard-error code should be `INVALID_DESCRIPTION`. Review the use of the `spdx` 0.13.6 crate and its transitive `smallvec` dependency instead of embedding SPDX list data. Review the decision that an admitted boundary pre-release alone does not trigger `VERSION_REQ_SPANS_LINES`; this preserves the explicit `>=0.7.1, <0.8` example despite plain interval matching admitting `0.8.0-rc.1`. Filesystem checks and graph-dependent diagnostics remain pending.

### IR-221: Parse lock-file structure

- **Decision:** Add `lock::parse` and a spanned syntax tree for the format header, root fields, package entries, sources, checksums, imports, capabilities, compiler requirements, and resolved edges. Keep lock identities as spellings so validation can admit the implicit `sai:core` and `sai:alloc` packages. Reject conflict markers before lexing and keep lexical diagnostics separate from lock syntax errors.
- **Reason:** `sai.lock` shares the package lexer and validated version models, while its fields and implicit package identities differ from package declarations. A syntax-only stage makes the lock grammar inspectable before cross-field and canonical validation.
- **Review:** Tests cover Git and path entries, every field shape, test requirements, multiline lists, requirement parsing, comments within requirements, malformed root fields, missing root blocks, and conflict markers. A fixture exposed a conflict between package-contract newline continuation for `dir` and the lock grammar's separate `dir` field. The lock lexer now retains `only` as a continuation but treats `dir` as a field boundary, matching the canonical lock layout; package-contract lexing remains unchanged. The lock specification records this exception. Initial hostile review found that raw source slicing passed comments inside requirements to `VersionReq::parse`, rejecting lexically valid noncanonical locks. Requirement text now comes from the token spellings with trivia omitted, while diagnostics keep the original byte span; regression tests cover root and package requirements plus a continued compiler requirement. Follow-up hostile review found no remaining actionable issues. Validation passes: 98 package tests, 929 workspace tests, warning-denied Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check`.
- **Human review:** Review the lock-specific continuation set: it resolves the contradiction between the shared package lexer rule and the canonical lock layout, with the canonical `source` and `dir` fields kept on separate lines. The parser preserves comments as trivia and omits them from semantic values; comment placement across grammar boundaries follows the lock lexer’s newline rules.

### IR-222: Remove the Co-Author trailer from the repository's first commit

- **Decision:** Interpret “the first commit” as the repository root commit, `Add SAI language and implementation specification`, because the earliest implementation commit on `codex` had no Co-Author trailer. Remove the root commit's `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>` line and rewrite the local `main` and `codex` histories. Keep the pre-rewrite branch tips under `refs/original`; do not push the rewritten refs.
- **Reason:** The requested trailer appeared only in the root commit. A commit's message contributes to its object ID, so changing that message necessarily changes every descendant commit ID. Rewriting both local branches is required to remove that trailer from the history they share. Keeping the rewrite local avoids changing the remote repository without a separate push request.
