Using both inputs keeps the review aligned with the actual final-source selection and preserves alternate lower-version sources still present in the selected graph.
- **Review:** The hostile review found that unused Git URLs must be canonicalized before review output and that `dir "."` should be normalized to the repository root; the collector now applies the same URL canonicalizer and removes that directory marker, with a regression using uppercase scheme/host, a default port, a trailing slash, and `dir "."`. It also found the source-clause example note retained an old list number, which is now corrected, and requested coverage for an unselected candidate that names the exact selected version; a regression verifies that candidate clause is retained. Final hostile review found no remaining P0–P2 issues. All 287 `sai_package` tests and 1,122 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Resolve the mismatch with distribution §4.7.1 by appending an `Unused source clauses` heading and the JSON key `unused_sources` after `Needs approval`. Each entry names the requiring package (and whether it is the root), the version it named, and the source clause. Path pins are treated as used because every pin is checked to prove one source identity; a root override or lock floor marks all incoming requirement clauses unused. The CLI-results schema rejects unknown fields, so this changes the schema shape; the command-line implementation has not shipped, so revise its version-1 schema before any consumer depends on it. If the schema is released first, a versioned compatibility path is needed.
- **Remaining scope:** Render the retained clauses in human and JSON package reviews when review generation and the command-line package workflow are implemented; no concrete package-review formatter or package loader exists in this workspace yet.

### IR-253: Read bounded local package manifests

- **Decision:** Add a local package directory value that canonicalizes an absolute directory using host filesystem semantics, verifies it is a directory, safely opens its `sai.package`, captures those bytes once, and parses the captured snapshot in a caller-selected package role. Use the canonical path only for local directory identity; redact it from `Debug` and errors. Resolve dependency paths through normal filesystem semantics so symlinks are followed before later `..` components. Reject non-regular manifests and cap every manifest input at 16 MiB before lexing; use Unix `O_NONBLOCK` while opening special-file paths.
- **Reason:** Resolver §2.7.1 requires local path pins to compare after filesystem resolution, while the path spelling still determines the actual package directory. Reopening the manifest after checking its type could parse a replacement file or block on a FIFO. A shared parser-side size check also covers VFS and direct byte-slice callers, not only the new filesystem helper. The cap bounds parser memory for untrusted manifests. Add `libc` as an explicitly allowed `sai_package` dependency for the Unix nonblocking flag; the package crate remains `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`.
- **Review:** Hostile review found and drove fixes for pre-normalizing `link/../dep` before symlink resolution, absolute-path leakage through derived `Debug`, reopening a checked manifest (including a FIFO blocking case), and applying the 16 MiB limit only in the filesystem helper. The final implementation resolves paths through the OS, redacts `Debug`, reads from one checked file descriptor, and enforces the limit in both the bounded filesystem reader and the shared parser before lexing. Tests cover ordinary local resolution, symlink aliases, symlink-plus-parent semantics, package-role parsing, immutable parse snapshots, missing/non-directory/special-file sources, path redaction, and over-limit input through both the filesystem and parser APIs. All 295 `sai_package` tests and 1,130 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. Final hostile review found no remaining P0–P2 issues.
- **Human review:** The 16 MiB limit is a new fixed package-manifest limit and is recorded in distribution §4.4.4/N-G-51. It is large enough for unusually extensive dependency declarations while bounding parser input memory; any change must update both the shared parser and the local reader. Unix-only `libc::O_NONBLOCK` prevents a race from opening a FIFO from hanging the resolver, and the opened descriptor's metadata determines whether the manifest is a regular file. Canonical local paths remain available to loader code for pin comparison but are omitted from debug and error formatting under N-H-7.
- **Remaining scope:** The helper is not yet called by the MVS source provider, VFS package graph, Git tree reader, lock generation, or diagnostic renderer. Callers must report parser errors and run package semantic validation before supplying contracts to MVS.

### IR-254: Resolve local path pins from captured directories

- **Decision:** Index opened local package directories by concrete package version after checking their manifest syntax and exact header identity. Treat repeated registration of the same directory as idempotent and reject a second directory for the same version. Require each local path pin's source base to match its recorded requirer; leave Git-tree bases to the Git provider. On supported Unix targets, retain directory handles, resolve child paths through `openat`, read manifests from the resulting handles, and compare local directory identity by device and inode. Check that the canonical path still resolves to the same opened directory; return an error if it changed. Validate all pins through `validate_resolver_path_pins` and return the first opened directory so the caller can parse the same captured manifest snapshot. Other hosts fail closed for local dependency resolution because this implementation cannot yet preserve handle-relative identity there.
- **Reason:** A canonical path can be replaced after its manifest is read, making later path pins resolve against a different tree. Resolver §2.7.1 also requires path comparisons to follow filesystem symlinks before later `..` components, which lexical pre-normalization cannot preserve. Handle-relative resolution binds the pin to the requiring directory that supplied the manifest; returning the opened winner prevents a second path lookup from silently selecting another source. Failing closed on hosts without this guarantee prevents an unresolved race from becoming a successful but incorrect source comparison.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found the path-replacement race in the original path-only implementation. The implementation now retains open directory handles and checks the canonical spelling against the opened target; a regression moves the requiring directory and confirms resolution fails rather than selecting the replacement tree. A later review found the same issue remained in non-Unix fallback code; that path now returns `PlatformNotSupported`. The architecture dependency table and allowlist were updated for `rustix` 1.1.5 and its transitive crates. Final hostile review found no remaining P0–P2 issues. All 299 `sai_package` tests and 1,134 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Device/inode identity is used only within one host load to compare local directories; it is not a lock identity and does not imply that directory contents are immutable. The first pin's manifest bytes are the snapshot returned to the caller; subsequent source-file reads still belong to the loader. Windows and the excluded Unix targets currently reject local dependency resolution until an equivalent handle-relative implementation is provided. Review whether that platform limitation is acceptable before shipping local path resolution there.
- **Remaining scope:** Connect the directory index and captured manifest snapshots to the MVS source provider and package contract loader, validate complete package semantics before contracts enter MVS, implement Git-tree path resolution, and feed the resulting identities and diagnostics into reload auditing, lock generation, package reviews, and the CLI.

### IR-255: Load dependency contracts from validated local path snapshots

- **Decision:** Add `LocalPackageDirectory::build_dependency_contract` to parse the manifest bytes captured when a local directory was opened, run the existing package semantic validation and resolver-contract adapter, and check the resulting concrete package version against the MVS request. Add `canonicalize_resolver_path_pins` to return canonical keys in provenance order after comparing every pin. Add source-provider APIs that pass those exact keys to the contract loader, including the identity-aware audit variant; retain compatibility wrappers that discard the keys. Give resolver contract build failures detailed display text and expose them through the local contract-load error chain.
- **Reason:** Reopening a local path after pin comparison could read a different manifest, even if the directory spelling stayed the same. Returning the canonical keys from the provider's own per-call cache lets the loader parse the exact directory snapshot used for pin validation, while package/version and semantic checks keep invalid contracts out of MVS.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that the loader reopened a path after canonicalization; the integration regression now replaces the manifest between callbacks and verifies the loader uses the captured 1.2.0 snapshot. A second review found that a caller-owned reusable snapshot cache could return an old contract in a later resolver run; the cache was removed and canonical keys now remain in the provider's internal per-call cache and are passed directly to both ordinary and identity-aware loaders. Error display and source chaining were expanded to retain syntax and semantic validation details. Final hostile review found no actionable P0–P2 findings. All 301 `sai_package` tests and all 1,136 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Local loaders must use `select_with_source_provider_and_canonicalized_pins` or its identity-aware counterpart when a path pin is selected, and must build the contract from the first returned key, which corresponds to the selected source. The compatibility wrappers intentionally preserve their older callback shape and discard those keys; they are unsuitable for a loader that must retain the validated local snapshot. The current key handoff covers `PathPin` selections; root path overrides and path-valued lock-floor sources need their own snapshot handling. An opened local source retains the manifest bytes and directory handle, but does not freeze the package's other files or provide a lock checksum.
- **Remaining scope:** No production package loader or CLI calls these APIs yet. Add snapshot handling for root path overrides and path-valued lock sources, implement Git-tree source and path resolution, hash and verify complete source contents, connect selected-source reload auditing and package diagnostics, and integrate the results with lock writing, package reviews, and CLI commands.

### IR-256: Bind root path overrides to indexed snapshots

- **Decision:** Add `LocalPackageDirectoryIndex::resolve_selected_path_source` to supply local directory snapshots to the source-provider loader. For path pins, require the captured key list to match the selected pin count and require all returned directories to share one identity, then return the selected first snapshot. For a root path override, require the root provenance and base to match, require a snapshot indexed under the selected package version, re-resolve the root-relative path, compare its directory identity with that snapshot, and return the indexed snapshot. Reject a path-valued lock floor because resolver §2.10.3 excludes path entries from floors. Preserve filesystem/path errors in the `Error::source` chain.
- **Reason:** A root override's package version may have been discovered before the MVS loader runs. Reopening its manifest can pair that version with a different dependency graph, even when the replacement declares the same version. The indexed snapshot ties the selected version to the bytes that supplied it, and the identity comparison rejects a root-relative path that has since been retargeted. Lock path entries are working copies and are not valid MVS floors under the current lock rules.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that root overrides were re-resolved without comparing against the directory snapshot used to discover their version, and that the generic source map could supply an invalid path lock floor. The loader now requires and returns the indexed snapshot after checking the root-relative directory identity, and rejects path lock floors. A second review requested a regression for directory replacement; the test now retargets a symlink to another directory containing the same package version and asserts failure. A separate regression replaces the indexed manifest in place and verifies that the original captured manifest is loaded. Final hostile review found no remaining actionable P0–P2 findings. All 304 `sai_package` tests and 1,139 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The caller must index the local directory snapshot that supplied each root override's package version and reuse that per-load index when loading contracts. If the configured path no longer resolves to that directory, resolution fails rather than silently switching sources. The manifest is captured, but package files other than `sai.package` are not frozen or hashed by this helper. Path lock floors remain rejected unless a future lock-format rule explicitly permits them.
- **Remaining scope:** No production package loader or CLI calls these APIs yet. Wire the local source index into root path-version discovery and provider loading; implement Git-tree source and path resolution; hash and verify complete source contents; connect selected-source reload auditing and diagnostics; and integrate these results with lock writing, package reviews, and CLI commands.

### IR-257: Discover and index local dependency paths from one snapshot

- **Decision:** Add `LocalPackageDirectoryIndex::index_dependency_path` to resolve a package-relative local path from an already indexed base, capture and parse the dependency manifest, derive its concrete package version, and insert that same directory snapshot under the version. Retain parser diagnostics on syntax and missing-header failures. Keep same-version registration of the same directory idempotent; reject one directory identity registered under different package versions in the same index.
- **Reason:** Root path overrides and path requirements need a concrete version before the MVS context can be built. Resolving the path once for its version and again for its contract could mix two manifest snapshots. Returning the version from the same indexed directory snapshot closes that gap, while rejecting multiple versions for one directory prevents a manifest edit during resolution from creating inconsistent package entries.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that a manifest update between discoveries could register the same physical directory under two versions. The index now checks directory identity across existing entries and reports the conflicting versions; a regression changes the manifest version between path discoveries and verifies rejection. Tests also verify that a later in-place manifest change does not replace the indexed snapshot, parse diagnostics remain accessible, and missing bases and invalid paths fail explicitly. Final hostile review found no remaining actionable P0–P2 findings. All 308 `sai_package` tests and 1,143 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Use one `LocalPackageDirectoryIndex` for local path-version discovery and subsequent provider loading within a resolver run. This method checks manifest syntax and package identity/version only; callers must report captured parse diagnostics and run full package semantic validation before a contract enters MVS. It handles local filesystem paths only; path sources inside Git trees remain the Git provider's responsibility.
- **Remaining scope:** No production package loader or CLI calls this API yet. Wire it into path-package graph discovery and root path override context construction; validate complete contracts before MVS; implement Git-tree source and path resolution; hash and verify complete source contents; connect selected-source reload auditing and diagnostics; and integrate the results with lock writing, package reviews, and CLI commands.

### IR-258: Discover root path overrides atomically

- **Decision:** Add `LocalPackageDirectoryIndex::index_root_path_overrides` to parse root overrides from the opened root directory, require that directory identity and its parsed manifest match the indexed root snapshot, and discover each local override through the same staged index used by dependency paths. Return the concrete package-version map only after every override succeeds, and require each discovered package identity to match its declaration. Preserve each failing declaration's package name and source span for duplicate, path-discovery, and identity-mismatch errors; retain the nested path error as the error source.
- **Reason:** Resolver context needs concrete root override versions before selection begins. Manually constructing that map can associate a declared identity and version with the wrong directory, while mutating the live index as declarations are processed can leave partial state after a later failure. A staged index binds version discovery to captured child snapshots and makes failure atomic. Directory identity plus parsed-manifest equality distinguishes another directory with identical text and a changed manifest at the same path; raw-byte identity is unnecessary because comments and other non-semantic formatting do not alter parsed resolver data.
- **Review:** An adversarial pass found that comparing parsed manifests alone could accept identical text from a different root directory. The helper now takes the opened `LocalPackageDirectory` and checks that it is the indexed directory as well as matching its parsed snapshot; regression coverage also rejects an edited manifest at the same directory. Tests verify atomic rollback after a later identity mismatch and source-span retention and formatted diagnostics for duplicate, identity-mismatch, and path-discovery errors. The final adversarial review found and prompted a fix for omitted package names and byte spans in formatted errors; it then found no remaining actionable P0–P2 issues. All 312 `sai_package` tests and 1,147 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Callers must supply the indexed root directory used for this resolver load and reuse the resulting index when loading selected override contracts. The helper discovers local directory versions and checks identity; callers still need to report parse diagnostics and run full package semantic validation before a contract enters MVS. Path overrides whose source is inside a Git tree remain the Git provider's responsibility.
- **Remaining scope:** No production package loader or CLI calls this API yet. Wire it into root path-version discovery and resolver-context construction; validate complete contracts before MVS; implement Git-tree source and path resolution; hash and verify complete source contents; connect selected-source reload auditing and diagnostics; and integrate these results with lock writing, package reviews, and CLI commands.

### IR-259: Discover root path requirements before resolver context

- **Decision:** Add `LocalPackageDirectoryIndex::index_root_path_requirements` to derive root targets and discover only root `from path` requirements included for at least one root target. Include root test requirements because resolver §2.5.1 places them in every resolution. Skip a path source when a root Git override replaces that package, or when an indexed root path override replaces that requirement's compatibility line. Resolve each remaining path relative to the indexed root, return `ResolverPathKey` mappings, and commit discoveries atomically. Preserve the requirement identity, path spelling, source span, and nested error on failure.
- **Reason:** `ResolverContractContext` needs concrete root path-package versions before it can build the root MVS contract. Discovering every textual path unconditionally would open target-filtered requirements and path sources that overrides have replaced. Following resolver §2.5 and §2.7.3 while resolving from the captured root snapshot gives the context the needed package versions without loading irrelevant sources or mixing path spellings and snapshots.
- **Review:** Tests cover active root path requirements, root test requirements, target-filtered paths, path and Git override precedence, and atomic rollback with a source-located path error. Hostile review checked filtering, test-edge inclusion, compatibility-line handling, and snapshot use against resolver §§2.5 and 2.7.3 and found no actionable P0–P2 issues. All 315 `sai_package` tests and 1,150 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The method discovers sources but does not perform full package semantic validation or check that the discovered version satisfies the declared version requirement; `build_resolver_contract` remains responsible for those checks before MVS. Root test requirements are discovered even for commands that later build without test analysis, because the lock and resolution graph cover test edges by specification. Dependency manifests still need a separate target-aware path-discovery step once their contracts are read.
- **Remaining scope:** No production loader calls this helper. Integrate it with root resolver-context construction, add corresponding path-requirement discovery for selected dependency contracts, implement Git-tree source and path resolution, validate complete contracts before MVS, verify full source contents, and connect the results to source reload auditing, lock writing, diagnostics, package reviews, and CLI commands.

### IR-260: Extend resolver context for discovered dependency paths

- **Decision:** Let `ResolverContractContext::with_path_packages` create an immutable derived context that merges newly discovered `ResolverPathKey` entries while preserving the root context binding and root-owned path entries. Identical mappings are idempotent; a key already associated with another concrete version is an error. Add `LocalPackageDirectoryIndex::index_dependency_path_requirements` to read one indexed dependency snapshot, include only non-test requirements matching at least one root target, apply root path and Git source overrides, and atomically index the remaining local paths.
- **Reason:** MVS discovers dependency manifests as it visits package versions, but those manifests may contain path requirements needed to build their contracts. A fixed path map could not accept those discoveries without either rebuilding the root binding or weakening stale-context checks. Immutable derived contexts let loaded contracts retain the same root identity, while the local index binds each discovered version to the captured directory snapshot.
- **Review:** Adversarial review found no actionable P0–P2 issues. Tests cover adding dependency mappings while preserving root binding, rejecting conflicting mappings without mutating the prior context, root-target filtering, omission of dependency test edges, root path/Git override precedence, and atomic failure with requirement source provenance. All 320 `sai_package` tests and 1,155 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The loader should retain the latest derived context as it discovers dependencies and use it to build subsequent contracts. Path requirement identity and version-range checks still run in `build_resolver_contract`; indexing alone does not make a contract valid. The index provides local filesystem sources only; Git-tree path requirements require the Git provider.
- **Remaining scope:** No production resolver loader or CLI calls these APIs. Integrate root and dependency path discovery into source-aware MVS loading; implement Git-tree package/path reads; validate full contracts and source contents; connect source reload auditing, lock updates, diagnostics, package reviews, and CLI commands.

### IR-261: Own resolver contexts after root discovery

- **Decision:** Add `ResolverContractContext::into_owned` to copy its path-package and root path-override maps into shared owned storage while preserving the root binding token and all validated root metadata. Represent root path overrides as borrowed or owned values, matching the existing path-package map. Compare borrowed and owned root maps by contents rather than storage variant.
- **Reason:** Root discovery naturally builds maps as temporary values, while a concrete source-aware loader must retain the evolving context after those setup values leave scope. Owning a context gives the loader a stable result for final-source planning without weakening the stale-root binding check or requiring self-referential result types.
- **Review:** A hostile review checked borrowed/owned comparisons, root-token preservation, derived-context behavior, and map lifetimes and found no actionable P0–P2 issues. A regression drops both original maps before checking the owned context, verifies both maps and root override pins remain available, and confirms a root contract built before conversion remains bound. All 321 `sai_package` tests and 1,156 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** `into_owned` intentionally clones the maps so the returned context can outlive the setup scope. Callers should use it at resolver boundaries where that longer lifetime is required; ordinary borrowed contexts remain available for short-lived adaptation.
- **Remaining scope:** This makes a long-lived context possible but does not itself load packages. Connect local path discovery and captured snapshots to source-aware MVS selection, then add Git-tree loading, full source verification, final-source reload auditing, lock generation, diagnostics, and CLI integration.

### IR-262: Load local path packages through source-aware MVS

- **Decision:** Add `resolve_local_path_packages` to index a root snapshot, discover root path overrides and path requirements, build an owned resolver context, and run source-aware MVS using captured local directory snapshots. Extend the context as valid dependency contracts reveal more local paths, retain source identities and partial results on provider failure, and expose final-source planning. This provider reports Git sources as unsupported for a separate Git loader.
- **Reason:** The package crate had MVS orchestration, local path discovery, and snapshot-aware contract builders, but no production adapter connected them. Keeping this adapter local-only lets it implement path identity and filesystem snapshot guarantees without pretending that Git URLs or Git-tree-relative paths have local semantics.
- **Review:** Hostile review found two P2 issues: pin canonicalization reopened an indexed package and could pair its discovered version with changed same-version manifest bytes; and paths discovered for a contract that later failed validation could poison independent candidates. Path pins now compare directory identity and manifest bytes with any indexed selected-version snapshot and load that indexed snapshot; dependency discoveries are staged and committed only after contract validation succeeds. The pre-existing canonicalized-snapshot behavior remains for path pins that were not previously indexed. Regressions cover both findings, transitive path loading and final-source planning, root path overrides, and explicit Git failure with partial MVS state. Final hostile review found no remaining actionable P0–P2 issues. All 326 `sai_package` tests and 1,161 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The new high-level entry point resolves local filesystem sources only; Git dependencies, including Git-backed root overrides, return a clear provider failure with the partial selection available. Exact manifest-byte matching deliberately fails closed when a path package changes during one resolver run, even if its declared version remains the same. The API does not yet fetch or hash full package trees.
- **Remaining scope:** Implement Git repository and Git-tree path loading, checksum and cache verification, final-source reload comparison, selected-graph diagnostics, lock generation and validation, package review decisions, and CLI wiring.

### IR-263: Hash and validate Git trees

- **Decision:** Add a pure helper that accepts flattened Git entries and computes the `sai1:` tree digest from raw blob bytes, with byte-sorted paths, source-only `.sai`/`out` exclusions, symlink/submodule warnings, portable path validation, and collision rejection. Add the same `sai1:` encoding for artifact bytes. Pin collision comparison to Unicode 16.0.0 NFC and default full case folding using generated Unicode data checked into the package crate. Use `sha2` 0.11.0 for the normative SHA-256 implementation.
- **Reason:** The lock format specifies SHA-256 and Unicode behavior, while the existing stable-hash crate serves a different BLAKE3-based compiler purpose. Unicode 16.0.0 matches the workspace's pinned normalization and category tables. The available case-folding crate found during investigation uses Unicode 9.0.0, while the current ICU case-mapping crate exceeds the Rust 1.85 MSRV; vendoring the official Unicode 16.0.0 C/F mappings keeps the behavior version-aligned and reproducible. The generated data records the source digests for `CaseFolding.txt` and `DerivedCoreProperties.txt`, and the Unicode license notice is included.
- **Review:** The fixed package tree-hash vector passes. Tests cover source/WIT selection, path rules, executable-mode equivalence, symlink/submodule warnings, full-fold and NFC collisions, duplicate entries, artifact hashing, and safe rendering of hostile path bytes. Hostile review found that lossy error formatting could pass terminal control sequences through; error paths now escape invalid bytes, Unicode control/format/separator characters, backslashes, and backticks. A later pass found warning paths could still hide trailing spaces and invisible marks; rendering now also escapes whitespace, all mark categories, and Unicode 16.0.0 default-ignorable characters. Warning values expose that safe representation. Final hostile re-review closed both findings and found no remaining actionable P0–P2 issues. All 337 `sai_package` tests and 1,172 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The API consumes a flattened Git-object inventory but does not walk repositories or load blobs; the future `gix` provider must supply every reachable entry once without checking out files, following symlinks, or fetching gitlinks. Callers must convert returned warnings and errors into package diagnostics, use `TreeHashWarning::display_path` when rendering warning paths, and compare the checksum with the lock entry. Unicode 16.0.0 is now part of the lock path-collision and warning-display behavior; changing the Unicode data version requires a deliberate spec and dependency update.
- **Remaining scope:** Implement the `gix` repository/tree reader and Git-relative package sources, wire source and WIT hashes to lock verification, verify artifacts, materialize verified content into the cache, and connect checksum failures and tree warnings to resolver diagnostics and CLI flows.

### IR-264: Add the in-process Git object database

- **Decision:** Add `gix` 0.87.1 with default features disabled and SHA-1/SHA-256 object support enabled.
- **Reason:** Git lock entries permit both 40-digit SHA-1 and 64-digit SHA-256 commit IDs. Version 0.87.1 supports the workspace's Rust 1.85 MSRV; the newer release inspected during implementation requires Rust 1.88. Disabling default features keeps unrelated Git operations and integrations out of `sai_package`.
- **Review:** A hostile dependency review found no selected crate or resolved dependency declaring an MSRV above Rust 1.85. The `gix` crate itself has only `sha1` and `sha256` enabled and no default features; `cargo tree` also shows `gix-protocol` currently brings its default `gix-transport` dependency transitively. The package tree reader does not call transport APIs. The review also found the architecture's allowed-dependency rule had not named `gix`; §1.3.1 and §1.3.2 now list it and tie its transitive closure to `Cargo.lock`. A Rust 1.85 toolchain build was not available during this review.
- **Human review:** Future readers should open the object database directly with an explicit hash kind and isolated options; they must not let package content trigger Git configuration, hooks, attributes, filters, LFS, or submodule behavior, per package/04 §4.2.3 and §4.3.4.
- **Remaining scope:** Build the bounded `gix` object reader and route commit/tree checksums through it.

### IR-265: Read and hash package trees from Git objects

- **Decision:** Add a reader over an explicit Git `objects/` directory and object-hash kind. Read the locked commit and package subtree directly from Git objects, then pass regular-file bytes, modes, symlinks, and gitlinks to the `sai1:` tree hasher. Reject object-database alternates; do not read Git configuration, refs, a worktree, attributes, filters, or submodules. Enforce the package-file limits and add a 64 MiB limit per inspected commit/tree object, a 1,000,000-entry walk budget, and 64 MiB cumulative path-byte budget.
- **Reason:** The lock hashes the tree at an exact commit and package directory. Direct object reads avoid checkout behavior and repository configuration, while file and metadata headers can be checked before loading contents. The existing file-count and byte limits do not bound very large trees made mostly of ignored entries or warnings, so the entry budget limits parser work. The metadata cap limits one allocation while leaving roughly twice the raw tree size needed for 100,000 maximum-length SHA-256 entries. The cumulative path budget bounds copies retained for traversal, collision checks, and warning output.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found four issues: collisions between file names and case-folded directory prefixes were missed; object bytes were not checked against their Git IDs; tree-entry limits were applied after full-tree decoding; and gix could resolve alternates after the initial pre-check. The reader now detects prefix/file-directory collisions, hashes every loaded commit/tree/blob against its requested ID, checks entry limits during decoding, and initializes gix's alternate list once before disabling refresh. The second review found that a hostile local process could race an `info/alternates` file into place between the pre-check and gix's initial refresh; gix may inspect that alternate's pack metadata before the reader rejects it. gix 0.87.1 exposes no local-only initialization option. The reader now documents the stable-object-directory precondition, which is met by unique fetch staging directories and immutable cache entries. Concurrent hostile host mutation is outside the trust model in 04 §4.1.2; the second reviewer found no remaining actionable P0–P2 findings under this boundary. A new test builds two separate pack indexes without a multi-pack index, removes all loose copies, and reads commit/tree/blob objects through the packed ODB. Pack generation is enabled only in the test dependency graph; `cargo tree -p sai_cli -e features --edges normal -i gix-pack` confirms that `generate` and `streaming-input` do not reach the shipped binary. A follow-up hostile review found no test-helper or feature-isolation issue, but caught imprecise wording about `gix-pack` being absent from the shipped dependency graph; the test plan now states that only the pack-generation APIs and features are test-only. Tests also cover same-size tampered commit/tree/blob objects, alternates present at open, alternate files added after open, and early entry-limit rejection. All 353 `sai_package` tests and 1,188 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The 64 MiB metadata-object cap, 1,000,000 inspected-entry cap, and 64 MiB cumulative path-byte cap extend normative N-G-51. Prefix collision detection also rejects paths whose case-folded directory components would map to conflicting file-system entries; this follows the cross-host materialization guarantee in 03 §3.4.3. These choices preserve the documented 100,000-file limit and bound work from malformed or mostly ignored trees. The caps and expanded collision rule should be reviewed if support for unusually large metadata-heavy repositories or a narrower path-collision contract is required. The reader assumes the caller supplies a stable Git object directory; its production call sites must continue to use private staging or immutable cache content. The current gix API cannot prevent a hostile same-host process from racing an alternate into the first ODB refresh.
- **Remaining scope:** Add annotated-tag peeling and remote tag listing/fetch, connect the reader to source/WIT/artifact lock verification, cache verified trees, and report failures and warnings through resolver diagnostics and CLI commands.

### IR-266: Peel Git release tags to commits

- **Decision:** Add `GitTreeReader::peel_tag_to_commit` for lightweight and annotated tag object IDs. Verify every loaded tag and terminal commit against its requested object ID, require each tag's declared target kind to match the actual object, and reject non-commit terminal objects. Limit each annotated tag object to 64 MiB and each chain to 64 tags. Do not verify tag signatures.
- **Reason:** The resolver needs an exact commit before it can check the lock and hash a package subtree. Type checks prevent a malformed tag from claiming it points to a commit while naming a tree or blob. Per-object size and chain-depth limits bound metadata reads and CPU work; signature verification is explicitly outside the v0.1 transport contract.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found two P2 issues: declared/actual target-kind mismatches discarded the actual kind, and the public metadata-limit docs omitted tag objects. `InvalidTagTargetKind` now preserves both kinds for mismatches in either direction, and the limit docs include commits, tags, and trees. Re-review found no actionable P0–P2 issues. Tests cover lightweight and nested annotated tags, non-commit targets, malformed tag objects, mismatches in both directions, the depth boundary, and same-size tag-object tampering. All 358 `sai_package` tests and 1,193 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The 64-tag chain limit and application of the existing metadata-object cap to tag objects extend normative N-G-51. Confirm the bound is adequate for legitimate release-tag conventions; signatures remain unverified as specified in package/04 §4.3.2.
- **Remaining scope:** List and fetch release tags over the Git smart protocol, then connect peeled commits to lock verification, package source loading, checksum diagnostics, cache materialization, and CLI flows.

### IR-267: Parse bounded Git release-tag listings

- **Decision:** Add a parser for advertised Git refs that accepts only canonical version tags under the exact `refs/tags/v` or `refs/tags/<dir>/v` prefix, ignores other names, sorts accepted tags by version precedence, and caps each listing at 1,000,000 refs, 64 MiB of ref-name bytes, 1,000,000 pre-release version identifiers, and 100,000 canonical release tags. Identical duplicate refs are idempotent; a canonical ref repeated with another object ID is rejected.
- **Reason:** Resolver and CLI operations must not infer versions from malformed or unrelated refs. Exact-prefix matching prevents tags for sibling packages in a monorepo from entering the listing; rejecting `..` prevents selecting a tag namespace outside the repository; round-tripping parsed versions through their canonical formatter rejects aliases; sorting makes downstream review deterministic; aggregate count and byte limits bound ignored advertisement data as well as accepted names; an identifier-count limit bounds allocations in pre-release version parsing; and conflicting duplicate IDs must not leave a version associated with an order-dependent target.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that ignored refs and long version names were not bounded, and that conflicting duplicate refs could make the selected object ID depend on advertisement order. The parser now checks total ref count and cumulative name bytes before filtering, caps pre-release identifier parsing, deduplicates identical refs, and rejects conflicting IDs. It also rejects Git package directories that escape the repository. Hostile re-review found no remaining actionable P0–P2 issues. Tests cover canonical and malformed refs, exact directory filtering, root-directory normalization, escaping directory rejection, ordering, all four listing limits, and duplicate handling. All 367 `sai_package` tests and 1,203 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The added per-listing limits of 1,000,000 advertised refs, 64 MiB of ref-name bytes, and 1,000,000 pre-release identifiers extend N-G-51. They bound parser work and retained version/ref data while leaving room for the existing 100,000 canonical-tag limit; all advertised refs, including unrelated names, count toward the first two aggregate limits.
- **Remaining scope:** Connect this parser to a ref advertisement that performs no object fetch, then implement exact release-tag/commit fetching, lock verification, source loading, checksum diagnostics, cache materialization, and CLI flows.

### IR-268: Enforce ref-advertisement limits during Git protocol parsing

- **Decision:** Locally patch the workspace-pinned `gix-protocol` 0.65.1 parser so both protocol v1 and v2 reject advertisements above 1,000,000 refs or 64 MiB of cumulative ref-name bytes before retaining the rejected ref. Count symbolic targets toward the byte limit and count peeled v1 pseudo-records as the same logical ref as v2 peeled attributes. Return limit failures through the existing `InvalidData` I/O error variant to preserve the public `gix-protocol` error enum's exhaustiveness behavior.
- **Reason:** `gix` 0.87.1's public ref-map API collects the full server response before callers can inspect it, and a server can ignore the v2 prefix request. Applying `git_tags.rs` limits afterward would therefore allow unbounded retained refs and would not meet package/04 §4.4.4. The local parser patch applies the existing documented limits while parsing both wire formats.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that symbolic-target strings were outside the byte budget, v1 peeled names were double-counted relative to v2, and a new public error variant would break exhaustive downstream matches. Those issues are fixed and have focused regressions. Ten vendored parser tests pass in both the combined blocking/async build and the async-only build, covering both adapter configurations. The unused upstream `async-std` test dependency was removed to keep the test dependency set within the architecture allowlist. All 1,202 workspace tests, warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. The crate is a workspace member, and §15.12.4 explicitly enables the optional features needed to run these cases.
- **Human review:** The patch is pinned to the exact gix-protocol version selected by `Cargo.lock`; every gix upgrade must rebase and re-run this patch's protocol tests. The fixed limits follow N-G-51. `InvalidData` preserves the upstream public error-enum shape, and IR-269 maps its typed source to `PACKAGE_TOO_LARGE`.
- **Remaining scope:** Implement exact tag fetching and lock checks, then complete Git source loading, cache materialization, and CLI flows.

### IR-269: List remote release tags through an isolated Git transport

- **Decision:** Use the workspace-pinned `gix` client to request only `refs/tags/v*` or `refs/tags/<dir>/v*`, then run the bounded canonical tag parser on the returned ref map without fetching objects. Initialize a unique temporary bare repository with isolated configuration. Force protocol v2 when available, verify HTTPS certificates through Rustls' platform verifier, require host-key verification against OpenSSH's default host-key database, disable terminal prompts, and suppress transport details in SAI diagnostics. Read only trusted `credential.helper`, `credential.username`, `credential.useHttpPath`, and `credential.protectProtocol` settings from the XDG Git config and `~/.gitconfig`, including unconditional `include.path` files; read only `HTTPS_PROXY`/`https_proxy` and `NO_PROXY`/`no_proxy` for proxy settings. Patch `gix-transport` 0.59.2 so reqwest does not implicitly consume proxy environment variables and uses a 20-second connect timeout and 60-second per-request timeout. For SSH subprocesses, enforce a 60-second absolute stdout-read deadline and drain bounded stderr lines through EOF. Do not infer authentication failure from SSH stderr because remote-command stderr shares the same pipe; retain only recognized connection/DNS error kinds, and never expose raw stderr in SAI diagnostics. Kill and wait for the subprocess and join its output readers on cleanup or retry. Preserve the annotated tag ref's direct object ID and ignore its peeled advertisement ID.
- **Reason:** The package contract allows only the requested release-tag namespace and requires listing to fetch no objects (§4.3.1). The isolated repository prevents system or project Git settings, environment overrides, URL rewriting, filters, and hooks from changing transport behavior. Filtering trusted user config to credential settings preserves configured authentication while preventing unrelated settings from redirecting or weakening transport. Mandatory known-host verification and TLS checks implement §4.2.3. Mapping the typed protocol-parser limit source to `PACKAGE_TOO_LARGE` retains the exceeded count without parsing error text. Explicit proxy handling and finite connect, request, and SSH stdout-read deadlines bound stalled transport work. Bounded stderr parsing preserves recognized DNS/connection error kinds, avoids authentication inference from multiplexed stderr, and prevents an unbounded stderr line or channel from blocking the process.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found an incorrect XDG path, ignored unconditional credential includes, a missing SSH read deadline, and the unnecessary native TLS/OpenSSL dependency. These are fixed: the config-path/include regressions are covered, and the client now uses Rustls with the platform trust store. Later reviews found SSH reader backpressure, subprocess leaks on retry, deadline bypasses, and a short EOF grace period that could miss a stderr classification. The transport now cleans up the subprocess before retrying, continues draining stderr after its first recognized connection/DNS error, waits for stderr completion after stdout EOF within the absolute deadline, and does not infer authentication failure from multiplexed stderr. This avoids a remote command causing gix to retry and erase credentials by printing an SSH-shaped denial. Regressions cover cleanup, full stderr draining, authentication-shaped stderr remaining unclassified, delayed stderr classification, EOF deadlines, and queued stdout after expiration. A later review found predictable scratch paths and an overbroad privacy claim: the suffix now uses the standard library's randomized hash seed, and the documentation states the Unix mode-bit guarantee without claiming inherited ACLs are removed. HTTPS listing now applies the isolated repository's explicit HTTP options. Focused tests cover transport options, refspec construction, traversal rejection, trusted credential filtering, proxy-variable allowlisting, typed limit mapping, and object-free ref listing. The current full workspace tests, warning-denied Clippy, formatting, and diff checks pass.
- **Human review:** Conditional `includeIf` files are not evaluated because they depend on invocation repository/branch context that the isolated temporary bare repository does not represent. Review whether those conditional credential settings must be supported. The 60-second absolute SSH stdout-read deadline may reject unusually slow advertisements; HTTPS instead has a 60-second per-request timeout, so multiple HTTP requests/retries are not capped by one overall deadline. SSH authentication failures are reported as a generic fetch failure; the implementation deliberately avoids retrying or erasing credentials based on stderr that may have come from the remote command. OpenSSH host keys are checked against its default host-key database. Some recognized stderr text is temporarily held inside the transport's `io::Error`, but raw text is not exposed through SAI diagnostics. Credential helpers from trusted user configuration remain executable by design. Scratch directory names include a randomized hash suffix to resist predictable-name pre-creation, and Unix mode bits deny group/other access. The implementation does not clear inherited filesystem ACLs, so scratch directories are not treated as an ACL isolation boundary. Review these compatibility boundaries before using the resolver to access private repositories with specialized credential configuration.
- **Remaining scope:** Wire remote tag listing into the resolver. The exact-tag fetch and lock-check primitives are tracked in IR-270; complete package contract validation, cache insertion, resolver diagnostics, and CLI integration there.

### IR-270: Fetch exact release tags and verify locked source trees

- **Decision:** Add an exact-tag fetch operation using one literal source ref and one private destination ref, with implicit tag following disabled and shallow depth one. Initialize the temporary repository with the object-hash kind obtained from the bounded ref listing. Use the fetch handshake's direct ref object ID, ignore any peeled advertisement hint, peel the fetched object chain with `GitTreeReader`, hash only the requested package directory, and optionally compare the peeled commit and `sai1:` source checksum with lock values. Provide a separate locked-release API whose tag name and object-hash kind come from the lock, so it works when the tag was already absent before the call. If a locked tag is absent, request only the locked commit object ID and expose the fallback through `FetchedGitRelease::tag_missing()` so the caller can emit `TAG_MISSING`. Share a 4 GiB response-body budget across tag fetch and commit fallback; enforce it on HTTP response bodies and SSH stdout, and map its typed failure to `PACKAGE_TOO_LARGE`. Map recognized upload-pack missing-object responses to `VERSION_NOT_FOUND` while keeping transport failures generic. Return the tag, commit, checksum, and skipped-entry warnings after the temporary repository is removed.
- **Reason:** A ref listing is intentionally object-free, so fetching the exact ref is the next step needed to reach and verify package contents (§4.3.1–§4.3.3). A literal refspec and `Tags::None` restrict requested objects; depth one avoids transferring commit history while still providing the commit's tree closure. Reusing the bounded object reader applies tag-chain, metadata-object, tree-entry, path, and file limits and validates object IDs before lock checks. Comparing the peeled commit, rather than the annotated tag object ID, allows a publisher to recreate an annotated tag on the same commit without falsely reporting `TAG_MOVED`. The same bounded transport budget follows the operation across reconnects, including deleted-tag fallback.
- **Review:** Package tests cover nested annotated tags, exact ref selection, shallow history, SHA-1/SHA-256, locked fetch when the tag was already absent, missing-commit classification, moved commits, checksums, namespace rejection, and transfer-limit enforcement; all 387 `sai_package` tests pass. Transport boundary tests cover reqwest response bodies, SSH subprocess stdout, and curl's optional callback path; the curl feature compiles and passes warning-denied Clippy. Hostile review caught that libcurl's cancellation error could mask the typed size-limit failure; the response adapter now prioritizes `PACKAGE_TOO_LARGE` after overflow. The full workspace tests and warning-denied Clippy pass. Formatting and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The API reports a deleted tag through a boolean; the future resolver integration must turn it into the specified `TAG_MISSING` warning. Missing-commit classification recognizes a bounded set of Git upload-pack rejection phrases; other failures, including authentication and transport failures, remain generic. The 4 GiB budget counts HTTP response bodies and SSH stdout; HTTP headers and request bodies are excluded. Reqwest and subprocess readers read no more than the remaining budget plus one byte to detect overflow. The optional curl backend aborts on the first callback that crosses the budget and forwards only the allowed prefix to Git. With libcurl's default 16 KiB body callback limit, the wire response may exceed the budget by at most that one callback chunk; a regression supplies a 16 KiB callback and verifies that no excess reaches Git. Unix mode bits restrict group/other access, but inherited ACLs are not cleared.
- **Remaining scope:** Connect the `TAG_MISSING` signal to a user-visible warning; read and validate `sai.package`; retrieve source, WIT, and artifact files and checksums; materialize and verify the package in the cache; and connect fetched results and failures to resolver diagnostics and CLI commands.

### IR-271: Read and validate fetched dependency manifests

- **Decision:** Extend the bounded Git tree walk to return the root regular-file `sai.package` bytes alongside the source checksum. Apply the normative 16 MiB manifest cap from the Git blob header before reading its contents, and reuse the same object-ID checks and package traversal limits for hashing and manifest extraction. Parse fetched bytes with dependency role and retain the full parse output on `FetchedGitRelease`; build the resolver contract only when the caller supplies the current root contract context and validation context. Require the fetched tag version and the contract's declared identity/version to match the resolver's selected package version. Map an absent root manifest to `MISSING_PACKAGE_FILE`, oversized manifests to `PACKAGE_TOO_LARGE`, and tag/contract identity mismatches to `PACKAGE_IDENTITY_MISMATCH`.
- **Reason:** Resolver §2.6.2 requires every Git release's contract to exist, parse as a dependency, pass semantic validation, and declare the exact requested identity and version. Extracting it during the checksum walk avoids a second traversal and ensures the manifest bytes are the same object content covered by the source checksum. Applying the manifest bound before loading prevents a malformed tree from making the parser allocate an unbounded input. Retaining parse diagnostics allows source-located errors to be reported against the dependency manifest.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover the combined checksum/manifest walk, the size limit, missing manifests, dependency-role parsing, valid resolver-contract construction, identity/version mismatch, and preservation of source-located parse failures. Hostile review found that fetched identity/version mismatches lacked a canonical diagnostic mapping; mismatches now map to `PACKAGE_IDENTITY_MISMATCH`, while syntax and semantic failures retain their typed source-located errors. Hostile re-review confirmed the fix and found no remaining actionable P0–P2 issues. All 396 `sai_package` tests and 1,294 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Contract construction depends on an externally built `ResolverContractContext`; the resolver must populate it with all discovered path-package versions before calling this API. Callers must translate `InvalidContract`'s preserved parse and validation records into package diagnostics; its direct diagnostic-code accessor intentionally returns `None`. Git-relative sibling paths, WIT and artifact verification, cache materialization, and user-visible `TAG_MISSING` reporting remain separate steps.
- **Remaining scope:** Integrate this contract loader with source-aware MVS, read and verify all package files and their source/WIT/artifact checksums, materialize immutable cache entries and tag records, then connect failures and warnings to lock creation, package review, and CLI operations.

### IR-272: Retain checksum-verified Git source files

- **Decision:** Return the selected regular files, the parsed dependency manifest, source checksum, and warnings as one `GitPackageSourceTree` from a single bounded Git-object walk. Preserve exact blob bytes and validated package-relative paths, sort files by unsigned UTF-8 path bytes, and omit symlinks, gitlinks, `.git` paths, and source-only `.sai`/`out` paths according to the source checksum rules. Keep the source snapshot and `FetchedGitRelease` non-`Clone` so callers cannot accidentally duplicate a package-sized in-memory payload. Return a fetched release only after any locked source checksum matches.
- **Reason:** Cache materialization needs the same files whose Git blobs were checked and whose bytes formed the source checksum; a second tree walk or checkout could mix snapshots or apply host Git filters. Returning one owned snapshot lets later cache code write exactly the bytes that were verified, while reusing the manifest and tree bounds. The non-`Clone` API avoids implicit copies that could multiply memory use up to the documented per-package limit.
- **Review:** Tests verify selected-file membership, exact bytes, byte-sorted paths, dependency-role manifest parsing, agreement with the independent source hash, exclusion of `.sai`, `out`, and `.git`, and omission of symlinks and gitlinks. Hostile review checked selection, byte identity, ordering, bounds, and lock-check handling and found no actionable P0–P2 issues. All 397 `sai_package` tests and 1,295 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** A fetched package may retain up to the normative 1 GiB selected-content limit in memory until resolution consumes or drops the snapshot. The eventual cache writer should avoid additional whole-package copies and must create read-only, non-executable files atomically under the cache. This step does not yet compute separate WIT or artifact checksums, materialize cache entries, or record tags.
- **Remaining scope:** Add WIT and artifact checksum verification, write verified snapshots and tag records into the immutable global cache, implement Git-relative sibling package loading, and connect fetch results to source-aware MVS, lock updates, package review, diagnostics, and CLI commands.

### IR-273: Materialize verified source snapshots in the global cache

- **Decision:** Add `PackageCache` with the documented environment-based location resolution and `v1/{git,src,tags,tmp,lock}` layout. Materialize the exact non-cloneable `GitPackageSourceTree` snapshot under its `sai1:` content address by staging regular files in `tmp/`, setting non-executable read-only modes, and atomically renaming the completed directory into `src/`. Reuse an existing entry only after matching its exact file set, bytes, directory structure, and read-only modes; otherwise remove and rebuild it from the freshly verified snapshot. Serialize publication with an OS lock where supported and use exclusive file sharing on Windows. Consume the fetched release into one source-tree value so its checksum, manifest, and files remain bound together.
- **Reason:** Package bytes must be written from the same bounded Git-object snapshot whose checksum was checked, without checkout filters or a second tree read. Content-addressed deduplication and atomic publication prevent partial package trees from becoming cache hits. Comparing existing entries against the verified snapshot catches corruption before this API returns an existing path.
- **Review:** Hostile review found a read-only regular-file cleanup failure on Windows, reuse of a hard-linked cache file, a macOS cross-parent rename permission requirement, a first-use directory-creation race, and stale-temp cleanup that could remove an active writer's staging tree. Repair now clears the read-only attribute before removing a regular-file entry, but skips that chmod for multiply linked Unix files so the external inode remains unchanged. Regression tests cover both corrupt hard-linked file entries and hard links inside a cache tree. Unix cache reuse requires one link, and recursive cleanup does not change permissions on a multiply linked inode. Concurrent layout creation accepts another process's successfully created directory and validates it. Automatic stale-temp deletion was removed because it could race active writers; ordinary failures still clean their own staging directory. A macOS regression confirms that cross-parent rename from `tmp/` to `src/` fails while the staged root is read-only and succeeds after briefly granting owner write access, followed by immediate resealing. The XDG rule was corrected to apply only on non-macOS Unix. Final hostile re-review found no remaining actionable P0–P2 issues. All 415 `sai_package` tests and 1,313 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** macOS requires the staged root to be owner-writable during cross-parent rename. If the process terminates after publication but before resealing, the entry can remain writable; callers receive no path until resealing completes, and the internal checksum-to-path helper is private. Future cache readers must still use a validating/locking API rather than trusting a previously retained path. Windows readonly attributes do not provide directory ACL protection, and non-Unix hard-link counts are not checked here; Windows ACL and link behavior were not executable in the installed target environment. Cache directory ACLs are not explicitly reset. Targets without the supported advisory-lock API rely on atomic directory publication for this source-only step. Abrupt termination may leave unused directories in `tmp/`; safe cleanup is deferred until it can distinguish active staging.
- **Remaining scope:** Add tag and repository records and verified cache reads, including the specified `CACHE_CORRUPT` and offline behavior. Verify WIT and artifact checksums, load Git-relative sibling paths, integrate cached source and tag data with source-aware MVS and lock handling, then connect warnings, failures, package review, and CLI operations.

### IR-274: Remove only inactive stale source staging directories

- **Decision:** Pair each `tmp/source-*` staging directory with a sibling `.lock` activity file, opened exclusively before the directory is created and retained until publication or cleanup finishes. Refresh the activity marker timestamp after writing the complete tree. A later materialization removes a staging directory only when both the directory and activity marker are at least 24 hours old, it can acquire the activity lock non-blockingly, and it rechecks those ages. On Unix, remove an activity marker while still holding its lock. For markerless legacy directories on supported Unix targets, also require that the PID encoded in the staging name no longer exists before atomically claiming a marker and deleting the old tree; platforms without a reliable process check leave markerless legacy directories untouched.
- **Reason:** Package/04 §4.4.5 requires abandoned temporary directories to be removed after 24 hours, while concurrent materializations must be allowed. Age alone is insufficient because an active slow writer can leave its root directory timestamp unchanged for a long time. The activity lock protects current writers. For pre-marker Unix writers, the encoded PID must also be gone; a recycled PID can conservatively prevent cleanup rather than risk deleting live work.
- **Review:** Hostile reviews found that markerless legacy staging directories were skipped, concurrent cleaners could race with a fresh writer, a still-running pre-marker process could be mistaken for an abandoned tree, Windows orphan marker files could accumulate until staging-name allocation failed, a valid package file could collide with an in-tree timestamp sentinel, and the process check needed a narrower target cfg. Cleanup now atomically claims an eligible missing marker with `create_new` before rechecking age, checks legacy writer PIDs on supported Unix targets, unlinks Unix markers while their lock is held, and releases then unlinks Windows markers while the existing name still prevents a new writer from claiming it. The activity age is refreshed on the marker itself, and cleanup requires both marker and tree to be stale. Regression coverage includes the timestamp-sentinel filename as package content, recent marker preservation, dead and live legacy writer PIDs, an already-claimed legacy directory, locked staging preservation, stale staging removal, and concurrent cache initialization and writers. Final hostile re-review found no actionable P0–P3 findings and confirmed the documented Windows and legacy-marker limitations. All 421 `sai_package` tests and 1,319 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Unix uses non-blocking `flock`; Windows uses an exclusive-share activity handle. Windows behavior was not run in the installed target environment. On Unix, a recycled PID can conservatively leave an old markerless directory; Windows and targets without a reliable process-liveness check preserve markerless legacy directories. Targets without the implemented locking primitive also disable stale cleanup rather than risk deleting a live writer's directory. This protects `source-*` staging created by `PackageCache`; production Git fetch scratch is covered by the same cache cleanup as of IR-275. Scratch directories left in the operating-system temp directory by older toolchain versions are outside the new cache cleanup and may remain.
- **Remaining scope:** Add validated source reads, including `CACHE_CORRUPT` and offline behavior. Verify WIT and artifact checksums, load Git-relative sibling paths, and integrate cache records with source-aware MVS, lock handling, diagnostics, and CLI operations.

### IR-275: Keep Git fetch scratch under the global cache temporary lifecycle

- **Decision:** Place production Git fetch repositories under the configured cache's `v1/tmp/` directory, and pair each directory with the same `.lock` activity marker used by source staging. Run stale-temp cleanup before creating a repository. Keep unit-test defaults in the operating-system temp directory, while explicitly testing the production cache path through a cache-injected constructor.
- **Reason:** Package/04 §4.4.1 requires in-progress fetches to live under the global cache, and §4.4.5 requires the next writer to reclaim interrupted temporary directories older than 24 hours. Sharing `PackageCache` cleanup gives Git object scratch the same concurrency and stale-directory guarantees without scanning or deleting unrelated system-temp files.
- **Review:** Hostile subagent review checked cache/tmp placement, activity-lock lifecycle, and stale-cleanup interactions and found no actionable findings. Tests cover production-path placement, active-lock preservation, and stale Git-temp removal. All 424 `sai_package` tests and 1,322 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Existing Git scratch directories left in the operating-system temp directory by earlier toolchain versions are not migrated or deleted automatically. The installed target environment did not exercise Windows-specific lock cleanup. Cache-location errors continue to be mapped through the existing generic Git temporary-directory failure until resolver diagnostics and CLI usage-error mapping are integrated.
- **Remaining scope:** Add validated source reads, including `CACHE_CORRUPT` and offline behavior. Verify WIT and artifact checksums, load Git-relative sibling paths, and integrate cached sources and fetch results with source-aware MVS, lock handling, diagnostics, and CLI operations.

### IR-276: Persist Git repository keys and release lookup records

- **Decision:** Derive each Git repository directory from the SHA-256 of its canonical URL. Store tag-to-commit and `(URL, commit, directory)`-to-source-checksum records under `v1/tags/`, using domain-separated, length-prefixed SHA-256 keys so user-controlled strings never become path components. Encode each bounded record with a versioned header, generation, and payload digest. Keep two alternating record slots: readers select the highest generation, and writers replace only the inactive slot while holding the cache lock. Stage writes under `tmp/`. Permit tag records to change after the caller has completed moved-tag checks and review, while refusing a different checksum for the same immutable source identity.
- **Reason:** Package/04 §4.4.1 defines the repository key and the two lookup mappings. Package/03 §3.8.2 requires offline version selection to use previously observed tag commits, and verified commits need a stable source-checksum lookup. Hashing identities bounds filenames and avoids separator ambiguity. Alternating slots preserve the previous complete record while a replacement is published, including on Windows where replacing an existing file requires a remove-and-rename sequence.
- **Review:** Hostile review first found that Windows readers could observe a remove-and-rename gap; two alternating generation slots now keep the active record available while replacing the inactive one. Follow-up review found that readers could race between metadata inspection and file open, and that a corrupt stale slot could mask a valid newer record. Open-time `NotFound` is now treated as a missing slot. Each record stores a generation and its complement: a digest-corrupt slot is ignored only when these intact fields prove it is older than the valid slot; a corrupt newer or unorderable slot still fails closed. Tests cover the simulated inactive-slot gap, concurrent readers during updates, stale versus newest slot corruption, and recovery by rewriting an older corrupt slot. Final hostile review found no actionable issues. The package has 432 passing tests and the workspace has 1,330; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** `record_git_tag` assumes the caller has already compared any locked commit and obtained approval before recording a moved tag. Record payload digests detect accidental corruption but do not authenticate a cache against its owner. A damaged slot whose generation cannot be trusted, or could be newer than the valid slot, fails closed rather than risk silently rolling back to stale tag data. The API is not yet wired into resolver/offline flows and does not persist Git objects, WIT checksums, or artifact checksums. Windows-specific behavior was not executable in the installed target environment.
- **Remaining scope:** Add validated source reads, including `CACHE_CORRUPT` and offline behavior. Verify WIT and artifact checksums, load Git-relative sibling paths, and integrate the records with source-aware MVS, lock handling, diagnostics, and CLI operations.

### IR-277: Verify materialized package source trees

- **Decision:** Add `PackageCache::verify_source` to stream and validate an existing content-addressed source tree against its requested `sai1:` checksum, requiring the documented path set, manifest, resource limits, file and directory modes, and single-link regular files. Use capability-relative directory traversal and no-follow file opens; compare open-handle identities before and after hashing. Add `remove_corrupt_source`, which takes the publication lock, repeats the verification, preserves valid and missing entries, and removes only a freshly confirmed corrupt entry. Reuse the verifier when a materialization encounters an existing cache entry. Isolate Windows' stable hard-link-count query in `sai_windows_fs`; explicitly allow that one documented `GetFileInformationByHandle` unsafe block and declare its Windows metadata APIs in the architecture.
- **Reason:** Package/04 §4.4.2 requires `sai fetch` to re-hash cached locked sources and repair a corrupt entry, while §4.4.5 rule 5 forbids cache garbage collection. Interpret rule 5 as prohibiting deletion of valid entries and general GC; the specific checksum-addressed corruption repair required by §4.4.2 remains allowed. Streaming with bounded buffers and limits prevents an attacker-controlled cache tree from forcing unbounded memory use. Capability-relative traversal, no-follow opens, stable handle checks, and handle-relative permission changes prevent symlink or path swaps from redirecting reads and cleanup. Stable Rust does not expose Windows hard-link counts, so the small platform wrapper permits validation without weakening `sai_package`'s `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`.
- **Review:** Hostile reviews found and drove fixes for same-size content rewrites with restored modification time, Windows hard-link counts through an unstable standard-library API, symlink races in permission changes, Windows sharing violations during removal, a nondeterministic retry test, a missing direct Windows dependency, an undocumented unsafe exception, parent-path swaps during cleanup, and Windows directory reparse points being sent to `remove_file`. The implementation now checks Unix change-time metadata, uses the isolated stable Windows wrapper, opens files without following symlinks, changes permissions through verified file handles, anchors cleanup and deletion to one validated parent capability, retries Windows sharing violations for at most five seconds, and dispatches directory reparse points to `remove_dir` based on their Windows attribute bits. Focused regressions cover these cases. Final adversarial re-review found no remaining actionable issues within the package trust model. The 49 cache-verification tests and all 448 `sai_package` tests pass; all 1,346 workspace tests, warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. The standalone `sai_windows_fs` crate cross-checks for `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu`; the full package cross-check stops in `aws-lc-sys` because `x86_64-w64-mingw32-gcc` is not installed.
- **Human review:** The current implementation assumes the configured global cache root remains stable for the lifetime of a cache operation. Replacing that root concurrently is tampering with the global cache, which §4.1.2 excludes from the v0.1 trust guarantee. Windows directory-symlink coverage is checked in but could not run on this host; the test skips only when the Windows environment denies symlink creation. The full Windows package build remains unverified until a MinGW cross-compiler is available. Filesystem targets that do not support the no-follow permission fallback fail closed, so they may leave a corrupt entry in place and return an I/O error. The five-second Windows lock retry and the narrow `sai_windows_fs` unsafe exception should be revisited if the platform/API support changes.
- **Remaining scope:** Connect these primitives to `sai fetch` so every locked source is checked, corrupt entries are re-fetched online or produce the documented offline error, and `CACHE_CORRUPT` warnings reach users. Verify WIT and artifact checksums, load Git-relative sibling paths, and integrate cache records with source-aware MVS, lock handling, diagnostics, and CLI commands.

### IR-278: Verify locked WIT and artifact checksums

- **Decision:** Extend the bounded Git-object package walk to compute a separately declared WIT directory checksum and exact artifact-byte checksums alongside the source checksum. Retain only path-and-digest metadata for the additional tree walks. Add a lock-derived expectation type and require locked Git fetches to compare source, optional WIT, and the complete target-to-artifact checksum set before returning the fetched release, so callers cannot cache a partially verified locked package. Filter source-only exclusions before hashing their bytes and use binary search over sorted package files for artifact lookup.
- **Reason:** Package/03 §§3.5–3.7 gives source, WIT, and artifact distinct checksum meanings, and locked reads must bind all content to the selected lock entry. Walking Git objects avoids checkout filters and applies the same object and resource bounds as source hashing. Digest-only metadata keeps the extra WIT walk from retaining a second copy of package contents; the artifact checksums cover the exact regular-file bytes selected by the manifest.
- **Review:** The first hostile pass found that locked fetches checked only the source checksum, artifact lookup could become quadratic, and source-excluded files were hashed before filtering; these were fixed by checking every recorded checksum, binary-searching sorted files, and filtering before blob hashing. Later passes found repeated hashing of shared artifacts and memory amplification from invalid artifact targets. Checksums are memoized per file, repeated targets are deduplicated, and only known component targets produce checksum records; regression tests verify one hash per shared file and no retained records for 10,000 distinct unknown targets. Review also confirmed that no in-repository production caller currently connects a lock entry to the checked fetch API; the CLI and Git-backed source loader do not exist yet, so this remains an explicit integration step rather than a completed `sai fetch` flow. The review confirmed that the root-WIT behavior follows the direct rule in §3.5.2 and identified its tension with §3.5.3. All 454 `sai_package` tests and 1,352 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. Tests cover matching and mismatching source/WIT/artifact values, absent lock-side WIT or artifact entries, root and nested WIT trees, shared artifact files, and bounded digest-only walks.
