- **Human review:** When `wit "."` selects the package root, follow package/03 §3.5.2 literally: the WIT walk applies the Git tree rules except for the source-only `.sai` and `out` exclusions, so those paths can affect the WIT checksum even though they do not affect the source checksum. This appears to be in tension with §3.5.3's statement that WIT files are also covered by the source checksum. The implementation preserves the more specific hashing rule; clarify this edge case in the specification before treating it as settled semantics. Also, until a production caller connects lock entries to `fetch_locked_release_tree`, the package does not yet provide an end-to-end locked fetch guarantee.
- **Remaining scope:** Connect the lock expectation to Git-backed source-aware MVS and `sai fetch`; persist and validate source, tag, WIT, and artifact data in the cache; support Git-relative sibling packages; connect checksum failures and tree warnings to diagnostics, review decisions, lock updates, and CLI behavior.

### IR-279: Build locked Git fetches from lock entries

- **Decision:** Add `fetch_locked_package_tree`, which accepts a validated `LockPackage`, extracts its Git source, optional repository directory, locked commit, and source/WIT/artifact checksums, then calls the locked remote fetch path. Reject missing or duplicate fields, path sources, noncanonical URLs, invalid content directories, malformed checksums, artifacts without a WIT checksum, invalid targets, and invalid commits before transport; report malformed lock entries as `LOCK_CORRUPT`.
- **Reason:** The checksum-verification API must receive one coherent set of values from the lock. Requiring every caller to unpack `LockPackage` independently risks omitting WIT or artifact checksums or passing a commit from another entry. Converting once at the package boundary keeps the commit and all expected content hashes bound to the same lock entry.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found malformed checksum values could reach the network before producing `LOCK_CORRUPT`; the adapter now validates every consumed checksum field first. Re-review found that noncanonical URLs, reserved output/state directories, and artifact entries without WIT checksums could also pass the extraction step; the adapter now rejects those cases, validates component targets, and tests the public API for these malformed entries. The final implementation passes 34 focused Git remote tests, all 461 `sai_package` tests, all 1,359 workspace tests, warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check`. Hostile review of the extraction and cache-cleanup boundaries found no actionable P0–P2 issues.
- **Human review:** Callers must still run full lock syntax and semantic validation; this adapter defensively validates the fields it consumes. The helper accepts Git entries only. It currently fetches from the remote on every call; cache-first lookup, offline behavior, materialization, tag/source records, and command-level use are separate remaining work.
- **Remaining scope:** Wire the lock-aware cached fetch into source-aware MVS and the CLI, and connect its outcomes to warnings and diagnostics.

### IR-280: Anchor cache cleanup to the configured root

- **Decision:** Require cache-tree deletion and marker-file cleanup to receive their trusted root. Open that root as a capability, traverse each intermediate layout directory relative to the preceding capability, and reject symlinks and Windows directory reparse points before removal. Carry the root into staging and Git-temp cleanup, and replace inactive cache record slots through their verified parent capability.
- **Reason:** Checking only the immediate parent allowed an ancestor such as `v1` to be a symlink; two opens of that same resolved outside parent could pass identity checks and remove external files. Deleting a staging tree and then removing its activity marker through an ambient path left the same escape during the second operation. Capability-relative traversal validates every component below the configured cache root and pins each removal to the opened parent.
- **Review:** Hostile review found the ancestor-symlink tree deletion and a follow-on activity-marker deletion that bypassed the boundary. Both removals now traverse capabilities, and regressions verify that a symlinked `v1` cannot remove an external tree or marker file. Tests cover 461 `sai_package` cases and 1,359 workspace cases; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. Follow-up hostile review found no actionable issues within the documented local-cache trust boundary.
- **Human review:** The configured cache root remains the trust boundary and is assumed stable during an operation, consistent with IR-277 and package/04 §4.1.2. A reviewer also found that cap-std 4.0.3's Windows recursive deletion reconstructs a path and is not atomic against a concurrent ancestor reparse-point replacement. The implementation keeps static symlink/reparse-point checks and capability-relative parent validation, but does not add custom Win32 recursive deletion: package/04 §4.1.2 and §4.11 explicitly exclude a compromised host or cache, which is the local-user cache-tampering race in question. Reconsider only if the trust model is expanded to cover hostile concurrent cache mutation.
- **Remaining scope:** Connect the lock-aware cache operation to the CLI and source-aware MVS, including user-facing corruption warnings and offline diagnostics.

### IR-281: Fetch locked Git packages through the verified cache

- **Decision:** Add `fetch_locked_package_to_cache` to validate the consumed lock fields, consult package checksum metadata and source cache before network access, re-hash source content on each fetch, remove freshly confirmed corrupt source entries, and report an offline miss when a complete verified entry is unavailable. On a network fetch, compare the commit and all locked checksums before recording the package checksum set, materializing source, and updating source and tag records. Bind checksum metadata to the canonical URL, commit, and normalized package directory; compare stored source/WIT/artifact checksums with the lock rather than treating a metadata-key hit as proof that the lock matches. When auxiliary metadata is missing, accept a source-only cache hit only if the lock has no WIT/artifact checksums and the verified cached manifest declares neither WIT nor artifacts; the source checksum then covers all locked content.
- **Reason:** Package/04 §§4.3.2 and 4.4.2 require cache-first locked reads, checksum verification before materialization, source re-hashing by `sai fetch`, corruption removal, and offline failure without a network fallback. Identity-bound metadata preserves the independently computed WIT and artifact checksums when only source files are materialized. Comparing stored values reports a checksum mismatch for a bad lock instead of mislabeling known content as an offline miss.
- **Review:** An initial hostile pass found a P1: records keyed only by checksum values could be reused across commits whose source checksums match but whose root-WIT checksums differ. The metadata is now keyed by URL, commit, and directory, and the reader returns the recorded checksums for direct comparison. A second pass found offline corruption errors discarded `CACHE_CORRUPT` context and cached checksum mismatch messages omitted package/URL/commit; both paths now retain structured context. A third pass found valid source-only cache entries incorrectly required auxiliary metadata; fallback now parses the cached manifest and requires it to declare no WIT or artifacts, so an incomplete lock cannot take the source-only path. Regressions cover source/WIT/artifact metadata reuse, a wrong locked WIT checksum, a different commit with the same source checksum, failed-offline warning retention, source-only cache hits, and rejection when a cached manifest declares WIT but the lock omits its checksum. All 465 `sai_package` tests and 1,363 workspace tests pass; warning-denied Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. Final hostile re-review found no actionable P1/P2 issues.
- **Human review:** The helper still requires callers to run full lock syntax and semantic validation. Its cache corruption and missing-tag outcomes are returned as structured data, but user-facing `CACHE_CORRUPT`, `TAG_MISSING`, and `OFFLINE_FETCH_REQUIRED` diagnostics are not yet wired to a command. `wit "."` still follows the source/WIT exclusion interpretation recorded in IR-278 and requires specification review.
- **Remaining scope:** Connect this operation to source-aware MVS and `sai fetch`, resolve and load Git-relative sibling packages, preserve warnings on failed fetches, and complete diagnostic, lock-update, and CLI integration.

### IR-282: Normalize Git-relative sibling package paths

- **Decision:** Add a distinct `RepositoryPackageDirectory` path type. It accepts portable normalized repository-relative paths, including top-level `.sai` and `out`, but rejects unnormalized `..`. `normalize_git_relative_package_directory` combines the requiring package's repository directory and a prevalidated dependency path lexically, treats an absent base as the repository root, rejects attempts to pop above that root, and represents the root as `.`. Use the new type consistently for manifest `dir`, lock validation, resolver source validation, Git tree reads, release-tag namespaces, and cache records.
- **Reason:** Package/02 §2.7.2 requires lexical normalization and repository-boundary checks. Package/01 §1.6.1 restricts `.sai` and `out` only for content paths (`source`, `wit`, and artifact paths); applying that restriction to repository package directories rejects valid sibling dependencies.
- **Review:** Hostile review found a P2 because normalized paths under `.sai` and `out` were incorrectly rejected as content paths; the separate repository-directory type now fixes that across parsing, lock validation, resolver validation, tree reads, tags, and cache records. Follow-up review found that printable spaces are valid package paths but invalid Git ref names; tag listing and exact fetch now validate a probe ref with gix and return `InvalidPackageDirectory` before network access. Regressions cover root and nested bases, traversal and escape, manifest and lock acceptance, cache and tag use under `out`, and tag rejection for `.sai` and spaces. All 469 `sai_package` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. Final hostile re-review confirmed both findings are fixed and found no actionable P1/P2 issues.
- **Human review:** Repository directories such as `.sai/pkg` remain valid for commit-pinned sibling resolution. When a caller needs a release tag, the implementation rejects paths that cannot form a valid Git ref instead of inventing a new tag encoding. Package/02 §2.6.1 derives a ref by placing the directory directly under `refs/tags/`; review whether such direct Git sources are intentionally unsupported or need a different encoding. The sibling source provider still needs to load these directories from the requiring package's locked commit.
- **Remaining scope:** Use the normalized directory with the requiring package's locked Git commit, validate the sibling manifest identity and version, and wire `PATH_ESCAPES_REPOSITORY` into package diagnostics and source-aware MVS.

### IR-283: Read typed package directories from Git commits

- **Decision:** Add `GitTreeReader::read_commit_repository_package_source_tree`, which accepts a validated `RepositoryPackageDirectory` and maps its `.` root sentinel to the empty Git path. Route remote release-tree reads through this typed method. Keep the existing byte-slice reader public for compatibility and low-level callers.
- **Reason:** Git-relative sibling resolution needs to read arbitrary normalized package directories at the requiring package's commit, including directories below `.sai` and `out`. A typed entry point carries the normalization invariant from path composition through tree reading; the existing bounded tree walk remains the single implementation of object verification, manifest parsing, and resource limits.
- **Review:** Added regressions for typed root handling and nested package directories below both reserved paths. All 471 `sai_package` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. The hostile review found no actionable P0–P2 findings.
- **Human review:** Retaining the byte-slice reader preserves its existing public interface. New resolver code should prefer the typed entry point so repository-directory normalization is represented in its API; the byte-slice path continues to use the same internal Git-tree path validation.
- **Remaining scope:** Fetch a sibling package from the requiring package's locked commit, validate its manifest identity/version, then connect Git-backed path requirements to source-aware MVS and package diagnostics.

### IR-284: Fetch a Git package from an exact repository commit

- **Decision:** Add `fetch_git_package_source_tree_at_commit`, which validates the canonical Git URL, initializes an isolated temporary bare repository using the commit's object-hash kind, requests the exact commit under the fixed fetch budget, and reads the requested normalized repository directory through `GitTreeReader`. Do not inspect release tags. Report a missing sibling commit with a repository-commit-specific error.
- **Reason:** Package/02 §2.7.2 pins a Git-relative path package to the requiring package's commit and explicitly says the sibling is not fetched through its release tag. Reusing the existing exact-commit transport and bounded tree reader preserves the same object-integrity and package-size protections as ordinary Git release fetches.
- **Review:** Tests fetch a nested sibling package from a commit whose release tag was removed, report a missing commit with the path-source error, and reject a local `file://` URL at the public boundary. All 474 `sai_package` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. Hostile review found no actionable P0–P2 findings.
- **Human review:** Each call currently fetches into a new temporary repository; it does not yet reuse the normative `git/<repo-key>/` object cache. The local successful-fetch fixture calls the internal helper because the public URL policy excludes `file://`; public URL rejection and the repository setup used by the existing release-fetch path are covered separately. Before using this API from resolution, validate the sibling's declared identity and version, compare locked checksums when available, and connect its verified files to the content cache.
- **Remaining scope:** Validate the fetched sibling contract, integrate Git-backed path pins and their source identities into source-aware MVS, and surface missing paths and version conflicts through resolver diagnostics.

### IR-285: Bind and validate fetched Git sibling packages

- **Decision:** Return a `FetchedGitPackage` that binds the canonical repository URL, exact commit, normalized package directory, and verified source snapshot. The public fetch operation and tests share the same result-building helper. Factor package-manifest contract construction so release tags and Git path packages both verify the full declared identity and exact selected version; keep the release-tag version check as an additional release-only condition.
- **Reason:** A Git path source is identified by repository, commit, and normalized directory, and MVS must not accept a manifest for another identity or version. Reusing contract construction keeps Git releases and siblings under the same dependency-role validation while preserving the sibling's distinct commit-pinned source semantics.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found a P2 because a success test manually assembled `FetchedGitPackage` and therefore did not check production provenance binding. The public operation and tests now use the same helper to populate URL, commit, directory, and snapshot; re-review confirmed the finding closed and reported no remaining actionable P0–P2 issues. All 475 `sai_package` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The exact selected version is checked while building the contract; the resolver still has to check that version against each incoming semantic-version requirement and line. The successful local transport fixture exercises the shared result-building helper with `file://`; the public operation rejects that scheme, so successful public URL canonicalization is covered by inspection rather than a live HTTPS/SSH test.
- **Remaining scope:** Discover and normalize path requirements from Git sibling manifests, use their source identity in MVS path-pin canonicalization and reload auditing, compare locked checksums, reuse the repository/content caches, and report resolution failures through diagnostics and CLI commands.

### IR-286: Resolve Git sibling paths from a pinned source identity

- **Decision:** Bind a fetched Git package to a `GitPackageSourceIdentity` containing its canonical repository URL, exact commit, and normalized repository directory. Resolve dependency paths against that identity and fetch the resulting package at the same commit without consulting tags. Validate the path before transport setup. Keep invalid path syntax, repository escape, and missing sibling package as distinct errors; classify an unavailable exact repository commit as `FETCH_FAILED`, not `VERSION_NOT_FOUND`.
- **Reason:** Package/02 §2.7.2 makes a Git path package relative to the requiring package's directory and pins it to the requiring package's commit. The source identity makes all three components available to path-pin canonicalization and prevents the sibling fetch from drifting to a release tag or another commit. The diagnostic contract reserves `VERSION_NOT_FOUND` for a missing release tag and defines `PATH_PACKAGE_NOT_FOUND` for an absent path package.
- **Review:** Adversarial review found and drove fixes for path validation running after temporary-repository setup, path failures using release-tag wording, exact-commit fetch failures using tag-specific wording, missing sibling directories mapping to `MISSING_PACKAGE_FILE`, and unavailable sibling commits mapping to `VERSION_NOT_FOUND`. The public API now validates paths before setup, returns source-neutral fetch text, maps missing siblings to `PATH_PACKAGE_NOT_FOUND`, and maps unavailable exact commits to `FETCH_FAILED`. Final hostile re-review found no actionable P0–P2 findings. All 479 `sai_package` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** A sibling package's identity and version remain checked by `FetchedGitPackage::build_dependency_contract`; the path fetch itself only validates and reads the commit tree. Because the diagnostics define no separate code for an unavailable path-source commit, use `FETCH_FAILED` to avoid incorrectly reporting that a release tag is missing. Each sibling currently gets a fresh temporary Git repository; successful public HTTPS/SSH fetching is not integration-tested, and the new API is not yet connected to MVS or the shared repository/content cache.
- **Remaining scope:** Discover Git-relative path requirements in fetched manifests, load them through source-aware MVS using the complete identity for pin comparison and reload auditing, compare lock checksums, reuse cached Git objects and content, and surface the resulting diagnostics through the package workflow and CLI.

### IR-287: Carry immutable source identity through Git releases

- **Decision:** Store `GitPackageSourceIdentity` in `FetchedGitRelease` and expose it while preserving `commit_id()` as a delegating accessor. Production release fetches construct their transport and provenance URLs from the same canonical URL. Keep the local `file://` transport adapter available only in test builds.
- **Reason:** Source-aware MVS needs a release's canonical repository URL, exact commit, and normalized package directory together to compare Git path pins and audit reloaded packages. Returning only the commit loses the repository and package-directory components needed for that identity.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that an initial internal URL pair could fetch one repository while recording another as provenance. The production URL representation is now a single-value variant, while the intentionally separate local fixture transport is `cfg(test)` only. A regression rejects a noncanonical identity URL before opening transport. The final hostile re-review found no actionable P0–P2 findings. All 480 `sai_package` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The existing `commit_id()` API remains source-compatible. Successful public HTTPS/SSH fetching is still not exercised against a live remote, and this identity is not yet consumed by MVS.
- **Remaining scope:** Discover Git-relative path requirements in fetched manifests, load them through source-aware MVS using the complete identity for pin comparison and reload auditing, compare locked checksums, reuse cached Git objects and content, and surface the resulting diagnostics through the package workflow and CLI.

### IR-288: Index Git source identities by exact requirer

- **Decision:** Add `GitPackageSourceIdentityIndex` to associate each `MvsRequirer` with the canonical repository URL, exact commit, and normalized package directory that supplied it. Keep `Root(package_version)` and `Package(package_version)` as separate keys even when their package versions are equal. Maintain a reverse B-tree keyed by the complete Git identity to reject one source being assigned different versions without rescanning all loaded packages. Canonicalize Git path pins only after confirming that `relative_to` matches the requirer's package version, then resolve the relative directory against that exact source identity.
- **Reason:** Root and dependency packages can have the same declared package version while coming from different sources. Collapsing the requirer to `MvsPackageVersion` would then let a root path pin use the dependency's Git provenance, or prevent both identities from being retained. The path pin must inherit the selected requirer's immutable commit and directory without consulting release tags.
- **Review:** Hostile review identified that the initial index keyed entries only by package version and therefore merged root and dependency identities. The key now preserves the `Root`/`Package` role, and a regression proves that both same-version entries resolve the same relative path from their own distinct directories. A reverse B-tree makes identity conflict checks logarithmic and keeps equality independent of whether the root or dependency identity was inserted first. Additional tests cover idempotent insertion, conflicting identities or package versions, missing requirers, mismatched path bases, and repository escapes. The final hostile review found no actionable P0–P2 issues. All 483 `sai_package` tests and 1,381 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The index is a resolver primitive and is not yet populated or consumed by the source-aware MVS provider. Its uniqueness check assumes one exact Git source identity (URL, commit, directory) cannot declare two package versions; manifest validation binds a fetched source to its declared version. The same-version root/dependency regression uses two normalized directories in one fixture commit to exercise distinct provenance.
- **Remaining scope:** Populate the index as root and dependency contracts load, use it to canonicalize Git path pins during source-aware MVS, preserve identities through selected-source reload auditing, compare locked checksums, reuse cached Git objects and content, and surface failures through diagnostics and CLI commands.

### IR-289: Look up captured identities by exact requirer

- **Decision:** Add `ResolverSourceIdentitySnapshot::get(&MvsRequirer)` to retrieve the captured source identity for a root or dependency while preserving their distinct roles. Use this accessor when final-source planning validates root-relative and package-relative path bases.
- **Reason:** The snapshot deliberately stores the root identity separately from the dependency map, so a root and dependency can share a package version without sharing source identity. Requiring the full `MvsRequirer` at lookup prevents a root path from accidentally resolving through the dependency entry (or vice versa), and a mismatched root package returns no identity.
- **Review:** Two independent hostile reviews confirmed that lookup preserves the Root/Package distinction, rejects a root version mismatch, and uses final identities for selected dependency bases while retaining captured identities for the root and unselected package bases. A regression uses the same package version for distinct root and dependency identities. All 484 `sai_package` tests and 1,382 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** The accessor makes the snapshot's existing role distinction available to later Git-backed path canonicalization. It does not decide whether a package version can validly occur as both the root and a selected dependency; graph validation remains responsible for that rule.
- **Remaining scope:** Use exact-requirer identity lookup while loading mixed local and Git packages through source-aware MVS, canonicalize Git sibling pins against their requiring commits, and retain identities through locked-source reload auditing.

### IR-290: Discover Git-relative path requirements

- **Decision:** Add `discover_git_path_requirements` for a fetched release or commit-pinned package. Resolve paths against the requiring package's complete Git identity, filter dependency test edges and target-excluded requirements, and apply root path/Git overrides using the same rules as local path discovery. Cache fetched sibling snapshots by canonical URL, exact commit, and normalized repository directory; check that the fetch callback returned that exact identity and that the sibling manifest declares the required package. Retain `PackageParseOutput` on parse-related failures so callers can report source diagnostics.
- **Reason:** Package/02 §2.7.2 requires sibling paths to be read from the requiring package's exact commit and normalized directory, without looking up a release tag. Reusing one fetched snapshot per identity avoids repeated reads when distinct path spellings normalize to the same directory. Matching local discovery filters ensures excluded or overridden edges do not trigger sibling fetches.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that invalid sibling manifests initially lost their parse diagnostics and that the first success test only exercised `"."`. The error now exposes the retained parse output, and tests cover a real nested sibling, target/test/override filtering, repository escape before fetch, requirer-version mismatch, malformed sibling diagnostics, and single-fetch behavior for two path spellings that normalize to one identity. Final hostile re-review found no actionable P0–P3 findings. All 489 `sai_package` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Discovery extracts the sibling's declared identity and version; source-aware MVS still needs to enforce each requirement's version range and line, validate the complete dependency contract, canonicalize path pins with `GitPackageSourceIdentityIndex`, and detect cycles and conflicting pins. The regression that checks normalized-identity reuse deliberately makes its second requirement disagree with the sibling manifest, so discovery returns the expected identity mismatch after reusing the first snapshot.
- **Remaining scope:** Connect discovery to the source-aware MVS loader, retain sibling identities through final-source reload auditing, validate pin conflicts and version constraints, compare locked checksums, reuse Git/content caches, and surface failures through package diagnostics and CLI commands.

### IR-291: Preserve requirer roles in path-package keys

- **Decision:** Store `MvsRequirer` in `ResolverPathKey`. Require an explicit requirer in `ResolverPathKey::new`, and provide `package` and `root` convenience constructors. Build keys from the parsed manifest role in contract construction and preserve the role through local and Git path discovery. Reject Git discovery when a root requirer does not match the root package bound to its resolver context.
- **Reason:** A root and a dependency can declare the same package version and path spelling while resolving to different snapshots. Keying only by `MvsPackageVersion` collapses those values and can attach one source's path package to the other contract. Requiring the role at construction makes the distinction explicit and prevents a root key from being silently treated as a dependency key.
- **Review:** Hostile reviews found that Git path discovery did not validate a root requirer against its resolver context and that the first Root test fixture had a mismatched context and omitted a root Git override. Discovery now rejects the mismatch before fetching; the regression uses a matching root manifest, verifies both Root and Package keys, and retains the override that filters the excluded path. All `ResolverPathKey` call sites were migrated to role-aware constructors. Final hostile review confirmed the Root/Package fixes and found no remaining functional P0–P3 issues. All 490 `sai_package` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** This changes the public source API: `ResolverPathKey::new` now accepts `MvsRequirer`, the `requiring_package` field is replaced by `requirer`, and Git discovery takes the exact requirer role. The previous key could not represent both root and dependency values for the same version/path, so a compatibility shim would preserve ambiguity. Keep the explicit API while the workspace is at 0.1.0; the existing release policy records 0.2.0 as the next publication boundary for public API breaks. Downstream callers must use `root` or `package` (or pass `MvsRequirer` to `new`) and access the version through `requirer.package_version()`.
- **Remaining scope:** Connect Git path discovery to source-aware MVS, validate sibling contracts and version constraints during loading, canonicalize path pins with the exact source identity, retain identities through final-source reload auditing, compare lock checksums, reuse Git/content caches, and surface resolution errors through diagnostics and CLI commands.

### IR-292: Fetch the Git release selected by MVS

- **Decision:** Add `fetch_selected_git_release` to consume an exact `ResolverSourceSelection`. Reuse a validated lock entry only when its package version, canonical URL, and package directory match; require that entry for a lock-floor selection. Otherwise, derive and query only the selected version's canonical tag, then fetch it through the existing bounded tree-validation path. Keep path sources on the exact-commit Git sibling API.
- **Reason:** Package/02 §2.6.1 requires the resolver to request exactly the version selected by MVS. Full release-tag enumeration is reserved for `sai update` and `sai add`, and using it during resolution can fail for repositories with many unrelated releases. Lock-floor selections must remain pinned to their validated commit and checksums.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that the initial implementation listed all tags and that exact-tag parsing reused a pre-release identifier limit reserved for listings. Both were removed from the resolver path. A regression fixture with 100,001 release tags confirms exact lookup still finds `v1.2.3`; a missing requested tag remains distinguishable. Final hostile re-review found no actionable P0–P3 issues. All 494 `sai_package` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** Exact lookup compares the advertised full ref with the canonical ref derived from the selected version; it does not infer a version by scanning other tag names. The public URL policy remains HTTPS/SSH, so the large-tag lookup regression uses the internal local-transport helper rather than a live network remote. Validate successful public transport against representative HTTPS and SSH servers before relying on server-specific ref-prefix behavior.
- **Remaining scope:** Connect selected Git release and commit-pinned sibling loading to a mixed local/Git source-aware MVS provider, retain Git identities for path-pin canonicalization and final-source reload auditing, compare lock checksums, reuse Git and content caches, and surface resolver failures through diagnostics and CLI commands.

### IR-293: Preserve root and dependency local path identities

- **Decision:** Store the root directory separately from dependency directories in `LocalPackageDirectoryIndex`, keyed by the full `MvsRequirer` role at lookup. Add `index_dependency_path_from` for role-explicit path discovery, and migrate root path overrides and requirements to `Root` while dependency requirements use `Package`. Keep the version-only compatibility method when only one role matches; return `AmbiguousRequirerRole` if the same version is indexed as both root and dependency.
- **Reason:** A root and a dependency may declare the same package version while representing different source trees. A version-only directory map would either reject this valid state or resolve one source's relative path against the other tree. Source-aware MVS also uses `Root` and `Package` as distinct identities, so local indexing must preserve the same distinction through pin canonicalization and selected-source loading.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found a source-compatibility concern from adding a public error variant and asked for tests of reverse insertion order and selected-source resolution. The compatibility method now fails closed when role is ambiguous. Regression tests verify separate same-version bases, root and dependency pin canonicalization, deterministic rejection of one directory assigned different versions in both insertion orders, and selection of the dependency directory when its version equals the root's. Final hostile review accepted the tests and found no additional findings. All 497 `sai_package` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** `LocalPackagePathIndexError` is a public exhaustive enum, so adding `AmbiguousRequirerRole` can break downstream exhaustive matches. Keep the variant because silently choosing either directory would make the compatibility API unsafe for the newly supported same-version state. Treat this as an intentional 0.2.0 API-boundary change, consistent with the repository's release policy. Existing callers that can encounter both roles must migrate to `index_dependency_path_from` and pass `MvsRequirer` explicitly.
- **Remaining scope:** Integrate local and Git package loading through one source-aware MVS provider. Preserve the exact requirer source identity during Git path-pin canonicalization and selected-source reload auditing, connect verified sources to lock and cache handling, and report resolution failures through package diagnostics and CLI operations.

### IR-294: Resolve and audit mixed local and Git sources

- **Decision:** Add a mixed-source MVS loader that retains distinct root and dependency contexts, loads local releases, exact Git releases, and commit-pinned Git siblings, and indexes each loaded package under its exact `MvsRequirer`. Use a Git sibling lock entry only when its canonical repository URL, commit, and normalized directory exactly match the resolved identity; verify every checksum recorded by that lock entry. After MVS, resolve final source identities, reusing a read-time Git identity only when its canonical URL and normalized directory still match, reload changed sources and their path requirements, then expose the reloaded contracts and comparison context through `MixedSourceFinalAuditPlan`.
- **Reason:** Package/02 §2.8.3 requires every selected version to be audited against its final source after `Check`; sources that differ may be accepted only when the included dependency edges and source-bound path pins remain equivalent. A single role-aware loader is needed so local and Git dependencies use the same version selection while path pins continue to inherit the exact source of their requirer.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found two P1 gaps: Git sibling locks were not checked against the exact source identity and recorded checksums, and final source planning did not reload changed sources for contract comparison. Both were addressed with exact-identity lock selection and checksum verification, plus final identity planning, path-requirement discovery, reloads, and contract auditing. The follow-up review found a P2 gap where a Git path reload could accept a fetched package from another identity; final reloads now verify the returned identity, and an end-to-end regression returns an identical package/version from another repository and requires an audit failure. A P3 review note found that this mismatch was classified as lock corruption; it now uses a dedicated source-mismatch fetch error and the regression asserts `FETCH_FAILED`. The final hostile re-review found no actionable P0–P3 findings. All 501 `sai_package` tests and 1,399 workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** The new mixed-source APIs are package-resolution primitives and are not yet wired into the driver or CLI. `GitRemoteReleaseFetchError::GitPathSourceIdentityMismatch` is a new variant on a public exhaustive enum; retain the dedicated fetch classification rather than reporting lock corruption, and include the variant in the planned 0.2.0 public API boundary. Git path lock matching treats entries for another exact commit as stale rather than using their checksums as a floor, because a path pin is bound to the requirer's commit. Confirm this lock lifecycle when integrating it with lock-file update and write behavior. No persistent Git repository or package-content cache is connected to this loader yet.
- **Remaining scope:** Integrate the mixed-source provider and final-source audit into the package workflow; emit source-located resolver diagnostics, including `DIVERGENT_SOURCES`; complete lock-file lifecycle, persistent Git/content cache reuse, and CLI commands; then run the package and compiler conformance suites.

### IR-295: Bind mixed-source divergence diagnostics to their inputs

- **Decision:** Add a `RootDatabase` adapter that emits `DIVERGENT_SOURCES` only when the final audit plan belongs to the exact mixed-source MVS load and every workspace manifest matches the exact manifest bytes used to build that load's resolver contracts. Bind audit plans with the resolver's opaque load token. Record a SHA-256 digest of each accepted manifest at parse time, retain only the fixed-size digest in resolver contracts and workspace manifest locations, and return no digest for an oversized manifest rejected before parsing. Preserve the existing equality semantics of `PackageParseOutput` and `ResolverContract` by excluding provenance digests from their `Eq` comparisons.
- **Reason:** The diagnostic combines comparisons from final-source reloads with source spans from the workspace snapshot. A plan from another resolution or a stale snapshot can otherwise attach valid-looking but unrelated source clauses and locations to a divergence. Comparing bounded digests proves the manifest-byte association without retaining another full parse tree for each visited dependency.
- **Review:** Hostile review found a P2 cross-load pairing flaw, fixed by validating the audit plan's load token. It also found that retaining complete parsed manifests in every contract and diagnostic location could amplify memory use for manifests near the 16 MiB limit; these copies were replaced with fixed-size digests. Two P3 issues were fixed: resolver-contract equality again ignores non-contract manifest metadata, and oversized inputs expose an unavailable digest rather than a misleading zero digest. A stale documentation statement was updated. Regression tests cover a mismatched plan, stale root and dependency manifests, real divergence from a changed local path source, equality behavior, and unavailable oversized-input digests. Final hostile review found no P0–P3 findings. All 503 `sai_package` tests and 10 `sai_driver` tests pass; all 1,403 workspace tests, warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** `RootDatabaseError` gains public variants for a mismatched audit load and mismatched manifest contract; callers with exhaustive matches must account for them at the next public API boundary (0.2.0 under the repository's release policy). Snapshot-to-contract matching relies on SHA-256 collision resistance rather than retaining and comparing full manifest bytes. The adapter emits only actual divergence comparisons; callers still need to surface final-source identity and reload failures.
- **Remaining scope:** Integrate mixed-source MVS and final-source audit into the package workflow; convert selection, identity, and reload failures into source-located diagnostics; produce package review data for non-winning source clauses; finish lock lifecycle and persistent cache reuse; implement package CLI commands; then run package and compiler conformance suites.

### IR-296: Report final-source identity failures

- **Decision:** Add package diagnostic mapping and a `RootDatabase::mixed_source_identity_diagnostics` adapter for classified final-side source identity failures. Reuse the divergence adapter's load-token and manifest-digest checks before attaching source locations. Build each diagnostic from the final source-selection location, falling back to the final requirement edge, and include the package/version, source, failure detail, and requirement chain. Map missing local paths/manifests to `PATH_PACKAGE_NOT_FOUND`, invalid path syntax to `INVALID_PACKAGE_PATH`, oversized manifests to `PACKAGE_TOO_LARGE`, repository escapes to `PATH_ESCAPES_REPOSITORY`, Git identity mismatch to `FETCH_FAILED`, and other Git fetch failures to their existing resolver classification. Keep internal consistency failures and local I/O failures without a precise package diagnostic classification on the audit plan.
- **Reason:** A final identity failure prevents the required final-source audit before a contract comparison exists, so `DIVERGENT_SOURCES` cannot describe it. Package/02 §2.13.1 requires package resolution failures to retain the source-backed requirement chain that led to the affected node. Binding the diagnostic to the same load and manifest bytes prevents unrelated current source text from being presented as the cause.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found a P2 where `MvsExplanationGraph::new` suppressed independent identity failures whenever MVS retained a root-cycle error. The diagnostic now uses the root-cycle-aware explanation builder for incomplete selections, and a regression places the identity failure on an independent `acme:c` branch. Follow-up review also found that the initial test put the failure on the cycle itself; the fixture now covers `acme:a -> acme:b -> root` alongside the separate `acme:c` branch. Final hostile re-review found no actionable findings. All 504 `sai_package` tests, 11 `sai_driver` tests, and 1,405 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** These new package and driver diagnostic APIs are public and should be included in the next public API boundary (0.2.0 under the repository release policy). The mapping intentionally leaves permission, race, and platform-specific local I/O errors unclassified; the package workflow must still present those retained failures. Final-source reload and contract-audit failures are not converted by this change.
- **Remaining scope:** Report non-identity reload and reloaded-contract audit failures with final-manifest source locations; report unclassified local I/O through the package workflow; then integrate the diagnostic collector into package commands and run package/compiler conformance.

### IR-297: Report final-source package identity mismatches

- **Decision:** Add `MixedSourceFinalReloadError` mappings and a `RootDatabase::mixed_source_reload_diagnostics` adapter for direct final-source package identity/version mismatches with known selected nodes. Require the package diagnostic helper to receive the originating mixed-source load and its audit plan together, and reject a mismatched load token. Report expected/found package and version values at the node's final source selection location (falling back to its source-bound MVS chain), retaining distinct mismatch details while deduplicating identical diagnostics. Leave non-identity inner path, fetch, parse, contract, and global reload failures on the audit plan until final manifests have their own snapshot-bound locations.
- **Reason:** Package/02 §2.13.4 requires a diagnostic to identify the affected node and preserve its requirement chain. The audit plan retains enough provenance for direct identity mismatches, but final-manifest path edges and parse errors cannot safely use locations from the original workspace snapshot. Restricting this step to proven node-level mismatches avoids highlighting unrelated source while keeping the remaining failures available to the package workflow.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found two P2 issues: the package helper could pair a source plan and failures from another MVS load, and deduplication discarded distinct mismatch details for one package. The helper now accepts the load-bound audit plan and validates its token; exact duplicate diagnostics are removed while distinct details remain, with a focused regression for deterministic deduplication. The review also found that the remaining-scope note overstated pending contract failures; it now distinguishes non-identity failures. The driver regression switches a local path symlink after MVS selects `acme:target@1.0.0`, verifies a single diagnostic for repeated identical symptoms when the final source declares `acme:target@2.0.0`, and checks the requirement chain and absolute-path redaction. Final hostile re-review found only that this review record still said checks were pending; that note is corrected here, with no remaining actionable code findings. All 505 `sai_package` tests, 12 `sai_driver` tests, and the full workspace suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** These public package and driver diagnostic APIs, plus `MvsDuplicatePackageLineDiagnosticError::MixedSourceAuditLoadMismatch`, add a classified reporting boundary. Confirm that direct expected/found package/version mismatches are the only reload failures eligible for locations from the original MVS snapshot, distinct failures on one selected package remain separately visible, and other reload failures stay available through the audit plan until final-manifest snapshots and locations are added.
- **Remaining scope:** Add final-source snapshots and location binding for inner path/fetch/parse failures; report reloaded contract audit failures; integrate the collector into package commands; then run package/compiler conformance.

### IR-298: Report safe final-source path reload failures

- **Decision:** Extend the load-bound reload diagnostic adapter to classify local package-path lookup failures and local path-source conflicts, Git path discovery failures, and typed Git path fetch failures when a selected package node is known. Anchor them only to the original source selection or original MVS requirement chain, and construct details from fixed text or validated package/version fields. Map tag-only Git fetch errors from exact-commit path loads to `FETCH_FAILED`. Keep missing-base/index inconsistencies, conflicts where one directory is registered under multiple package versions, final-manifest parse/header/contract failures, and global reload failures on the audit plan.
- **Reason:** These failures can be attributed to a selected package without using a span from its changed final manifest. Original MVS manifests are already bound to the originating load and exact bytes, so their source-selection clause and requirement chain remain safe locations. Final-manifest spans and path text cannot be reused against that snapshot, and raw filesystem/Git error formatting can disclose host paths or remote metadata. Exact-commit path loads do not consult release tags, and a directory/version index conflict does not prove that any version requirement was violated.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found two P2 classification issues: Git sibling path fetches could inherit release-tag-only codes (`VERSION_NOT_FOUND`/`TAG_MOVED`) although they fetch an exact commit, and a directory registered under two package versions was mislabeled `PATH_VERSION_MISMATCH` without a requirement to check. Exact-commit path fetch diagnostics now map those release-tag-only errors to `FETCH_FAILED`; directory/version index conflicts remain unclassified on the audit plan. Final hostile re-review found no actionable findings. Regression tests cover local missing-path reporting/redaction, Git metadata redaction and exact-commit code mapping, and the unclassified directory/version conflict. All 509 `sai_package` tests, 13 `sai_driver` tests, and the full 1,412-test workspace suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that the selected package's original source-selection clause and MVS chain provide enough context for a path failure discovered in its changed final manifest, even though the newly introduced inner edge is not shown. Local path-source conflicts use `CONFLICTING_PATH_SOURCES`; one directory registered under multiple package versions remains unclassified because no requirement is available to validate. `MissingBaseDirectory` is treated as an internal index inconsistency rather than a user missing-path error. Final manifests with syntax, header, or contract errors remain unreported here because the audit plan does not retain a snapshot to bind their byte offsets.
- **Remaining scope:** Retain and bind final-manifest snapshots for parse/header/contract diagnostics; cover remaining reload failures and contract-audit failures; integrate all diagnostics into package commands; then run package/compiler conformance.

### IR-299: Bind local final path failures to their manifest edge

- **Decision:** Retain the exact bytes of a local selected package's final `sai.package` when path-requirement indexing fails. Add those bytes to a temporary augmented workspace snapshot under a deterministic synthetic dependency path and keep their locations in a separate map from read-time MVS manifests. Use a final-manifest path requirement as the primary location only after reparsing the captured bytes and verifying the exact span, package identity, and relative path from the reload error. Keep the original MVS requirement chain attached to its read-time manifest locations.
- **Reason:** The newly discovered inner path edge belongs to the changed final source and cannot be indexed into the original manifest snapshot. Capturing and rebinding the exact bytes allows the diagnostic to highlight the right clause while keeping the MVS explanation tied to the source that produced the selected graph. Separate location maps prevent final-source requirements from being mistaken for graph-selection provenance. Exact-edge validation prevents an error-provided span or path from being attached to unrelated final text.
- **Review:** The driver regression changes a local path after MVS, verifies that the primary span selects the full path requirement in the final manifest, checks that the original root requirement remains in the chain, confirms the failing relative path does not appear in the message, and retrieves the highlighted bytes through the snapshot returned with the diagnostics. A package regression verifies that changed package headers are accepted for final-source span binding and that mismatched paths or spans are rejected. The first hostile review found a P1: diagnostics referenced synthetic files absent from the caller's snapshot, and the API discarded the augmented snapshot. `MixedSourceReloadDiagnostics` now returns the snapshot with the diagnostics; a focused regression proves the final source and original chain files are both available. Final hostile re-review found no actionable issues. All 510 `sai_package` tests, 13 `sai_driver` tests, and the full 1,413-test workspace suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that synthetic paths of the form `dep:<package>@<version>/.sai/final-source-<ordinal>/sai.package` are suitable diagnostic-only identities for changed final sources. At this slice, only local `PathRequirementDiscovery` failures were bound; IR-300 and IR-301 extend that mechanism to Git path failures and package identity mismatches.
- **Remaining scope:** At this slice, bind Git path-discovery and other final-source failures to their exact source files, cover contract-audit failures, integrate diagnostics into package commands, and run package/compiler conformance. Later slices address the Git path and package identity cases; syntax, semantic contract, contract-audit, and workflow integration remain.

### IR-300: Bind Git final path discovery failures to their manifest edge

- **Decision:** Retain exact Git `sai.package` bytes when final path-requirement discovery fails, for both selected Git releases and reloaded Git path packages. Carry the originating requirement package, raw relative path, and parser byte span on Git path-resolution, sibling-fetch, source-identity, and sibling-package-identity errors. Bind classified failures to that edge only after reparsing the retained final manifest and validating the exact package, path, and span; keep MVS requirement-chain locations attached to the original read-time manifests.
- **Reason:** A final Git source can differ from the source used during MVS, and a path failure may be introduced only in that final manifest. The original snapshot cannot safely supply its byte range. Retaining the exact tree bytes and edge provenance lets the existing synthetic final-manifest snapshot logic report the right clause without trusting an error's unverified span or exposing repository metadata in the message.
- **Review:** Regressions cover both final Git path-pinned packages and final Git releases selected after MVS. They verify the exact retained manifest bytes, package/path edge, byte span, and `PATH_ESCAPES_REPOSITORY` classification; the path-pinned case also runs diagnostic generation and verifies the primary span against the exact final Git manifest bytes. Existing driver coverage verifies the augmented snapshot keeps original MVS manifests available while final-source diagnostics use a synthetic source location. Initial hostile review identified the public enum's source-compatibility break and requested diagnostic-location coverage; the break is documented as an intentional next API-boundary change, and diagnostic generation/source resolution are exercised. A second review requested coverage for the final Git release branch, which is now covered. Final hostile review found no actionable P0–P3 findings. All 512 `sai_package` tests and 1,415 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that showing a path requirement from the changed final Git manifest as the primary location, while showing the original MVS chain separately, gives package authors sufficient context. `GitPathRequirementDiscoveryError` is public and its affected variants now carry a `GitPathRequirementEdge`; this is an intentional source-compatibility break for the next API boundary (0.2.0 under the repository's release policy). Downstream exhaustive pattern matches must account for the changed public shape.
- **Remaining scope:** Bind remaining final syntax and semantic-contract failures plus contract-audit failures to exact source files; integrate these diagnostics into package commands; then run package/compiler conformance.

### IR-301: Bind final package identity mismatches to their changed header

- **Decision:** Retain exact final manifest bytes when local final-directory indexing or local/Git contract reload fails. Extract the final package header and its source span from the retained diagnostic snapshot, and use that span for a classified package identity/version mismatch only after its package and version match the error's `found` identity. Cover Git release mismatches detected during final Git path-requirement discovery as well as local index/contract and Git contract mismatch errors. Keep the original MVS requirement chain bound to the read-time manifests.
- **Reason:** A changed final source may declare a package identity or version different from the node selected by MVS. The read-time header describes that old node and cannot highlight the changed declaration. Capturing the exact final bytes and matching the parsed header against the typed `found` value prevents a stale or unrelated error identity from moving the diagnostic to the wrong text.
- **Review:** Regressions verify that local and Git final identity mismatches retain exact bytes, that local and Git diagnostics resolve their primary spans to the changed final header, and that final-header lookup rejects a mismatched identity. A Git release whose header changed is detected earlier by Git path-requirement discovery; the diagnostic adapter recognizes that typed `RequirerVersionMismatch` and binds it to the validated header. The first hostile review found a P2 duplicate: the Git path-discovery and contract-reload passes both recorded the same typed package identity mismatch. Diagnostic collection now collapses entries with the same selected package, code, and exact found identity while preserving every reload failure in the audit plan. The Git regression verifies that only one diagnostic is emitted and that its primary span selects the final header. Final hostile re-review found no actionable findings. All 513 `sai_package` tests and 1,416 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** The primary location uses the changed final package header, while the selection chain remains attached to the original manifests. The diagnostic-only synthetic file path is `dep:<package>@<version>/.sai/final-source-<ordinal>/sai.package`; confirm that retaining this stable, non-physical path in the returned snapshot is suitable for downstream diagnostic consumers. The public `MvsDuplicatePackageLineDiagnosticError` gains `FinalManifestIdentityMismatch`; adding this enum variant requires downstream exhaustive matches to update, so it is recorded as a source-compatibility change for the next API boundary (0.2.0 under the repository release policy).
- **Remaining scope:** Bind semantic contract and contract-audit failures to exact snapshots; integrate final-reload diagnostics into package commands; implement the concrete package workflow and conformance coverage. IR-302 binds final lexical and parser errors, including missing package declarations and malformed header syntax.

### IR-302: Bind final manifest syntax errors to retained source

- **Decision:** Parse every retained final manifest snapshot for the dependency role, preserve lexical or parser issues with their original byte spans, and emit them from the load-bound reload diagnostic adapter against the matching synthetic final-source file. Recover every malformed UTF-8 subsequence from VFS decode metadata because the package parser stops at the first one. Map package-contract error kinds to their registered diagnostic codes; the parser's `ExpectedToken("newline between declarations")` sentinel means a required separator is missing, so emit `EXPECTED_TOKEN` with `expected_tokens: ["NEWLINE"]`. Preserve all expected-token sets in diagnostic data. Emit at most 32 distinct syntax issues per final manifest and mark the final diagnostic when further issues were omitted, bounding repeated MVS chain data while retaining a clear truncation signal.
- **Reason:** Final-source changes can make a selected package manifest syntactically invalid even though the read-time manifest used for MVS was valid. The read-time bytes cannot safely supply the new syntax location. The audit plan already retains exact final bytes for classified reload failures, so reparsing those bytes and binding parser byte ranges to their synthetic snapshot avoids stale or guessed locations. Reusing package-contract codes follows `docs/package/01-package-contract.md` §1.21, while fixed messages avoid exposing host paths or source metadata. A per-manifest cap prevents millions of token errors from multiplying into millions of diagnostics that each repeat the dependency chain; the omission marker makes that bound visible.
- **Review:** Initial hostile review found a P1 diagnostic-amplification risk and three P2 correctness gaps: the newline sentinel was mapped to the wrong code, later malformed UTF-8 sequences were lost, and `EXPECTED_TOKEN` lacked its structured expected-token data. Follow-up reviews also found collapsed token alternatives and parser token-kind descriptions without structured sets. The adapter now caps each final manifest at 32 distinct issues and marks truncation, maps the missing separator to `EXPECTED_TOKEN`, reads all malformed byte ranges from VFS metadata, and maps every `ExpectedToken` description currently used by the manifest parser to its actual literal or token-kind alternatives. Regressions cover that mapping inventory, malformed byte ranges, independent reload failures, exact final-source locations, and the emitted cap/omission marker. Final hostile re-review found no remaining concrete findings. All 1,420 workspace tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that emitting lexical/parser errors from final manifest snapshots is the desired failure surface alongside any independently classified reload failure, and that capping output at 32 syntax issues per manifest with an explicit omission marker is a good usability and resource tradeoff. The parser's sentinel describes a missing newline, so it uses `EXPECTED_TOKEN`; the VFS provides original-byte spans for every malformed UTF-8 subsequence. `PackageParseErrorKind::ExpectedListSeparator` is public and exhaustive; carrying its expected-token set changes its variant shape and requires downstream exhaustive matches to update. Record this as an intentional source-compatibility break for the next public API boundary (0.2.0 under the repository's release policy).
- **Remaining scope:** Bind contract-audit failures to exact final-source snapshots; integrate final-reload diagnostics into package commands; implement the concrete package workflow and conformance coverage. IR-303 binds final package semantic validation errors.

### IR-303: Bind final package semantic validation errors to retained source

- **Decision:** Extract `PackageValidationError`s from local and Git final contract reload failures, map every `PackageValidationErrorKind` to its registered package-contract code, and bind each validated span to the corresponding retained final manifest snapshot. Keep the original MVS requirement chain on each diagnostic. Emit at most 32 semantic errors per final manifest and place an omission flag and note on the last emitted error.
- **Reason:** A final manifest can parse but violate package contract rules after MVS selected its version. Those failures are part of the final-source reload result, but the useful span is absent from the read-time manifest. The audit plan retains the exact bytes and typed error spans generated while validating those bytes, so the diagnostic adapter can report the failure without guessing a location or rescanning the source tree. Fixed messages provide context while the primary span highlights the relevant declaration and avoids copying unbounded manifest values into repeated diagnostics.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found three test gaps: the mapping assertion compared only the set of codes, the truncation assertion did not pin the omission marker to the final emitted diagnostic, and the Git final-source semantic-error branch lacked integration coverage. Tests now assert every validation-kind/code pair, require the marker only on item 32, and exercise a changed Git path source that independently produces a path failure and `UNKNOWN_TARGET` bound to the exact final manifest bytes. Final hostile re-review found no actionable issues. All 1,421 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that package semantic errors from a changed final dependency should be emitted alongside independently classified reload failures, and that a 32-error cap with an explicit omission marker is an appropriate output/resource bound. Messages use fixed descriptions while the primary source span carries the exact offending text. Root-only validation variants are mapped defensively but are not expected from dependency contract reloads.
- **Remaining scope:** Bind internal contract-audit failures to exact snapshots; integrate final-reload diagnostics into package commands; implement the concrete package workflow and conformance coverage. IR-304 and IR-305 handle the internal audit and final path-comparison diagnostics.

### IR-304: Report internal final contract audit failures

- **Decision:** Convert structural failures from the final contract audit and unexplained missing source identities for path comparisons into one `INTERNAL_COMPILER_ERROR` diagnostic. Suppress a missing-contract audit result only when a source-located reload or identity diagnostic is reportable for that package. Aggregate fixed failure categories, omit source identities and paths, include the Rust source location and deterministic build ID in the record, sort ordinary diagnostics by the standard diagnostic ordering, then append the one internal error.
- **Reason:** These failures mean the source-aware loader and audit disagree about validated internal state; they do not identify a package declaration that a user can repair. A reload or identity failure explains a missing audit input only when it has its own reportable diagnostic. Treating an unreported failure as an explanation could otherwise leave the user with no diagnostic. One aggregate internal-error record follows the toolchain's invariant-failure contract and prevents local paths or Git identity metadata from leaking. Although ordinary `primary: null` diagnostics sort first, §1.10.3 and §11.4.6 explicitly require the internal error to follow all diagnostics produced before the failure.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that unreportable reload/identity failures could suppress an internal error, the record lacked the build ID and Rust source location, globbing mishandled valid TOML and metacharacters in the checkout path, package sorting lacked workspace-member rank, the build ID omitted symlink targets and excluded members, Cargo was not watching glob parents for newly added workspace members, and an absent `library/` directory was not watched for later creation. These were addressed with reportability-gated suppression, source/build metadata, TOML parsing, escaped-root glob expansion, `workspace.exclude` filtering, symlink traversal with cycle detection and target watch paths, glob-parent directory watches, a watch on the optional library path, and membership derived from final local source identities (falling back to read-time identities when final identity is unavailable) under the canonical root directory. Cross-checking §1.10.3 and §11.4.6 caught an intermediate attempt to apply the ordinary locationless-first order to the ICE; the final code preserves the explicit ICE-last exception while testing locationless-first ordering among ordinary diagnostics. Tests also cover unexplained missing contracts, root/member/dependency and span ordering, glob expansion under a `/tmp/sai[glob]` root, a valid temporary workspace with exclusions, a symlink-target edit that reruns the build script and changes the build ID, a newly added package matching an unchanged workspace member glob, and creation of the optional library directory after the initial build. **Review required:** the final tiebreaker still uses Rust `Debug` because the shared canonical diagnostic-record serializer has not been implemented.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** The build ID is generated in `sai_package/build.rs` from the workspace version, `rustc -vV`, target, profile, the comma-separated `SAI_BUILD_FEATURES` override (or enabled `sai_package` features when absent), `Cargo.lock`, and files under the declared workspace members and optional `library/`. The current workspace has no distribution command or crate features. When `cargo xtask dist` is implemented, it must pass the exact release feature set through `SAI_BUILD_FEATURES`; centralize ID generation there if more than one compiler crate needs the value.
- **Remaining scope:** Integrate final-audit diagnostics into package commands; complete the concrete package workflow and conformance coverage.

### IR-305: Bind final-source path comparison failures to their source clauses

- **Decision:** Retain the exact manifest bytes for every successfully reloaded source, then report classified path-resolution failures from the `Actual` side of final contract comparisons. Bind a non-root-relative request to the unique matching package/path entry in its retained final manifest; bind a root-relative request to the unique matching root `use path` override. Revalidate the selected span against the same snapshot. Attribute each diagnostic and MVS chain to the dependency contract being compared, even when the primary location is a root override. Keep `Expected` failures and local filesystem failures without a safe diagnostic classification on the audit plan, and omit the failing relative path from messages and diagnostic data. Emit at most 32 path-resolution diagnostics per requiring manifest and mark the last emitted diagnostic when more failures were omitted.
- **Reason:** A final path lookup can fail after its contract loaded successfully, so the final manifest must remain available to identify its path clause. Root overrides supply path spellings relative to the root manifest, even when a dependency contract triggered the comparison. The request's source package and the compared dependency are therefore distinct for root-relative paths; the diagnostic location follows the source clause, while its package and selection chain explain which comparison failed. An expected-side failure belongs to the read-time source and cannot be attributed to the final manifest. Existing package diagnostic mappings safely classify missing local packages, oversized manifests, invalid paths, and Git repository escapes; lower-level I/O and directory-change failures have no matching package code. A per-manifest cap prevents a changed manifest with many failing path clauses from producing an unbounded number of diagnostics with repeated MVS chains.
- **Review:** A hostile review found that root-relative requests would be looked up in the final dependency manifest even though their path comes from a root override; location binding now uses the root manifest and checks its package, override target, path, and uniqueness. A second review found that the diagnostic package and MVS chain were taken from the root-relative path source rather than the dependency comparison; the adapter now preserves the compared package separately. Tests verify final-manifest and root-override spans, reject mismatched or ambiguous requests, bound failures independently per source manifest, and induce both Expected and Actual missing-path failures after source loading; only the Actual failure is reported, with its exact source clause, compared-package chain, and no relative path copied into diagnostic data. Local and Git successful-reload tests assert exact final bytes that differ from the read-time manifest; Git failure fixtures also verify retained bytes.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that classification remains limited to existing stable package errors and that unclassified filesystem failures stay on the audit plan. Review the 32-diagnostic per-manifest cap and explicit omission marker as a usability/resource tradeoff. An empty diagnostic vector must not be treated as audit success: callers must still require the final audit to be equivalent. Syntactically invalid dependency paths should be rejected during manifest validation before this comparison; their comparison-level mapping is defensive. The public `MvsDuplicatePackageLineDiagnosticError` gains `PathRequestLocationMismatch`; downstream exhaustive matches must account for it, so record this as a source-compatibility change for the next API boundary (0.2.0 under the repository release policy).
- **Remaining scope:** Integrate final-audit diagnostics into package commands; complete the concrete package workflow and conformance coverage; replace the provisional `Debug` diagnostic-record tiebreaker when the shared canonical serializer is implemented.

### IR-306: Reconstruct lock conflict sides as preserved bytes

- **Decision:** Add a lock-conflict parser that recognizes the same marker spellings as ordinary lock parsing and reconstructs the `ours` and `theirs` inputs byte-for-byte. Copy common lines to both outputs, select each branch's lines for its corresponding output, and omit diff3 base lines. Reject nested, unbalanced, or out-of-order markers with a typed error. Reuse the marker recognizer from `sai.lock` parsing so both paths agree on which lines are markers.
- **Reason:** `sai lock` must parse both reconstructed sides before combining floors and known content (package/03 §3.9.2), while every other command must reject a lock containing any marker (§3.9.1). A byte-preserving lower-level operation keeps line endings and spans intact and avoids embedding lock semantics in a future CLI layer. The resolver and CLI do not yet consume the returned sides.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that the multiple-conflict test fixture contained only one region. The fixture now has two distinct regions, checks the exact bytes of both reconstructed sides, and parses both sides. Follow-up reviews found that a repeated `=======` was mislabeled as a duplicate base and that the base-after-separator branch lacked a regression; both were fixed with distinct error kinds and tests. Final hostile review found no actionable findings. All 528 `sai_package` tests and 1,433 workspace tests pass; warning-denied `sai_package` Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the merge policy copies all text outside conflict regions to both sides, discards any diff3 base section, and treats an end marker before `=======` as corrupt. The implementation preserves the input bytes, including mixed line endings, rather than normalizing them before parsing.
- **Remaining scope:** Validate both sides, combine their lock floors and known content under §3.9.2, resolve the merged contract, atomically write the resulting lock, and integrate the workflow into `sai lock`.

### IR-307: Combine version floors from validated conflict sides

- **Decision:** Parse and run lock-local semantic validation on the reconstructed `ours` and `theirs` locks before merging floors. Keep both parsed locks in the output. For every package node not pinned by a path entry on either side or by an explicitly supplied current-root override pin, use the higher of the two side versions as its floor. Return side-tagged parse/validation failures and fail closed if a validated package identity cannot form an MVS node.
- **Reason:** Package/03 §3.9.2 requires Mode B floors to be the higher version from either side and excludes pinned nodes. Path entries encode path pins in the lock, while current root overrides come from the current manifest and cannot be safely inferred from stale lock edges. Lock-local validation is required before interpreting entries; graph consistency may be repaired by Mode B and is therefore not treated as lock corruption here.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover higher-version selection, path pins found on either side, explicit current-root override pins, and side-aware syntax and semantic errors on both sides. The first hostile review found the semantic-error branch lacked tests; regressions now require both sides to reject syntax-valid corrupt locks while retaining side identity and validation errors. Final hostile review found no further actionable issues. All 533 `sai_package` tests and 1,438 workspace tests pass; warning-denied `sai_package` Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that any path entry on either side suppresses the floor for its `(identity, compatibility line)`, even when the other side records a Git source, and that callers pass current root override pins separately. The returned floors do not establish Mode A usability or validate graph reachability; resolution performs those checks.
- **Remaining scope:** Reconcile known Git content and capability review baselines from both sides, combine duplicate entries from clean textual merges, resolve the current root contract in Mode B, atomically write the lock, and integrate the command workflow.

### IR-308: Reconcile known content and review baselines from lock sides

- **Decision:** For each Git entry, retain known content keyed by identity, compatibility line, exact version, and canonical source (`url` plus normalized repository directory). When both sides have the same version and source, require commit, source checksum, WIT checksum, and every artifact checksum to agree; otherwise return a typed conflict carrying both records. Preserve records for distinct sources separately. Build the previous capability footprint per node as the union of both sides' `capabilities` and all target `imports`, including empty footprints for existing path nodes.
