- **Reason:** Package/03 §3.9.2 requires known content to be reusable only when same-source records agree, and defines the approval baseline as the union of both sides' footprints and previously approved sources. Directory `"."` is accepted but denotes the same repository root as an omitted `dir`, so it is normalized for source comparison.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover conflicting commit/source/WIT/artifact checksums, identical-content reuse, distinct source retention, root-directory normalization, and capability union including artifact imports. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 538 `sai_package` tests and 1,443 workspace tests pass; warning-denied `sai_package` Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that commit and all three checksum classes are the complete same-source content identity for v1, and that source equality uses canonical Git URL plus repository directory. Different source identities are retained independently for the resolver to select and review.
- **Remaining scope:** Combine entries produced by clean textual merges, connect these baselines and floors to Mode B selection, atomically write the result, and expose the behavior through `sai lock`.

### IR-309: Split duplicate nodes from clean textual lock merges

- **Decision:** When a syntax-valid lock has exactly two entries for the same package identity and compatibility line, treat their file-order occurrences as the two sides of a merge: the first occurrence goes to `ours`, the second to `theirs`. Copy every non-duplicate entry to both reconstructed sides. Reject three or more occurrences as corrupt instead of guessing how to group them. If no duplicate node exists, return `None` after ordinary lock-local semantic validation.
- **Reason:** Package/03 §3.9.3 requires `sai lock` to treat duplicate entries as the sides used by §3.9.2, but does not define how to assign the otherwise indistinguishable serialized occurrences or what to do with more than two. A stable file-order rule makes diagnostics reproducible; rejecting larger groups avoids silently discarding or merging lock records. Keeping singleton records on both sides preserves the common lock baseline.
- **Review:** Focused regressions cover floor and footprint merging, same-source content disagreement, more than two entries, distinct compatibility lines, retaining singleton entries on both sides, exact file-order assignment, ordinary clean-merge errors without a fabricated side, and unsupported-format precedence. Hostile review first identified the unspecified `ours`/`theirs` assignment, so §3.9.3 now defines file order as the deterministic tie-break and says groups larger than two are corrupt. Follow-up review found that unsupported formats must take precedence over duplicate counts and syntax errors, and that ordinary clean-merge failures must not be labeled as `ours`; typed errors and a preflight of both marker sides now preserve those distinctions. Final hostile re-review found no actionable issues. All 546 `sai_package` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied `sai_package` Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm file order is an acceptable deterministic tie-breaker for the two duplicate records, and that groups larger than two should fail `LOCK_CORRUPT` rather than attempting a heuristic reconstruction.
- **Remaining scope:** Connect duplicate handling, floors, known content, and review baselines to Mode B selection; atomically write the resulting lock; and expose the workflow through `sai lock`.

### IR-310: Look up merged lock content by the selected Git source

- **Decision:** Expose a lookup keyed by exact package identity/version and the selected canonical Git URL plus normalized repository directory. Return no record for path sources, invalid source values, or a source not present in either merge side.
- **Reason:** A clean merge can contain the same exact package version from distinct sources. The resolver may select either source from the merged manifests, so a package-version-only lookup could reuse the other source's commit and checksums or discard reusable metadata.
- **Review:** A focused regression creates two known records for the same identity/version from separate Git URLs and verifies each lookup returns its own commit. It also verifies `dir "."` matches the repository root and a different directory does not match. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 547 `sai_package` tests pass; warning-denied `sai_package` Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that selected-source equality is canonical Git URL plus repository-relative package directory, and that an unmatched source must fall back to ordinary release-tag fetching in the eventual resolver integration.
- **Remaining scope:** Use source-aware records during Mode B fetch and final-source reloads, retain all side records through resolution, and complete lock writing and `sai lock` integration.

### IR-311: Resolve lock merges with source-aware MVS candidates

- **Decision:** Pass both lock sides' Git package records into Mode B selection and final-source auditing. For a floor-only version recorded under multiple Git sources, choose the first canonical `(URL, normalized directory)` key; a source named for the selected version still wins. Match a reusable package record by exact identity, version, URL, and directory. When the same identity/version/source has equal commit and checksums but different remaining lock metadata, keep the bytewise-smaller canonical package block.
- **Reason:** Package/03 §3.9.2 requires source clauses naming the selected version to take precedence over floor metadata, known content to be reused only from the selected source, and the merge result to be independent of side order. The selected graph may later acquire an edge naming a different source after the floor source has already been loaded, so final-source reloads need the full candidate catalog too. The remaining lock fields can describe different resolution histories even when the underlying Git content agrees; canonical block ordering makes this internal candidate value deterministic without treating those fields as content checksums.
- **Review:** Initial hostile review found that same-source records with different `requires` metadata retained whichever side was visited first, and that no integration test drove a floor-only read through a later named-source change and final audit reload. The catalog now selects by canonical serialized package bytes, and regressions check side-order independence plus a lock-floor read from source A followed by a final-source reload from source B using B's exact lock record. Final hostile re-review found no actionable issues. All 551 `sai_package` tests and the full workspace suite pass; warning-denied `sai_package` Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm bytewise canonical package-block ordering as the tie-break when validated same-source lock records agree on commit and checksums but differ in other fields. This picks one side's non-content metadata for the exposed candidate record; actual MVS and final-contract comparison continue to use the package manifests.
- **Remaining scope:** Finish lock-merge approval handling and connect conflict-marker and clean-merge resolution to `sai lock`.

### IR-312: Write canonical lock files atomically

- **Decision:** Add a package API that validates and canonically serializes a parsed lock, skips an exact byte-for-byte match, and otherwise replaces a regular `sai.lock` through a temporary file in the same directory with 16 random bytes encoded as 32 lowercase hexadecimal characters. Flush the temporary file before replacement, sync the parent directory where supported, create temporary files with mode `0600` on Unix, and remove stale regular temporary files only when their names match that generated pattern. Require callers to hold the package directory's exclusive `.sai/lock` for the entire operation.
- **Reason:** Package/03 §3.7.2 requires canonical output, complete replacement rather than partial writes, durable file contents, and cleanup of abandoned temporary files. Same-directory replacement keeps the old or new lock visible across interruption. The workspace lock is a caller responsibility because this package crate has no command/session abstraction that can acquire it.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that an unchanged lock skipped stale-file cleanup, a symlink swap could make the byte comparison follow a link, a FIFO destination could block before regular-file validation, cleanup could delete similarly prefixed user files, Windows directory handles needed backup semantics, existing-file comparison allocated without a bound, the FIFO regression could hang indefinitely, Windows paths were not checked for embedded NULs, post-replacement directory-sync errors did not say that the new lock was already installed, and the architecture dependency/unsafe-boundary inventory was incomplete. Cleanup now precedes the unchanged fast path and only removes regular files with the exact generated suffix pattern; existing bytes are compared through a no-follow, nonblocking regular-file handle on Unix and a reparse-point-aware handle on Windows, with file size checked first and contents compared in a fixed-size buffer; the Windows directory handle includes backup semantics and its replacement wrapper rejects NUL; and the FIFO regression runs in a bounded subprocess. The writer has a distinct installed-but-directory-sync-failed error. API preconditions and architecture dependency/unsafe-boundary documentation were also clarified. Final hostile re-review found no actionable P0–P3 issues. All 558 `sai_package` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied package Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. The `sai_windows_fs` Windows GNU target check passes. The whole-package Windows target check was attempted but blocked because this host lacks the `x86_64-w64-mingw32-gcc` compiler required by `aws-lc-sys`.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the writer's precondition that the caller holds `.sai/lock`, that stale regular files matching the writer's exact `.sai.lock.<32 lowercase hex characters>` pattern may be removed while that lock is held, and that Unix mode `0600` plus directory syncing where supported meet the lock-file durability contract. The public error enum also distinguishes failure to sync the directory after replacement, which means the canonical lock is installed but crash durability is uncertain; callers must surface that state accurately.
- **Remaining scope:** Build lock records from the resolver's final graph and content metadata; integrate the guarded writer and workspace lock across the full `sai lock` command; expose conflict resolution through that command.

### IR-313: Build a lock AST from the final resolved graph

- **Decision:** Add a crate-private builder that combines the root contract, selected MVS graph, dependency contracts, and content-derived package fields into `LockParseOutput`. Derive all lock `requires` fields from the resolver contracts and selected graph rather than accepting them from content metadata. Require exact package-version sets for contracts and metadata, a shared root resolver context and target set, a one-to-one match between included requirements and graph edges, and exact selected versions for direct path pins and root path overrides. Run lock-local and lock-graph validation before returning the AST. Defer canonical serialization until the caller has checked graph-dependent capability names.
- **Reason:** Package/02 §§2.5, 2.7, 2.10 and Package/03 §§3.2–3.7 require the lock to capture the included edges and final selected versions, preserve path pins, and pass lock validation before writing. Root targets must remain bound to the resolver context so target-filtered edges are neither omitted nor added incorrectly. Capability metadata is normalized during serialization, so graph-dependent capability checks must run first. No production caller yet binds content metadata to completed source selection, final-source audit, and verified fetches; keeping the assembly boundary crate-private prevents callers outside the package crate from supplying arbitrary source and checksum fields before that provenance exists.
- **Review:** The initial hostile review found that a public builder could accept source and checksum metadata without provenance; the API and metadata type were made crate-private. A later review showed crate-private visibility alone still let any package module construct that metadata, so its fields are now private and it has no public constructor; future metadata production must be added inside the audited assembly boundary. Review also required binding contracts to the same root targets/context and matching every requirement to exactly one graph edge. A further pass found that a replaced public MVS root could be paired with a still-bound contract, and that mutable requirement metadata could downgrade a path pin to an ordinary minimum. The contract now retains the validated manifest identity/version separately, its requirement list is private, and MVS metadata validation compares both path-pin flags against each retained resolver pin; the builder continues to enforce exact versions for direct path pins and root path overrides. Eleven focused tests cover canonical lock construction, target filtering and test edges, missing metadata, forged requirements, graph mismatches, both pin forms, pin metadata tampering, cross-root contexts, a replaced root package, dropped root-cycle requirements, and lock floors above the requirement minimum. Final hostile review found no P0–P3 findings. All 569 `sai_package` tests and 1,474 workspace tests pass; warning-denied package Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the builder should remain crate-private until its caller can bind each metadata record to audited final-source identity and verified content. Confirm graph-dependent capability validation belongs before canonical serialization, which may remove capabilities covered by another name.
- **Remaining scope:** Connect audited source/content records to the builder, run capability escalation checks before canonical serialization, integrate workspace lock lifetimes into commands and the LSP, and integrate resolution and lock writing into `sai lock`.

### IR-314: Acquire shared and exclusive workspace locks

- **Decision:** Add a persistent empty `.sai/lock` with shared/exclusive OS locking and RAII release. Canonicalize the package directory once, reject non-directory roots and unsafe `.sai`/lock entries, open the lock relative to the verified `.sai` handle on Unix, and retain no-delete-share directory handles on Windows so the package root and `.sai` cannot be renamed while a guard is held. A waiting callback runs once before blocking. Participating processes must not remove or replace `.sai/lock` while a guard may exist; arbitrary external filesystem mutation is outside the advisory lock contract.
- **Reason:** `docs/impl/13-cli.md` §13.5.4 requires readers to share the lock, writers to hold it exclusively, and waiters to report once before blocking. A persistent empty lock path ensures cooperating processes open the same object. Descriptor-relative Unix opening and retained Windows directory handles prevent path redirection during normal acquisition. Advisory locks cannot stop an unrelated process from unlinking and recreating the Unix lock path, so the contract is scoped to participating toolchain processes and the file remains persistent.
- **Review:** Hostile review found a package-directory alias race on Windows and the Unix lock-file replacement limitation. The Windows path now pins the canonical package root before preparing `.sai`, while retaining the `.sai` handle and opening the lock without delete sharing. The Unix replacement limitation is explicitly documented as outside the cooperating-process contract. A fresh hostile review found no actionable P0–P3 issues. Thirteen focused lock tests and all 1,487 workspace tests pass; warning-denied `sai_package` Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. The `sai_windows_fs` Windows GNU target check passes. A whole-package Windows target check is blocked by the host's missing `x86_64-w64-mingw32-gcc`, required by `aws-lc-sys`.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that canonical package paths are the public `WorkspaceLockGuard::path()` representation, that Windows directory handles may prevent package moves until commands finish, and that arbitrary external mutation of `.sai/lock` is outside the lock guarantee. Toolchain commands must preserve the lock entry for the whole guard lifetime.
- **Remaining scope:** Acquire shared locks for read-only commands and the LSP lifetime, hold exclusive locks across complete write commands, connect the audited package graph to the guarded writer in `sai lock`, and implement global package-cache locking.

### IR-315: Bind atomic lock writes to an exclusive workspace guard

- **Decision:** Add a writer that accepts an already-held `WorkspaceLockGuard`, rejects shared guards, and writes `sai.lock` under the guard's canonical package directory. Keep the path-based atomic writer crate-private so external package callers cannot bypass the exclusive-lock precondition.
- **Reason:** `docs/impl/13-cli.md` §13.5.4 requires a write command to hold one exclusive guard for its full duration. Having the writer acquire a second guard risks self-deadlock and would not cover the command's earlier workspace reads. Deriving the destination from the guard keeps the lock and file path bound to the same canonical workspace.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that a public path-based writer could bypass the lock precondition and that the new error summary incorrectly mentioned lock acquisition. The path-based helper is now crate-private and the error description matches its guarded-write behavior. Regressions cover exclusive success, shared-lock rejection, guard retention after serialization failure, and a package-directory symlink alias. Final hostile review found no actionable P0–P3 issues. All 586 `sai_package` tests and 1,491 workspace tests pass; warning-denied package Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm command callers should acquire the exclusive guard before reading workspace inputs, retain it for the full command, and use this guarded writer only after graph-dependent capability validation.
- **Remaining scope:** Add `sai_cli` and wire the complete `sai lock` lifecycle to this API; produce metadata from audited source contents and capability analysis; integrate lock lifetimes into other commands and the LSP.

### IR-316: Complete asynchronous Windows workspace-lock operations

- **Decision:** Give each Windows range-lock and unlock request its own manual-reset event. When `LockFileEx` or `UnlockFileEx` returns `ERROR_IO_PENDING`, wait with `GetOverlappedResult` before releasing the stack `OVERLAPPED` or event. Set the low bit on `OVERLAPPED.hEvent` to suppress IOCP packets that would otherwise retain the stack pointer. For a defensive pending result from a nonblocking lock, cancel and drain it before returning; report `WouldBlock` when it was canceled or rejected.
- **Reason:** `File` can be opened with `FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED` and associated with an I/O completion port. Returning before the operation completes can leave the kernel using a dead stack pointer; waiting only on an event can also leave a completion-port packet holding that pointer. Per-request events keep concurrent operations independent, while the low-bit convention prevents an undrained packet from escaping the wrapper.
- **Review:** Hostile reviews found the pending-operation lifetime risk, a race in the first async test, an IOCP packet lifetime risk, and an incorrect completion-port creation sentinel in the regression. The wrappers now drain pending requests, the test synchronizes on the actual `ERROR_IO_PENDING` result, event low bits suppress packets, and the test creates and associates an IOCP with a null existing-port argument. Final hostile review found no actionable P0–P3 issues. The `sai_windows_fs` Windows GNU all-target check and warning-denied Clippy pass; the workspace's 1,491 tests pass on this macOS host. Windows-only tests compile but could not be executed here.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the cancellation outcome mapping and the per-request event/IOCP suppression behavior. Run the Windows-only filesystem tests on native Windows CI before relying on their runtime coverage.
- **Remaining scope:** Run Windows-only tests on native Windows CI; continue integrating the workspace guard across the remaining CLI commands and LSP.

### IR-317: Initialize a missing lock for a dependency-free root

- **Decision:** Expose a narrow `create_root_only_lock_if_missing` package API for a validated root contract with no active resolver edges. While the caller's exclusive workspace guard is held, read `sai.package` without following links, enforce the 16 MiB manifest limit, and compare its SHA-256 digest with the resolver contract before building the ordinary root-only lock AST through the existing selected-graph validation path. Create canonical bytes under that same guard and reject any existing `sai.lock` directory entry instead of replacing it. Keep the general final-graph builder and content metadata construction crate-private until audited source and checksum provenance can be supplied.
- **Reason:** Package/03 §3.7.1 rule 2 requires a package with no dependencies to receive a lock containing the header and root block. A no-dependency case is the smallest useful first integration boundary for the lock builder; it does not invent content metadata or skip final-source auditing for dependencies. `--locked`/`--frozen`, approval summaries, lock reconciliation, and existing-lock validation remain command-level lifecycle responsibilities.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found a P2 cross-workspace contract mix-up: the initial helper could write a valid but unrelated package's root metadata. The helper now binds the contract to the guarded workspace by comparing the bounded, no-follow manifest read's digest and rejects shared guards before reading; regressions cover a mismatched workspace contract, a manifest symlink, an oversized manifest, and a shared guard. Final hostile review found no actionable P0–P3 findings. All 596 `sai_package` tests and 1,501 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. The external-filesystem-mutation race remains outside the documented cooperating-process lock contract.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the exported root-only helper's caller contract: the root contract must come from the same package manifest read while the supplied exclusive guard is held. Confirm that refusing every existing directory entry, including malformed locks and symlinks, is the right behavior for this creation-only primitive; command-level `sai lock` must separately parse, reconcile, and update existing locks. The new `WorkspaceLockWriteError::DestinationAlreadyExists` variant and public root-lock error surface should be included in the next public API boundary (0.2.0 under the repository's release policy).
- **Remaining scope:** Add CLI package discovery and the `sai lock` lifecycle, including `--locked`/`--frozen`, lock validation and Mode A/Mode B resolution, content metadata from audited sources, capability review and approval handling. Integrate workspace-lock lifetimes into the other commands and LSP.

### IR-318: Implement CLI root-package discovery

- **Decision:** Add the initial `sai_cli` crate and a deterministic package-discovery API. Resolve relative start paths against an explicit current directory, canonicalize the start path (following start-directory symlinks), and search that directory and its ancestors for the nearest `sai.package` whose metadata identifies a regular file. Ignore a `sai.package` directory and broken symlink; treat a symlink to a regular file as a regular manifest. Return the canonical package directory and distinguish missing packages from filesystem and start-path errors.
- **Reason:** `docs/impl/13-cli.md` §13.2.1 defines the ancestor walk and start-directory symlink behavior. Taking the current directory explicitly makes relative-path semantics deterministic in tests and keeps package lookup independent of the eventual process entry point. Separating `NoPackageFound` lets the command layer map it to `NO_PACKAGE_FOUND` with the correct CLI exit code.
- **Review:** Seven focused discovery tests pass for nearest-package selection, current-directory resolution, directories and broken manifest links, start-directory symlinks, no-package results, and non-directory starts. Hostile review found no actionable P0–P3 findings. All 1,508 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that a `sai.package` symlink whose target is a regular file qualifies as a regular manifest. The specification explicitly says to follow start-directory symlinks and ignore broken manifest links, but does not separately state the treatment of a valid manifest symlink. This crate currently provides the discovery library only; the `sai` binary, argument parser, `NO_PACKAGE_FOUND` diagnostic mapping, and command dispatch remain to be integrated.
- **Remaining scope:** Complete command-line parsing and the `sai` binary entry point; map package discovery failures to structured usage diagnostics; implement help/version, package commands, and stage-aware command dispatch.

### IR-319: Parse shared CLI options

- **Decision:** Add a shared parser that recognizes global options before and after the command, preserves the original post-command token order, and marks the positions occupied by parsed global options and their separate values. This lets later command-specific parsing validate option-value adjacency before skipping globals. Preserve path values as `OsString`, including non-UTF-8 paths. A value option does not consume `--` or another recognized global option; that next global option is parsed and the preceding value option reports a missing value. Accept `-C <dir>` and `--directory=<dir>`, but reject the undocumented `-C=<dir>` form. The separator is valid only after a command; a commandless invocation reports a missing required command, except for top-level help and version requests.
- **Reason:** `docs/impl/13-cli.md` §13.3.1 requires left-to-right parsing, global options on either side of the command, exact enumeration values, repeatable boolean options, conflicts for repeated value options, and passthrough after `--`. Preserving the original token sequence prevents a global option from shifting a command-specific option's value. Parsing a recognized option that follows a missing value ensures subsequent conflicts are still diagnosed. §13.3.1 defines `=<value>` only for long options, and §13.3.5 requires diagnostics to identify the actual offending argument token and report missing required positional arguments.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found four actionable issues: stripping globals could shift a command-specific value, a value option could consume `--`, commandless `--` handling contradicted the documented syntax, and inline-value diagnostics reported only the value while pointing at the full option token. A second review confirmed those fixes and found two more: a known global option used as a preceding value hid later option conflicts, and `-C=<dir>` accepted an undocumented form. The parser now preserves raw order with global-token indices, treats `--` and following recognized globals as missing-value boundaries, requires the command before the separator, reports the full offending inline token, and rejects short-option `=` syntax. Final hostile review confirmed all six fixes and found no further actionable P0–P3 issues. All 21 focused parser tests, 28 `sai_cli` tests, and 1,529 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, `git diff --check`, and Windows GNU all-target CLI checks pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that `--` before a command is a missing-command usage error, consistent with the documented `sai ... <command> ... [-- PASSTHROUGH]` syntax, and that top-level help/version still work without a command. Confirm that a recognized global option token cannot serve as a separate path value (the path can be supplied with `--directory=<dir>`). The CLI binary, subcommand parsing, diagnostic-code mapping, help/version output, and exit-code handling are not yet connected to this parser.
- **Remaining scope:** Add command/subcommand validation and the `sai` binary; map parser errors to `UNKNOWN_OPTION`, `MISSING_ARGUMENT`, `INVALID_OPTION_VALUE`, and `CONFLICTING_OPTIONS`; implement command dispatch, help/version, and output/exit-code behavior.

### IR-320: Render top-level help and version text

- **Decision:** Add a help-only ordered command display catalog matching `docs/impl/13-cli.md` §13.1.2, plus deterministic top-level usage and version renderers. The verbose version reports the reference implementation's supported targets only, filtered into canonical target order: `wasm32-core wasm64-core wasm32-wasi`. Keep compound command spellings in a `display_name` field so they are not mistaken for dispatch keys.
- **Reason:** CLI §13.3.7 specifies the command-list order, the version fields, and the verbose lines. `docs/targets/targets-and-profiles.md` §2.2 requires printed targets to use canonical order and include only targets the toolchain implements. `docs/impl/15-conformance-and-testing.md` §15.9.5 lists the reference implementation's targets and excludes `wasm64-wasi`.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that the initial target list included unsupported `wasm64-wasi`; this was removed and the command-name field was clarified as display-only. A follow-up found the CLI §13.3.7 example still showed that unsupported target, so the example now matches the reference implementation's actual target set. Final hostile review confirmed the command descriptions/order and version/language/WASI line order with no code findings. All 31 `sai_cli` tests and 1,532 workspace tests passed at that earlier checkpoint; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, `git diff --check`, and Windows GNU all-target CLI checks also passed at that time. The executable is now wired with help/version handling and a `new` command.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that `sai --version --verbose` reports the current reference implementation target set in canonical order and that the §13.3.7 example reflects that implementation. Confirm that display strings such as `component build` and `bindgen wit` remain separate from future typed dispatch metadata. The `-C, --directory` option is accepted globally, but has no effect for `sai new` because that command neither discovers a package nor resolves its destination relative to a package root; command help now says so explicitly.
- **Remaining scope:** Render each command's complete option table, validate command-specific arguments before staged-feature diagnostics, implement SIGINT cleanup/reporting, and move package creation behind the driver/package API.

### IR-321: Create packages without discovering an enclosing workspace

- **Decision:** Treat `sai new` as the one write command that does not acquire `.sai/lock`. It validates that the destination is new or empty, creates a randomly named staging directory, publishes that directory as `src` with an atomic no-replace rename, and then publishes the three documented files atomically, with `sai.package` last. This avoids opening or writing into a competing `src` directory. Rollback removes a published file or `src` directory only after its file identity matches the entry created by this invocation; it leaves the destination root directory in place because create-then-open identity capture cannot prove ownership against a racing empty replacement. JSON `files[].path` values are package-relative; `directory` echoes the normalized argument as required by §13.6.1, including an explicitly supplied absolute path.
- **Reason:** §13.6.1 says `sai new` does not search for or modify an enclosing package, writes exactly three files, and accepts an explicit destination. Acquiring the enclosing package lock would violate that discovery boundary; creating a `.sai/lock` in the new package would violate the exact-file contract. Publishing the manifest last prevents package discovery from seeing a partially populated package. Package-relative file paths satisfy the file-record path rule in §13.6 and §11.5.4 while keeping absolute destination handling useful.
- **Review:** The file publisher uses descriptor-relative `RENAME_NOREPLACE` on Linux, Android, macOS, iOS, and Redox, and a Windows move that refuses an existing destination. Staging emptiness is checked through a no-follow directory handle that denies delete sharing; that handle is closed before a separately verified renameable handle is opened only for publication. After publication, the directory is reopened through a no-follow, delete-denying handle before writes begin. Root-path identity is checked before manifest publication and again before success; `src` is checked at both points as well. Unix path identity reads use descriptor-relative `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`; the symlink-to-original substitution regression verifies that such a link is not published. Temporary-file identity cleanup is armed before writing and flushing, so ordinary write failures do not leave partial staging files. Targets without atomic no-replace directory moves use same-directory hard-link publication for files, which provides complete-file visibility and refuses replacement but depends on hard-link support; directory publication returns `Unsupported` on those targets. Regressions verify that competing entries are preserved, staging and temporary-file replacements are not adopted or deleted, and root/source replacement is detected. Rollback no longer removes the destination directory: after a create-then-open race, the command cannot prove that the opened directory is the one it created, so deleting it could erase a competing empty directory. A failed command can therefore leave an empty destination. A failure before identity capture for a newly created staging file or directory may still leave its random hidden entry. Arbitrary same-user mutation can still race path-based rename/unlink checks; Windows path identity also retains a narrow metadata/open race. Rollback remains best effort. Repeated SIGINT requests cancellation rather than forcing process exit. Final hostile review found no new P0–P2 issue and confirmed the normal partial-write cleanup; it classifies the remaining same-user/shared-directory races and rare identity-capture residue as limitations. The `sai_windows_fs` GNU target check, 27 focused package-creation tests, 81 CLI tests plus one binary integration test, warning-denied workspace Clippy, and the serial full workspace test run pass. Formatting and `git diff --check` pass. A `sai_package` Windows cross-check is blocked before project code by the missing `x86_64-w64-mingw32-gcc` required by `aws-lc-sys`.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that `sai new` should not synchronize with commands running in a containing package when its destination lies inside that package's source tree; confirm last-manifest publication and best-effort rollback are sufficient for the exact-three-files contract. Confirm that echoing an explicitly supplied absolute path in JSON `directory` is acceptable even though generated file paths remain package-relative. Confirm that hard-link file publication is acceptable where atomic no-replace rename is unavailable, and that directory creation may fail on those same less common targets rather than publish an unsafe path. Confirm that preserving an empty destination after failure is preferable to risking deletion of a competing directory in the create-to-open race, and that a rare identity-capture failure may leave a random hidden staging entry. Confirm that the remaining same-user mutation boundary is acceptable.
- **Remaining scope:** Complete final hostile review, rerun the full workspace gates after the latest race fixes, verify JSON and human output against §13.6.1, then continue with Stage 0 commands.

### IR-322: Aggregate CLI usage errors and honor command option semantics

- **Decision:** For known commands that are not yet implemented, collect shared/global parsing errors and command-specific validation errors before reporting `FEATURE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`. Interpret `add --version <requirement>` as the command option after `add`, even though `--version` is also a global display flag. Accept identity-based `add` only for the nine shipped standard package identities from D-PKG-04; classify insecure, unsupported, and credential-bearing Git URL arguments with their dedicated diagnostics and redact user information, query strings, and fragments in rejected URL arguments. Reject `fmt --stdin-kind` without `-`, `add --path` with `--version` or `--dir`, repeated or malformed `add` selectors, invalid `add` requirements, and malformed, non-UTF-8, or repeated `update` selectors. Preserve original argv locations for conflicts involving inline option values. After package creation returns, give an observed cancellation priority over a success or filesystem error.
- **Reason:** CLI §13.3.5 requires all usage errors on one command line to be reported together and assigns exit code 130 priority over other outcomes (§13.3.4). The synopsis and option table in §13.6.9 define `add --version` as a version requirement; command-specific interpretation resolves its spelling collision with the global flag. Sections §13.6.7, §13.6.9, and §13.6.11 define the formatter, path-add, and duplicate-update conflicts.
- **Review:** Regressions cover simultaneous global and command errors, interruption racing with another operation result, the `add --version` parser precedence, formatter/add conflicts including inline argument locations, invalid add requirements and sources, and malformed, non-UTF-8, or repeated `update` selectors. Hostile review found that any syntactically valid identity was accepted for `add`, URL error classes were collapsed, and non-UTF-8 `update` selectors were skipped. Those cases now receive the specified standard-package check, dedicated URL diagnostics, and invalid-value reporting. Rejected URLs redact user information, query strings, and fragments in source-validation errors and in later `add` conflicts; scp-like help preserves the SSH user name. A Unix regression confirms a non-UTF-8 `add` source is rejected. The review also found that `src` could be replaced before its first open and then adopted. Creation now captures and rechecks the random staging directory identity, rejects non-empty staging directories, and publishes the opened directory as `src` using a no-replace rename. Regressions check that staging and temporary-file replacements are not adopted or removed, root replacement is preserved during rollback, and persistent `src` replacement is detected after manifest publication. A follow-up review found that partial writes could leave a temp file; identity-guarded cleanup is now active before writing or flushing. Final hostile review confirmed those fixes and found only the documented same-user path races and rare identity-capture cleanup gap. Focused CLI/package tests, warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, `git diff --check`, and the serial full workspace test run pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the exception that `--version` immediately following `add` always means the dependency requirement, while the same spelling before the command requests toolchain version output. This follows the more specific `sai add` synopsis and resolves an ambiguity in the general global-option placement rule.
- **Remaining scope:** The current parser still does not exhaustively validate every documented option combination and value for every not-yet-implemented command. Implement the Stage 0 `check`, `fmt`, and `explain` workflows and expand command validation as those workflows are wired.

### IR-323: Generate the diagnostic list from Appendix A

- **Decision:** Generate the `sai explain --list` snapshot from the diagnostic rows in `docs/spec/appendix-a-diagnostic-codes.md` with `tools/update_summary_snapshot.py`; compile the checked-in snapshot so packaged builds do not need the workspace docs. Derive `hard` versus `lint` from the row's severity marker and preserve each row's Appendix declaration ordinal. Remove inline Markdown code delimiters, replace links with their labels, remove surrounding whitespace and one final period; when a summary exceeds 80 Unicode scalar values, shorten it at the last whitespace before the limit and append an ellipsis. Sort list entries by diagnostic-code bytes, and break candidate-distance ties by the preserved declaration ordinal. A workspace test compares all snapshot rows to Appendix A, including raw summaries. Unknown `sai explain <CODE>` arguments use the registry's Damerau–Levenshtein candidate rule; known codes continue to report the documented Stage 0 feature as not implemented until complete explanation records are authored.
- **Reason:** Appendix A is the checked-in source of truth for diagnostic names, classes, and descriptions, while the current lint registry already carries default levels separately. The generated snapshot keeps builds independent of repository docs, and the workspace test exposes drift between the snapshot and Appendix A. A distinct list path makes useful Stage 0 registry information available without fabricating the complete per-code examples required by §11.13.2. The Appendix contains summaries longer than the registry's 80-character limit, so the list view shortens them deterministically and keeps each row on one line.
- **Review:** Verify the snapshot generator/parser against all Appendix A sections, including cells containing `|` in summaries; keep the generated list byte-sorted even though `Code::ALL` preserves legacy enum order; test all 493 names, raw summary, class, and declaration-order agreement, JSON escaping, candidate order, non-UTF-8 arguments, and output failures. A hostile review found that a global-option value equal to `--list` could be mistaken for the command flag; the dispatch now excludes every token consumed by global option parsing, with a regression test. Consider replacing derived/truncated summaries with a curated registry field and reconciling the Appendix summaries with §11.2.4.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Decide whether list summaries should remain derived and truncated from Appendix A or whether the normative source should be edited to provide a separate, concise summary for every code. Also confirm that `sai explain <CODE>` may remain unavailable while `sai explain --list` is implemented.
- **Remaining scope:** Add the complete metadata fields and Markdown records for every code, including compilable examples and fixes where appropriate, then remove the individual-code feature gate.

### IR-324: Build the formatter document printer before language-specific layout

- **Decision:** Add the `sai_fmt` crate and implement the shared document model and printer from formatter §14.3: scalar-value widths, four-space lazy indentation, independent group fitting, forced-group propagation, conditional branches, deferred line suffixes, exact verbatim text, final-newline handling, and speculative `Choice` selection by first-line fit. Memoize equivalent concatenation expansions and group-fit results using persistent stack identities. Represent observed group modes in a canonical 32-bit persistent trie, and skip fitting a trivially empty group when no `IfBroken` observes its ID. Keep the CLI formatter unavailable until package/source builders, verification, and file-operation rules are implemented.
- **Reason:** The canonical formatter requires a stable layout engine before syntax-specific document builders can be reviewed. Choice decisions are cached by document-node identity after speculative evaluation so nested alternatives are not re-evaluated exponentially when the selected document is printed. Canonical mode-state identity merges equivalent choice paths regardless of assignment order, while observing only modes read by `IfBroken` avoids unnecessary state growth and empty-group lookahead.
- **Review:** Hostile reviews found and fixed several correctness and complexity defects: later groups were initially measured flat instead of choosing their own layout; nested line suffixes could be deferred past the same break; embedded verbatim breaks did not flush suffixes; a final verbatim newline incorrectly scheduled indentation for following docs; `fits` ignored a rest `Choice` whose active alternative starts with a break or an unforced group that breaks at its start, including a nested group; `Flat` alternatives accepted direct hard lines and multiline verbatim; and choice-end lookahead omitted pending line suffixes, including grouped and nested suffixes flushed before a rest `Choice` break. Follow-up reviews found and fixed repeated empty-group suffix fitting, history-based group-mode identities that failed to merge reverse assignment orders, quadratic negative-lookup storage, and remaining fit launches at the renderer and leading-probe entry points. The final hostile review found no remaining actionable P0–P2 findings. Regressions cover line-width boundaries, suffix ordering, nested and forced groups, leading breaks, active `IfBroken` selection, choice fallbacks, 1,500 nested choices, reversed group-mode assignment order, linear trie storage, 400 empty sibling groups with an exact fit-call count, and the leading-probe fast path. All 65 formatter tests and all 1,685 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the formatter’s specified first-line Choice preference and the choice-node selection cache as acceptable implementation decisions. Confirm that the canonical group-mode trie and the empty-group fast path are semantically transparent: the fast path applies only when no `IfBroken` refers to that group ID. Confirm that process-unique persistent-stack IDs are an acceptable cache identity. The `sai fmt` command remains unavailable until both document builders, semantic verification, and safe file updates are complete.
- **Remaining scope:** Complete hostile review and §14.3.4 edge-case verification, add package/source document builders and semantic verification, then wire `sai fmt` with the documented refusal and file-update behavior.

### IR-325: Preserve package syntax and trivia at the formatter boundary

- **Decision:** Keep the original input byte slice beside the package parse output and a formatter-local token stream. Do not retain tokens on every `PackageParseOutput`: the long-lived package graph keeps its current memory and equality behavior. The formatter performs one additional linear token-only lexing pass and reuses the parser-owned trivia instead of retaining a duplicate trivia stream. Attach each comment to the nearest significant token as leading or trailing according to whether it starts on that token's line; retain its original trivia span, physical line, and line-break gap. Compute attachments with one ordered token/trivia sweep, and skip the formatter lexing pass when the parser's 16 MiB manifest limit is exceeded. Classify lexical and grammar errors as refusals, but keep structural errors and every later validation non-refusing, including invalid version requirements such as `>` and a bare version, while the original token stream remains available for future rendering.
- **Reason:** `PackageFile` is a semantic projection: it drops repeated package headers and some invalid values, and it has no token-leading/trailing trivia relation. Formatting from it alone can silently omit source. Original byte spans and the formatter-local token stream provide the basis to preserve such inputs without changing the memory cost for package resolution and validation. The fixed package manifest limit is enforced before lexing, as required by package/04 §4.4.4 and the general formatter parser-limit rule in §14.2.3.
- **Review:** Hostile review first caught an overly broad AST-only design; the adapter now keeps source bytes and token spans alongside the parser's trivia. Further review found and fixed a quadratic comment/token search and clarified that unsupported `>` and bare version requirements are validation errors, so they must remain non-refusing under §14.12.1. The formatter-specific lexer path now avoids allocating a second trivia stream while preserving token and error output. Attachments advance through tokens, trivia, and line breaks once; oversized inputs bypass the formatter lexing pass. Tests cover BOM/CRLF offsets, leading/trailing/EOF comments, comments around continued `from` clauses, multiline nested comments, blank-line counts before and after tokens, malformed and structural input token retention, parser versus lexer version errors, all refusal categories, a 1,000-comment sweep, a comment attached to a removable comma, and the size limit. Final hostile review found no actionable adapter defect; the remaining punctuation rehoming requirement belongs to the document builder. All 80 `sai_fmt` tests and 624 `sai_package` tests pass; the 1,701-test workspace run, warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting check, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that one additional linear token-only lexing pass, reusing parser-owned trivia, is acceptable to avoid retaining tokens on every package parse result. Confirm that an over-limit package manifest is a formatter refusal under §14.2.3 and package/04 §4.4.4; §14.12.1 now states this exception explicitly. The package builder must rehome comments attached to commas before canonical list output omits punctuation.
- **Remaining scope:** Build the package document builder from the lossless input adapter, including comment placement and fixed versus width-sensitive lists; explicitly rehome comments attached to canonicalized punctuation. Verify token/value preservation, declaration order, comment attachments, parse equivalence, and idempotency before exposing `format_package`; then implement the source builder and CLI file-update rules.

### IR-326: Index lossless package surface structure

- **Decision:** Add an internal syntax index over the formatter's token stream. It records every top-level declaration range and generated top-level newline, matches brace pairs, and records each brace list's owner, parent list/item, item spans, and direct separators. Classify a list as line-separated or comma-or-line from its grammar context, so commas inside requirement version ranges remain part of their entry. Keep brace metadata only for brace tokens and flatten item/separator spans into shared arrays; avoid token-sized maps. Store token and range indices as checked `u32` values because the package parser's fixed 16 MiB input limit, including its synthetic newline and EOF tokens, keeps all valid indices below `u32::MAX`. Keep the index private and intermediate; do not expose `format_package` before every package declaration and verification rule has a renderer.
- **Reason:** The semantic package AST intentionally omits duplicate declarations and invalid values, while the formatter must retain their original spellings. An index of token spans lets subsequent builders operate on the complete source shape without duplicating or weakening package parsing. Grammar-context classification avoids treating a version-comparator comma as a list separator. Compact checked indices reduce the cost of brace metadata while retaining an explicit overflow error if a future limit changes.
- **Review:** Tests account for every token across declaration/newline/EOF ranges, preserve repeated package declarations, validate direct-token coverage within brace lists, distinguish every brace owner including `requires test`, nested `only`/`grant`, profile budget, `authors`, `deny`, `policy`, and `lints`, cover comparator commas across physical lines, test optional empty lists versus an empty artifact-list grammar error, verify repeated profile parent links across comments, and scale across 1,000 repeated declarations. Hostile review caught and prompted removal of quadratic declaration scans and token-sized scratch arrays; brace parent/item links are now stored directly, and item/separator ranges share flattened arrays. Final hostile review found no remaining range-indexing defect and confirmed that valid token and entry counts stay within the `u32` bound. A pathological near-limit file of repeated empty lists may use about 96 MiB for this index, plus parser/token storage. All 87 `sai_fmt` tests and 1,708 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the line-separated/comma-or-line classification from enclosing grammar context, the `u32` bound derived from the fixed package input limit, and the choice to keep this syntax index internal until the package renderer and losslessness checks are complete. Consider the bounded worst-case index memory alongside parser/token storage.
- **Remaining scope:** Use the syntax index to build a complete `targets` document, including flat and broken forms, comment rehoming, and preserved blank-line gaps; then extend the builder to all declaration forms and add semantic/losslessness verification.

### IR-327: Add an incremental `targets` document builder

- **Decision:** Add a private builder for one `targets` declaration. Render fitting lists as `{ a, b }`, broken lists as one comma-free entry per line, and empty lists as `{}`. Preserve entry spelling, order, duplicates, and unknown values. Until comment placement and comma-comment rehoming are implemented, return an explicit error for comments attached within the declaration instead of producing a lossy document. Keep this builder internal; `format_package` remains unavailable.
- **Reason:** The package formatter can be built and verified one grammar form at a time. Explicit refusal makes the current boundary safe and lets later comment support be validated independently without silently dropping source.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that broken lists collapsed blank lines and that width-boundary and duplicate/unknown-target cases needed explicit tests. The builder now preserves at most one blank line in broken form by counting raw LF bytes between target tokens (comments are refused within this declaration), tests exact fit at 100 columns and overflow at 101, and retains duplicate and unknown target spellings. The follow-up hostile review found no additional defects and confirmed both findings were resolved. All 95 `sai_fmt` tests and 1,716 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the private, comment-free intermediate builder and explicit error boundary are appropriate while the formatter is incomplete. Review comment placement, comma rehoming, and blank-line preservation before the builder is used to format user files.
- **Remaining scope:** Add leading/trailing and comma-anchored comment handling, preserve blank-line gaps, test parse equivalence and idempotency, then build every remaining package declaration before enabling `format_package`.

### IR-328: Preserve comments in `targets` documents

- **Decision:** Render comments attached to the `targets` keyword, braces, entries, and separators. Rehome comments from removed commas according to §14.6.1; retain comment order and entry order; normalize CR LF to LF and trim trailing whitespace on every comment line. Preserve one blank line between broken-list units while removing blank lines after `{` and before `}`. Force empty lists containing comments to break, omit the flat-layout space between a final inline block comment and `}`, and cache the package refusal classification once per input. Restrict each declaration's comment scan to its byte range. Keep the builder private.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.6 and 14.12 require comments to follow their token attachments, define how comments move from removed punctuation, and specify blank-line edges and empty-list behavior. Scanning all comments or parser errors once per declaration would make repeated declarations scale quadratically.
- **Review:** Hostile review found five defects: empty lists containing inline block comments stayed flat; a final inline block comment had an extra space before `}`; multiline comments retained trailing whitespace; empty comment-only lists could preserve a blank line after `{`; and declaration building repeatedly scanned package diagnostics and all comments. The renderer now breaks the empty commented list at the outer indentation, fixes both blank-line edges and final-comment spacing, trims each comment line without changing its interior text, caches refusals, and uses a binary-searched comment-span window. A follow-up review questioned a multiline comment between `targets` and `{`; inspection confirmed the package lexer emits a `NEWLINE` there and the parser rejects that grammar, so the formatter must refuse it under §14.12.1.2. A regression verifies that refusal. All 108 `sai_fmt` tests and 1,729 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm comma-comment rehoming and the chosen treatment of comment-only empty lists against the canonical examples. Confirm that the span-scoped scan and cached refusal list are appropriate internal performance choices. This builder still renders only `targets`; EOF comments remain the enclosing package document builder's responsibility.
- **Remaining scope:** Build the remaining package declaration forms; add semantic equivalence and idempotency verification before exposing `format_package`; then implement source formatting and the CLI file-update rules.

### IR-329: Reuse comma-list rendering for capability declarations

- **Decision:** Factor the canonical comma-or-line list renderer so `targets`, `allow`, and `deny` use the same comment, separator, blank-line, and width handling. Require the exact brace-list owner and matching declaration keyword. Accept only `WORD` entries in `targets`; accept `WORD` or `PKG_PATH` entries in `allow` and `deny`. Preserve source spelling, order, duplicates, and semantic-invalid values; leave their diagnostics to validation.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.12.2 gives these declarations the same brace-list layout, while package/01 §§1.8 and 1.11.2 define different token kinds for target names and capability names. Reusing one builder keeps punctuation rehoming and comment behavior consistent without weakening the grammar boundary.
- **Review:** Hostile review confirmed the wrapper owner checks, declaration keyword checks, and entry-token classes match the package parser and spec; it found no actionable issues. Regressions cover flat and broken capability lists, `PKG_PATH` interfaces, empty lists, duplicates, and comments attached to removed commas. All 111 `sai_fmt` tests and 1,732 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that `allow` and `deny` formatting must preserve semantically invalid capability values and leave their diagnostics to package validation, as §14.12.1.2 does not refuse later validation errors.
- **Remaining scope:** Extend the list builder to nested `only`/`grant` clauses and other brace-list declarations, then build entry lists and complete the package-level semantic and idempotency verification.

### IR-330: Format the `authors` string list

- **Decision:** Extend the shared comma-list builder to `authors` and require `String` tokens for its entries. Print each valid string from its original source span, preserving its existing quote and escape spelling, order, duplicates, and decoded value. Keep empty-list, width, comment, and comma-rehoming behavior shared with the other comma lists.
- **Reason:** Package/01 §1.17.4 defines `authors` as a free-form string list; formatter §14.4.3 prints valid simple string tokens verbatim, and §14.12.2 assigns `authors` the same brace-list layout as `targets`, `allow`, and `deny`.
- **Review:** Hostile review confirmed owner and keyword validation, the `String` token boundary, original-span rendering, and unchanged behavior for the other shared-list owners; no actionable issues were found. Regressions cover escaped string source spelling, empty lists, and comments that force a broken list. All 113 `sai_fmt` tests and 1,734 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that valid author strings should retain their original simple-string quote and escape spelling, as §14.4.3 requires for simple strings.
- **Remaining scope:** Build other package declaration forms, including nested `only`/`grant`, policy and lint declarations, then complete package-level semantic equivalence and idempotency verification.

### IR-331: Format simple string declarations

- **Decision:** Add a private document builder for the two-token declarations `source`, `wit`, `description`, `license`, and `repository`. Require the expected keyword plus one `String` token, and print the string from its original source span. Retain comments attached to both tokens; keep each declaration on one line and let an over-width string declaration overflow rather than splitting it.
- **Reason:** Package/01 defines these declarations as one keyword plus one string. Formatter §14.4.3 keeps simple string spelling and escapes verbatim, and §14.12.2 requires the other one-line declarations not to break.
- **Review:** Hostile review confirmed the exact declaration shape, keyword/token-kind boundary, original-span string rendering, comment attachment handling, and no changes to shared lists; no actionable issues were found. Regressions cover all five keywords, spacing, escaped string spelling, and inline comments. All 115 `sai_fmt` tests and 1,736 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that the five supported keywords are an appropriate initial boundary and that valid strings should keep their original source spelling, including escapes.
- **Remaining scope:** Format the remaining package declarations and nested brace lists, then implement complete package-document assembly, semantic equivalence, and idempotency checks before exposing `format_package`.

### IR-332: Format allocator declarations

- **Decision:** Extend the shared one-value declaration renderer with `allocator <PKG_PATH>`. Require exactly the `allocator` word and a `PackagePath` token, preserve the path's original spelling and declaration-attached comments, and print the declaration on one line with canonical spacing. Keep semantic package validation outside this builder.
- **Reason:** Package/01 §1.15 defines the allocator declaration as `allocator PKG_PATH` and requires its package to be declared elsewhere; formatter §14.12.2 rule 7 keeps one-line declarations unbroken. The builder therefore checks syntax while leaving whether the named package exists to validation, consistent with §14.12.1.2.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no issues in token acceptance, refusal behavior, source preservation, or comment ordering. It confirmed that a standalone trailing comment is owned by EOF and must be emitted by the future package-document assembler, rather than by this declaration builder. Regressions cover canonical spacing, a syntactically valid unknown package path, and comments around the declaration. All 117 `sai_fmt` tests and 1,738 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that semantically unknown allocator paths should still be formatted and diagnosed by package validation, and that the complete package assembler will preserve EOF-owned comments.
- **Remaining scope:** Add builders for remaining package declarations and nested lists; preserve EOF comments in package assembly; then verify semantic equivalence and idempotency before exposing `format_package`.

### IR-333: Format `export world` declarations

- **Decision:** Add a private one-line builder for `export world` with either a `PackagePath` world name or a `WORD ('/' WORD)*` path. Normalize spaces between ordinary tokens and remove spaces around `/`; preserve token spelling and attached comments. Reject malformed token ranges without panicking, and leave semantic world validation to package validation.
- **Reason:** Package/01 §1.3 defines both world-name forms, and §1.9 assigns their meaning and semantic checks. Formatter §14.12.2 rules 2 and 7 require no spaces around `/` and prohibit breaking one-line declarations; §§14.6.1–14.6.2 define comment attachment and spacing.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that block comments beside `/` needed a separating space after the comment, and that an out-of-bounds declaration range could panic. The builder now inserts that separator and validates the range before slicing. Regression tests cover both slash-adjacent comment positions and the invalid range. Follow-up hostile review confirmed both findings resolved and found no new issues. All 121 `sai_fmt` tests and 1,742 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that syntactically valid but semantically invalid world names should be formatted and diagnosed by package validation, and that slash-separated names retain the original token spellings.
- **Remaining scope:** Format the package identity and compiler declarations, entry-list declarations, policy and lint lists, and the remaining metadata; then assemble the full package document with EOF comments and verify semantic equivalence and idempotency before exposing `format_package`.

### IR-334: Format the package identity declaration

- **Decision:** Add a private builder for exactly `package PKG_PATH @ VERSION`. Preserve the original package-path and version spellings and attached comments, and normalize spacing to one space around `@`. Do not enforce first-declaration, uniqueness, or semantic identity rules in this builder.
- **Reason:** Package/01 §§1.3–1.4 define the declaration shape and identity validation. Formatter §14.12.2 rules 1–2 define declaration placement and spacing around `@`; §§14.6.1–14.6.2 define comment retention. Structural and semantic errors do not make `format_package` refuse input under §14.12.1.2.
- **Review:** Hostile review confirmed four-token shape and token-kind checks, safe range access, comment handling, canonical `@` spacing, and preservation of semantic-invalid package paths; no actionable issues were found. Regressions cover whitespace normalization and comments around all four tokens. All 123 `sai_fmt` tests and 1,744 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that paths rejected only by package identity validation, such as a `PKG_PATH` containing `/`, should retain their spelling during formatting, and that first-position and duplicate-declaration diagnostics remain outside this builder.
- **Remaining scope:** Format the compiler requirement, entry-list declarations, policy and lint lists, and remaining metadata; then assemble the complete package document with EOF comments and verify semantic equivalence and idempotency before exposing `format_package`.

### IR-335: Format compiler version requirements

- **Decision:** Add a private `compiler` declaration builder that canonicalizes comparator/version adjacency and comma spacing while preserving each token's source spelling. Retain a lone bare version and the unsupported `>` comparator when they are parsed as `InvalidVersionRequirement` diagnostics rather than formatter refusals. Force a line break after a trailing line or multiline block comment when another requirement token follows, indenting that continuation by four spaces; preserve an input blank-line gap between a forced trailing comment and the next own-line comment.
- **Reason:** Package/01 §§1.3, 1.5, and 1.10.2 define the comparator tokens and version-requirement meaning. Formatter §14.12.2 rules 2 and 6 specify operator/version and comma spacing and normally keep a requirement unbroken; §§14.6.2–14.6.4 require comments and existing blank lines to survive. `PackageInput` explicitly excludes `InvalidVersionRequirement` from refusal, so the builder preserves those values while refusing lexical or grammar errors.
- **Review:** Hostile review found a duplicate forced line break between a trailing line comment and a following own-line comment, which inserted a blank line not present in input. The builder now reuses the existing break and adds a second only when `has_blank_line` confirms one in source. Regression tests cover both adjacent and blank-separated comments. Follow-up hostile review confirmed the fix and found no new issue. Regressions also cover canonical comparator spacing, unsupported and bare forms, inline comments, and line-comment continuation. All 127 `sai_fmt` tests and 1,748 workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that a forced continuation after comments uses four-space indentation, and that unsupported `>` and bare-version values should be normalized only for spacing while remaining available for diagnostics.
- **Remaining scope:** Format entry-list declarations, policy and lint lists, and remaining metadata; then assemble the full package document with EOF comments and verify semantic equivalence and idempotency before exposing `format_package`.

### IR-336: Format component artifact entry lists

- **Decision:** Allow `artifact component {}` as syntactically valid and update the package grammar accordingly. Render artifact entries as one `WORD STRING` pair per four-space-indented line, even for one entry; render an empty list as `{}`; preserve comments and at most one blank line between entries. Keep semantically invalid target names and content paths in source spelling for later validation. Bind each syntax index to its source slice, require lists to belong to that index, and return errors from range accessors instead of panicking on mismatched inputs.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.12.2 requires every NL-separated entry list, including artifacts, to be broken and says empty lists print `{}`. The previous package grammar and parser disallowed empty artifact lists, although hybrid packages may omit artifacts and artifact-only packages already receive `MISSING_ARTIFACT_FOR_TARGET` for each uncovered target. Making the list optional reconciles those rules while preserving semantic diagnostics. Index/input and list/index binding checks keep the internal builder boundary safe when callers combine values from different parses.
- **Review:** Hostile review found two P2 range-safety defects: a list from a different syntax index could make unchecked entry/separator access panic, and a syntax index built from a different input could make token indexing panic. Checked accessors now verify list ownership and source identity before token access. Regressions cover both mismatches; follow-up hostile review confirmed the fixes and found no remaining P0–P2 issue. Tests cover one- and two-entry layouts, empty lists, comments, blank lines, invalid target/path preservation, empty hybrid validation, and missing artifacts for artifact-only packages. All 133 `sai_fmt` tests and 625 `sai_package` tests pass; the full workspace test run, warning-denied Clippy, formatting check, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that an empty artifact declaration is valid syntax, with artifact-only completeness enforced by `MISSING_ARTIFACT_FOR_TARGET`; confirm hybrid packages may declare an empty list; and review preservation of semantically invalid target/path spellings and artifact-list comments.
- **Remaining scope:** Build the other line-separated package lists (`requires`, `overrides`, and profile bodies), finish policy and lint lists and remaining metadata, then assemble the complete package document with EOF comments and verify semantic equivalence and idempotency before exposing `format_package`.

### IR-337: Format package overrides

- **Decision:** Add a private renderer for NL-separated `overrides` entries. Accept exactly `PKG_PATH use artifact`, `PKG_PATH use path STRING`, `PKG_PATH use git STRING`, and `PKG_PATH use git STRING dir STRING`; render each entry on its own line, normalize token spacing, preserve comments and at most one blank line between entries, and keep syntactically valid but semantically invalid source values for package validation. If a comment forces a break before the continuation word `dir`, indent that continuation by eight spaces.
- **Reason:** Package/01 §§1.12 and 1.2.6 define the four override token shapes and permit `dir` to continue on the next physical line. Formatter §14.12.2 requires entry lists to break between entries and says overrides do not break for width; comments still retain their token attachment under §§14.6.1–14.6.3, so a safe comment-forced continuation may be necessary before `dir`. Eight spaces represent one continuation level inside the four-space-indented entry.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no P0–P2 issues. Tests cover all override forms, an empty list, inline and own-line comments, a line-comment-forced `dir` continuation, blank-line compaction, and a semantically invalid path. All 136 `sai_fmt` tests and 625 `sai_package` tests pass; the full workspace test run, warning-denied Clippy, formatting check, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that a comment-forced `dir` continuation should be indented eight spaces even though ordinary overrides stay on one line, and that override values with later semantic errors should retain their source spelling.
- **Remaining scope:** Build `requires` and `requires test` entries with their nested `only` and `grant` lists, then format profile bodies, policy and lint lists, and remaining metadata; finally assemble the package document with EOF comments and add semantic equivalence and idempotency verification before exposing `format_package`.

### IR-338: Format requirement entries and comment-forced continuations

- **Decision:** Add a private renderer for `requires` and `requires test` entries with a package path, version requirement, and optional `from path` / `from git` source (including `dir`). Normalize `@`, comparator, and source spacing while preserving token spelling, comments, entry order, and blank-line gaps. Retain semantically invalid version tokens for package validation. Width does not split version requirements, but comments may force physical continuation after `@`, a comparator, or a comma; those tokens are not package-line terminators, so the lexer emits no `NEWLINE`. A comment may force `dir` onto its own continuation line at eight-space indentation, retaining its continuation safety and one indent level beyond the entry.
- **Reason:** Package/01 §§1.2.6, 1.3.1, and 1.10 define the requirement token shapes and continuation words. Formatter §§14.6 and 14.12 require comment retention, canonical spacing, one entry per line, and eight-space clause continuations. Preserving a line comment attached to `@`, a comparator, a comma, or the source URL sometimes requires a physical break; using only non-terminating version tokens and the `dir` continuation word keeps the formatted tokens in the same parsed requirement. The parser already classifies unsupported and bare version forms as later validation diagnostics rather than syntax refusals.
- **Review:** Hostile review first found rejected valid comment placements, an over-indented comment-forced `dir`, and an extra space before a comment on `@`. The builder now accepts safe physical continuations, keeps `dir` at eight columns, and suppresses the duplicate space. The package contract's canonical-layout summary and formatter §14.12.2 / N-H-62 now describe the same comment exceptions. Follow-up hostile review found no remaining P0–P2 issues. Regressions cover regular and empty lists, both declaration headers, path/Git sources, comparator spacing, semantic-invalid `>` versions, source comments, blank lines, comments after `@` / comparators / commas, and `dir`; the formatted output is parsed again to verify syntax. All 140 `sai_fmt` tests and warning-denied crate Clippy pass; formatting and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comment-forced physical continuation inside a version requirement is consistent with the wording “version requirements are never broken,” and that a comment-forced `dir` line at eight spaces preserves the intended clause layout. Confirm that the formatter may handle semantically invalid version tokens without refusing them.
- **Remaining scope:** Format profile bodies, policy and lint lists, and remaining metadata; then assemble the package document with EOF comments and add semantic equivalence and idempotency verification before exposing `format_package`.

### IR-339: Format nested requirement capability clauses

- **Decision:** Extend the private requirement renderer to recognize `only` and `grant` in their documented order, validate each nested list against its owning requirement entry, and render the clauses on separate eight-space continuation lines through the shared brace-list builder. Keep flat lists on one line when they fit, break long or commented lists into twelve-space entries, and print an empty `grant` as `{}`. Accept only the syntax-defined token kinds; preserve unknown target and capability spellings for package validation.
- **Reason:** Package/01 §§1.10.1, 1.10.5, and 1.10.8 define clause order, list contents, and the valid empty `grant`; formatter §14.12.2 rules 3, 5, and 9 define list layout, indentation, and safe breaks before `only` and `grant`. Reusing the indexed list renderer keeps comment attachment and wrapping behavior consistent with other package brace lists.
- **Review:** Hostile review found no P0–P2 issue. It identified missing test coverage for comments attached to nested keywords and braces; regression coverage now includes those positions and a leading list-item comment. An initially attempted line comment between `only` and `{` was correctly rejected by package grammar, so the retained keyword-comment case uses a block comment.
