Tests also verify parser acceptance and idempotent formatting for both requirement headers. Follow-up hostile review found no remaining concrete issue. The full workspace test suite, warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatter check, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that an empty `grant {}` remains valid and is printed inline, while an empty `only` list remains rejected by package grammar; confirm that unknown target and capability names should retain their spelling for semantic diagnostics, and that nested-list comments follow the shared brace-list layout.
- **Remaining scope:** Format policy and lint lists and remaining metadata; then assemble the package document with EOF comments and add complete semantic-equivalence and idempotency verification before exposing `format_package`.

### IR-340: Format profile bodies and nested budgets

- **Decision:** Render each `profile` body entry on its own indented line and format its `allow` and `budget` child lists through their owner-bound renderers. Validate the profile header, list kind, child-list parent, and parent-entry index before formatting. Budget entries retain their metric and numeric source spellings while canonicalizing spacing around `<=`. When a broken comma-or-line list has an entry beginning with the contextual continuation word `from`, `dir`, `only`, or `grant`, retain a comma before that entry so the package lexer cannot join it to the previous line.
- **Reason:** Package/01 §§1.2.6, 1.3.1, 1.10.7, and 1.16 define profile entry lists, budget entries, and the continuation set; formatter §14.12.2 requires one profile entry per line, canonical budget spacing, and safe list breaks. Owner and parent checks prevent a list from another syntax index or profile from being formatted under the wrong declaration.
- **Review:** Hostile review found that omitting every comma in broken lists made the lexer suppress `NEWLINE` before a continuation word used as a syntactically valid list value, so reparsing could merge entries. Broken budget and shared comma-list renderers now retain the separating comma in that case, and regressions cover both `targets` and a budget metric named `from`, including parser acceptance and idempotency. Final hostile review found no actionable P0–P3 issues. All 144 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that profile bodies should be printed one entry per line and that syntactically valid but semantically unknown budget metrics keep their source spelling for later validation. Confirm the contextual-word comma exception is appropriate even though ordinary broken lists omit commas.
- **Remaining scope:** Format policy and lint entries, then assemble the package document with EOF comments and add complete semantic-equivalence and idempotency verification before exposing `format_package`.

### IR-341: Format policy and lint entries

- **Decision:** Extend the shared comma-list renderer to validate and render two-token policy entries (`deny WORD`) and lint entries (`allow|warn|deny UWORD`) while retaining each token's spelling and attached comments. Keep each pair on one line unless a comment forces a break before a policy value in the package continuation set; print that continuation at eight-space indentation. Preserve unknown policy values and lint names for package validation.
- **Reason:** Package/01 §§1.2.6, 1.12, and 1.13 define the entry token kinds, continuation words, and semantic validation. Formatter §14.12.2 rules 3, 7, and 9 require canonical list layout and safe breaks. Unknown values are semantic diagnostics, so formatting them must not silently rewrite or discard them.
- **Review:** The hostile review found no actionable P0–P3 issues. Regressions cover flat and broken layouts, comments between and after tokens, comment-forced `from` continuations, empty lists, parser acceptance, and idempotency. All 145 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that a comment-forced policy value in the continuation set should be indented eight spaces, and that unknown policy values and lint names should keep their source spelling for semantic diagnostics.
- **Remaining scope:** Assemble all package declarations into the package document, preserve EOF-owned comments, implement semantic-equivalence and idempotency verification, and expose the public `format_package` entry point.

### IR-342: Assemble and verify complete package formatting

- **Decision:** Assemble every top-level package declaration in source order, retain at most one blank line between declarations and EOF-owned comments, and finish empty input with the formatter's required newline. Pre-index each declaration's top-level brace list and first leading-comment offset in linear passes so assembly does not rescan all lists or comments for every declaration. Expose `format_package` only after building the complete package document; verify the output by re-lexing and reparsing, comparing significant token kinds and spellings while omitting only `NEWLINE`, EOF, and indexed brace-list separator commas, comparing parser diagnostic kinds and ordered normalized comments, and formatting the output again to require a byte-identical fixed point. Preserve the failed verification check and first input byte offset, where available, in `PackageVerificationFailure`. Return the underlying package lexer and parser errors, with their original byte spans, inside `PackageFormatError::Refused`.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.1, 14.11, and 14.12 require a lossless canonical package result, refusal details, and idempotency. The package grammar allows list commas to change during layout but keeps comparator commas significant; the syntax index distinguishes these cases. Linear pre-indexing avoids quadratic behavior on large manifests containing many declarations and comments. `format_package` accepts bytes without a file identity, so constructing a workspace `Diagnostic` at this boundary would lack a meaningful file location. Returning the parser's typed errors keeps their codes, messages, and precise spans available for CLI mapping once the caller provides a source identity. This is a deliberate API-shape deviation from the abstract `Refusal { diagnostics: Vec<Diagnostic> }` sketch and should be reviewed.
- **Review:** Hostile review found and the implementation fixed three classes of issues: duplicate blank-line accounting around leading comments, omitted parser size-limit details in refusals, and repeated scans that made package assembly quadratic in declaration/comment count. The review also found that output-only token or diagnostic changes could lose their input location; these now map to the input EOF, including the parser size-limit case where the output has no token stream. Verification failures retain the failed check and location. A second hostile pass found no remaining actionable issues. The focused package formatter suite (153 tests) and the full workspace test suite pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the typed lexer/parser errors are the right package-library boundary for refusals, with CLI diagnostic mapping deferred until a file identity is available. Confirm that sequence comparison for package comments is an acceptable stronger check because package declaration order is preserved. Confirm that empty `sai.package` input is formatted as a single newline while its missing-package structural diagnostic remains non-refusing.
- **Remaining scope:** Add explicit package file paths, directory traversal and workspace discovery to `sai fmt`; implement its atomic file updates; then build source formatting and conformance checks.

### IR-343: Format package input from `sai fmt` stdin

- **Decision:** Implement `sai fmt --stdin-kind package -` first. Read at most one byte beyond the package-manifest limit. Human mode writes canonical output; JSON mode places it in the documented `fmt` result. `--check` writes nothing and reports `UNFORMATTED_FILE` at the first differing line with the expected canonical line. Package refusal diagnostics map source byte spans to `<stdin>`. Pass the configured `--max-errors` as a retention limit for lexical and parser error records while continuing the complete lex and parse passes and preserving exact total and formatter-refusing error counts; `--max-errors=0` keeps the documented unlimited behavior. Mark lexical errors on their tokens so diagnostic truncation cannot change parser recovery. Skip the formatter's second token-only lex pass when the parser already established that the input must be refused. After formatting and diagnostic construction, check cancellation before emitting a result. Escape terminal controls only when writing formatted text to a terminal; keep pipe and JSON output byte-preserving. Render diagnostic source rows from unexpanded source text, use display-width checkpoints for bounded windows, and preserve visible caret placement for EOF, lone-CR, BOM, wide-character, and multiline locations.
- **Reason:** The package formatter API is verified, while safe filesystem replacement and source formatting are separate unfinished pieces. Stdin provides a usable package formatter path without writing files. Bounded diagnostic retention prevents a single valid-size input with millions of lexical or parser errors from allocating millions of error records, while full parsing and exact totals preserve the documented behavior that `--max-errors` changes only the number of printed diagnostics. Token-local lexical-error state preserves parser recovery after detailed error records are discarded. Skipping a duplicate tokenization on an already-refused input avoids allocating a second token stream. UTF-16 and display-width checkpoints keep long-line diagnostics linear while avoiding the large expansion caused by escaping every control character before clipping. Terminal-only escaping avoids interpreting untrusted comment controls while preserving exact formatter data for pipes and JSON. Cancellation cannot interrupt the synchronous formatter internals, so the command reports interruption at the next boundary after formatting or diagnostic preparation.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found and the implementation fixed empty EOF annotations landing on the last character, quadratic UTF-16 column indexing, unbounded retained comment diagnostics, CR omission, control-character expansion before clipping, missing post-format cancellation checks, and terminal control passthrough. The second review found remaining unbounded lexical and parser error vectors, Bidi-control candidates, and cancellation latency inside the synchronous formatter. The diagnostic vectors are now bounded for the CLI path while exact counts and full parse work are retained; formatter-specific refusal errors have their own bounded list so earlier non-refusing parser errors cannot hide a later grammar refusal. The normative SAI specification lists only U+202A–U+202E and U+2066–U+2069 as bidi controls, so the wider candidates were not added to lexer classification; terminal rendering continues to escape additional unsafe controls for display. A third review found that comparing only the retained parser-error prefix could miss a changed diagnostic later in the stream; the parser now maintains a SHA-256 fingerprint over every parser-emitted error kind, and formatter verification compares it as well as retained details and exact counts. Follow-up review confirmed the fix and found that parser-emission order is not always source order because list diagnostics are collected after ordinary parse errors; the API documentation now describes the fingerprint's actual emission order. Cancellation still cannot interrupt synchronous formatter internals and is observed at the next safe boundary. Regressions cover these cases, exact error totals under `--max-errors=1`, detection of changed diagnostics beyond the retained prefix, canonical output, JSON records, `--check`, source locations, and a diagnostic at the end of a 1 MiB line. Full workspace tests, warning-denied Clippy, formatting checks, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that package stdin is a useful first `fmt` path; source stdin remains a stage-0 `FEATURE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`. Confirm that terminal output may escape control characters in preserved comments while redirected and JSON output stay exact. Confirm the bounded API's split error counters, refusal list, and full-stream fingerprint are an acceptable way to honor `--max-errors` without changing full parse work. The parser's warning list remains complete because warnings are not covered by this flag. The Rust API treats `max_errors=0` as retaining no details; the CLI maps its documented `--max-errors=0` unlimited convention to `usize::MAX`.
- **Remaining scope:** Add explicit package file paths, directory traversal and workspace/member discovery; implement atomic updates; add source formatting and source diagnostics; then implement implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-344: Format explicitly selected package files

- **Decision:** Implement explicit file and directory selection for `sai fmt`, sorting and deduplicating canonical physical file paths. Use opened directory capabilities for recursive traversal, reject a symlink when opening an explicit traversal root, and open children relative to the already-open directory so replacing a child path with an external symlink cannot escape the selected tree. On Unix, use `openat`/`renameat`/`unlinkat` with no-follow and nonblocking flags; on Windows, use parent-handle-relative `NtCreateFile` and handle-based `SetFileInformationByHandle` rename/removal, with directory handles denying delete sharing. Retain a no-follow-opened manifest handle and its stable file identity through the post-lock scan, and compare file and parent-directory identities with a fresh scan so replacing a processable manifest or parent directory with another object at the same path forces a retry. Read the manifest through that retained handle. Source files remain `FEATURE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, so their file identity handles are not retained. Share one directory capability per directory, including for explicit file lists. Require every child-operation API name to be one normal path component. Do not follow directory symlinks; allow an explicitly named symlink to a regular file by resolving it, and silently skip an explicitly named symlink to a directory. Skip hidden directories and a package's sibling `out/`. Acquire every discovered package lock in canonical path order before reading inputs, using shared locks for `--check` and exclusive locks for writes; poll cancellable lock attempts and print the waiting line once across selection retries. Re-scan after acquiring the locks and retry if the selected paths, parent directories, processable file identities, or their package roots changed while waiting. Check cancellation during traversal, before replacement, and after replacement. Send human progress to stderr, suppress it for `--quiet` and JSON, and escape control characters. Process paths and result arrays in canonical path-byte order. Reject non-UTF-8 paths before building CLI result paths so distinct operating-system paths cannot collapse through lossy conversion. Retain package locks through final diagnostics and result output. Create the cleanup guard before the temporary file so resource exhaustion cannot leave an untracked temporary. Preserve existing permissions during handle-relative atomic replacement. Report source files as `FEATURE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` and continue processing package manifests because `sai_fmt::format_source` is not implemented yet.
- **Reason:** CLI §13.6.7 specifies path selection, traversal exclusions, ordering, and refusal behavior; §13.5.4 requires workspace locking and atomic replacement; §13.3.6 defines progress channels and suppression. Capability-relative traversal and single-component validation constrain child operations to the selected directory even if a checked path is concurrently replaced by a symlink or parent traversal. Pinning each processable manifest while locks are acquired prevents identity reuse after unlink and ensures the bytes read are from the selected object. Source files are currently refused without reading or writing their contents, so keeping one identity handle per such file would add descriptor pressure without affecting the command's behavior. Sharing directory capabilities keeps descriptor use dependent on distinct parent directories rather than the number of explicit file arguments. Windows identities use `GetFileInformationByHandleEx(FileIdInfo)` and its 128-bit file ID, which is available on the declared stable Rust toolchain; Windows synchronous handles include the required `SYNCHRONIZE` access right for valid temporary rename and cleanup operations. Nonblocking Unix file opens avoid a cancellation hang if a regular input is replaced with a FIFO between selection and the input open. `--check` shares the package lock and may therefore create `.sai/lock`; the CLI contract now states that it does not rewrite source files and may create this internal lock bookkeeping file.
- **Review:** The first hostile review identified a path-based directory traversal race that could follow a concurrently installed symlink, stale selection when a file is added while waiting for package locks, incomplete cancellation checks, progress written to stdout and printed despite `--quiet`, JSON lock-wait output, the internal lock-file side effect of `--check`, and a mismatch in explicit directory-symlink handling. The second review found that later path-based reads and replacement could escape after the selected parent directory was swapped, cancellation could hang on a FIFO, lock-wait output could repeat after a retry, lossy paths could collide in JSON, processing order could diverge from the specified path order, and locks were released before final output. The third review found a missing Windows `SYNCHRONIZE` access right, same-path replacement of a directory or file that the post-lock scan did not detect, child-operation helpers that accepted `..` or absolute paths, and a temp-file cleanup guard installed too late. A fourth review found that Rust's Windows `MetadataExt` identity methods are unstable, file IDs could be reused after dropping the original handle, and explicit file lists retained duplicate parent-directory handles. Windows identity now comes from `GetFileInformationByHandleEx(FileIdInfo)`; processable manifest handles stay open across the comparison and are used for reading; source identity handles are omitted because source files are not yet processed; directory capabilities are shared by canonical parent path. A sixth review found that hard-link names in one directory were conflated and that a manifest replaced after the post-lock scan could be overwritten. Deduplication now includes the child name, the selected manifest handle stays open through publication, and publication rechecks both its identity and original bytes after staging the temporary file. A seventh review found that identity alone misses in-place edits and that dropping the original handle permits inode reuse; the selected handle now remains open through publication and the precondition compares the exact original bytes as well as file identity. The hard-link regression test is enabled for Unix and Windows. An eighth review confirmed those protections, requested direct coverage for reads after a path replacement, and found only the documented race between the final comparison and rename; `fmt_input_read_uses_the_selected_handle_after_path_replacement` now covers the former. A portable filesystem API does not provide an atomic compare-and-replace primitive, so an external change after that final check and before rename remains a narrow race and is recorded for review. Regression tests cover symlink rejection, directory and file identity changes at the same path, handle-relative atomic replacement, component-boundary escape attempts, deterministic path selection, explicit-list directory sharing, stable file identity, cancellation, output channels, `--check`, path encoding, distinct hard-link names across supported platforms, and refusal to overwrite replaced or in-place edited manifests. The full workspace test suite and all 143 `sai_cli` tests passed; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and diff checks passed. The Windows all-targets check for `sai_windows_fs` passed. The full `sai_cli` Windows cross-check could not pass the `aws-lc-sys` build because `x86_64-w64-mingw32-gcc` is unavailable in the environment.
- **Superseding follow-up:** Windows identity now uses `GetFileInformationByHandleEx(FileIdInfo)` and its 128-bit file ID, avoiding the legacy index's ReFS uniqueness limit. Physical package roots are deduplicated before locking so bind-mounted aliases cannot make the command wait on its own lock. Explicit directory roots resolve to the filesystem's canonical spelling only after a no-follow open and identity check, covering case-insensitive path aliases. File identity deduplication includes both the parent directory and child name, so bind-mounted aliases collapse while distinct hard-link names remain separate write targets. The post-lock comparison scan drops traversal capabilities immediately; only processable manifests retain a file and parent handle, and a manifest's parent handle also pins its package root when possible.
- **Follow-up reason:** The legacy Windows file index is not unique on every supported filesystem. Path-only root deduplication can acquire two lock names for one physical lock file, and case-only directory aliases bypass path-set deduplication. Retaining full capabilities for the comparison scan doubled descriptor use while all package locks were held. Using 128-bit file IDs, physical root identity, canonical root spelling, and a capability-free comparison scan address these cases while keeping original manifest handles pinned for identity comparison and reading.
- **Follow-up review:** The fifth hostile review identified the legacy Windows ID limit, bind-mounted package aliases, case-insensitive explicit directory aliases, and descriptor growth during rescans. The fixes add Windows `FileIdInfo`, physical file/root identity deduplication, canonical spelling verification, deterministic traversal ordering, shared parent capabilities, and metadata-only comparison scans. Focused CLI tests and Windows filesystem all-target checks pass; the full workspace test suite, warning-denied Clippy, formatting and diff checks, and another hostile review are pending.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that an explicitly supplied symbolic link to a directory is silently skipped, and that `--check` may create `.sai/lock` while never rewriting source files. Confirm that external paths use a current-directory-relative JSON path when no package root exists. Review the narrow race after the final identity and byte-content check: no portable compare-and-replace filesystem operation can eliminate a concurrent external write or replacement in the interval before rename.
- **Remaining scope:** Add source formatting and diagnostics, implicit workspace discovery, and conformance checks for directory traversal and filesystem races.

### IR-345: Prepare source input for formatting

- **Decision:** Add a public `parse_source` helper that keeps the decoded `SourceFile`, its snapshot, and the lossless CST together. Return a source-specific `SourceRefusal` with separate input-limit, lexical, and parser diagnostic vectors, and keep `SourceInputError` separate from the package formatter's typed lexer/parser refusal API. Retain parser diagnostic origin in `sai_syntax::Parse` so diagnostics whose codes are shared with validation can be classified correctly. Exclude `CLAUSE_ORDER`; keep validation diagnostics and warnings available without refusing formatting. Represent source-size overflow as `IMPLEMENTATION_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`. Bind helper diagnostics to the VFS `<stdin>` sentinel because this byte-only API has no caller-supplied source identity.
- **Reason:** The source formatter consumes a lossless CST and must distinguish parser diagnostics from validation diagnostics according to formatter §14.2.2. The package formatter operates on a different grammar and its `Refusal` fields contain package-specific error types, so reusing that type would conflate incompatible diagnostics. A source larger than 16 MiB is rejected by the shared decoder before lexing and therefore needs a source-limit diagnostic at this API boundary. The parser can stop with an internal `UnsupportedItem` error when an error token reaches a top-level item; if parsing cannot recover after a lexer error, the helper still returns the available lexer refusal rather than masking it as an internal failure.
- **Review:** Regressions verify malformed UTF-8 refusal, simultaneous lexical and parser diagnostics when parsing recovers, parser recovery and invalid-attribute-target refusal, formatability of `CLAUSE_ORDER`, `UNKNOWN_ATTRIBUTE`, and `MISSING_FN_BODY`, the exact oversized-source diagnostic, reconstruction of source text from CST tokens and trivia after BOM removal, and preservation of parser diagnostic origins across query memoization and source rebinding. A final hostile review caught that the normative refusal rule omitted the source-size limit and that the pending source API still referenced the package-only error type; both contracts now identify the limit diagnostic and source-specific error types. The helper lexes once to classify lexical errors and then calls the public parse API, which lexes again; this duplicates linear work while keeping the formatter independent from parser internals. Decode errors other than the specified size limit, snapshot construction failures, and hard parse failures without a lexer diagnostic remain generic `InternalError`s.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the public helper and source-specific refusal types as an addition to the documented formatter entry point. Confirm that diagnostics need the separate input-limit category, that unrecoverable front-end failures without diagnostics are reported as internal errors, and that callers remap the `<stdin>` source sentinel when they format a named file.
- **Remaining scope:** Build source CST-to-document formatting, printer integration, normalization and preservation checks, `format_source`, source CLI diagnostics, and conformance coverage; then implement implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-346: Start a fail-closed source document builder

- **Decision:** Add a test-only document-builder prototype, initially for files made only of unannotated, zero-argument functions. Each function has either an empty body or one single-line literal `let` statement followed by a final name expression. Validate the typed CST structure, emit four-space block indentation, preserve statement and top-level blank-line rules, normalize hexadecimal digits and float exponent markers, and use the documented RHS choice to move a long literal to a continuation line. Return an internal `UnsupportedShape` result for comments in lexer or CST trivia, including demoted doc comments, or any other shape. Do not retain lexer output, and do not expose `format_source` until the formatter can satisfy the complete source-format contract. Simple typed parameters were added separately in IR-347.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.1–14.3 and §14.11 require canonical output, meaning preservation, comment preservation, idempotency, and output verification. A generic token-spacing pass that retains arbitrary input line breaks would not meet layout independence (§14.1.2 / N-H-44), while prematurely publishing a formatter that rejects valid source would contradict the exact refusal contract (§14.2.2 / N-H-43). A small syntax shape gives a concrete end-to-end document/printer unit with only verified safe block breaks (§14.5.1 and §14.5.5), while fail-closed behavior prevents incomplete output from escaping.
- **Review:** An initial broad token/trivia pass was rejected after hostile review because preserving arbitrary source breaks would violate canonical layout, NEWLINE safety cannot be inferred from token kinds alone, and comment attachment and verification need to be handled explicitly. A compile check also found that retaining lexer output in production had no consumer; it was removed. Follow-up review found that the prototype needed to inspect the CST, normalize hex digits, choose a safe RHS layout, and check parse-tree equivalence; those were added. The second hostile review found no P0/P1 issues and requested normalized-CST verification of the wrapped RHS branch. That regression is included, and final hostile review found no remaining actionable issues for that version. The prototype has since expanded to empty bodies, multiple functions, more scalar literals, and top-level blank lines. A later hostile review found that misplaced doc comments demoted to CST trivia were silently dropped; the builder now rejects comment trivia from the complete green tree, including leading and trailing trivia, and regression tests cover leading, trailing, block, and demoted doc comments. Follow-up hostile review found no actionable P0–P2 issues. Seven focused `sai_fmt` tests cover the source-document slice before the subsequent parameter-list expansion.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that the source builder should remain private until full-source support and verification are ready, and that this small function shape is a reasonable first vertical slice.
- **Remaining scope:** Broaden function signatures and parameter types, then production support to comments and the complete expression/body grammar; implement source verification; then expose `format_source`, integrate CLI diagnostics, and implement implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-347: Format simple function parameter lists

- **Decision:** Extend the private test-only source document builder to accept immutable named or `_` parameters whose types are one unqualified, nongeneric uppercase path segment. Format parameter lists with the documented group/line-break rules, including a trailing comma only when the list breaks. Keep `var`, `self`, generic or qualified types, function clauses, comments, and all other unsupported forms fail-closed. Keep the public `format_source` entry point deferred.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.3–14.5 and §14.7.4 define line breaking and parameter formatting. The printer already implements groups and conditional punctuation, so this slice exercises the canonical width-driven parameter-list layout without needing to infer safe breaks from source newlines. Restricting type forms to one path segment makes every emitted token explicit and avoids silently passing through unhandled type syntax.
- **Review:** Focused tests cover empty, one-parameter, multi-parameter, and width-broken lists, spacing independence, underscore parameters, insertion and removal of trailing commas, output reparsing, normalized CST shape, and fixed-point output. Hostile review found no P0–P2 issues and requested cases for an underscore parameter and for inserting a trailing comma when the input has none; both cases and the normalized-CST comparison were added, and follow-up review found no remaining P0–P2 issues. All 172 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; Clippy, formatting, and diff checks also pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that the initial source formatter slice should accept only immutable parameters with single-segment path types while broader types and modifiers are implemented.
- **Remaining scope:** Add compound parameter and return types and function clauses; then broaden the body/expression grammar. Implement comments and verification before exposing the source API.

### IR-348: Format mutable parameters and simple return types

- **Decision:** Extend the private test-only source builder to format `var` on named or `_` parameters and a return type that is one unqualified, nongeneric uppercase path segment. Keep `self` parameters, parameter attributes, qualified or generic types, clauses, comments, and unsupported bodies fail-closed. Preserve the existing width-driven parameter-list group and emit ` -> Type` on the function head.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.7.4 requires `var name: Type` and `) -> Ret` in the function head. This slice adds the supported modifier and return position while reusing the same guarded type grammar as simple parameter types; no line break is introduced where it could alter tokenization.
- **Review:** Tests cover `var` and `_` parameters, a simple return type, an arrow accepted on a new input line but normalized onto the head, return-type-driven width breaking, reparsing, CST preservation, and fixed-point output. Follow-up hostile reviews caught that the width case exceeded the limit even without its return type, that §14.7.4 omitted `_` parameters, and that its receiver forms were incomplete. The test now proves the no-return form fits on one line and the return suffix forces a break; the formatter rule includes `_`, `var _`, `self`, `var self`, and all three permitted receiver types. Final hostile review found no remaining issue. All 174 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; Clippy, formatting, and diff checks also pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that this intermediate formatter should accept `var` parameters and simple return types before support for compound types and clauses is complete.
- **Remaining scope:** Add self parameters, compound types, and signature clauses; continue through body and expression forms, comments, preservation verification, CLI integration, and implicit discovery.

### IR-349: Format simple self receivers

- **Decision:** Extend the private source-document slice to accept exactly one first `self` parameter with type `Self`, `borrow Self`, or `borrow mut Self`, optionally prefixed with `var`. Preserve its position before named parameters and format the type from its AST shape. Reject duplicate or later `self` parameters, parameter attributes, associated `Self` paths, and all other receiver types.
- **Reason:** The grammar and spec/05 §5.8.2 define self receivers and their allowed types. Recognizing the receiver AST node allows the formatter to canonicalize spacing in `borrow mut Self` while preserving every token and the receiver's parameter position.
- **Review:** The first hostile review found that a second `SelfParam` could be silently dropped because the AST accessor returns only the first. The builder now counts direct `SelfParam` children and refuses duplicates. Regressions cover duplicate and misplaced receivers, each supported receiver type with and without `var`, associated paths (including under `borrow`), receiver-driven list wrapping, spacing normalization, reparsing, CST preservation, and fixed-point output. A second hostile review found two test-coverage gaps: the original width case already exceeded the limit without a receiver, and plain `self: borrow mut Self` lacked coverage. The test now proves the no-receiver signature fits within the limit and the receiver makes it wrap, and exercises that receiver without `var`; the reviewer confirmed both fixes and found no remaining actionable issue. All 175 `sai_fmt` tests, the full workspace suite, Clippy, formatting, and diff checks pass. Receiver formatting is tested on isolated function nodes because enclosing impl, trait, and interface-resource items are not supported yet.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that the initial receiver support should be limited to the three specified forms and should remain available in the test-only builder as an isolated function signature until impl/trait/interface-resource item formatting is added.
- **Remaining scope:** Add compound types and signature clauses, then support enclosing impl/trait declarations; continue through body and expression forms, comments, verification, CLI integration, and implicit discovery.

### IR-350: Format compound parameter and return types

- **Decision:** Extend the private test-only type renderer recursively for qualified path types and per-segment generic arguments, `Self` associated paths, `borrow` and `owned` wrappers, tuple and grouped types, inferred `_`, type arguments, scalar constant arguments, and associated type bindings where the parser permits them. Canonicalize scalar integer spellings, use width-aware delimited lists with conditional trailing commas, and keep function-pointer types and clauses fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.5.3, 14.7.4, and 14.10.2 specify recursive type formatting, generic-list layout, and safe breaks. Using typed AST nodes keeps token ownership explicit and allows the renderer to preserve tuple grouping and singleton commas while normalizing only list layout and integer spelling.
- **Review:** Hostile review found two width failures: long inner types after `borrow`/`owned`, and inside grouping parentheses. The renderer now gives these wrappers safe, width-aware breaks; regressions use long names to check exact layouts, the 100-column bound, reparsing, CST preservation, and fixed-point output. A flat-layout regression caught that `SoftLine` removed the required space after `borrow mut` and `owned`; these separators now use `Line`, which prints a space flat and breaks only when needed. Nested paths, `borrow`/`owned`, tuples, grouped and inferred types, scalar const arguments, generic trailing-comma normalization, and long generic arguments in parameter and return positions are covered. Follow-up hostile review found no remaining actionable issues. All 178 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace suite pass; Clippy, formatting, and diff checks pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that this recursive type subset is a useful next source-formatter slice while function-pointer types, signature clauses, and enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource formatting remain deferred.
- **Remaining scope:** Add function-pointer types, function generic parameters and clauses, then enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations; continue with body and expression forms, comments, verification, CLI integration, and implicit discovery.

### IR-351: Format function-pointer types and effect sets

- **Decision:** Add `EffectsClause` as an AST child of `FnPtrType`, then extend the private source renderer for safe/unsafe function-pointer types, type parameter lists, optional return types, and effect sets with ordered effect paths. Use delimited-list groups for parameters and brace-list groups for effects, preserve empty `effects {}`, remove brace-list commas only in broken layouts, and leave function declaration clauses unsupported.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.5.3–14.5.4, 14.7.4, and 14.10.2 define pointer signatures and effect-set layout. The parser already builds effect clauses under `FN_PTR_TYPE`, but the AST schema omitted that child; adding it exposes the parsed structure without token-text guessing. Retaining effect order follows spec/08 and formatter §14.7.5.
- **Review:** Regressions cover safe and unsafe pointers, optional returns, empty and ordered effects, long parameter/effect lists, brace-list comma normalization, reparsing, CST preservation, fixed-point output, and refusal of function declaration clauses. Hostile review confirmed the generated `effects()` accessor and AST child counts, and found no actionable issues. All 180 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace suite pass; Clippy, formatting, and diff checks pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that function-pointer effect paths retain their written order, empty effect sets stay inline, and function-pointer support can precede general function declaration clauses.
- **Remaining scope:** Add function generic parameters and declaration clauses, then enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations; continue with body and expression forms, comments, verification, CLI integration, and implicit discovery.

### IR-352: Format function borrow and effect clauses

- **Decision:** Extend the private source-document slice to format function declaration `borrows` and `effects` clauses in the normative order, one indented clause per line, with the body opening brace at the function's base indentation. Format ordered provenance paths and effect paths with the shared width-aware brace-list renderer, omit commas in broken lists, and accept both comma- and newline-separated parsed lists. Continue to refuse function generics, `where`, `requires`, and `ensures`.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.5.4, 14.7.4, and 14.7.5 define brace-list layout, signature clause order, indentation, and body placement. The parser represents the braces, list separators, and provenance path segments as direct clause tokens and child nodes; checking those ownership boundaries before rendering avoids dropping syntax while allowing both separator forms accepted by the grammar. The source formatter is still a test-only prototype, so unsupported signature forms remain fail-closed until their complete layouts and preservation checks are implemented.
- **Review:** Regressions cover empty and nonempty borrow lists, `self` and qualified provenance paths, standalone and combined clauses, correcting reversed clause order, width-based broken lists without commas, reparsing, normalized CST preservation where order is unchanged, and fixed-point output. The hostile review checked parser clause ordering and provenance structure against the formatter rules and found no actionable issue. All 183 `sai_fmt` tests and all workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that implementing declaration `borrows` and `effects` before function generics, `where`, `requires`, and `ensures` is a useful increment, and that this prototype should canonicalize the two supported clauses while continuing to reject the other signature forms.
- **Remaining scope:** Add enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations, broader bodies and expressions, comments, preservation verification, public source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-353: Format unbounded function generic parameters

- **Decision:** Extend the private source-document slice to format ordered, unbounded type parameters and typed `const` parameters on function declarations. Use the documented `<…>` delimited-list renderer, including width breaks and conditional trailing commas, and reuse the recursive type renderer for const parameter types. Keep parameter attributes and type-parameter bounds fail-closed until their complete layouts are supported.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.5.3 and 14.7.4 specify generic parameter list spacing, width behavior, trailing commas, and placement before function parameters. The AST exposes type and const parameters in source order through `GenericParamList::params`; formatting from that sequence preserves interleaving. This narrow slice adds signatures such as `fn identity<T>(value: T) -> T` and `fn make<const N: USize>()` without partially formatting bounds.
- **Review:** Regressions cover generic type parameters, typed const parameters, their interleaving, generic names used in parameter and return types, canonical trailing-comma removal, the combination with supported function clauses, width-driven multiline lists, reparsing, normalized CST preservation, and fixed-point output. Hostile review found that an unbreakable long return type could force a short generic list onto multiple lines. The builder now evaluates the documented `fn name<…>(` prefix width independently and keeps the generic list flat whenever that prefix fits; regressions cover both long parameters and an overflowing return identifier. Follow-up hostile review confirmed the correction, checked flat-width overflow handling and long-list breaking, and found no further actionable issues. All 187 `sai_fmt` tests and all workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that unbounded type and const parameters are a useful first generic-function slice while generic bounds and attributes remain unsupported in the private prototype.
- **Remaining scope:** Add enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations, broader bodies and expressions, comments, preservation verification, public source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-354: Format generic trait and function bounds

- **Decision:** Extend the private function-signature renderer to format type-parameter bounds made of trait paths or `Fn`/`FnMut`/`FnOnce` sugar, including associated-type bindings and optional effect sets. Break a long `+`-separated bound list only after the plus, indenting the continuation one level. Continue to reject generic parameter attributes and the unsupported function clauses recorded in IR-352.
- **Reason:** Spec/03 §3.5.1 defines trait and function-trait bounds and their effect caps; formatter §§14.5.3 and 14.7.5 specify type lists, bound layout, and breaks after `+`. Existing recursive type, generic argument, function-pointer parameter, and effect-set renderers provide canonical nested formatting. Validating AST-owned tokens and children keeps bound separators and effects attached to their declared bound while retaining source order.
- **Review:** Focused regressions cover trait bounds, associated-type bindings, function-trait parameters and returns, bound effects, single- and multiple-parameter trailing-comma normalization, plus-separated lists, width-driven breaks after `+`, reparsing, normalized CST preservation, and fixed-point output. Hostile review found that valid function bounds with a trailing parameter comma were rejected; the renderer now accepts and canonicalizes that separator. Follow-up hostile review checked single- and multiple-parameter cases, bound order, effects, and line breaking and found no remaining P1–P3 issues. All 189 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that supporting trait and function-trait bounds with effect caps before generic attributes and `where` clauses is a useful formatter increment.
- **Remaining scope:** Add enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations, broader bodies and expressions, comments, preservation verification, public source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-355: Format function where clauses

- **Decision:** Add canonical `where` clause formatting to the private function-signature renderer. Keep fitting clauses flat; when the clause exceeds the line width, put `where` on its own continuation line and each predicate on a deeper continuation. Keep bounds in source order, wrap only after `+`, and preserve parentheses around bounds when present. Keep parser-recovered generic-parameter attributes fail-closed because the grammar defines them only as `INVALID_ATTRIBUTE_TARGET`.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.7.4–14.7.5 specify function clause order, continuation indentation, flat and broken where layouts, and bound breaks. The parser exposes predicate and bound nodes in source order while keeping wrapper parentheses as direct tokens on `WherePred`; validating the ordered child/token sequence lets the formatter retain parentheses needed for effect-capped bounds and reject malformed shapes. Generic-parameter attributes have no valid target in spec/15 §15.2.1, so formatting them is not a supported source form.
- **Review:** Focused regressions cover flat predicates, multi-predicate width breaks, plus-separated bounds, parenthesized function bounds with effect caps, canonical clause order with `borrows` and `effects`, reparsing, normalized CST shape, and fixed-point output. Hostile review checked parser/AST ownership, parenthesized bounds and effect caps, refusal behavior, line breaks, and clause ordering and found no actionable P1–P3 issues. All 191 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that parser-recovered attributes on generic parameters should remain fail-closed because they always produce `INVALID_ATTRIBUTE_TARGET`, and that parentheses around where bounds should be preserved as written.
- **Remaining scope:** Add enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations, broader bodies and expressions, comments, preservation verification, public source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-356: Format basic function contract clauses

- **Decision:** Format `requires` and `ensures` in canonical order after `where`, `borrows`, and `effects`, retaining the relative order of repeated clauses. Support literals, names, format strings, field access, generic and positional calls, method chains, and comparison expressions. Break an overlong comparison at its operator using safe parentheses when needed. For member chains, collect call-bearing `.lident` segments, retain intervening and trailing fields as segments, and attach `.UIDENT` operations to the preceding segment. Keep other expression forms fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.7.4–14.7.5 requires each contract on its own continuation line, keeps the expression on the keyword's line, and delegates expression breaks to §14.9. The typed AST lets the prototype canonicalize clause order while retaining expression structure. Inserted parentheses make top-level comparison breaks safe. A document-local group allocator keeps comparison, parenthesis, and member-chain layout state distinct; the chain segmentation follows N-H-50 (§14.9.5).
- **Review:** Regressions cover repeated and out-of-order contracts, literal and format-string expressions, generic methods, empty call arguments, fields and calls between member-chain segments, trailing fields, `.UIDENT` field and call joins, one-segment versus multi-segment chains, protected and explicit-parenthesis comparisons, inserted comparison parentheses, reparsing, normalized CST shape, and fixed-point output. Hostile review found and drove fixes for generic method arguments, empty argument-list layout, format strings, member-chain segmentation, shared group IDs, final fields, and `.UIDENT` joins and call-bearing segment counts. The final hostile pass found no actionable issues. All 200 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that this initial contract-expression subset should remain fail-closed for other valid expressions until their layouts are implemented.
- **Remaining scope:** Expand operator and expression forms, then enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations, broader bodies, comments, preservation verification, production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-357: Format binary operator chains

- **Decision:** Extend the private contract-expression renderer across every `BinOp` precedence level in spec/03 §3.13.1; keep the separately represented `RangeExpr` fail-closed for a later step. Flatten each maximal same-precedence chain iteratively, retain each written operator and operand in source order, and reject a comparison tree with more than one comparison operator. Break a chain before every operator; use inserted parentheses for broken unprotected chains and indented continuation lines in protected or explicitly parenthesized contexts. Allocate each expression group a distinct per-document ID.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.9.2–14.9.3 defines the precedence groups, non-associative comparisons, safe operator break locations, and context-dependent layouts. A work stack avoids recursion proportional to source chain length, which can be large under the 16 MiB input limit. Reusing the typed AST and exact operator tokens preserves precedence and source spelling; the separate `RangeExpr` node can be implemented independently.
- **Review:** Regressions cover mixed precedence, mixed operators at one level, logical and bitwise operators, flat output, inserted-parenthesis breaks, protected and explicit-parenthesis layouts, reparsing, syntax-shape preservation, fixed-point formatting, and a 4096-operand chain. Hostile review found that recursive flattening could exhaust the stack on long valid input; the collector now uses an explicit work stack. Final hostile review found no actionable issues. All 203 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that malformed chained comparisons should remain fail-closed when parser recovery produces more than one comparison operator in the same tree.
- **Remaining scope:** Add prefix and range expressions, postfix and primary expression forms, broader function bodies, enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations, comments, preservation verification, production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-358: Format prefix and range expressions

- **Decision:** Format all six prefix operators using the canonical spacing of §14.9.1, flatten prefix chains iteratively, and format `RangeExpr` with its written `..` or `..=` token and optional endpoints without spaces around the operator. Give range endpoints ordinary expression formatting. When a prefix or range is the entire contents of explicit parentheses, treat its nested operands as protected rather than as the parenthesized expression itself. Keep malformed or recovered operator shapes fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.9.1–14.9.3 define prefix spacing, range adjacency, endpoint breaks, and the rule that a chain nested inside parentheses is protected even when the surrounding expression includes a prefix or a range. Iterative prefix collection avoids adding formatter recursion proportional to unary-chain length. Typed AST children and the exact range token preserve operator and endpoint order.
- **Review:** Tests cover each prefix operator, canonical whitespace, a 256-operator prefix chain, exclusive and inclusive ranges, open endpoints and a fully open range, endpoint precedence, endpoint chains that require inserted parentheses, explicit-parenthesis indentation, reparsing, syntax-shape preservation, and fixed-point formatting. The first hostile pass found that `ParenthesizedWhole` leaked through prefix and range wrappers; the renderer now maps their nested expressions to `Protected`, with regressions for both cases. The hostile follow-up found no actionable issues. All 208 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that the private formatter should canonicalize the supported prefix and range forms while continuing to fail closed for other valid expression shapes and parser-recovered malformed ranges.
- **Remaining scope:** Add postfix and primary expression forms, broader function bodies, enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations, comments, preservation verification, production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-359: Format tuple, array, and postfix expressions

- **Decision:** Add unit, tuple, array, implicit-member, tuple-index, indexing, `?`, and `.await` expression formatting to the private source builder. Use the shared delimited-list renderer for tuples and arrays, retaining the singleton-tuple comma and adding trailing commas only for broken lists. Keep index contents protected and attach all postfix operators directly to their operand. Extend member-chain collection across `.await`, `.INT`, indexing, and `?`: `.await` and `.INT` start segments, while indexing and `?` attach to the preceding segment or to the head before the first call. Continue to reject named and record-update call arguments.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.5.3 and 14.9.1–14.9.5 define list layout, postfix attachment, implicit members, index protection, and the complete set of member-chain segments. Typed AST ownership checks distinguish parentheses that group one expression from tuple delimiters, validate tuple-index spellings, and ensure only the index interior can break. Keeping intervening postfix operations in the member-chain group ensures the whole chain's width is measured and all safe segment breaks are applied.
- **Review:** Regressions cover unit and empty arrays, singleton and multi-element tuples, flat and broken arrays, implicit-member calls, tuple indexing, indexing with an operator chain, `?`, `.await`, and long chains that combine pre-call indexing, method calls, post-call indexing, `?`, `.await`, `.INT`, and a later method call. Additional cases distinguish `.await` and `.INT` on a call head from those before the first call on a name head. Tests also cover reparsing, normalized syntax shape, fixed-point output, and the 100-column bound. The hostile pass found that postfix operations could escape the member-chain width group; they are now collected with their segments or attached suffixes. The hostile follow-up found no actionable findings. Validation passed: 212 `sai_fmt` tests, full workspace tests, Clippy with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check`.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that staging the expression formatter in small supported subsets is appropriate; named and record-update arguments are covered by IR-360, while other expression forms remain fail-closed.
- **Remaining scope:** Broaden function bodies, support enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations, comments, preservation verification, production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-360: Format named and record-update call arguments

- **Decision:** Render positional, named, and record-update arguments in their original order. Keep named labels and their source spelling, format each value in a protected context, require `..base` to be the final item, and omit its trailing comma when the list breaks.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.5.3 and 14.9.4 define argument list layout and the special final record-update rule. Walking typed AST children in source order preserves mixed argument order. The parser reports non-final base arguments; the formatter also checks the position so recovered or unexpected shapes fail closed.
- **Review:** Tests cover mixed positional and named arguments, record updates in both flat and broken lists, trailing-comma removal, argument order, reparsing, normalized syntax shape, fixed-point output, and the 100-column bound. ### IR-361: Format basic function-body statements

- **Decision:** Render nonempty function blocks as one statement per indented line, preserving at most one blank line between statements. Support simple binding `let` and `var` declarations, assignments, `assert` statements, and expression statements. Use the documented right-hand-side choices for supported expressions; keep destructuring patterns, `let … else`, and unsupported expression forms fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.5.5, 14.8.1–14.8.3, and 14.9.7 require broken block bodies, safe statement separators, blank-line preservation, and width-aware right-hand sides. Restricting bindings to a simple identifier avoids partially formatting patterns; typed AST accessors plus direct-token checks make unsupported or recovered shapes fail closed.

Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 215 `sai_fmt` tests and full workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.

### IR-362: Format return, break, and continue expressions

- **Decision:** Format `return`, `break`, and `continue`, preserving optional labels and values. Keep values after `return` and `break` on the keyword line while allowing the value expression to break internally according to its own rules.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.9.8 rule 6 defines these forms and explicitly forbids moving their values to a continuation line. Typed AST accessors preserve optional fields; direct-token checks reject malformed recovered shapes.
- **Review:** Tests cover each keyword with and without a value or label, preserve label spelling, reparse and compare syntax shape, check fixed-point output, and verify that an overlong return value remains on the keyword line. ### IR-363: Format block and conditional expressions

- **Decision:** Format ordinary block expressions and `if` expressions with ordinary expression conditions, including recursively nested `else if` and final `else` blocks. Keep `else` on the line of the preceding closing brace. Continue to reject `if let` conditions.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.8.1 and 14.9.8 prescribe broken block layout and the same-line `else` form. The typed AST distinguishes ordinary and let conditions; the expression-only subset can be rendered without pattern formatting or semantic analysis. Conditions use the unprotected expression context so a binary chain that breaks receives the documented parentheses before the following block brace.
- **Review:** Tests cover `if` with and without `else`, `else if`, empty and nonempty blocks, a source newline before `else`, ordinary block expressions, let initializers, call arguments, long-condition wrapping, reparsing, fixed-point output, and fail-closed `if let`. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 219 `sai_fmt` tests and full workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that supporting expression conditions before `if let` is an appropriate staged formatter boundary.
- **Remaining scope:** Add if-let, loop, match, closure, and unsafe/async/region expressions; destructuring and let-else statements; enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations; comments; preservation verification; production source formatting; CLI integration; and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-364: Format `loop` expressions

- **Decision:** Format labeled and unlabeled `loop` expressions, with the loop body rendered using the shared block formatter. Preserve the label spelling and put the loop keyword on the same line as the label, even when the source breaks after the label's colon.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.9.8 rule 4 specifies the canonical label and loop layout, and §14.8.1 requires loop blocks to use the broken block form. The AST provides a separate `LoopLabel` node, allowing the formatter to validate the label and colon without parsing label text or resolving its target.

### IR-365: Format `while` expressions

The existing `Condition` wrapper distinguishes expression conditions from let conditions, and sharing its renderer with `if` keeps those layout guarantees consistent.

### IR-366: Format `for` expressions with simple patterns

- **Decision:** Format labeled and unlabeled `for` expressions whose pattern is a simple binding or `_`. Binding modes `var`, `borrow`, and `borrow mut` are retained. Format `for await` syntax as written for source preservation, while leaving its Stage 5 feature validation to the semantic phase. Keep destructuring and other pattern forms fail-closed.

### IR-367: Format `async` and `region` block expressions

- **Decision:** Format `async {}`, `async move {}`, and `region name {}` expressions, using the shared block formatter for their bodies. Keep plain `region` paths as ordinary path expressions.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.8.1 and 14.9.8 rule 7 require broken block layout for these forms. The async AST exposes its optional `move` token, while the contextual `region` prefix is recognized only from a non-raw `LIDENT` spelled `region` followed by a binding name and block. Token checks preserve that boundary without reserving the word.

### IR-368: Format single-expression `unsafe` blocks

- **Decision:** Format an `unsafe` block with exactly one expression statement as `unsafe { expr }` when its complete flat form fits; otherwise use the broken block layout. Keep empty blocks compact and blocks with declarations or multiple statements broken. When measuring the one-line alternative, recursively select the flat branch of nested document choices so a nested block cannot hide a multiline fallback from the width check.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.8.1 rule 3 gives single-expression unsafe blocks the same fit-based exception as closures, while rule 4 keeps empty blocks as `{}`. The nested-choice rule is needed because a document choice can otherwise make an enclosing flat candidate appear to fit while the nested expression later chooses a broken layout.
- **Review:** Tests cover empty, short single-expression, nested short, declaration, multiple-statement, overlong single-expression, and nested overlong blocks, with reparsing, syntax-shape preservation, and fixed-point formatting. The first hostile review found that a nested `Choice` could conceal an inner multiline fallback and cause the enclosing unsafe expression to select a broken inner form on its purportedly flat line. The renderer now measures the recursively first (flat) choice branch; the regression confirms both nested blocks break when the complete flat form exceeds the width. Hostile follow-up review found no actionable issues. All 226 `sai_fmt` tests and full workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that empty unsafe blocks should remain compact and that only a single expression statement qualifies for the fit-based flat form.
- **Remaining scope:** Add match and closure expressions, if-let and while-let, other pattern forms, destructuring and let-else statements, enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations, comments, preservation verification, production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-369: Format `if let` and `while let` with simple patterns

- **Decision:** Extend `if` and `while` condition rendering to accept let conditions whose pattern is a simple binding or `_`, reusing the existing binding-mode renderer. Format the initializer with the ordinary unprotected expression context so broken operator chains receive the required parentheses before the condition's block. Keep other patterns fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.9.8 rules 1, 2, and 4 give let conditions the same keyword-line and block placement as expression conditions. The parser exposes pattern and initializer separately, so a conservative pattern subset can be emitted without flattening a larger pattern tree that has no formatter yet.
- **Review:** Tests cover binding and wildcard patterns, `borrow mut`, an `else if` chain, labeled `while let`, long initializers that break with parentheses, reparsing, syntax-shape preservation, fixed-point formatting, and fail-closed destructuring patterns. All 229 `sai_fmt` tests and full workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.

### IR-370: Format closure expressions and hugged call arguments

- **Decision:** Format `fn` and `move fn` closures with supported parameters (`name`, `name: Type`, `var name`, and `_`) and optional return types. A closure body with one expression uses a flat alternative only when the entire closure fits; otherwise it uses the broken block form. For a final positional or named closure argument, try the fully flat call, then a hugged form with flat preceding arguments and closure header plus a broken body, then the ordinary broken list. Do not hug a closure that is not the final argument.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.8.1, 14.9.5, and 14.9.6 define the fit-based closure exception and three call-argument layouts. Measuring the whole flat closure avoids breaking only its parameter list when the body is the part that exceeds the width. Testing the hugged alternative through its opening brace selects it only when the first line fits.
- **Review:** Tests cover move and ordinary closures, supported parameter/return-type forms, empty and single-expression bodies, multi-statement bodies, long single-expression bodies, final positional and named closures, a non-final closure, hug-to-broken fallback, and member-chain interactions where a forced closure body is in the direct or parenthesized head, a non-final segment, or the final segment. Tests also cover reparsing, syntax-shape preservation where no permitted parentheses are inserted, fixed-point output, and line width. Hostile review requested the fallback and member-chain cases, then found that a forced closure in a call head was omitted from the chain decision. The chain now scans the head and its descendants. A final hostile review found no actionable issues and confirmed that final-segment hugging remains local to its argument list. The final-segment regression exposed double indentation when a closure body broke inside an otherwise flat chain; separator indentation is now scoped to the break, while segment indentation is conditional on the chain group being broken. All 240 `sai_fmt` tests, full workspace tests, warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that the first closure pass should limit parameters to the listed forms, apply hugging only when the closure is the final argument, and retain the extra continuation indentation produced for a forced closure inside an author-written parenthesized chain head.
- **Remaining scope:** Expand closure parameter/type support, add let-else statements, enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations, comments, preservation verification, production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-371: Format patterns in conditions, loops, and let statements

- **Decision:** Format all pattern forms in §14.10.1, including bindings and modes, `@`, or-patterns, literals and ranges, tuples, parentheses, slices with optional rest bindings, constructor patterns with positional/named/rest arguments, and implicit-member patterns. Remove an optional leading `|`, preserve a singleton tuple comma, and omit a trailing comma after a final constructor rest pattern. Use the renderer in `if let`, `while let`, `for`, and ordinary `let` statements; keep `let … else` fail-closed.
- **Reason:** The formatter's pattern grammar already defines canonical spacing and list layout. Reusing one recursive renderer removes the earlier condition/loop subset and formats destructuring consistently. `let … else` has a separate block-layout rule, so it remains outside this pattern-only step.
- **Review:** Tests cover every pattern family, binding modes, empty and singleton tuples, named constructor fields, slice and constructor rest patterns, inclusive and open-ended ranges, leading-pipe removal, list and or-pattern width breaks, reparsing, syntax-shape preservation after permitted separator changes, and fixed-point output. Hostile review found the implementation-status summary stale and it has been updated. The reviewer retracted its slice-comma finding after checking the shared delimited-list helper: broken lists already select the trailing-comma branch, and a regression test confirms the comma for a broken single-element slice. An 86-character repeat produced a 101-column element line because an unbreakable token and its required comma cannot fit; the regression uses a 91-character binding that stays within the line limit. The final review noted that the exclusive open range `10..` lacked a direct regression case; the `if let` pattern matrix now formats, reparses, and checks fixed-point output for it. All 242 `sai_fmt` tests and full workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that the general pattern renderer should also expand `if let`, `while let`, and `for` beyond the basic subset and support destructuring `let` statements without `else`; confirm that `let … else` remains deferred.
- **Remaining scope:** Add `let … else`, broader statement forms, enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations, comments, preservation verification, production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-372: Format match expressions and arm lists

- **Decision:** Format the match scrutinee on the keyword line, then format arms as a brace list. An arm formats its pattern, optional `if` guard, and `=>` right-hand side. Block-like right-hand sides stay on the arm line and force the arm list to break. In a broken list, parenthesize an open-ended range body only when another arm follows it.
- **Reason:** This follows §§14.5.4, 14.9.7–14.9.8, and N-H-59. The shared brace-list document already supplies the flat comma-separated and broken comma-free layouts. Rendering block-like right-hand sides without an outer flat choice allows their forced line breaks to propagate to the list group.
- **Review:** Tests cover empty and flat lists, trailing commas, guards, destructuring and leading-pipe patterns, long scrutinees, width breaks, nested forced blocks, moved long right-hand sides, non-final versus final open-ended ranges, and value-preserving multiline simple, triple-quoted, raw, and byte strings. Hostile review found and drove fixes for multiline literals being rejected, nested forced blocks not breaking their arm list, CRLF width accounting, continued byte strings, and generated spaces before CRLF; follow-up review found no remaining issues. Its trailing-comma finding was retracted after a dedicated regression confirmed the existing separator loop accepts it. All 250 `sai_fmt` tests and `cargo test --workspace` pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that block-like arm bodies should force the entire arm list to break, and that only non-final open-ended range bodies need formatter-inserted parentheses.
- **Remaining scope:** Add `let … else`, broader statement forms, enclosing impl/trait/interface-resource declarations, comments, preservation verification, production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-373: Format `let … else` statements

- **Decision:** Format `let` statements with the shared pattern renderer and optional type annotation, then append an else block after the initializer. Keep `else {` on the initializer's final line. Include the else suffix in the initializer's right-hand-side alternatives so a moved initializer carries the block's indentation and the full line width accounts for `else {`.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.8.1, 14.8.2, and 14.9.7 require the canonical broken block and keep `else {` attached to the initializer. Scoping the suffix with the selected right-hand-side alternative preserves that placement while keeping the else block nested under a moved initializer.
- **Review:** Tests cover ordinary and typed destructuring patterns, a newline before `else`, an `if` initializer with its own `else`, and moved flat or broken initializers, including cases where the trailing `else {` crosses the line-width limit. They check reparsing, syntax-shape preservation, fixed-point output, and line width. Hostile review found that a moved initializer initially left the else block at the outer indentation; the suffix now participates in the right-hand-side choice, and regressions check the block body and closing brace. A final hostile pass found no remaining actionable issues. All 250 `sai_fmt` tests and `cargo test --workspace` pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that a moved initializer should carry the `else` block with it, indenting the block relative to the continuation line, and that optional type annotations remain supported on `let … else`.

### IR-374: Format function modifiers and bodyless signatures

- **Decision:** Format function heads with optional `pub` or `export` visibility, then `unsafe`, then `async`, before `fn`. Accept an optional body; when absent, end the document after the head or the final signature clause.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.7.4 defines this modifier order and explicitly permits bodyless trait, interface, and extern signatures. Clause layout continues to follow §14.7.5.
- **Review:** Tests cover `pub async`, `export unsafe async`, bodyless signatures with return types, and bodyless signatures with contract clauses. They check reparsing, syntax-shape preservation where applicable, and fixed-point output. The metadata now comes from the rendered function document; a regression covers a long modifier-prefixed parameter list followed by another function. Final hostile review found no remaining actionable issues. All 251 `sai_fmt` tests and `cargo test --workspace` pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that a bodyless signature should be retained and formatted whenever represented by a `FnDecl`, with any context-specific validity reported separately.
- **Remaining scope:** Add impl, trait, and interface-resource wrappers, comments, preservation verification, production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-375: Format inherent, trait, and interface impl declarations

- **Decision:** Extend the private source document builder to format `unsafe? impl<G> Trait for Type`, `impl<G> Type`, and `impl iface` declarations. Render optional `where` clauses on their own indented line with the opening brace on the next line. Always break nonempty impl member bodies, preserve member order and at most one blank line between members, and format associated type definitions and function declarations. Validate direct tokens, child ranges, target roles, brace tokens, and module-path segment order before emitting output. Keep declaration/member docs and attributes fail-closed while comment and attribute formatting remain unsupported.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.7.6 requires canonical layout for each impl target, broken nonempty member bodies, and retained blank-line gaps. Spec/03 §3.7 distinguishes the three target shapes and limits impl members to associated type definitions and functions. Reusing the existing type, where-clause, function, and block renderers keeps nested formatting consistent. Explicit role/range checks prevent similar AST node kinds or recovered shapes from being confused; rejecting annotations avoids dropping syntax that this slice cannot yet render.
