- **Review:** Tests cover inherent, generic trait, and unsafe interface impls; generic targets with associated-type bindings; where clauses; methods, receivers, associated type definitions, empty bodies, reversed member order, preserved blank lines, fail-closed annotations, reparsing, normalized syntax shape, and fixed-point output. The first focused run exposed that braces belong to the child `ItemList`, not the `ImplDecl` direct-token stream; validation was corrected to check each node at its owning CST level. Clippy then identified four needless borrows, which were removed. Hostile review of the latest code found no actionable issues. The 253-test `sai_fmt` suite, full workspace tests, warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting check, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that semantically invalid `pub` on a trait or interface impl method should retain its spelling for diagnostics, and that docs/attributes on impl declarations and members should remain fail-closed until their formatting rules are implemented.
- **Remaining scope:** Add trait and interface-resource wrappers, then comment support, source-preservation verification, production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-376: Format trait declarations and associated type declarations

- **Decision:** Extend the private source formatter for `pub? unsafe? trait Name<G>: Bounds`, optional `where` clauses, associated type declarations, and bodyless or default method declarations. Share the validated `ItemList` body renderer with impl declarations so nonempty trait bodies always break and retain member order and blank-line gaps. Keep docs and attributes fail-closed while comment/attribute layout remains unimplemented. Update formatter §14.7.6 to include optional `pub` visibility.
- **Reason:** Spec/03 §§3.3 and 3.7 permit `pub` on traits and forbid visibility markers on trait items; spec/04 §4.6.2 says a trait's visibility governs all its items. Formatter §14.7.6 requires broken member bodies and §14.7.5 defines the shared `where` layout. The previous formatter table omitted `pub` even though the grammar accepts it, so preserving visibility requires correcting that row. `export trait` remains a parser refusal under the grammar's `export` restriction.
- **Review:** Tests cover public and unsafe traits, generic parameters, supertrait bounds, `where` clauses, bounded associated types, bodyless methods with their own clauses, default methods, empty bodies, preserved member blank lines, fail-closed annotations, syntax-shape preservation, fixed-point output, line width, and a wrapped header followed by another item. Hostile review found no actionable findings in the implementation or shared item-list renderer. All 256 `sai_fmt` tests and full workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the formatter-table correction for `pub` visibility and the existing parser refusal for `export trait`; confirm that trait/member docs and attributes remain fail-closed until their canonical comment and attribute layout is available.
- **Remaining scope:** Add interface-resource wrappers, then comment support, source-preservation verification, production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-377: Format interfaces and interface resources

- **Decision:** Format `pub? interface name` declarations containing function heads and interface resources. Format a resource without a body as `resource Name`; when a body is present, render it as a broken item list, including `{}` for an explicitly empty body. Preserve the order of interface members and resource methods. Validate each contextual `interface` and `resource` token by both its `LIDENT` kind and exact spelling, plus the AST child ranges and brace tokens. Keep interface type/enum members, docs, and attributes fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Spec/03 §3.9 defines the interface/resource member sets and makes both keywords contextual identifiers. Formatter §§14.5.5 and 14.7.6 require broken nonempty bodies and `{}` for empty bodies; retaining an explicit empty resource body also avoids deleting brace tokens outside the formatter's permitted token changes. Reusing the function and `ItemList` renderers gives function heads and nested bodies the same canonical spacing and blank-line behavior.
- **Review:** Tests cover public interfaces, bodyless interface functions, resources with no body, explicitly empty resource bodies, method-bearing resources, unsupported type members, fail-closed attributes, reparsing, syntax-shape preservation, and fixed-point output. The initial compile check caught that these contextual keywords are emitted as `LIDENT`, not dedicated keyword token kinds; validation now checks both token kind and spelling. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 258 `sai_fmt` tests and full workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm preserving the distinction between a resource with no body and a resource with an explicit empty `{}` body, and confirm that interface record and enum members plus docs/attributes should remain fail-closed until their renderers are implemented.
- **Superseded:** IR-378 adds plain type-alias members; record and enum members remain fail-closed.

### IR-378: Format plain type aliases

- **Decision:** Format top-level and interface plain type aliases, including visibility and generic parameters, by reusing the existing type renderer. Keep record declarations, refined aliases, interface enums, docs, and attributes fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Spec/03 §3.6 defines aliases separately from record bodies, and §3.9 permits aliases inside interfaces while excluding refined aliases. The formatter already renders the alias right-hand-side type forms, but record fields and refinement predicates have separate layout rules and need their own validated renderers.
- **Review:** Tests cover simple and generic top-level aliases, tuple and generic right-hand-side types, interface-member order with aliases, long unbreakable right-hand sides, line width, top-level separation, parse-shape preservation, fixed-point output, and fail-closed records, refinements, enums, and attributes. The first hostile review found that alias right-hand sides did not use the §14.9.7 move alternatives; the renderer now shares the movable right-hand-side layout and a regression covers the width and item-separation failure. The follow-up hostile review found no remaining actionable issues. All 261 `sai_fmt` tests, full workspace tests, warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that plain aliases should be supported before record declarations and refined aliases, and that annotation/comment handling should remain fail-closed until those formatting rules are implemented.
- **Superseded:** IR-379 adds record declarations and fields.

### IR-379: Format record type declarations

- **Decision:** Format top-level and interface record declarations with optional visibility, generic parameters, `where` clauses, and fields with optional `pub`. Always break nonempty field lists, print empty records as `{}`, remove field-separator commas, and preserve at most one blank line between fields. Keep declaration/field docs and attributes fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Spec/03 §§3.6 and 3.9 define record and interface type shapes; formatter §§14.5.4, 14.6.4, and 14.7.3 require declaration field lists to stay broken, preserve blank-line gaps, and move a `where` clause to its own continuation line. Strict child-range and token checks prevent recovered or annotated field shapes from being emitted incompletely.
- **Review:** Tests cover public and generic records, fields with and without `pub`, comma removal, field blank-line preservation, a `where` clause, compact empty records, interface records, reparsing, separator-normalized syntax shape, and fixed-point output; records with attributes remain fail-closed. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 262 `sai_fmt` tests and full workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that declaration fields should be kept on separate lines even when the whole record is short, that empty records should remain compact as `{}`, and that fields with docs or attributes should remain fail-closed until those rules are implemented.

### IR-380: Format enum declarations and variants

- **Decision:** Format top-level and interface enums with visibility, generic parameters, and `where` clauses. Support unit, tuple-payload, and named-payload variants plus optional signed integer discriminants. Always break nonempty variant lists, preserve one blank line between variants when present in the input, and remove outer variant separator commas. Normalize discriminant integer literals with the shared integer formatter, including uppercase hexadecimal digits. Keep declaration/variant docs and attributes fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Spec/03 §§3.6 and 3.9 define enum declarations and their interface use; formatter §§14.5.3–14.7.3 require delimited payload lists, broken variant lists, and empty `{}` bodies. §14.6.4 keeps blank lines between format units such as variants, while removing them inside delimited payload lists. §14.1.3 applies hexadecimal digit normalization to integer tokens regardless of their syntactic role. The discriminant wrapper owns the `=` sign, optional minus token, and integer token, so validation checks those direct tokens before formatting them.
- **Review:** Tests cover generic and public top-level enums, `where` clauses, unit/tuple/record variants, signed discriminants, hexadecimal normalization, empty enums, interface enums, wrapped payload layout, fail-closed docs/attributes, parse-shape preservation, and fixed-point output. The first hostile review found that blank lines were incorrectly preserved inside wrapped tuple and record payload lists; this was removed, and regression coverage verifies those gaps disappear. A source audit also caught the need to apply shared hexadecimal normalization to discriminants. Follow-up hostile review found no remaining actionable issues. All 265 `sai_fmt` tests and workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the supported variant shapes, canonical hexadecimal discriminants, and that enum and variant docs/attributes should remain fail-closed until their layout rules are implemented.

### IR-381: Format distinct type declarations

- **Decision:** Format top-level plain `distinct` declarations with optional visibility, generic parameters, and optional `pub` on the base type. Use the shared type renderer and right-hand-side layout, including moving long bases to the continuation line. Keep refined distinct types, docs, and attributes fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Spec/03 §3.6 and formatter §14.7.3 define distinct type declarations. Formatter §14.9.7 applies its five right-hand-side alternatives after `=`, and the parser marks contextual `distinct` as an `LIDENT`, so the renderer verifies its exact spelling along with the direct `=` and optional inner `pub` tokens.
- **Review:** Tests cover outer and base-type visibility, generic plain distinct types, moved long bases, item separation, parse-shape preservation, fixed-point output, and fail-closed refinements and annotations. Hostile review found no actionable issues in contextual-keyword handling, child/token order, visibility, or RHS layout. All 267 `sai_fmt` tests and workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the formatting of base-type `pub`, moving a long base type, and keeping refinements and annotations fail-closed until their rules are implemented.
- **Remaining scope:** Add refinement clauses and top-level resource types, then declaration docs and attributes, comment support, source-preservation verification, production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-382: Format top-level resource declarations

- **Decision:** Format top-level resources with optional visibility and an optional field list. Reuse record field formatting, preserve the distinction between a resource with no field body and one with an empty `{}` body, and keep resource and field docs/attributes fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Spec/03 §3.6 and formatter §14.7.3 rule 5 define resource declarations; nonempty resource field lists are always broken and use the shared field layout, while an omitted body and an explicit empty body are distinct syntax shapes. `resource` is contextual and is validated as an exact `LIDENT`.
- **Review:** Tests cover public resources with fields, blank-line preservation between fields, empty resources, resources without field bodies, parse-shape preservation, and fixed-point output; declaration docs/attributes remain fail-closed. Hostile review found no actionable issues in keyword validation, visibility, body-shape distinction, or shared field rendering. All 268 `sai_fmt` tests and workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm visibility, optional field-list behavior, preservation of omitted versus empty bodies, and fail-closed docs/attributes.
- **Remaining scope:** Add refinement clauses, then declaration docs and attributes, comment support, source-preservation verification, production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-383: Format refined aliases and distinct types

- **Decision:** Format refinement predicates on top-level aliases and distinct types, including generic forms retained by the parser for later validation. Put `where` on the type line when the complete refined right-hand side fits; otherwise keep the base type on that line when possible and move `where` to a four-space continuation. If the base type itself needs to move or break, keep its own layout and put `where` on the following continuation. Preserve unavoidable overflow from an unbreakable token. Reuse the same alias renderer inside interfaces when the parser produces the complete syntax tree. Keep declaration docs and attributes fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.7.3 and 14.9.7 define refined aliases and distinct types, and §14.2.2 keeps semantic validation diagnostics from refusing otherwise formattable syntax. The parser exposes the refinement clause as a distinct child, so the formatter validates its ordered child range and `where` token before rendering its predicate. Spec/03 §3.9 excludes refined types from interfaces; the source formatter retains a parser-complete alias spelling there so formatting does not discard syntax needed for diagnostics.
- **Review:** Tests cover flat and continuation predicates, exact 100/101-column selection, generic refined aliases and distinct types, interface aliases, a broken tuple base, a moved broken generic base, unavoidable overflow, reparsing, syntax-shape preservation, and fixed-point output. Hostile review initially found missing coverage for broken and moved-broken right-hand sides; regressions now cover those branches and the overflow fallback. Follow-up hostile review found no actionable issues.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the `where` continuation placement, preservation of generic refined declarations for diagnostics, and formatting parser-complete refined alias syntax inside interfaces even though the language specification disallows it there. Confirm declaration docs and attributes should remain fail-closed until their formatting rules are implemented.
- **Superseded:** IR-384 implements outer docs and attributes for supported declarations and members; comment handling remains fail-closed.

### IR-384: Format declaration docs and attributes

- **Decision:** Format outer docs and attributes on supported declarations, impl/trait/interface members, record/resource fields, and enum variants. Keep doc text and remove trailing ASCII spaces and tabs. Format attribute arguments from the grammar's positional and named forms, including nested lists, identifiers, strings, and integers; use the shared delimited-list layout and spacing around `=`. Continue to refuse ordinary comment trivia, module-header docs, demoted docs, generic-parameter attributes, and other unsupported shapes.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.6.5, 14.7.1, and 14.7.3 specify docs and attribute placement, text trimming, nested argument spacing, and field/variant prefixes. Spec/15 §15.2.1 defines the accepted attribute argument forms and distinguishes syntax preservation from semantic target diagnostics. Strict child-range, direct-token, and name-kind checks ensure each prefix is emitted exactly once; preserving syntactically valid but semantically invalid attributes keeps diagnostics available.
- **Review:** Tests cover multiple outer doc lines, trailing whitespace, NBSP preservation, attributes on aliases, records, fields, resources, refined/distinct types, enums, variants, functions, and supported impl/trait/interface members; nested, named, string, integer, empty, and width-broken attribute arguments; source trailing-comma normalization; comment-trivia refusal; reparsing; normalized syntax shape; and fixed-point output. Hostile review found two test/normalization gaps: Unicode `trim_end()` could remove NBSP from doc text, and `ATTR_ARGS` was missing from trailing-comma shape normalization. Both were fixed with regressions; follow-up review found no remaining actionable issues. The 275-test `sai_fmt` suite and full workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that doc trailing whitespace means ASCII spaces and tabs, preserving non-ASCII whitespace as comment text; confirm the supported annotation subset while comment trivia, module docs, generic-parameter attributes, and variant payload-field attributes remain fail-closed.
- **Remaining scope:** Add ordinary comment formatting and source-preservation verification, then production source formatting, CLI integration, and implicit workspace discovery.

### IR-385: Expose and verify the source formatter API

- **Decision:** Promote the existing source document builder from test-only code into the production library and add `format_source`. Reparse the result, compare iterative CST normal forms, compare normalized comment multisets, and require byte-identical second-pass output. Return a distinct `UnsupportedShape` error for valid syntax outside the current builder subset.
- **Reason:** The document builder already covers a substantial subset but had no production caller. Exposing it with verification makes that work usable while ensuring output syntax, permitted token/layout changes, and fixed-point behavior are checked. The normative error list does not include an unsupported-subset variant; reporting that known implementation boundary separately avoids misclassifying valid unsupported syntax as an internal compiler failure.
- **Review:** Hostile review found and drove fixes for output parse failures being over-classified, missing syntax-difference offsets, and output-size refusals being misreported as syntax errors. Output internal errors are preserved, repeat-pass refusal is reported as an idempotency failure, and normal-form events retain input byte offsets mapped through removed BOMs; focused regressions cover lexical/parser/size classification, same-length and length-mismatch offsets, BOM mapping, and closure-parameter trailing commas. The final hostile pass found no actionable issues. Review also confirmed that valid comments, shebangs, unsupported items, and `use` sorting remain outside the current subset; §14.2.2 explicitly identifies this as a conformance gap, not a normative refusal. All 289 `sai_fmt` tests and full workspace tests pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the temporary `SourceFormatError::UnsupportedShape` API extension and the use of ASCII spaces/tabs when normalizing trailing comment and doc whitespace. Ordinary comment trivia remains refused, so comment preservation is currently exercised for docs tokens and empty comment sets only.
- **Remaining scope:** Add ordinary comment and module-header formatting, remaining source syntax, explicit source-file CLI processing, and implicit workspace discovery. IR-386 adds source stdin processing.

### IR-386: Format source input from stdin in the CLI

- **Decision:** Route `sai fmt -` with the default or explicit `--stdin-kind=source` through the production source formatter. Read at most 16 MiB into memory; after crossing the limit, drain stdin without retaining the rest so the diagnostic reports the exact byte length. Map decoded diagnostic ranges back to raw input byte offsets, including a BOM and malformed UTF-8. Report valid-but-unsupported source shapes as `FEATURE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` with an empty `refused` list; reserve `refused` for inputs rejected by the formatter rules.
- **Reason:** CLI §13.6.7 defaults stdin to source and requires accurate source diagnostics and a result record. Formatter §14.2 defines the refusal set; a temporary document-builder gap for valid syntax must not be mislabeled as a syntax refusal. VFS source locations use decoded-text offsets, while CLI diagnostics name original input bytes.
- **Review:** Hostile review found and prompted fixes for decoded/raw offset mismatches, unsupported-shape result classification, and truncated size-limit values. Regressions cover BOM and malformed UTF-8 spans, exact over-limit byte counts, JSON result semantics, cancellation-aware reads, and nested diagnostic data serialization. The follow-up hostile review found no remaining actionable issues. The 40 focused formatter CLI tests, all 149 `sai_cli` tests, full workspace tests, warning-denied workspace Clippy, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm draining an oversized stdin stream to EOF is the preferred behavior to report its exact byte length, and confirm that a valid but temporarily unsupported syntax reports a feature diagnostic while remaining absent from `refused`.
- **Remaining scope:** IR-387 adds explicit `.sai` file and directory-selected source formatting. Implicit workspace discovery, ordinary comment and module-header formatting, and other unsupported source syntax remain.

### IR-387: Format explicitly selected and directory-discovered source files

- **Decision:** Route `.sai` inputs selected by path or directory traversal through `format_source`. Honor `--check`, report source refusal and valid unsupported-shape results consistently with stdin, and atomically replace changed files while preserving permissions. Retain one parent-directory capability per canonical parent directory, verify selected file identity after opening, and retain only the top `--max-errors` source diagnostics while preserving the full error count and deterministic order. Drain oversized files without retaining excess bytes so size diagnostics report the exact input length.
- **Reason:** CLI §13.6.7 specifies source formatting for selected files and directory traversal, including check behavior and atomic writes. Reusing the verified library entry point keeps stdin and path formatting consistent. Shared parent capabilities bound descriptor use when many explicit inputs share a directory; identity checks prevent a replaced path from being formatted or overwritten as though it were the originally selected file. Bounded diagnostic retention makes `--max-errors` effective before conversion into CLI diagnostics. Exact size reporting requires reading through EOF after the limit is crossed.
- **Review:** Tests cover explicit source `--check` and atomic formatting, directory traversal, refusal versus unsupported-shape JSON behavior, exact oversized-file length, source diagnostic limits, and shared parent capabilities. The first hostile review found that explicit files opened one parent handle each and that all source diagnostics were converted before applying `--max-errors`; both findings were fixed and covered by regressions. Follow-up hostile review found no actionable issues. `cargo test --workspace`, the 44 focused formatter CLI tests, warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that draining an oversized selected file to EOF to report its exact size is acceptable, and that valid-but-unsupported syntax should produce `FEATURE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` while remaining absent from `refused`. Confirm shared parent-directory handles plus file-identity checks are the right balance for safe atomic writes and descriptor use, and that diagnostic truncation should preserve the total count and deterministic first errors.
- **Remaining scope:** Implement implicit workspace discovery and the formatter's remaining source syntax, especially ordinary comments and module-header docs.

### IR-388: Discover and format implicit workspace inputs

- **Decision:** When `sai fmt` has no explicit path or stdin marker, resolve the nearest package from `--directory` or the current directory. Traverse local `from path` edges in `requires` declarations for each visited package, and traverse `requires test` path edges only for the root package; never resolve or fetch Git packages. Read external path-package manifests only to continue that graph, and format only the root package and packages whose canonical directories are inside the root package. Select each in-workspace `sai.package` and every `.sai` file under its declared source directory, including files inside hidden and nested `out` directories because the contract says every file below that source directory. Do not follow symbolic links while walking source directories. Report invalid package paths and incomplete manifests rather than treating omitted declarations as a complete graph, but continue formatting inputs discoverable from valid declarations. External-manifest diagnostics refer to the workspace root manifest and omit external source locations. Bound retained discovery diagnostics by `--max-errors` while preserving exact error totals. Snapshot package manifests and the selected input set, acquire locks for every owning package (including every package aliasing one canonical manifest target), and revalidate before writing.
- **Reason:** CLI §13.6.7 requires no-path formatting for the root and transitive workspace members, and §13.2.1 defines package discovery from the starting directory. Package contract §1.6 constrains source paths and permits artifact packages without a source directory; its dependency graph ignores test-only requirements of dependency packages, while root test requirements belong to the root graph. External local dependencies can point back into the workspace transitively, so reading their manifests is necessary to find all members; modifying them or exposing their paths in diagnostics would exceed the specified root/workspace selection. Hidden and nested `out` exclusions belong to explicit directory search, while implicit source selection says every `.sai` file below the declared source directory. Bounded retention protects parser and diagnostic work from large malformed manifests without losing the total error count. Package ownership must come from the logical package directory because the physical target of a manifest symlink can lie outside the package, and a shared target can represent several independently locked packages.
- **Follow-up decision:** Keep the first `--max-errors` discovery issues under the same path/span/code/message ordering used by final diagnostics. Represent successful manifest reads by SHA-256 fingerprints rather than retaining all file contents; read a bounded manifest prefix only when a retained diagnostic needs a source location, and verify its fingerprint before using the span. Retain logical package ownership and a bounded read error even if a workspace manifest cannot be read. Associate every selected source file with the package that declared each containing source directory as well as its nearest package root, and lock all owners. For a symlinked workspace manifest, also lock the nearest package containing its physical target when one exists. Before formatting, require each selected manifest's bytes to match every in-workspace discovery fingerprint; refuse the manifest if a digest is missing, oversized, or different. Use the workspace root as the JSON path base and classify invalid external package paths as `INVALID_PACKAGE_PATH`. During workspace formatting, preserve each selected manifest's logical-path-to-canonical-target mapping before physical-path deduplication and compare those mappings with discovery before writing; reject mismatches so retries can rediscover a stable graph. Use the logical source path for successful human progress output and diagnostics, including package-lock failures on external target roots.
- **Follow-up reason:** Discovery order is not output order, so retaining by traversal order can omit a diagnostic that should be shown. The workspace root gives stable package-relative JSON references regardless of the process working directory. Fingerprints keep revalidation proportional to the number of visited manifests rather than their aggregate contents; the parser and diagnostic renderer retain at most one manifest body at a time. A file may be selected by an outer package's source directory while residing beneath a nested package, so a failed outer lock must prevent writes to that file too. Logical manifest paths avoid exposing canonical targets of external symlinks. Physical-path deduplication can hide that one logical package manifest was retargeted to another already-selected manifest; retaining each binding through both selection passes detects that race even when the selected physical file set appears unchanged. A canonical target may also belong to a separate package, whose lock must be held before writing the shared manifest. A failed discovery read must contribute an error and signature change so it cannot silently produce a successful incomplete run. Human output uses logical paths even when the canonical target belongs to another package.
- **Review:** Tests cover root and transitive member discovery through an external local package, artifact packages without guessed source directories, cycles, missing local paths, malformed manifests, invalid source paths alongside valid local dependencies, hidden and nested `out` source files, `--check`, workspace lock creation, single-owner and shared-target manifest symlinks, and keeping external package source files untouched. New regressions verify final-order `--max-errors`, workspace-relative JSON error paths, `INVALID_PACKAGE_PATH` for invalid external paths without exposing external locations, lock rejection when a selected manifest target is not in the discovery snapshot, detection of a retargeted manifest that deduplicates with a member manifest, target-package lock acquisition for a symlinked manifest, and safe human progress paths for external manifest symlinks. The 68 focused formatter CLI tests, bounded package-parser test, formatting checks, warning-denied workspace Clippy, and full workspace suite passed before the latest two review fixes; their focused manifest tests pass, and full revalidation is in progress. Hostile review identified four P2 findings—retention order, JSON path bases, invalid external path classification, and aggregate manifest-body retention—and subsequent passes identified selection-time manifest retargeting, successful progress path leakage, unreported transient read errors, and missing physical-target package locks. Fixes and regression coverage are in place; final hostile re-review is pending.
- **Latest review update:** The final fingerprint check now refuses selected workspace manifests if their bytes differ from discovery or if discovery could not capture a digest. A source-change regression covers both changed bytes and missing fingerprints. Target-package lock failures now use the logical workspace manifest path, with regression coverage that checks JSON redaction and verifies that no write occurred. The additional adversarial findings around transient read failures, target lock acquisition, and target-path leakage have corresponding fixes and tests; rerun the complete formatter suite and workspace suite after this fingerprint guard.
- **Final review:** This supersedes the pending verification note above. The final hostile pass found no actionable issues. All 72 focused formatter CLI tests, the full workspace test suite, warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass with the fingerprint guard and logical lock-error paths included.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm the choice to read external local manifests without formatting them and to report their errors against the workspace root manifest; follow root test-only path requirements but ignore those of dependency packages; preserve total error counts while bounding retained diagnostics; select hidden and nested `out` `.sai` files below a declared source directory; and skip symbolic links during source traversal. Confirm that a manifest symlink shared by multiple packages should be formatted once while acquiring every owning package lock.
- **Additional human review:** Confirm that a symlinked in-workspace manifest that targets another package's `sai.package` should acquire locks for both its logical workspace owner and the physical target package. It is still formatted as the logically selected workspace manifest, while results and diagnostics use its workspace path.
- **Remaining scope:** Add the formatter's remaining source syntax, especially ordinary comments, module-header docs, and `use` declarations.

### IR-389: Format module headers

- **Decision:** Format the `DOC_INNER` lines in a module header at column 1, trimming trailing ASCII spaces and tabs. Format the contextual `unsafe module` declaration on its own line. Separate module docs from `unsafe module`, and the module header from the first item, with exactly one blank line. Permit a header-only file and keep an empty file empty.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.6.4–14.6.5 define the module-header order, line placement, and blank-line boundaries. The implementation validates the direct token sequence and contextual spelling before emitting it so malformed or recovered header shapes remain fail-closed. Ordinary comment and shebang trivia and `use` declarations remain unsupported.
- **Review:** Tests cover docs-only and `unsafe module`-only headers, multiple module-doc lines, the blank line between docs and the declaration, separation from the first item, trailing doc whitespace, header-only files, empty files, reparsing, syntax preservation, and fixed-point output. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 291 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that trailing ASCII spaces and tabs should be removed from module-doc text, and that module-doc lines stay consecutive while blank lines separate module docs from `unsafe module` and the module header from the first item.
- **Remaining scope:** Add ordinary comments and shebangs, demoted docs, `use` declarations, and the formatter's other unsupported source syntax.

### IR-390: Format and order top-level use declarations

- **Decision:** Move top-level `use` declarations after the module header and before other items. Keep private imports before `pub use` re-exports; sort each group by the canonical declaration text without visibility, docs, or attributes, using a stable sort and retaining duplicates. Sort selective import items by original name and then alias, with unaliased names first. Keep `UseItems` flat without inner brace spaces when it fits; when it breaks, print one item per line without commas. Move declaration docs and attributes with their use declaration. Continue to refuse ordinary comments and shebangs.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.6.4 and 14.7.2 define the use-block position, grouping, ordering, duplicate behavior, line layout, and prefix movement. Syntax-preservation normalization orders declarations by visibility and canonical key with stable ties, and normalizes selective items by their full syntax signatures. The builder validates use-item separators to accept both comma-separated input and formatter-produced newline-separated lists.
- **Review:** Tests cover private/public ordering, canonical path ordering, selective item and alias ordering, moving declarations from below other items, placement after module docs, width breaks without commas, prefix movement, stable duplicate retention, reparsing, normalized syntax preservation, and fixed-point output. The first hostile pass found two issues: moved imports could create false blank lines between ordinary items, and normalization could hide reordering of equal-key declarations with different prefixes. Both fixes have regression tests; re-review found no remaining actionable findings. All 298 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm stable retention of duplicate imports, unaliased-before-aliased tie ordering, and that ordinary comments and shebangs remain unsupported until their attachment rules are implemented.
- **Remaining scope:** Implement ordinary comments and shebangs, demoted docs, and the formatter's other unsupported source syntax.

### IR-391: Format detached file-header and EOF comments

- **Decision:** Format line and block comment trivia only when it forms a detached prefix before the first top-level syntax node or occurs as leading trivia of `EOF`. Keep file-header and EOF comments at column 1, trim trailing spaces and tabs on every comment line, normalize source line endings to LF, and preserve at most one blank line between comments and neighboring content. Treat demoted doc comments as ordinary comments in these supported positions. Keep attached comments, inline trailing comments, nested comments, and shebangs fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.6.1–14.6.5 distinguish trivia attachment, detached comments, EOF placement, line trimming, and blank-line retention. Restricting the prefix to detached comments prevents an attached comment from being left behind when a leading `use` is sorted; accepting EOF comments only from EOF leading trivia preserves the CST's attachment rule. The remaining comment positions require token-aware attachment during item and list layout, so they stay unsupported until that machinery exists.
- **Review:** Tests cover detached line and multiline-block file headers, use sorting with a detached header, module-header placement, comment-only files, EOF comments, demoted EOF docs, trailing-whitespace trimming, blank-line retention, rejection of attached/nested/trailing comments, reparsing, syntax preservation, and fixed-point output. Hostile review found no actionable issues and confirmed demoted docs moved to EOF leading trivia follow the CST attachment rules. All 301 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace suite pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that this incremental slice should accept only detached file-header comments and EOF-leading comments, while attached, trailing, nested, and shebang comments remain fail-closed until their token-aware layout is implemented.

### IR-392: Preserve comments attached to top-level declarations

- **Decision:** Preserve own-line comments attached to top-level declarations and trailing line or block comments at top-level syntax boundaries, including the module header and `use` declarations. Keep attached comments with their declaration when `use` declarations are sorted. Print trailing line comments as line suffixes; print trailing block comments verbatim after one separating space. Continue to reject detached inter-item comments, nested comments, comments inside a top-level declaration, and shebangs.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.6.1–14.6.4 require attached comments to move with their token, line comments to finish that token's output line, block comments to keep their text and placement, and detached comments to stay in sequence position when units move. The source document builder can apply these rules safely at top-level syntax boundaries while refusing comments whose token-relative layout is not yet implemented.
- **Review:** Focused regressions cover attached comments moving with sorted uses, trailing line and single-line or multiline block comments, EOF placement after a trailing comment, reparsing, and fixed-point output. CLI regressions verify comment formatting through stdin and keep unsupported-shape reporting distinct from syntax refusals. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 303 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace suite, including doc tests, pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that top-level attached comments should move with declarations through use sorting, trailing line comments should force a line ending, and trailing block comments should retain their text at the top-level declaration boundary. Detached inter-item comments, nested comments, and shebangs remain fail-closed.
- **Remaining scope:** Implement token-aware comments inside declarations and lists.

### IR-394: Preserve detached top-level comments

- **Decision:** Split each top-level leading comment group at its last blank-line boundary. Keep the detached prefix separate from the attached suffix. Emit detached comments before ordinary items as standalone sequence units; collect detached comments preceding `use` declarations in input order and print them before the sorted use block. Let source gaps determine blank lines around detached comment units, and do not count a trailing line comment as making its item multiline.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.6.1 and 14.6.4 define detached comments as position-preserving units and preserve blank lines only when present between units. Section 14.7.2 requires detached comments among uses to appear before the sorted use block, while attached comments travel with their declarations. Splitting at the final blank-line boundary handles a detached heading followed by an attached comment without moving either incorrectly.
- **Review:** Tests cover detached comments between ordinary items, a detached prefix followed by an attached comment, detached comment groups before sorted uses, source blank-line preservation, trailing line comments beside single-line items, reparsing, and fixed-point output. Hostile review found two P2 spacing defects: multiline detached comment units and trailing line comments could force unrequested blank lines. Both were fixed with regressions; follow-up hostile review found no actionable issues. All 306 `sai_fmt` tests, all 72 formatter CLI tests, and the full workspace suite, including doc tests, pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm splitting comment groups at the last blank-line boundary, preserving detached comments in sequence between ordinary items, and placing detached comments from `use` declarations in input order before the sorted use block.
- **Remaining scope:** Implement token-aware comments inside declarations and lists.

### IR-393: Preserve source shebangs

- **Decision:** Accept a single `SHEBANG` trivia piece at source offset zero and print it as the first line, preserving its text except for the formatter's trailing-whitespace trimming and LF line ending. Preserve a blank line after it only when the input has one before its first file content. Keep shebang-only files and shebangs before comment-only files formatable; misplaced or duplicate shebang trivia remains fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.2.1, 14.6.4, and 14.6.5 require the shebang to stay first, preserve its text, remove trailing whitespace, and keep the blank-line boundary before the next file part. The lexer attaches shebang trivia at the beginning of the source, so offset and attachment checks make the accepted shape explicit.
- **Review:** Tests cover trailing whitespace, CRLF normalization, no-blank and blank boundaries before declarations, detached comments and module docs, comment-only and shebang-only files, reparsing, and fixed-point output. The CLI regression verifies a shebang and trailing comment through stdin. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 304 `sai_fmt` tests, all 72 formatter CLI tests, and the full workspace suite pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that shebang lines should be trimmed only at the end, emitted first with LF endings, and followed by a blank line only when the original input has one before the first file content.
- **Remaining scope:** Implement detached inter-item comments and token-aware comments inside declarations and lists.

### IR-395: Preserve comments at declaration-list boundaries

- **Decision:** Preserve own-line comments attached to the first token of record/resource fields and enum variants, trailing comments attached to a member's final token, comments attached to separators removed by formatting, and comments attached to the list's closing brace. Rehome comments from a removed separator to the neighboring member specified by §14.6.1. Keep comments in source order, use the list element's indentation, and preserve blank-line gaps. Restrict list scanning to the brace interior so comments after the closing brace stay with the declaration. Continue to refuse comments attached to interior tokens and other token-relative positions that are not yet formatted.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.5.4 and 14.6.1–14.6.4 require comments to follow their owning token, including comments transferred from removed commas, use the indentation of the corresponding list element, and preserve blank lines between units. Restricting support to verified member boundaries, removed separators, and closing-brace trivia prevents comments inside types, attributes, and expressions from moving to the wrong location.
- **Review:** Regressions cover comments before record/resource fields and enum variants, detached blank-line groups, comments before closing braces including an otherwise empty list, trailing comments on final member tokens, comments on removed commas, comments after closing braces, and multiple comments after a multiline block comment. Comments inside members remain fail-closed. The first hostile review found that scanning a list also consumed the closing brace's trailing trivia; the scan now excludes trivia outside the braces, with line and block suffix regressions. The second hostile review found that a subsequent trailing comment could remain on the closing line of a multiline block comment; the formatter now forces the required break, and follow-up review found no actionable issues. All 310 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace tests pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comments attached to fields/variants and removed separators move with the correct member, that own-line comments before members and closing braces retain their blank-line placement, and that interior-token comments remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Add token-aware comments inside list elements and statements.

### IR-396: Preserve comments at block statement boundaries

- **Decision:** Preserve own-line comments attached to the first token of each block statement, trailing comments attached to its final token, and own-line comments attached to the block's closing brace. Apply the existing broken-brace layout and blank-line rules to these comments, including comment-only blocks. Force single-expression closure and `unsafe` blocks to use their broken form whenever boundary comments are present. Continue to refuse comments attached to an opening brace or interior statement tokens. Collect trivia only from the boundary tokens so nested blocks do not rescan every ancestor's entire subtree.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.6.1–14.6.4 require comments to follow their owning token, preserve their source order and blank-line gaps, and use the indentation of their statement. Section 14.8.1 permits closure and single-expression `unsafe` blocks to flatten only when they contain no comments. Boundary-token collection keeps nested block processing linear in the syntax tree size.
- **Review:** Regressions cover comments before and after statements, detached blank-line gaps, comments before closing braces, comment-only blocks, nested blocks, closures, and `unsafe` blocks. A separate refusal case keeps comments attached to interior statement tokens unsupported. Hostile review found that a comment-only closure did not force a break in a non-final member-chain segment; the comment check now precedes the empty-body fast path, with a regression for the chain layout. Follow-up hostile review found no actionable findings and confirmed that the non-final closure breaks the chain while a final comment-bearing closure may remain flat. All 311 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace test suite pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comments attached to statement boundaries stay with the statement, comments before `}` use statement indentation, and boundary comments force otherwise-flat closure and single-expression `unsafe` blocks to break.
- **Remaining scope:** Support comments attached to opening-brace and interior statement tokens, then continue through the remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-397: Preserve comments trailing opening braces

- **Decision:** Preserve trailing line and block comments attached to the opening brace of source blocks, record/resource field lists, and enum variant lists. Render them after `{` before the first body element, including comment-only empty blocks. If a multiline trailing block comment is followed by another comment on that opening token, place the later comment on its own line at body indentation; keep opening-brace leading comments and interior-statement comments fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.6.1–14.6.3 require trailing comments to stay after their token, multiline block comments to be emitted verbatim followed by a forced break, and own-line comments to use the indentation of the following element. Putting the forced break and subsequent comments inside the body indentation scope makes the output stable when the later comment is reattached to the first element on the next parse.
- **Review:** Regressions cover opening-brace line comments, single-line and multiline block comments, comments-only empty blocks, record fields, enum variants, a multiline comment followed by another comment, fixed-point output, and rejection of own-line comments before the opening brace. The first hostile review found that a comment after a multiline opening-brace comment was emitted at the outer indentation and failed fixed-point verification; subsequent comments now render inside the body indentation scope. Follow-up hostile review found no actionable findings. All 313 `sai_fmt` tests and full workspace tests, including doc tests, pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comments trailing `{` stay on its line, that comments following a multiline block comment move to their own line at body indentation, and that own-line comments before `{` remain unsupported for now.
- **Remaining scope:** Support own-line comments attached to opening braces and comments attached to interior statement tokens, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-398: Preserve own-line comments before block opening braces

- **Decision:** Preserve own-line comments attached to the opening brace of a `BLOCK`, placing each comment before the brace and the brace on its own line. Keep the support limited to source blocks; own-line comments before record/resource field-list braces and enum variant-list braces remain fail-closed. Avoid a duplicate line break when a function's final signature clause already precedes the body on its own line.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.6.1 and 14.6.3 require an own-line comment to remain before its owner token and require that token to start a new line. Restricting the accepted owner to `BLOCK` avoids silently widening comment support to other brace-list syntaxes whose layout has different declaration-header rules. Reusing the block's own forced line break after a function clause avoids an extra blank line inside the declaration.
- **Review:** Regressions cover a block with statements, an empty block, a function with a `where` clause, fixed-point output, and fail-closed record/enum opening-brace comments. Hostile review caught the initial accidental acceptance of record and enum opening comments; the leading-opening target was removed from non-block lists and both cases now have refusal regressions. Follow-up hostile review found no remaining actionable issues. All 314 `sai_fmt` tests and full workspace tests, including doc tests, pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that block-opening comments appear before `{` at the brace line's indentation, that the function-clause separator is not duplicated, and that field-list/variant-list opening comments remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Support comments attached to interior statement tokens, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-399: Preserve comments after `let` and `var` keywords

- **Decision:** Preserve trailing line and block comments attached to `LET_KW` and `VAR_KW`, between the keyword and its binding. A line comment or multiline block comment forces the binding onto the next line at statement indentation; a single-line block comment remains inline. Comments attached to other statement-interior tokens remain fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.6.1–14.6.2 require comments to retain their token association and order; line comments stay on the token's line, while multiline block comments are emitted verbatim and followed by a forced break. The binding uses statement indentation because it begins the same logical statement rather than a newly nested block.
- **Review:** Regressions cover single-line block comments, line comments, multiline block comments, both `let` and `var`, output fixed-point, and the previously supported statement-boundary comments. Hostile review confirmed that only trailing comments on `LET_KW`/`VAR_KW` are accepted, other interior token comments remain fail-closed, and comment-bearing closure/`unsafe` bodies do not become flat. All 315 `sai_fmt` tests and full workspace tests, including doc tests, pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that a binding after a line or multiline block comment should return to the statement's indentation rather than use an extra continuation indent, and that other statement-interior comments should remain unsupported until their token layouts are implemented.
- **Remaining scope:** Support comments attached to other statement-interior tokens and expressions, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-400: Preserve comments after `assert`

- **Decision:** Preserve trailing line and block comments attached to `ASSERT_KW` after the keyword and before the condition. A line comment or multiline block comment puts the condition on the next line at statement indentation; an optional message remains attached to the condition. Comments on other interior statement tokens remain fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §§14.6.1–14.6.2 keep comments with their token, emit line comments at the end of that token's line, and force a break after a multiline block comment. Keeping the optional message after the condition preserves the statement's documented `assert expr, message` structure.
- **Review:** Regressions cover inline block comments, line comments, multiline block comments, optional messages, and fixed-point output. Hostile review found no actionable issues and confirmed that only comments on `ASSERT_KW` are accepted by this slice. All 316 `sai_fmt` tests and full workspace tests, including doc tests, pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that the condition after an `assert` comment should use statement indentation and that optional messages remain on the condition's line.
- **Remaining scope:** Support comments attached to other statement-interior tokens and expressions, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-401: Preserve comments after control-flow keywords

- **Decision:** Preserve single-line block comments attached to `RETURN_KW`, `BREAK_KW`, and `CONTINUE_KW` between the keyword and its following value or label. Keep the value or label on the keyword's line. Comments with a line break in this between-token position remain fail-closed; comments attached to the transfer expression's final token continue to use the statement-boundary layout.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.9.8 keeps `return` and `break` values on the keyword line, and §14.6.1 requires comments to remain attached to their tokens. A same-line block comment can be emitted between the keyword and next token without introducing a line break. Refusing line or multiline comments in that slot avoids changing token attachment or transferring a value to another statement; trailing comments after a value-less transfer remain safe under the already-supported statement-boundary rules.
- **Review:** Regressions cover a return value, a labeled break with a value, a labeled continue, canonical spacing, fixed-point output, and a multiline trailing comment after a bare return. Hostile review found no actionable issues and confirmed that only comments attached to the three transfer keywords enter this path. All 318 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace suite, including doc tests, pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comments immediately after `return`, `break`, and `continue` should be accepted inline only when they are single-line block comments, while comments with line breaks in the same token gap remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Support comments attached to other statement-interior tokens and expressions, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-402: Preserve comments after assignment operators

- **Decision:** Preserve single-line block comments attached to the `=` in `let` and `var` statements and to the selected assignment operator, placing each comment before the right-hand side. Keep comments with line breaks in those operator-to-operand positions, and comments on other statement-interior tokens, fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.9.7 defines the right-hand-side layout alternatives after `=` and assignment operators. A single-line block comment can stay inline without changing those alternatives or moving the expression; line and multiline comments force a separate continuation layout, so this slice refuses them until that placement is implemented and verified.
- **Review:** Regressions cover `let`, `var`, and compound assignment comments, canonical spacing, output fixed-point, rejection of line and multiline comments after operators, and refusal of comments on other statement-interior tokens. Hostile review found no actionable issues and confirmed that operator ownership is limited to `let`/`var` `=` tokens and the AST-selected assignment operator. All 320 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace suite, including doc tests, pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that assignment operators should accept inline single-line block comments only for now, while comments containing line breaks and comments on other interior tokens remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Support comments attached to other statement-interior tokens and expressions, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-403: Preserve comments after binding type colons

- **Decision:** Preserve single-line block comments attached to the type colon of `let` and `var` statements between `:` and the formatted type. Refuse comments with line breaks in that position; continue to keep comments on other type and statement-interior tokens fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.8.2 places the optional type annotation between a binding pattern and its initializer. Associating comments only with the direct colon token in a `let` or `var` statement keeps them out of nested type syntax; accepting only single-line block comments retains this position without introducing an unverified type continuation layout.
- **Review:** Regressions cover `let` and `var` annotations, combined comments after the keyword, type colon, and initializer operator, canonical spacing, and fixed-point output. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 321 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace suite, including doc tests, pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comments after a binding type colon should be accepted inline only when they are single-line block comments, while multiline comments and comments inside type syntax remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Support comments attached to other statement-interior tokens and expressions, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-404: Preserve comments after `if` keywords

- **Decision:** Preserve single-line block comments attached to an `if` keyword before its condition for block-leading `if` expressions, including nested `else if` branches. Keep comments attached to `else` and other interior control-flow tokens fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.9.8 keeps an `if` condition on the keyword's line. A single-line block comment preserves that placement. Nested `else if` keywords have their own token ownership, so the formatter collects their trailing comments from the nested `IF_EXPR` without attaching them to the outer `if`.
- **Review:** Regressions cover ordinary conditions, `if let`, nested `else if`, canonical spacing, and fixed-point output. The first hostile review found that nested `else if` keyword comments were rejected; rendering now collects that nested keyword's trailing comments and the follow-up hostile review found no remaining actionable findings. Comments on `else` remain fail-closed. All 322 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace suite, including doc tests, pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comments immediately after `if` should remain inline before the condition in both direct and nested `else if` branches, while comments attached to `else` remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Support comments attached to other statement-interior tokens and expressions, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-405: Preserve comments after `while` keywords

- **Decision:** Preserve single-line block comments attached to a `while` keyword before its condition for labeled and unlabeled `while` expressions, including `while let`. Keep line and multiline comments in that position, and comments attached to other control-flow tokens, fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.9.8 keeps a `while` condition on its keyword line. The label, when present, precedes `while`, so the formatter resolves the direct `WHILE_KW` token from the statement's `WhileExpr` instead of assuming it is the statement's first token. Single-line block comments preserve the condition's placement without changing the parse.
- **Review:** Regression covers an unlabeled condition and a labeled `while let`, canonical spacing, output fixed-point, and label behavior. Hostile review found no actionable issues and confirmed that the direct `WHILE_KW` lookup handles both labeled and unlabeled forms while unsupported comment shapes remain fail-closed. All 323 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace suite, including doc tests, pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comments immediately after labeled or unlabeled `while` should stay inline before the condition, and that comments containing line breaks remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Support comments attached to other statement-interior tokens and expressions, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-406: Preserve comments after `for` keywords

- **Decision:** Preserve single-line block comments attached to a `for` keyword before the loop pattern for labeled and unlabeled `for` and `for await` expressions. Keep line and multiline comments in that position, and comments attached to other loop tokens, fail-closed.
- **Reason:** Formatter §14.9.8 keeps the `for` pattern and iterable on the loop header line. A comment after `for` can precede either the pattern or the optional `await` modifier without changing that header. The formatter resolves the direct `FOR_KW` token from the statement's `ForExpr` so a label before the keyword does not change comment ownership.
- **Review:** Regressions cover an unlabeled `for`, a labeled `for await`, canonical spacing, and output fixed-point. Hostile review found no actionable issues and confirmed that direct `FOR_KW` ownership handles both label states and the await modifier while line-breaking comments remain fail-closed. All 324 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace suite, including doc tests, pass; warning-denied workspace Clippy, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comments immediately after labeled or unlabeled `for` should stay before the optional `await` modifier or pattern, and that comments containing line breaks remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Support comments attached to other statement-interior tokens and expressions, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-407: Preserve comments after `loop` keywords

- **Decision:** Preserve single-line block comments attached to `LOOP_KW` before the body for labeled and unlabeled loop statements and for loop expressions used directly as `let`/`var` initializers or assignment right-hand sides. Keep line and multiline comments in those positions, and loop comments nested in composite expressions, fail-closed.
- **Reason:** The loop body follows the keyword directly, and a same-line block comment can stay in that header position without changing the body layout. Initializers and assignment right-hand sides use the same expression renderer, so their root loop can preserve the comment without teaching arbitrary nested-expression builders to rehome it.
- **Review:** Regressions cover labeled and unlabeled loop statements, initializer and assignment loop expressions, canonical spacing, fixed-point output, line and multiline comment refusal in both statement and initializer positions, and fail-closed behavior when a loop with an attached comment is nested inside a composite expression. The first hostile review found the missing initializer case; the route was added and follow-up hostile review found no remaining actionable findings. All 328 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace test suite, including doc tests, pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that loop-keyword comments should be accepted for statement-leading loops and root initializer/assignment loop expressions, while comments nested deeper in composite expressions and comments with line breaks remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Thread token-owned comments through recursively nested expression renderers, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-408: Preserve comments after `match` keywords

- **Decision:** Preserve single-line block comments attached to `MATCH_KW` before the scrutinee for match statements and for match expressions used directly as `let`/`var` initializers or assignment right-hand sides. Keep line and multiline comments in those positions, and match comments nested inside composite expressions, fail-closed.
- **Reason:** The scrutinee immediately follows the `match` keyword, so a same-line block comment can remain in that header without altering the match-arm brace-list layout. Root initializer and assignment expressions can use the existing keyword-comment path, while nested expressions keep their own token ownership until their renderers accept explicit comment inputs.
- **Review:** Regressions cover statement, initializer, and assignment contexts, canonical spacing, fixed-point output, line and multiline comment refusal, and fail-closed behavior for comments on nested match expressions. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 331 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace test suite, including doc tests, pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comments immediately after `match` should remain inline before the scrutinee only for statement-leading and root initializer/assignment match expressions, while line-breaking comments and deeper nested match expressions remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Thread token-owned comments through recursively nested expression renderers and individual match-arm tokens, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-409: Preserve comments after `unsafe` keywords

- **Decision:** Preserve single-line block comments attached to `UNSAFE_KW` before the block for unsafe expressions used as statements or directly as `let`/`var` initializers or assignment right-hand sides. Keep line and multiline comments in those positions, and unsafe comments nested inside composite expressions, fail-closed.
- **Reason:** The block immediately follows the `unsafe` keyword. Keeping a single-line block comment in that gap preserves both the canonical flat layout for a fitting single-expression body and the broken layout used when the body or its comments force a break.
- **Review:** Regressions cover statement, initializer, and assignment contexts, canonical flat spacing, fixed-point output, line and multiline comment refusal, and fail-closed behavior when an unsafe expression with a keyword comment is nested inside a composite expression. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 334 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace test suite, including doc tests, pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comments immediately after `unsafe` should stay inline before the block only when they are single-line block comments in statement-leading and root initializer/assignment expressions; line-breaking comments and deeper nested expressions remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Thread token-owned comments through recursively nested expression renderers, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-410: Preserve comments after `async` keywords

- **Decision:** Preserve single-line block comments attached to `ASYNC_KW` before the optional `move` modifier and body for async block expressions used as statements or directly as `let`/`var` initializers or assignment right-hand sides. Keep line and multiline comments in those positions, and async comments nested inside composite expressions, fail-closed.
- **Reason:** In `async move { … }`, `move` belongs after `async` and its comment, so rendering the comment in that gap retains keyword order. The block still uses the existing layout that forces its body onto the documented broken lines.
- **Review:** Regressions cover statement, initializer, and assignment contexts with and without `move`, canonical spacing, fixed-point output, line and multiline comment refusal, and fail-closed behavior for async blocks nested inside composite expressions. Hostile review found no actionable issues. All 337 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace test suite, including doc tests, pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comments immediately after `async` should stay before the optional `move` modifier only as single-line block comments in statement-leading and root initializer/assignment expressions; line-breaking comments and deeper nested expressions remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Thread token-owned comments through recursively nested expression renderers, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-411: Preserve comments after `region` introducers

- **Decision:** Preserve single-line block comments attached to the `region` introducer before the region name for region expressions used as statements or directly as `let`/`var` initializers or assignment right-hand sides. Keep line and multiline comments in those positions, and region comments nested inside composite expressions, fail-closed.
- **Reason:** The region name is another `LIDENT`, so the formatter identifies the introducer by both token kind and exact token text. A single-line block comment can stay between the introducer and name without changing the region body layout.
- **Review:** Regressions cover statement, initializer, and assignment contexts, region-name separation, canonical spacing, fixed-point output, refusal of line and multiline comments, and fail-closed behavior for nested region expressions. Hostile review confirmed that keyword selection does not confuse the introducer with a region name that is also an `LIDENT`; no actionable findings remain. All 340 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace test suite, including doc tests, pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comments after the `region` introducer should stay before the region name only as single-line block comments in statement-leading and root initializer/assignment expressions; line-breaking comments and deeper nested expressions remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Thread token-owned comments through recursively nested expression renderers, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-412: Preserve comments after closure `fn` keywords

- **Decision:** Preserve single-line block comments attached to a closure's `FN_KW` before its parameter list for closure expressions used as statements or directly as `let`/`var` initializers or assignment right-hand sides. In `move fn`, keep the comment after `fn` and before the parameters. Keep line and multiline comments in those positions, and closure comments nested inside composite expressions, fail-closed.
- **Reason:** `FN_KW` follows the optional `move` token and immediately precedes the parameter list. Selecting that direct token preserves `move fn` order and keeps the comment associated with the keyword rather than the modifier or parameters.
- **Review:** Regressions cover statement, initializer, and assignment contexts with and without `move`, canonical spacing, fixed-point output, refusal of line and multiline comments, and fail-closed behavior for closure comments inside call arguments. Hostile review inspected the separate trailing-closure header path and found no actionable issues. All 343 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace test suite, including doc tests, pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
- **Human review:** **Review required:** Confirm that comments after closure `fn` should remain before the parameter list, following `move` when present, only as single-line block comments in statement-leading and root initializer/assignment expressions; line-breaking comments and deeper nested expressions remain unsupported.
- **Remaining scope:** Thread token-owned comments through recursively nested expression renderers and individual closure parameter tokens, then continue through remaining comment-bearing source syntax.

### IR-413: Preserve comments after `else` keywords

- **Decision:** Preserve single-line block comments attached to `ELSE_KW` before the block or nested `if` that follows it, and before the block of a `let … else` statement. This includes `if` expressions formatted recursively inside composite expressions. Keep line comments and multiline block comments in these positions fail-closed.
- **Reason:** `else` directly owns the branch that follows it, and the existing `if` expression renderer is shared by statements and recursively formatted expressions. Reading comments from the direct `ELSE_KW` token preserves the branch association for both `else if` and `let … else` without reassigning comments to the following block or nested `IF_KW`.
- **Review:** Regressions cover `if`/`else`, chained `else if`, `let … else`, root initializer and assignment expressions, nested call arguments, canonical spacing, and fixed-point output. Rejection cases first verify that parsing succeeds, then assert that line and multiline comments are rejected by the formatter. Two hostile reviews found no actionable issues, including review of comment ownership and nested expression rendering. All 345 `sai_fmt` tests and the full workspace tests, including doc tests, pass; workspace Clippy with warnings denied, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass.
